# Changelog

All notable changes to loom-core will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **Mills degraded-mode embedder policy evaluator** (`pkg/mills/council/degraded_policy.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/degraded_policy.go`): added a structured council/pipeline decision path for embedding provider overload, open circuit breakers, query degradation, and document fallback-vector signals so Mills can explicitly continue in degraded mode or fail closed before autonomous stages.
- **Mills local docs-guardrail gate before MR creation** (`pkg/mills/gates/docs_guardrail.go`, registered in `gates.Default()`, added to `post_implement_gate` in `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go` + the `mills-default-pipeline.yaml` template): Mills-authored MRs !1111/!1112 both escalated ($2.02 burned) because the implement stage wrote code with no CHANGELOG/docs update, so the repo's `guardrails:docs-cli` CI job failed and `ci_watch` escalated on the terminal pipeline — the failure surfaced only after a full pipeline + MR + CI round. A new deterministic post-implement gate now replicates `scripts/ci/check_docs_guardrails.sh` locally: a code-facing change (`cmd/`, `internal/`, `pkg/`, `scripts/`, `Makefile`, `go.mod`/`go.sum`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/`) with no accompanying doc update (`README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `AGENTS.md`, or `docs/`) fails **retryably** (never terminal), with a remediation-oriented reason that the runner threads into the gate-retry implement prompt via `StageRetryContext` — so the retry adds a CHANGELOG entry instead of the run paying for a doomed MR + CI round. Test-only and generated artifacts (`*_test.go`, `/testdata/`, `*_golden.*`, `/dist/`, `*.min.js/css`, `*.snap`, `*_mock.go`, `/mocks/`, `test_*.py`) are excluded exactly as the CI script excludes them, absolute spawn paths (k8s/harvester) are classified via path-segment suffix matching, and a `[skip-docs-check]` commit-message token opts out. The existing `implementDocsDiscipline` prompt (which already instructs the spawn to add a `## [Unreleased]` CHANGELOG entry in Keep a Changelog style) is the belt to this gate's suspenders. Table-driven tests cover code-without-docs → fail, code+CHANGELOG → pass, docs-only/test-only/generated-only → pass, `[skip-docs-check]` → pass, absolute paths, and registry wiring.
- **Mills telemetry degraded-mode health policy evaluator** (`pkg/mills/gates/health_policy.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/health_policy_test.go`): a deterministic `EvaluateTelemetryHealthPolicy` turns a `TelemetryHealthSnapshot` (pipeline failure rate, gate flake rate, judge-unparseable rate, retry-burn rate, queue depth, active backend incidents) plus a `TelemetryHealthPolicy` threshold set into a machine-readable `TelemetryHealthVerdict` — pass/degraded, a stable `code` (`ok`/`stale_telemetry`/`threshold_breach`/`backend_incident`), a bounded severity (`ok`/`warning`/`critical`), the derived `telemetry.MillsOperationalState`, per-signal checks, degraded dependencies, and active incidents. Council and pipeline decisioning can now drop into degraded mode deterministically when telemetry is stale, a policy threshold is breached, or a backend incident is active, rather than trusting unhealthy telemetry as a normal operating signal. `DefaultTelemetryHealthPolicy` ships conservative thresholds (10m max telemetry age, 25% pipeline failure, 10% gate flake, 5% judge-unparseable, 35% retry burn, queue depth 25).
- **Per-model cost + reliability attribution in stage telemetry** (`pkg/mills/store/migrations/013_stage_model_backend.sql`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_telemetry.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/{runner,dispatcher}.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/telemetry_cache.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/.../TelemetryPanel.svelte`, `telemetryHelpers.ts`): Mills now runs council/pipeline work across heterogeneous backends (local flexinfer qwen, litellm→OpenRouter kimi-k3/k2.7-code, anthropic, spawn agents) and the litellm commit made provider-reported `usage.cost` flow into stage costs — but the economics question ("what does each model tier cost and how reliable is it") had no surface. `stage_results` gains nullable `model` + `backend` columns (migration 013; historical rows bucket as "unknown" so totals stay complete), populated at the write path: the research worker surfaces the resolved FlexInfer model + `flexinfer`, the spawn worker surfaces the resolved agent/model + `spawn`. `GET /api/mills/telemetry/stages` gains a `model_economics` array (calls, total cost USD, error count/rate, avg duration per `(model, backend)` tier, sorted by spend), flowing verbatim through the HUD mills proxy. The Mill Telemetry panel gains a "Model economics" table (cost spend-bar, error-rate flag), null-normalized at the fetch boundary and rendered in sample mode via the extended fixture. A short-TTL (8s, per-window) in-memory cache on the telemetry stages handler collapses a burst of HUD polls into one aggregation while keeping sub-tick freshness.
- **Read-only Atlassian connector: mcp-jira Cloud fix + write gates + mcp-confluence deployment** (`cmd/mcp-jira/main.go`, `cmd/mcp-confluence/main.go`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `k8s/base/servers/{jira,confluence,gateway}/`): `jira_search` moves off `/rest/api/2/search` — removed on Atlassian Cloud in 2025, so the deployed server's search was dead-on-arrival — to `/rest/api/2/search/jql` with explicit fields (`JIRA_LEGACY_SEARCH=1` restores the old endpoint for Server/DC). The registry-advertised-but-never-implemented `jira_list_projects`/`jira_get_project` now exist, plus `jira_myself` as an auth probe. Write tools on both servers register only behind explicit env (`JIRA_MCP_WRITE_ENABLED=1`, `CONFLUENCE_MCP_WRITE_ENABLED=1` — mirroring `ICC_MCP_WRITE_ENABLED`), so the loom-hub fleet is read-only by construction; the gateway registry's jira `always_allow` drops the comment/transition write tools it had been allowlisting. mcp-confluence (previously built but unregistered and undeployed) gains a registry entry and loom-hub manifests mirroring jira; its Deployment schedules once `CONFLUENCE_*` keys land in `loom-secrets`. Makefile: mcp-jira was missing from `MCP_SERVERS`. Registration gating and Cloud-vs-legacy search-path selection are pinned by httptest-backed unit tests.
- **Mills LiteLLM backend for council reviewer lenses** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go` buildLiteLLMClient + per-lens binding, `config.go` `LITELLM_PROXY_URL`/`LOOM_MILLS_LITELLM_KEY`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go` provider-cost passthrough): council reviewers were hard-wired to the FlexInfer proxy, which does not federate the cluster LiteLLM gateway — so frontier open models routed via OpenRouter (kimi-k3, live through gitops!423 and verified end-to-end 2026-07-17: clean leading JSON envelopes, reasoning in a separate field, ≈$0.017/judge-shaped call) were unreachable by Mills. A reviewer lens with `backend: litellm` now binds a LiteLLM-backed client (same `/v1/chat/completions` + bearer protocol; lens model rides through, e.g. `or/kimi-k3`); a litellm lens without a configured gateway gets the fake reviewer so the misconfiguration surfaces in council notes instead of 404ing. Cost accounting prefers the provider-reported `usage.cost` (real OpenRouter charge) over the flat local-tier estimate, and litellm lenses attribute as frontier (not local) spend via the existing `IsLocal()` contract.
- **Mills ghost-spark auto-close** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go` SweepGhostSparks, `pkg/mills/store/dao_backlog.go` ListEscalatedWithMR, metric `mills_ghost_sparks_closed_total`): 91 of 141 backlog items sat in `escalated` (live 2026-07-17), many because merge-when-pipeline-succeeds merged the MR *after* the run escalated at the merge stage — the existing issue auto-close only fires when a later run succeeds, so nothing ever reaped them and a requeue would have redone merged work. A capped reconciler sweep (10 GitLab lookups/tick, oldest-first, home-repo items only — cross-repo MR IIDs belong to another project's sequence and must not resolve against the home project) now transitions such items escalated→merged behind the aggregate claim-version fence, closes their escalation issue, and counts abandoned-MR leftovers; non-resolving candidates sit out a re-check cooldown so they can't starve the drain.
- **Mills per-stage spawn agent override** (`pkg/mills/policy.go` `pipeline.stage_agents` + AgentForStage, SpawnWorker.AgentFor in `pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`): pr_self_review costs nearly as much as implement ($12.34 vs $17.59 in the 7d window) because every spawn-driven stage runs the same global agent. Policy can now pin an agent per stage (closed claude-code/codex/gemini vocabulary, parity-pinned against `pkg/mills/worker.ValidateAgentType` by test); precedence is `LOOM_MILLS_SPAWN_AGENT` env (break-glass) > policy map > default, and the wiring log reports each stage's effective agent. Behavior is byte-identical with an empty map.
- **HUD requeue action** (`internal/hud/frontend/.../PipelineRunDetail.svelte`, `requeuePipelineRun` in `stores/mills.svelte.ts`): escalated runs get a confirm-then-requeue button in the run drawer (admin-token gated, same machinery as other mutations), with the operator's decisions rendered inline — 201 "requeued as <run id>", the ghost-spark 409 "state is merged" phrased as already-completed, 401/403 mapped to the shared admin-token hint. Outcomes are discarded if the drawer moved to a different run while the request was in flight.
- **Council telemetry degraded-mode status surfacing** (`pkg/mills/council/status.go`, `pkg/mills/council/status_test.go`): council status projections now expose stable degraded-mode reason codes, dependency and incident details, and a policy verdict so automation and operators can see unhealthy telemetry without parsing prose.
- **Mills operational state model** (`pkg/telemetry/operational_state.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/health.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/breaker.go`): health gates and breaker telemetry can now distinguish `idle_healthy`, false-idle `idle_blocked`, and dependency `degraded` states when active incidents or degraded dependencies are present.
- **Mills stage/gate telemetry rollup endpoint + HUD "Mill Telemetry" panel** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_telemetry.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_telemetry.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/.../TelemetryPanel.svelte`, `telemetryHelpers.ts`): the operator recorded per-stage durations, costs, attempts, and gate outcomes but exposed no aggregate view — the 2026-07-16 telemetry review (`.loom/plan-mills-telemetry-optimization-2026-07-16.md`) had to reconstruct them by hand from 36 per-run detail fetches. `GET /api/mills/telemetry/stages?window=1d|7d|30d` now serves per-stage p50/p90/max/total durations, error rates, cost and retry-burn splits, per-gate pass/fail/skip/unparseable counts (`judged_by=flexinfer:unparseable`), an escalation funnel (last stage:outcome of escalated runs), and needle-classified failure classes from stage log tails. The HUD gains a Factory-family Telemetry panel (window selector, stage waterfall, gate health, escalation funnel, failure Pareto, retry-burn tiles; falls back to a committed real-data sample with a "sample data" badge when the endpoint isn't live) and the pipeline run drawer now shows the run's escalation class / failure class / retryable / external-dependency chips, which were on the wire but never rendered. KPI snapshots gain `retry_cost_usd` and `gate_unparseable_rate`.

### Changed
- **Mills requeue routed through the guarded transition; ghost-spark recheck map pruned on reap** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go`): the `?requeue=1` escalated→queued flip in `StartQueuedItemOpts` now goes through `Backlog.TransitionState` (the aggregate claim-version + from-state fence) instead of a blind `Backlog.Put`, for symmetry with the ghost-spark sweep's `closeGhostSpark` — a concurrent writer that already moved the item off escalated now fails cleanly rather than resurrecting a re-running item via last-writer-wins (wrong-state callers are unaffected and still 409 at the queued check). Separately, `closeGhostSpark` now prunes the process-local `ghostSparkRecheck` cooldown entry when an item is reaped as merged, so the map tracks only live escalated candidates. Reviewer-nit cleanups from the ghost-spark auto-close post-merge review; behavior-preserving with added regression tests.
- **HUD frontend TS ratchet wave: 64 components converted to `lang="ts"`** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/**/*.svelte`, plus `stores/{tasks,memory,workflows}.svelte.ts`, `utils/graphHelpers.ts`): under the strict `svelte-check` gate, the remaining plain-JavaScript Svelte components were converted from ignored-JSDoc typing to real TS prop/state types on the `$props()`/`$state` declarations (shared types imported from the stores; `DataTable`/`VirtualList` made generic; Badge variants constrained to the `BadgeVariant` union). 2 holdouts remain (`AgentTopology.svelte`, `EntityGraph.svelte`) because they import the untyped `d3-force` module — honest conversion needs a new dependency or an `any`-shim. Typing surfaced and fixed several real defects: `CommandPalette` read `sp.request?.description` (the field is `task_description`), so spawn entries always fell back to the raw spawn id; three `WeaverPanel` `<Badge>text</Badge>` badges rendered empty because `Badge` has no children slot (now `text={…}`), and its `statusVariant` returned invalid `'critical'`/`'neutral'` variants that silently rendered as info; `GraphFullView` ran `Object.entries(...).sort((a,b)=>b[1]-a[1])` over a `string[]` namespaces list (NaN no-op that put names in the count slot) and its drawer "Delete Entity" button passed `drawerEntity` after `closeEntityDrawer()` had already nulled it. Store interfaces gained fields the UI reads but the wire types omitted (`Task.resolved_deps?`, `TierStats.{max_items,ttl,compression_ratio}?`, `WorkflowSummary.progress?`). Behavior is otherwise unchanged; `svelte-check` stays at 0 errors / 35 warnings, 181 unit tests pass, and the `go:embed` build is green. A pre-existing dormant bug is flagged in-code but deferred: `PresencePanel`'s Worktrees tab reads a nonexistent `fleetStore.worktrees` and has always been empty.
- **Pinned FlexInfer judge/weaver models now degrade instead of storming a parked model** (`pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/model_unavailable.go`): a pinned `FLEXINFER_JUDGE_MODEL`/`FLEXINFER_WEAVER_MODEL` previously got NO fallbacks by design, so a 503 "parked behind a higher-priority primary" was retried against the same dead model (live 7d window: 24 research-stage errors, one run dialed the parked model 8×, 3 escalations). 503-parked now walks a degrade chain (`FLEXINFER_JUDGE_MODEL_FALLBACKS`/`FLEXINFER_WEAVER_MODEL_FALLBACKS`, else the registry role chain), a short per-model cooldown stops re-dialing inside the park window, the advisory research stage soft-skips with an explicit note when every candidate is unavailable, and `Classify` maps the parked/503 shape to transient instead of code-class.

### Fixed
- **loom-hub k8s servers can reach a kubeconfig from inside their containers** (`k8s/base/servers/{flux,helm,longhorn-k3s}/{configmap,deployment}.yaml`, `k8s/base/servers/k8s-harvester-infra/deployment.yaml`, new `k8s/hub_manifests_test.go`): every tool on the deployed `flux` server failed with `load kubeconfig: stat /Users/cblevins/workspace/platform/gitops/.kube/k3s.yaml: no such file or directory`. When these servers were promoted from local stdio targets to hub Deployments, the operator's **macOS** kubeconfig path was copied verbatim out of `mcp/context/registry.yaml` into the container env — a path that cannot exist in a Linux pod, with no volume mounted there and no ServiceAccount assigned. The two failure modes differed by client: mcp-flux hands `FLUX_KUBECONFIG` to `clientcmd` as an `ExplicitPath`, and a *missing* ExplicitPath is a hard error rather than an empty-config error, so `DeferredLoadingClientConfig`'s in-cluster fallback never engaged; the kubectl-backed mcp-k8s-ops servers (`longhorn-k3s`) failed more quietly by falling back to the pod's default SA and returning `Forbidden`. `flux`, `helm`, and `longhorn-k3s` now mount an in-cluster kubeconfig (SA token + CA) from their own ConfigMap at `/home/mcp/.kube/config` and run as the existing `loom-hub` ServiceAccount, whose ClusterRole already enumerated exactly these servers' Flux/Longhorn/Helm permissions — mirroring the `k8s-apps-k3s` server, the one that was hand-fixed and working. `k8s-harvester-infra` is **parked at `replicas: 0`** instead: it targets a *different* cluster (Harvester/RKE2 at 192.168.50.190), so an in-cluster kubeconfig would be wrong, granting it an SA would make it answer Harvester questions with k3s data, and the only Harvester credential in the namespace is the deliberately narrow `mills-spawn` VM-lifecycle token — binding that to a server exposing generic `k8s_apply`/`k8s_exec` would re-widen the blast radius that scoping removed. Two new manifest guards lock the class shut: no container env may carry a `/Users/...` host path, and every `*_KUBECONFIG` env must be covered by a volumeMount (accepting either a subPath file mount or an ancestor-directory mount). `ops-mcp` is a documented exemption — `cmd/mcp-ops/main.go` `resolveKubeconfig()` falls back to `K3S_KUBECONFIG` for *every* kubectl call including the mutating `harvester_vm_restart`, so mounting a working k3s kubeconfig there would silently retarget Harvester VM power-cycles at k3s; that one needs the Go fix first.
- **Devbox workspace sync no longer blows the 200 MB cap on compiled binaries** (`internal/devbox/backend/k8s_sync.go`, `k8s_sync_test.go`): every `devbox_exec` against loom-core failed with `sync payload exceeds 200 MB limit`, intermittently at first and then permanently, making the sandbox unusable for the repo it is most used on. Measured payload was **315.7 MB**, of which **202.6 MB was four compiled Go binaries sitting in the repo root** (`loom` 96.7 MB, `mcp-devbox` 70.8 MB, `loom-mills-operator` 26.2 MB, `custom-server` 8.8 MB) — so an ordinary `make build` silently bricked devbox for the whole repo. `isBinaryExcluded` had a comment asserting that extensionless root binaries "are caught by the size limit"; they were not caught by it, they *were* it. Extension- and name-based rules cannot separate `loom` from a source file, so compiled executables are now identified by **magic number** (ELF, Mach-O 32/64/universal, PE) and skipped. Only files carrying an execute bit are sniffed, bounding this to a handful of 4-byte reads per sync and never opening ordinary sources; executable shell scripts start with `#!` and are kept, and a binary magic in a *non*-executable file is treated as data (a test fixture), not a build output. Separately, `.pnpm-store` joins the exclude list — pnpm's content-addressed package store is the same category as the already-excluded `node_modules`, but project-local, and held ~74 MB under the HUD frontend. Verified end-to-end: payload 315.7 MB → 38.8 MB, a fresh sandbox syncs to 50 MB on disk with both root binaries confirmed absent, and `devbox_exec` returns `exit_code: 0` where it previously refused to start. The over-limit error also no longer reports a bare total — diagnosing this one required walking the tree by hand — and now names the largest files plus the two knobs that resolve it (`DEVBOX_SYNC_EXCLUDES`, `DEVBOX_MAX_SYNC_SIZE_MB`).
- **Devbox sandbox memory limit raised to 4Gi + memory kills named in the error** (`mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `k8s/base/servers/devbox/deployment.yaml`, `cmd/mcp-devbox/main.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/{k8s_wait,k8s_runtime,docker}.go`): a full `go test ./internal/...` in a devbox sandbox died with `failed to create runc console socket: procReady not received`, an error mentioning nothing about memory, while smaller package subsets passed. The sandbox memory *limit* was 1Gi and a cold-cache Go suite peaks at ~1.3Gi (measured 1354 MiB via `memory.peak` on the familyforge repo) — so the cgroup OOM killer reaped runc's init helper while the sandbox's PID 1 survived, meaning the container was never marked `OOMKilled` and `oom_killed` stayed false. `DEVBOX_DEFAULT_MEMORY_MB` goes 1024 → 4096 (and the code fallback 512 → 2048); this is a ceiling, not a request — pods still request 128Mi, so a higher limit costs nothing at schedule time and only caps runaway processes. Since 1Gi is under what an ordinary Go suite needs, this is raised globally rather than per-project. Both backends now recognize the runc-init-failure signature (`procReady not received`, `error during container init`, `unable to start container process`), read the sandbox's configured limit, and append a hint naming both remedies (`limits.memory_mb` in a repo-root `.devbox.yaml`, or `DEVBOX_DEFAULT_MEMORY_MB`). The hint only asserts an OOM kill when the kubelet confirms it via container status (current *or* previous state, since an OOM-killed container is Running again by the time an exec fails against it); otherwise it hedges, because a node under memory pressure produces the same error.
- **Presence panel Worktrees tab wired to real data** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PresencePanel.svelte`): the Worktrees tab (and the worktree count passed to the Agents tab) had always rendered empty because the panel read `fleetStore.worktrees`, a member that does not exist on the fleet store — the TS ratchet wave surfaced this and parked it as an explicit typed empty list. The panel now derives worktrees from `presenceStore.worktrees` (backed by `GET /api/worktrees`) and starts/stops `presenceStore` polling alongside the existing fleet watchdog poll in the mount effect; the presence poller self-gates on SSE health, so this adds an initial fetch, `agent.*` SSE subscriptions, and a 30s disconnected-only fallback rather than a hot poll.
- **Mill Telemetry panel error state + empty `escalations_by_class` encoding** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/TelemetryPanel.svelte`, `pkg/mills/kpi_writer.go`): on a hard telemetry fetch error (500/timeout) with nothing cached, the panel rendered the red "Telemetry feed failed" banner AND a perpetual "Loading telemetry…" spinner together, because the spinner branch was gated on `{:else if !report}` alone; it is now `{:else if !report && !error}`, so an errored panel shows only the banner (the top-level `ErrorBanner` carries the state). On the Go side, a window with zero escalations could serialize `escalations_by_class` as JSON `null` if `CountEscalationsByClassSince` ever returned a nil map; the KPI writer now guards it to a non-nil empty map so the field always encodes as `{}` (matching the repo's `[]`-not-null wire convention), pinned by `TestKPIWriter_EscalationsByClass_EmptyEncodesAsObject`.
- **Council CI incident classifier normalizes more third-party failures to `external_dependency`** (`pkg/mills/council/ci_incident_classifier.go`, `pkg/mills/council/ci_incident_classifier_test.go`, `internal/contracts/incident_classification_test.go`, `internal/contracts/testdata/council_ci_incident_external_dependency.golden`): the classifier gained stable dependency codes for `model_provider`, `logging_backend`, `object_storage`, `dns`, and `tls` (replacing the ad-hoc string literals), plus evidence signatures for logging/observability backends (Loki, Grafana, Prometheus, Tempo, OTel collector) and object storage (S3, GCS, MinIO, blob/artifact/cache storage). A recognized repository-independent dependency (`isRepositoryIndependentDependency`) now classifies as an `external_dependency` incident even when the failure is not otherwise flagged shared, branch-owned, or pre-checkout — those infrastructure outages never belong to a branch diff. A new `internal/contracts` golden pins the `CouncilCIIncidentClassification` external-dependency wire shape (class, wait-dependency disposition, dependency id, retry policy) so downstream council/escalation consumers read a stable contract.
- **Rubric judge false-fails from truncated score envelopes** (`pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`; every one of the 12 inspected `pr_self_review`/`spec_conformance` gate failures in the 7d window ending 2026-07-16): the judge default of 256 max tokens let thinking-style models burn the budget on a "Thinking Process:" preamble or truncate the `{"score":…}` JSON mid-array, and the parser only accepted complete JSON — real diffs then failed gates, burned retries ($11.15 of $33.41 total 7d spend went to attempt>1), and escalated runs whose stages had succeeded. Default is now 1024 (`FLEXINFER_JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS` override), recovery balance-repairs truncated envelopes and regex-extracts in-range scores (echoes of the prompt's example envelope are stripped so a truncated echo can't mint a fake 1.0), and a `finish_reason=length` response that still can't be recovered retries exactly once with doubled tokens and a JSON-only instruction. Kill-tested against the 7 captured production judge responses (`pkg/mills/clients/testdata/judge_unparseable_live_2026-07-16.json`).
- **ci_watch no longer escalates runs whose CI is merely slow** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`, `runner.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`; the single largest escalation sink — 5 of 22 escalations in the 7d window died at `pipeline poll timed out after 30m0s` with the pipeline still running): a poll-session timeout with the pipeline still pending/running now extends the watch in bounded 30m sessions up to `MILLS_CI_WATCH_MAX_MINUTES` (default 90), logging each extension into the stage log tail; at the hard cap the run escalates as a retryable external-dependency incident keyed on the stuck pipeline URL instead of a generic error.
- **GitLab stage hardening from the 2026-07-16 live-failure taxonomy** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/`, `pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`): `mr` adopts the existing open MR on 409 "already exists" (filtered by source+target branch) instead of erroring ×3; `merge` on a persistent 422 "Branch cannot be merged" rebases via the API, rides the fresh post-rebase head pipeline to terminal, and re-enters the standard not-ready recovery (a mid-settle 405 also routes there instead of surfacing raw); `cleanup` branch-delete failures log-and-succeed; the scope gate returns `skip` (advisory) for backlog items with no slices instead of a false fail (skips are excluded from `gate_pass_rate` and the eval gate score); a devbox quality gate reporting not-passed with zero executed checks classifies as retryable infrastructure instead of a test failure.
- **Mills external dependency degraded-mode escalation handling** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate_test.go`, `pkg/mills/store/external_incidents.go`): repeated matching external dependency incidents now switch escalation publishing into a distinct degraded-mode path with recurrence counts, labels, issue guidance, handoff context, and event metadata.
- **Mills policy threshold evaluators** (`pkg/mills/council/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/flakiness.go`, `pkg/mills/audit/thresholds.go`): deterministic stale-plan, flaky-gate, and operational-threshold checks now return machine-readable verdicts with stable codes, severities, reasons, and metrics for downstream council/pipeline policy wiring.
- **Embedding fallback degradation telemetry** (`pkg/codebase/embed`, `pkg/flexinfer`, `pkg/telemetry/embedding_fallback.go`): write-path embedding fallback and query keyword-degradation paths now emit reason-coded telemetry with provider/model labels, batch sizes, provider-overload classification, and breaker threshold/cooldown/timeout context so external dependency incidents can distinguish primary errors, provider overload, open breakers, missing secondary lanes, and secondary failures.
- **Cross-vendor session bridge in mcp-agent-context** (`pkg/vendorsessions`, `pkg/agentcontext/service_messages.go`, `service_vendorsessions.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_bridge.go`): four new tools let Claude Code and Codex see and reach each other by default. `agent_vendor_session_list`/`agent_vendor_session_search` give unified newest-first listing and capped substring search over both vendors' on-disk CLI transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/<flattened-cwd>/<uuid>.jsonl`, `~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`; roots overridable via `AGENT_CONTEXT_{CLAUDE,CODEX}_SESSIONS_DIR`) — previously nothing read Claude transcripts at all and codexwatch discarded content after event mapping. `agent_message_send`/`agent_message_inbox` add durable directed messages (new `agent_messages_v1` Qdrant collection, unread/read lifecycle, default 7-day expiry) with two best-effort live-delivery hints: the in-process presence nudge queue and a HUD `POST /api/agent/nudge` (`AGENT_CONTEXT_HUD_URL`, default `http://127.0.0.1:3333`; failures never block the store write). Auto-allow needs no per-tool policy work — Claude's `mcp__loom` allow rule and Codex's `approval_policy="never"` already cover proxied tools — but visibility did: the four patterns join the llm-core priority tail in `cmd/loom/proxy_tool_filter.go` and the llm-core cap rises 160 → 164 (`platform_profiles.yaml` in step), since the priority list already sat at ≈156/160 with ICC/PM enabled and tail additions would otherwise be silent no-ops. Antigravity's 100-tool set is unchanged. Pinned by `pkg/vendorsessions` fixture tests, message payload/nudge tests, and the extended `TestFilterProxyTools_LLMCoreDefaults`.
- **Repo-local proxy tool-profile overrides** (`cmd/loom/proxy_repo_config.go`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`): `loom proxy` now resolves `<repo>/.loom/proxy.yaml` from its working directory at startup and lets it shadow the `--tool-profile`/`--max-tools` CLI flags, with optional per-`--agent-hint` scoping under `agents:`. This is how Antigravity ICC sessions get the `icc-core` profile: Antigravity reads one home-level `mcp_config.json` pinned to `antigravity-core` for every repo, so per-repo shaping cannot happen vendor-side — `private/icc-project-workspaces/.loom/proxy.yaml` (`agents.antigravity.tool_profile: icc-core`) remaps only that repo's Antigravity sessions while llm-core vendors keep their 160-cap profile (which already reaches the ICC block). Overrides are validated against known profile names (unknown → stderr + ignored, so a typo cannot expose the unfiltered list to a 100-tool-ceiling client), and an applied override resets the cap to the new profile's default unless the file sets `max_tools`. `LOOM_PROXY_TOOL_PROFILE=<profile>` is the single-session escape hatch that beats both file and flags. The `icc-*` workflow skills in `skills-registry.yaml` now document the visibility prerequisite (`loom catalog enable icc` + icc-core on profile-limited clients). Pinned by `TestApplyProxyProfileOverrides*` (precedence, agent scoping, upward walk, unknown/malformed handling).
- **`loom agent worktree-cleanup-self`** (`cmd/loom/cmd_agent_worktree.go`): the subcommand generated SessionEnd hooks have been invoking to auto-release harness worktrees — it never existed, so the trailing `|| true` silently swallowed "unknown command" and the cleanup shipped inert. Now implemented as a thin wrapper over the `loom worktree gc` classifier: removes the named worktree only when merged into the default branch or clean with every commit on a remote; dirty/at-risk trees are always kept; keep decisions exit 0 so hook chains never fail. `TestSessionEndHookEmitsExistingSubcommand` locks the hook↔subcommand contract so it cannot rot again.
- **Vendor-spec manifest coverage for VS Code and OpenCode** (`pkg/generator/vendor_specs.yaml`, `TestGeneratedJSONConfig_OpenCodeShape`): `loom vendor-specs check` now asserts VS Code's `servers` root key and OpenCode's `mcp`/`type:"local"` config shape against live vendor docs.

### Changed
- **HUD frontend gains a strict static type gate** (`internal/hud/frontend/tsconfig.json`, `svelte-check`, `.gitlab-ci.yml` build:frontend): the Svelte 5 frontend was all-TypeScript but had no tsconfig — nothing ever type-checked it (esbuild only strips types). A strict tsconfig (`allowJs`/`checkJs: false` as the incremental ratchet for the remaining plain-JS components) plus `pnpm check` now runs first in the build:frontend CI job. Fixing the 47 errors it surfaced also fixed real defects: `Badge` had no `muted` style so cancelled/idle/offline statuses (and the plan helpers' invalid `'default'` variant, intended as "neutral" per its own comment) silently rendered active-info blue; `SandboxLive` passed an invalid `"warning"` StatusDot state that fell back to the unknown-diamond; `fleet` typed the wire's `[]string` process list as structured `Process[]`; `MetricCard`'s orphaned JSDoc made every optional prop required; and duplicated local `statusVariant` helpers in PlansPanel/ProjectsPanel were replaced with the canonical typed one from `utils/format.ts`.
- **Curated proxy tool profiles gained the landing-loop tools** (`cmd/loom/proxy_tool_filter.go`): `gitlab__merge_merge_request`, `agent_context__agent_worktree_release`, and `devbox__devbox_quality_gate` join the core profiles — the shipping skills instruct all three, but profile-limited clients (codex, antigravity) could previously open MRs they couldn't merge, allocate worktrees they couldn't release, and were steered to a quality gate they couldn't call.
- **Antigravity home config now targets the 2.0 shared central path** `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` (`pkg/sync/manager.go`); the pre-2.0 `~/.gemini/antigravity/mcp_config.json` is legacy (survives as a symlink where present), matching the already-central `config/hooks.json`.
- **Skills registry hygiene** (`mcp/context/skills-registry.yaml`): removed the stale duplicate ICC skill block (icc-daily-pm-loop/weekly-status/triage/handoff-prep were defined twice and the older copy — with "registry name TBD" placeholders — won generation); fixed the broken 5.5/6.5 step numbering in feature-dev and bugfix; upgraded the verification steps in feature-dev, bugfix, commit, and small-change-loop to name concrete quality-gate commands (`devbox_quality_gate`) instead of vague "run tests" prose.
- **Vendor spec refresh 2026-07-14** (`pkg/generator/VENDOR_SPECS.md`, `vendor_specs.yaml`): Codex docs pins moved to the canonical learn.chatgpt.com host (developers.openai.com 308-redirects); recorded Codex's removal of `approval_policy = "on-failure"`, hooks-default-on, and the 4 new hook events; Gemini's new `policyPaths`/`adminPolicyPaths` settings keys; Zed's `context_servers` reality and the Kilo 1.0 config migration as tracked follow-ups.

### Fixed
- **Mills live pipeline drawer no longer freezes at "Loading run detail…"** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`): `GET /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{id}` encoded a run's empty stage/gate sets as JSON `null` (Go nil slice) — and a live run has no gate outcomes until its first stage completes, so every in-flight run hit it. The drawer's gate sort then threw (`null` is not iterable) inside a `$derived`, Svelte tore down the component's effect tree, and the drawer wedged with a dead close button until page reload. The operator now encodes `[]` (same contract as the run-list endpoint, pinned by `TestHandlePipelineRuns_GetEmptyStagesGatesAreArrays`), and the HUD store normalises null/missing `stages`/`gates` before caching so an older operator can't wedge the UI during rollout skew (pinned by `mills.detailNormalize.test.ts`).
- **Spawn terminal cleanup acknowledgement** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_session.go`, `internal/hud/spawn_stop_race_test.go`): terminal spawn reaping now bounds presence deregistration retries and logs exhausted agent-context cleanup failures as a deterministic local terminal acknowledgement instead of leaving runtime-cleaned spawns cleanup-pending forever.
- **Mills incident policy classifier covers external dependency and infra saturation incidents** (`pkg/mills/council/ci_incident_classifier.go`, `pkg/mills/council/classifier.go`, `pkg/mills/council/classifier_test.go`): GitLab, gitlab-agent, Longhorn, ClickHouse, and runner resource-saturation signatures now map to stable policy classes/dependencies, with a dedup key that collapses repeated evidence for the same remediation owner.
- **Mills terminal-state guard** (`pkg/mills/store/state.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`): pipeline runs now treat terminal resolution as one-way, so a stale cleanup or Start error after `done` cannot emit a competing `escalated` outcome for the same run.
- **Mills council GitLab CI incident planning output** (`pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails.go`, `pkg/mills/council/classification_test.go`): guardrail outcomes now preserve the canonical `external_dependency_incident` class, GitLab dependency, and bounded failure evidence in planning notes.
- **Mills fixture-canary self-review no longer repeats the dedicated test stage** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): canary review spawns now inspect only the cumulative diff after the pipeline's tests stage passes, avoiding deterministic cold-compile timeouts that consumed every review attempt.
- **Mobile Live no longer retains stale active sessions** (`internal/hud/domain/mobile/handler_sessions.go`, `apps/loom-companion-ios`): active-session responses now use the Fleet presence join to hide durable agent-context rows whose heartbeat-backed agent is offline, while preserving fresh aliases and session-only agents. The iOS Live model also reconciles the canonical active set every 30 seconds, so a missed `session.end` SSE event self-heals without pull-to-refresh.
- **iOS first-load reliability and Live/Session parity** (`apps/loom-companion-ios`, `internal/hud`): the companion app now coalesces initial Work and Dashboard loads, keeps recovery actions visible above the floating tab bar, and gives connection failures actionable retry/remediation states. Mobile Live seeds from the canonical active-session tree, accepts both daemon and agent-context lifecycle events, refreshes linked context activity, and opens the standard Session detail. The web HUD now preserves `session_id` through context bridge/trace/stream responses, refreshes Live rows on context events, and shows the session identity in Stream and Timeline.
- **Embedded HUD no longer hard-fails init when spawn-state recovery can't reach the k3s API** (`internal/hud/embed.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`; observed live 2026-07-14). A transient `no route to host` to the k3s API at daemon boot failed `RecoverSpawnsContext` inside `initSpawnOrchestrator`, which aborted `StartMonitors`, which aborted `startEmbeddedHUD` — the daemon then served 404 on every `/api/*` route for its entire lifetime while the `:3333` listener stayed up. Spawn-backend failures are now non-fatal to HUD init: the HUD registers its routes regardless; on recovery failure the orchestrator is marked **degraded** (`Spawn` refuses new work so fresh records can't collide with unrecovered durable keyed rows; read routes keep serving) and recovery retries in the background with capped exponential backoff (1s → 60s) until the store is reachable, at which point the degraded flag clears and the reconcile/prune loops start — preserving the recovery-before-reconcile ordering that protects durable records from the lossy label-rebuilt path. Pinned by `TestSpawnRecoveryStoreUnreachableDegradesAndRecovers` (unreachable store at init → degraded + Spawn rejected; store returns → state recovered, flag cleared) and `TestStartMonitorsSpawnInitFailureKeepsHUDServing` (spawn construction failure → `StartMonitors` nil, `/api/status` still 200).
- **kilo.json now gets full schema + runtime validation** (`go.mod` fi-mcp-kit bump to v0.2.1-0.20260714193933-f42344ef2b59, [fi-mcp-kit!16](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/libs/fi-mcp-kit/-/merge_requests/16); `pkg/sync/generated_paths.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_validate.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_validate.go`): the kit reclassified kilocode as a JSON target with a vendored schema for the Kilo 1.0 kilo.json shape (top-level `mcp` map with local/remote servers, array commands, `environment`, millisecond timeouts, `permission` map keyed by `{server}_{tool}` globs) plus a runtime pass (command existence, env names, plaintext secrets). loom-core's interim skips are removed: `kitValidates()` is gone (sync validation runs for every non-empty generator target), `loom sync validate kilocode` and `loom validate schemas` route kilo.json through the kit validator instead of the JSON-syntax-only check. Verified: a generated kilo.json validates clean; an intentionally broken one (empty `command`, invalid permission value) surfaces schema errors.
- **Codex regen no longer emits false schema WARNs** (`go.mod` fi-mcp-kit bump to v0.2.1-0.20260714145635-d1b804b285bc, [fi-mcp-kit!14](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/libs/fi-mcp-kit/-/merge_requests/14)): the vendored Codex config schema in fi-mcp-kit's validator lagged the vendor and flagged valid keys (`default_tools_approval_mode`, `[features] hooks`/`multi_agent`, the `[agents]` table) as "additionalProperties not allowed" on every `loom sync codex --regen`. The schema is refreshed from https://learn.chatgpt.com/codex/config-schema.json, regression-tested, and guarded by a fi-mcp-kit CI drift job; regen verified warning-free (`config.toml validated against upstream schema`).
- **VS Code MCP config root key** (`pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`): the vscode target emitted `mcpServers`, which VS Code silently ignores — its `mcp.json` schema only reads `servers` (per code.visualstudio.com/docs/copilot/customization/mcp-servers). Generated `.vscode/mcp.json` and the user-profile `mcp.json` now use `servers`.
- **pre-commit debug-statement check test exclusion** (`scripts/hooks/pre-commit-native.sh`): a leaked Makefile-style `$$` in `grep -v '_test\.go$$'` made the exclusion match nothing, so `_test.go` files were wrongly flagged for `fmt.Println`; now `_test\.go$`.
- Removed the dead legacy hub-wrapper probe path `scripts/mcp/hub_wrapper.sh` (`pkg/generator/configs.go`) and the stale pointer to the removed `codex_hooks_smoke.sh` in the Codex hook-trust test docs.
- **ICC workflow tools now reach profile-limited clients** (`cmd/loom/proxy_tool_filter.go`, `pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`; 2026-07-14 audit). The `icc-*` workflow skills in `skills-registry.yaml` target codex and antigravity but instructed `icc__*`/`icc-capture__*` tools that never survived profile filtering: the llm-core priority list (~117 live matches) plus serverOrder quotas exhausted the 140 cap before tail-fill reached the `icc`, `icc-capture`, or `pm` servers (verified live: 140/140 and 100/100 with zero ICC tools). Two changes: (1) the llm-core cap is raised 140 → 160 and a 32-entry ICC/PM block (the tools those skills instruct) is appended to the priority tail, so codex/claude/kilocode see the full block while antigravity's 100 ceiling truncates before it — its tool set is byte-for-byte unchanged; (2) a new opt-in `icc-core` profile (`loom proxy --tool-profile icc-core`, 100-tool ceiling) pairs a slim developer core with the complete `icc` (55), `icc-capture` (15), and `pm` surfaces for ICC-focused sessions on any client, including antigravity. The profile selector is now table-driven (`selectProfileTools`) so future profiles are data, not code. Pinned by `TestFilterProxyTools_LLMCoreDefaults` (ICC block reachable), `TestFilterProxyTools_AntigravityCoreDefaults` (ICC block excluded), and `TestFilterProxyTools_ICCCoreProfile`. Note: the `icc` server must also be enabled in `catalog-state.yaml`; unmatched priority patterns cost no slots, so the block is inert where it stays disabled.
- **Fail-closed Mills maintenance admission and scalable S1c evidence contract** (`GET /api/mills/safety/quiescence`, crash leases, operator admission counters, scheduler/audit activity tracking, `cmd/mills-workflow-killtest`). With global `policy.enabled=false`, work-creating APIs and background schedulers stop admitting new work while the separately gated workflow engine may advance one singleton crash canary. The destructive three-run harness now binds REST, kubectl, and client-go to one frozen Kubernetes authority; proves exact GitRepository, Flux Kustomization, reviewed Deployment/ConfigMap, ReplicaSet, admitted PodSpec, container, and process identities across both crashes; brackets each UID-and-resourceVersion-preconditioned delete with fresh policy, lease, source, and process evidence; and rejects unbounded response/archive/census inputs before mutation. Evidence is written atomically as one predecessor-chained gate plus three canonical run files and reverified offline with strict paths, sizes, hashes, timestamps, cross-object bindings, and pure verdict recomputation. Operator response identity and per-process boot identity fail closed on stale routing or an unplanned restart. The proof establishes one spawn/pod/process lineage and one durable journal effect; external agent-turn/tool effects remain explicitly at-least-once.
- **Council incident classification planning context** (`pkg/mills/council/incident_context.go`, `pkg/mills/council/incident_context_test.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/incident_context.go`): classified CI failures and live pipeline failure classifications now normalize into council planning context so proposal decisions receive incident class, retry semantics, and external-dependency metadata.
- **Shared external dependency incident classification contract** (`internal/contracts/external_dependency_incident.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/external_dependency_incident_test.go`): defines the canonical `external_dependency_incident` value and pins council classifier, escalation rendering, and Mills pipeline failure metadata to the same contract.
- **Council external dependency incident normalization** (`pkg/mills/council/external_incident_rules.go`, `pkg/mills/council/brief.go`, `pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails.go`, `pkg/mills/council/classification_external_test.go`, `pkg/mills/council/brief_test.go`): workspace signals, classified CI summaries, and editor guardrails now normalize recognized third-party failures to the canonical `external_dependency_incident` class with dependency metadata before council planning or backlog mutation consumes them.
- **iOS companion: Mills shift report — the web Factory overlay's 24h story, ported to the Mills tab with share-sheet markdown** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/Mills/MillsShiftReport.swift` + `MillsShiftModels.swift`, `Sources/LoomCompanion/Views/Mills/ShiftReportSheet.swift`, MillsScreen entry card). A "Shift report" card under the Mills hero opens a sheet that re-reads the archive window (`GET /api/mills/pipeline/runs?state=terminal&limit=500` via the new `Endpoint.millsPipelineRuns(state:limit:)`) and narrates it with the SAME deterministic rules as the web's `shiftHelpers.ts` — bolts woven, sparks struck, pattern-book stamps (attribution via the new `/api/mills/backlog` + `/api/patterns` reads: run → backlog → PlanID → slug), retries with the worst run named, busiest local hour (only when a real peak exists), and pipeline-fuel spend. Sparks enrich in the background with their failing gate names via bounded (8) `GET /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{id}` detail fetches. The toolbar ShareLink hands the standup-pasteable markdown (per-spark gate breakdown + UTC departures table, byte-identical to the web export) to the iOS share sheet. All semantics live in `LoomCompanionKit` (not views), degrade to a calm quiet-shift on operator absence like the other Mills reads, and are pinned by `MillsShiftReportTests` (19 tests porting the web's vitest suite: window filtering, stats aggregation, pattern attribution, narrative determinism, markdown shape, wire decoding, degradation).
- **Mills council external dependency incident classification now covers Longhorn storage and CI-shaped infra signals** (`pkg/mills/council/classifier.go`, `pkg/mills/council/classifier_test.go`, `internal/contracts/incident_classification.go`): adds a narrow external-incident classifier facade, contract aliases for downstream consumers, and deterministic tests for GitLab CI and Longhorn/CSI storage evidence.
- **Factory shift report — the last 24 hours told as deterministic prose, exportable as standup markdown** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/shiftHelpers.ts`, `components/mills/ShiftReport.svelte`, FactoryPanel header action; closes concept 10 of `.loom/174-research-factory-view-inspiration-2026-07-08.md`). A "📋 shift report" button on the Factory panel opens an overlay that re-reads the archive window (same `fetchArchiveRuns` the bolt archive uses — never `pipelineHistory`) and narrates it: bolts woven, sparks struck, pattern-book stamps ("Pattern go-rest-service stamped twice — all merged on green"), retry counts with the worst run named, busiest hour (only when a real peak exists), and pipeline-fuel spend. No summarizer — same runs in, same words out. Sparks enrich in the background with their failing gate names via the new drawer-cache-free `millsStore.fetchArchiveRunDetail` (bounded to 8 fetches). One click copies (or downloads) a standup-pasteable markdown report with a per-spark gate breakdown and a departures table. Mobile-first: below 480px the modal becomes a bottom sheet. Pinned by `shiftHelpers.test.ts` (17 tests: window filtering, stats aggregation, pattern attribution, narrative determinism, markdown shape).
- **LLM-judged gates now resolve judge-vs-tests dissent with a cross-backend tiebreaker** (`pkg/mills/gates/llm_judge.go` `LLMGate.Tiebreaker`, `pkg/mills/clients/anthropic_judge.go`, operator wiring `buildGateTiebreaker`; escalations [#309](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/309)/[#310](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/310)). A single small judge sampled once was the sole gate authority, and the retry loop assumed re-spawning the agent could change a deterministic verdict — a judge fixation on a correct dynamic-WHERE SQL idiom burned three runs, nine review attempts, and ~$5.50 while the pipeline held stronger contradicting evidence (a green deterministic tests stage exercising the exact query). Now, when the primary judge FAILS a gate whose tests stage PASSED, the gate treats that as a dissent and asks one second opinion from a different model family (Anthropic, `LOOM_MILLS_GATE_TIEBREAKER_MODEL`, default `claude-sonnet-5`, `off` to disable): tiebreaker pass → gate passes with the overrule and the primary's dissent recorded in `JudgedBy`/reasons for audit; tiebreaker fail → corroborated fail and the retry loop proceeds with feedback worth a re-spawn; tiebreaker error → fail closed to the primary verdict. Zero cost on the happy path (fires only on dissent); degrades to primary-only when no Anthropic key is configured. Pinned by the `TestLLMGate_Tiebreaker*` matrix and `TestAnthropicRubricJudge_*`.
- **Mills audit CI failure taxonomy and classifier** (`pkg/mills/audit/ci_failure_classifier.go`, `pkg/mills/audit/ci_failure_classifier_test.go`): adds a deterministic audit-side classifier for recurring pipeline failure signatures, including `ci_watch` poll timeouts, terminal branch pipelines, GitLab merge/config errors, docs guardrail misses, GitLab service outages, runner infrastructure failures, and known external dependency incidents.
- **Mills failure classification now carries deterministic external incident metadata** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/failure_classification.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/failure_classification_test.go`): known GitLab authentication and container-registry blob-storage incidents classify as terminal configuration failures and expose stable external dependency IDs on the policy-facing failure record.
- **Council briefs now surface classified CI failures** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/store/types.go`, `pkg/mills/council/brief.go`, `pkg/mills/council/brief_test.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline_escalation_class_test.go`): recent escalated `ci_watch` runs with persisted failure-class metadata now appear in Mills/council summaries with run ID, backlog item, failure class, retryability, free-retry/terminal semantics, classifier provenance, and external dependency, and the brief exposes the same records plus snake_case source counts as structured JSON for API consumers.
- **Mills escalation classification now carries classifier provenance and full retry semantics** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/failure_classification.go`, `internal/contracts/escalation.go`; port of closed !1021 onto the grouped `EscalationClassification` model). The grouped `classification` block on escalated failure records, `pipeline.escalation.published` events, and handoff context previously exposed only `retryable`; it now also carries `classifier` (`mills-failure-classifier`), `free_retry`, and `terminal`, derived from the closed failure taxonomy, so downstream planners and operators read the full retry policy without re-deriving it from the class name. `FailureClassification` gained JSON tags plus the `FailureClassifierName` constant (stable wire shape pinned by `internal/contracts/testdata/escalation_failure_classification.golden`), handoff context gained a sibling `classification` key, escalation issue bodies and recurrence notes render the new fields, and `internal/contracts/escalation.go` re-exports the failure taxonomy as a stable contract for downstream consumers. Pinned by the extended `escalate_test.go` classification tests and the contracts goldens.
- **Escalation events now carry the failure classification** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go` `addClassificationToPayload`; rebased continuation of !1024, backlog `psl-plan-council-expose-ci-failure-class-in-mills-escalation-output-1`). The `pipeline.escalation.published` event payload previously omitted the classification main persists on the record; it now includes escalation class, policy failure class, retryability, and external-dependency fields via the existing `EscalationMetadata()`, so event consumers (HUD, audit, dedup) read class without re-fetching the run. Pinned by the payload assertions in `escalate_test.go`.
- **Scope-overlap serialization: sibling backlog items that declare files in the same package now run sequentially, not concurrently** (`pkg/mills/scope_overlap.go`, reconciler `tryStart` wiring, `pipeline.serialize_overlapping_scopes` policy knob — `*bool`, default ON, opt out with `false`; evidence: escalations [#290](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/290)–[#305](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/305)). The council decomposes a theme into several items whose slices name files in the SAME directory tree (2026-07-08/09: ten failure-classification items all pinned `pkg/mills/pipeline`); the reconciler dispatched them concurrently, so every resulting MR conflicted with its siblings — seven open MRs, zero mergeable, and the losers burned further attempts on merge-422/ci_watch escalations. `tryStart` now defers a queued item while a RUNNING same-repo item holds an intersecting scope envelope (same directory-envelope semantics as the scope gate: parent dirs of slice files, ancestor/descendant containment, glob static prefixes; slice-less canaries/bootstrapped items never block or get blocked, escalated items don't block so a wedged escalation can't starve the queue). The deferred item stays queued and the on-merge KickNow dispatches it within ~1s of the blocker landing. Pinned by `TestScopeEnvelope_*` and `TestReconciler_DefersOnScopeOverlap` (+ disjoint/opt-out/cross-repo cases).
- **Workflow run pause/resume/fail admin endpoints — the live-mitigation surface for a stuck imperative run** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_workflow.go`, `server.go` routes, `docs/MILLS_RUNBOOK.md` § "Pause / resume / fail a workflow run"). The 2026-07-09 wf-canary zombie loop exposed that the workflow-run REST surface was read-only: clearing two dead runs required a code deploy. `POST /api/mills/workflow/runs/{id}/pause` (running→paused), `/resume` (paused→running), and `/fail` (running|paused→error, terminal, optional audit-logged `{reason}`) now give the operator the same between-step controls the scheduler already honors (`scheduler_min.go` reloads the run and skips on `paused_at`/non-running state); invalid transitions return 409 with the current state. Admin-gated (`requireAdmin`); pinned by `TestHandleWorkflowRunLifecycle_*`.

### Changed
- **Kilocode sync target now emits Kilo 1.0's `~/.config/kilo/kilo.json` instead of the never-read `~/.kilocode/config.toml`** (`pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml`, `pkg/generator/configs_formats.go`, `pkg/generator/platform_profile.go` `schema_url`, `pkg/sync/manager.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_regen.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_validate.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_sync.go`, `pkg/generator/doctor.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_validate.go`, `pkg/generator/vendor_specs.yaml`, `pkg/generator/VENDOR_SPECS.md`). Kilo 1.0 (extension `kilocode.kilo-code` v7.x, rebuilt on the OpenCode engine) reads `kilo.json(c)` — global `~/.config/kilo/kilo.json`, project `kilo.json`/`.kilo/kilo.json` — with a top-level `mcp` map (`type: "local"` + array `command`, `environment`, millisecond timeouts) and a `permission` map keyed by `{server}_{tool}` globs (`loom_*: allow` replaces the legacy `always_allow`). Runtime evidence from the installed v7.4.5 bundle: `globalConfigDir()` = `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/kilo`, `MODERN=["kilo.jsonc","kilo.json"]`, zero references to `config.toml` — the old TOML emission was read by nothing. The emitter reuses the OpenCode JSON writer with a profile-driven `$schema` (`https://app.kilo.ai/config.json`); skills (rules/workflows) intentionally stay under `~/.kilocode/`. fi-mcp-kit still classifies kilocode as TOML, so kilo.json gets JSON-syntax-only validation until the kit learns the new surface. Docs: <https://kilo.ai/docs/automate/mcp/using-in-kilo-code>. Pinned by `TestGeneratedJSONConfig_KilocodeEmitsKiloJSON`.
- **Zed sync now targets the config Zed actually reads: `context_servers` in `~/.config/zed/settings.json`** (`pkg/generator/platform_profiles.yaml` zed target, `pkg/sync/merge_zed.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_zed.go`, `pkg/generator/vendor_specs.yaml` zed entry, VENDOR_SPECS.md § Zed). The old zed target emitted `mcp.json` (root `mcpServers`) into `~/Library/Application Support/Zed/` — a file Zed core never reads, and one the dormant loom-zed extension (which gets its config via the Zed extension API) never reads either, so the whole emission was inert. The generator now stages a `.zed/context_servers.json` fragment and `loom sync zed` merges it into the user-owned `~/.config/zed/settings.json` via a hujson (JSONC) patch: comments, foreign context servers, and every other settings key are preserved; an explicit `enabled: false` on a managed entry survives sync; an unparseable settings file aborts the sync instead of being overwritten. The merge also migrates the legacy nested `{"command": {"path", "arguments"}}` entry shape — verified 2026-07-14 to no longer parse as a stdio server in current Zed (`ContextServerSettingsContent` is `#[serde(untagged)]`; the Stdio variant requires a flat string `"command"` — see https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/docs/src/ai/mcp.md and `crates/settings_content/src/project.rs`) — to the flat `{"command", "args", "env", "timeout"}` shape, and cleans up stale `mcp.json` artifacts from both the repo staging dir and the legacy home path. `loom vendor-specs check` now covers Zed via the raw GitHub docs URL (zed.dev 404s non-browser fetchers). Pinned by `TestProfileDrivenConfig_ZedEmitsContextServers` and the `TestMergeZedContextServers_*` suite (comment preservation, foreign-server preservation, legacy migration, disabled-flag carry-over, unparseable-file refusal).

### Fixed
- **Council external incident proposals are constrained to local follow-up classes** (`pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails.go`, `pkg/mills/council/external_incident_rules.go`, `docs/external-dependency-incidents.md`): external dependency incident guardrails now drop file-backed proposals that do not implement repository-owned guardrails, docs, telemetry, or config, and keep the explicit empty-proposals omit reason when nothing local remains.
- **Mills transient-merge and fixture-canary recovery** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): retry GitLab's narrow `422 Branch cannot be merged` settling response after green CI, and keep safe fixture canaries from spending their implementation turn recompiling tests that the dedicated test stage already owns.
- **Hub dial handshake is now bounded — a wedged tunnel stream can no longer hang a tool call indefinitely** (`internal/daemon/daemon_new.go` hub `DialFunc`, `internal/daemon/daemon_tools_fetch.go` `initializeMCPTransportWithTimeout`/`hubInitTimeout`; observed live 2026-07-11). The hub pool's `DialFunc` ran the MCP initialize handshake on the caller's request context, which carries no deadline for a plain proxied `tools/call` — so when cloudflared edge churn accepted the WebSocket upgrade but never delivered the init response, the dial (and the gitlab `get_merge_request`/`merge_merge_request` calls queued on it) hung 1800s+ until the client aborted. The handshake now runs under its own 30s bound (mirroring the local DialFunc's existing init timeout) layered on any caller deadline, so a dead tunnel stream surfaces as a fast dial error and the pipeline's existing local-fallback path takes over. Pinned by `TestInitializeMCPTransportWithTimeout_*`.
- **TestToolRefreshDebounce_Coalesces no longer flakes on loaded CI runners** (`internal/daemon/tool_refresh_debounce_test.go`; observed 2026-07-11, pipeline 18242 job 175501: "expected 0 fires during rapid-batch (took 150.681283ms), got 1"). The test fired 20 schedule() calls with ~5ms sleeps against a 50ms debounce window and asserted zero mid-batch fires — but a single scheduler-jittered sleep overshooting the window makes a mid-batch fire legitimate debounce behavior, not a bug. The test now uses a 100ms window, measures the actual gap between consecutive schedule() calls, enforces the strict zero-mid-batch/exactly-one-fire assertions only when no gap overshot, and on jittered runs still pins coalescing via an overshoot-derived upper bound (at most one fire per overshot gap plus the final quiet-period fire). The quiet-period fire is awaited with a deadline poll instead of a fixed sleep. Coalescing semantics unchanged.
- **Secrets file backend no longer pops keychain dialogs at daemon startup or mints unrecoverable ephemeral master keys** (`pkg/secrets/file.go`; observed live 2026-07-11). `NewFileBackend` eagerly resolved the master key at construction — which `DefaultManager` runs unconditionally at daemon startup — so a loomd started in a security session without a default keychain (e.g. `launchctl kickstart` from a background shell) popped a blocking macOS "Keychain Not Found" dialog, and because the `KeychainBackend.Set` error was ignored, every keychain-less restart generated a DIFFERENT random key: a `secrets.enc` written under one ephemeral key was silently undecryptable after the next restart. The key now resolves lazily on first actual read of an existing secrets file or first write (construction and reads of a missing file never touch the keychain); a new key is minted and keychain-persisted only on write, and if persisting fails the backend logs a WARN and falls back to the long-promised-but-never-implemented machine-ID derivation (IOPlatformUUID / `/etc/machine-id` / hostname), which is stable across restarts. Existing keychain-stored and `LOOM_MASTER_KEY` keys keep working unchanged. Pinned by `TestFileBackend_ConstructionNoKeychainInteraction`, `TestFileBackend_KeychainSetFails_StableKeyAcrossRestarts`, and the mint/round-trip tests in `file_test.go`.
- **Council editor guardrails now drain non-actionable external incident proposals to the explicit empty-proposals fallback** (`pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails.go`, `pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails_test.go`): fileless proposals for purely external incidents (for example rerunning GitLab CI or changing provider credentials) are dropped before backlog mutation, `created` is reset to zero, and the guardrail note carries the stable `omit_reason` while preserving file-backed in-repo classifiers/runbooks.
- **Council proposal guardrails now persist the mandated external-incident omit reason** (`pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails.go`, `sidecar.go`, `editor_guardrails_test.go`): external dependency incidents that leave no repo-actionable proposal now set `sidecar.omit_reason` to `external dependency incident; no actionable in-repo follow-up`, while file-backed follow-up keeps its proposal and label.
- **mcp-browserkit heals over a stale `BROWSERKIT_PYTHON` instead of failing NotConfigured** (`cmd/mcp-browserkit/main.go` `browserkitPython`/`pythonCandidates`/`pythonHasDeps`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`; observed live 2026-07-10). The registry defaulted `BROWSERKIT_PYTHON=python3`, which overrode the binary's venv auto-discovery — so every generated config pointed at a bare interpreter without `flexinfer-browser-kit`, and the `screenshot` tool failed `NotConfigured` even with a fully provisioned `~/.config/loom/browserkit-venv`. The server now probes candidates (explicit override → managed venv → PATH `python3`) with the helper's own import and uses the first interpreter that actually has the deps; only a successful probe is cached, so installing deps fixes the server without a restart. The registry default is dropped so the override is only set when the user sets it. Verified end-to-end: a stdio `tools/call screenshot` with `BROWSERKIT_PYTHON=python3` forced now captures https://example.com via the venv. Pinned by `TestPythonCandidates_*`.
- **mcp-browserkit screenshot payloads are spec-compliant raw base64, not data URLs** (`cmd/mcp-browserkit/main.go` `normalizeImageBase64`). MCP `ImageContent.data` must be bare base64; the server wrapped it in `data:image/png;base64,…`, which spec-compliant clients reject as invalid base64. The data-URL prefix is now stripped/validated and the content `data` field carries raw standard base64. Pinned by `TestNormalizeImageBase64_*`.
- **mcp-memory no longer self-deadlocks on the first write with the default config** (`cmd/mcp-memory/main.go` `saveLocked`; regression-pinned by `TestAutoSaveDoesNotDeadlock`). Every mutating handler held `m.mu.Lock()` and then called `save()`, which re-acquired `m.mu.RLock()` — `sync.RWMutex` is not reentrant, so with `MEMORY_AUTO_SAVE` at its default (on) the first `create_entities`/`create_relations`/`add_observations`/delete call hung forever, never released the write lock, and wedged the whole server (all later calls block, the mcp-go concurrency semaphore fills). Tests missed it because they run with `autoSave: false`. The persist path is now caller-locked (renamed `saveLocked`) and never re-locks. The write is also now atomic (tempfile + rename) so a crash mid-write can't truncate the graph and wipe all persisted memory (`writeFileAtomic` convention), and `load` re-initializes nil maps from a `{"entities": null}` file instead of panicking on first insert (`TestLoadNullMapsReinitializes`). When `$HOME` is unavailable the graph anchors under `os.TempDir()` instead of a cwd-relative path.
- **mcp-git branch listing no longer drops or mislabels branches** (`cmd/mcp-git/main.go` `handleGitBranch`; `TestHandleGitBranch_ListsNonCurrentBranches`). The `--format=%(HEAD) %(refname:short) …` output was split with `strings.Fields`, which collapsed the single-space `%(HEAD)` column for non-current branches — so an upstream-less non-current branch (`" dev"`) was silently omitted and a tracking branch (`" feat origin/feat"`) reported its upstream as its name. The format now uses a literal unit-separator delimiter so empty columns stay addressable.
- **mcp-git rejects git option-injection through ref/branch/commit args** (`cmd/mcp-git/main.go` `checkRef`; `TestHandleGitDiff_RejectsOptionInjection` et al.). User-supplied refs were appended as bare positional tokens, so a leading dash smuggled flags into git: `git_diff` with `commit="--output=/path"` made `git diff` write an arbitrary file, and `git_checkout` with `branch="-f"` force-discarded the working tree. All ref/branch/commit/remote positionals (`git_diff`, `git_show`, `git_branch`, `git_checkout`, `git_push`, `git_pull`) now reject leading-dash values (git itself forbids such ref names, so nothing legitimate is refused), and `git_stash` validates `action` against a fixed set.
- **validate.asInt rejects NaN/Inf/overflow instead of returning garbage** (`pkg/validate/validate.go` `floatToInt`). A JSON number like `1e300` was converted with `int(n)` on an out-of-range float — implementation-defined in Go, in practice `math.MinInt64` — so `Int`/`IntRange`/etc. (used by ~50 servers) returned a garbage extreme; `NaN`/`Inf` likewise. These now fail the coercion (falling back to the default or a validation error). Integer-valued floats still truncate toward zero as before (unchanged, tested contract). Pinned by the extended `TestAsInt`.
- **mcperror.IsNotFound/IsServerError handle wrapped and typed-nil errors** (`pkg/mcperror/error.go` `codeMatches`). Both used a plain `err.(*Error)` type assertion, so a 404 wrapped with `fmt.Errorf("…: %w", e)` was missed, and a typed-nil `*Error` (what `Wrap`/`WrapAPI`/`OperationFailed` return for a nil input) stored in an `error` interface panicked on the `.Code` dereference. They now use `errors.As` with an explicit nil guard. Pinned by the new `TestIsNotFound` wrapped/typed-nil cases.
- **Codebase indexer no longer silently loses points when the embedding model's output dimension drifts** (`pkg/codebase/index_pipeline.go` `checkVectorDim`, `pkg/codebase/watch.go`; evidence: qdrant VM journal 2026-07-09/10 `Vector dimension error: expected dim: 1024, got 1536`). Bulk upserts run with `wait=false`, so Qdrant ACKs a dimension-mismatched vector into the WAL and only declines it at apply time — the point is lost with no client-visible error, and the stuck WAL entry re-logs a dimension error on every server restart (observed as a ~900/hour error stream while the server crash-looped). The existing collection guard checked only the FIRST non-empty vector of the first batch, so a mixed batch — cached vectors at the collection's dimension plus fresh vectors at a drifted dimension (morph-embedding-v3 changed 1024 → 1536 in place on 2026-07-09) — passed the guard and dropped every fresh point. Both the index pipeline and the watch path now validate every point's vector dimension against the ensured collection dimension before upsert and fail the batch loudly on mismatch. Pinned by `TestCheckVectorDim_RejectsMismatch`.
- **Rubric judges no longer fail correct code for hypothetical fragility** (`pkg/mills/gates/rubric_boilerplate.go` grounding instructions; escalation [#309](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/309)). The `pr_self_review` judge failed a correct dynamic-WHERE SQL builder at 0.50 with a finding that conceded correctness mid-sentence ("making the total count of placeholders match the args count, however, the logic is highly fragile … if the string concatenation is modified") — grading a hypothetical future edit, burning 3 review attempts and $1.34 on code whose tests had already passed. The shared grounding boilerplate now instructs judges to score only defects that exist in the diff AS WRITTEN — no "fragile if modified later", stylistic preferences, or robustness speculation — and states that a reason conceding current-correct behavior is not a scorable concern. Pinned by the extended template snapshot tests.
- **Council gray-band dedup now blocks reworded re-mints of recently-SHIPPED themes** (`pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go` `findGrayBandDuplicate`; evidence: [#308](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/308)). The gray band deliberately exempted merged candidates ("a similar successor to shipped work is legit follow-up") — but the council's brief doesn't know last week's item shipped, so it re-proposed the same deliverable reworded: main accumulated FIVE near-identical CI triage runbooks in five days (docs/ci-incident-triage.md, ci-triage-runbook.md, council-external-dependency-incidents.md, mills-escalation-and-dependency-failures.md, mills-incident-triage.md) and only the scope gate stopped the sixth. Recently-created merged items (within the existing 7-day gray-band window) now block; older merged items still never block, preserving legitimate follow-up to long-shipped work. Pinned by the flipped `recently merged lookalike blocks` + new `old merged lookalike does not block` subtests.
- **Cross-repo pipeline-merge audits now load the diff from the item's target project** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go` `gitlabMergedDiffLoader`; advisory issue [#264](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/264)). MR IIDs are per-project, but the audit diff loader always queried the operator's home project — so the flexdeck!247 S6 kill-test merge (README-only) was audited as loom-core!247 (Dockerfile CI-token work) and scored survival 0.20 with critical findings for the wrong MR's real diff. The loader now uses `ForProject(item.TargetProject)`; home-repo items are unaffected. Pinned by `TestGitlabMergedDiffLoader_CrossRepoUsesTargetProject`.
- **Pipeline-merge audits no longer flag union-merged CHANGELOG hunks as scope creep** (`pkg/mills/audit/diff_filter.go`, `triggers.go` `OnPipelineMerged`; advisory issues [#285](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/285), [#302](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/302)). After a union-merge cascade rescue, the merged MR's CHANGELOG hunk carries sibling MRs' entries as apparent additions, and the rubric auditors flagged every such merge as "massive scope creep" / "mega-commit". CHANGELOG.md sections (root only — nested paths stay) are now stripped from the audited diff with an explicit omission marker, and a changelog-only merge skips the audit entirely. Pinned by `TestStripChangelogFromDiff_*` and the `OnPipelineMerged` strip/skip tests.
- **A spawn pod that never becomes ready no longer escalates as a code failure** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`; escalation [#306](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/306), 2026-07-10). `pod creation failed: pod not ready: watch closed for pod spawn-…` — the devbox k8s backend's pod-readiness wait giving up before the pod reached Running — classified as `class=code` and burned implement attempts on a pod that never ran an agent. Pod-lifecycle readiness failures (`pod not ready`, `pod creation failed`, `watch closed for pod`, deleted-before-Running) now classify `ClassTransient` (free retry — the pipeline runner mints a fresh spawn per attempt, so nothing re-attaches to the dead pod), while an `image pull error` cause inside the same wrapper classifies `ClassInfra` (persistent registry/image misconfig a fresh pod hits identically). Pinned by the real-bytes fixtures in `error_class_test.go`.
- **Mills escalation event fallback classification now includes retry semantics** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate_test.go`): legacy failure records with only top-level escalation metadata now publish grouped classification objects with classifier provenance, `free_retry`, and `terminal` fields instead of retryability alone.
- **Rubric judges no longer fabricate "undefined symbol" compile failures — the top open-escalation burner** (`pkg/mills/gates/rubric_boilerplate.go`, `gates.go` `StageInput.TestsPassed`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go` `gateInputFor`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go` `composePrompt`; escalation [#304](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/304) and ~10 sibling escalations #288–#303, 2026-07-07..09). The `pr_self_review` judge scored real work 0.00 claiming `undefined: ClassInfra` et al. — package-local symbols the new file references that are defined elsewhere in the repo, invisible in the diff-only prompt. The grounding boilerplate's own wording ("do NOT reference symbols … not present in the diff") nudged the judge toward treating out-of-diff symbols as nonexistent; the gate then burned 3+ review attempts (~$2/run) on code whose deterministic tests stage had already compiled the full repo green. Two-part fix: (a) the shared grounding instructions now state the diff is a PARTIAL view of an existing repository and forbid build/compile claims outright (the judge cannot compile — that is the tests stage's job); (b) the rubric's promised-but-never-delivered test results now exist — `gateInputFor` stamps `TestsPassed` from the successful `tests` stage output and `composePrompt` renders "Tests stage: PASSED … treat build/compile health as verified". Pinned by the template snapshot tests, `TestRubricTemplate_TestsPassedRendersVerifiedNote`, and `TestRunner_GateInputStampsTestsPassed`.
- **Imperative workflow runs no longer zombie-loop on a terminally failed spawn** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go` `ErrSpawnTerminalFailure`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`, `pkg/mills/worker/adapter.go`, `pkg/mills/workflow/runtime.go`; observed live 2026-07-09 on `wf-canary-1780869560631332347` / `wf-canary-1783557460717425003`). When a workflow run's spawn reached a terminal non-completed status (`failed`/`stopped` — e.g. the HUD's "pod not found during reconciliation"), the runtime left the run `running` for the next tick to retry — but the deterministic idempotency key pins every resume AND re-dispatch to the same dead spawn, so the scheduler re-failed the identical resume every 60s forever, spamming a WARN pair per tick. Spawn clients now wrap the new `pipeline.ErrSpawnTerminalFailure` sentinel on terminal non-completed status (re-exported as `worker.ErrSpawnTerminalFailure` so the workflow layer keeps its worker-only dependency), and the runtime transitions the run to `error` (terminal, like quarantine) instead of retrying. Deploying this self-heals the two live zombies on their first post-rollout tick. Pinned by `TestRuntime_TerminalSpawnFailureFailsRun` and the sentinel assertion in `TestRun_FailedTerminalReturnsError`.
- **Mills now escalates known external dependency incidents without blind retries** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner_test.go`): classified external signatures are promoted to terminal retry-policy decisions so escalation metadata names the dependency incident explicitly.
- **Rubric judges no longer fail real slices for the harness's own diff truncation** (`pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go` `composePrompt` + tests; escalation [#301](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/301)). The judge prompt hard-capped the diff at 8 KiB and cut mid-hunk, so any routine feature slice (migration + store + runner wiring) overflowed it and the strict `spec_conformance` rubric scored the change as an incomplete implementation — "the diff is truncated at the most critical implementation point" — a $2.18 false escalation caused by the gate, not the author. The cap is now 32 KiB (matching the spawn capture cap), the cut lands on a line boundary so the judge never sees a torn diff line, and a truncated diff (by this cap OR an upstream capture marker) carries an explicit note telling the judge not to grade truncation as missing work.
