- **Merge-conflict 422s escalate on first sight instead of burning 3 identical retries** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`; escalation [#287](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/287)). GitLab's `status 422: Branch cannot be merged` rejection is deterministic — the branch needs a rebase — but classified as `code` it was retried verbatim 3× in ~1.5s total. Now `ClassConfig` (terminal), and the terminal-config escalation message names the rebase fix.
- **A recycled devbox sandbox pod no longer escalates as a code failure** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`; escalation [#289](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/289)). `unable to upgrade connection: container not found ("devbox")` — the kubelet rejecting an exec against a replaced pod — now classifies `ClassTransient` (free retry, a fresh exec lands on the replacement pod) instead of burning all attempts in ~4s as `class=code`. The devbox quality-gate error also now names the FAILING checks with their output tails (`devbox quality gate failed (1/1 checks failed: fmt[exit=1]: …)`) instead of the total count with no evidence ("failed: 1 checks"), so the classifier can see infra needles and the escalation is actionable.
- **A terminally-failed MR pipeline is no longer re-watched** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go` `ErrCIPipelineTerminal`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`; escalation [#292](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/292)). A `ci_watch` retry re-polls the SAME dead pipeline and returns the identical error within seconds (attempts 2 and 3 completed in 2.1s / 1.3s with zero new signal). The stage error now wraps a sentinel and the runner escalates on first sight, preserving `class=code` so the metric stays honest; a requeue after fixing the branch (or retrying the pipeline in GitLab) still recovers the item.
- **Restart spawn recovery is actually wired now — and runs before the reconcile loop** (`internal/hud/embed.go`; completes [#300](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/300), found by the second live S1c run). Two defects the !1010 re-drive couldn't survive: (a) `SpawnOrchestrator.RecoverSpawns()` — the wrapper that runs `resumePreRuntimeSpawns` and the new `recoverInterruptedSpawns` — **had no caller**; the embedded HUD only invoked the constructor's raw `RecoverFromStore`, so pre-runtime resume AND the interrupted-turn re-drive were dead code in production. (b) The reconcile loop started before recovery, and its discovered-untracked-pod path persists a lossy label-rebuilt record (empty request, no idempotency key) — observed live: it clobbered the keyed canary spawn's durable record, making it un-re-drivable even with working recovery. The embedded HUD now runs `RecoverSpawns()` synchronously after the K8s client is wired and strictly before `StartReconcileLoop`.
- **Spawn turns survive a mobile-hud restart: interrupted keyed spawns are re-driven, unkeyed ones fail fast** (`internal/hud/spawn.go` `recoverInterruptedSpawns`; [#300](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/300), found by the live S1c dual-crash kill-test). Agent turns are exec sessions driven by the mobile-hud process, so a restart kills every in-flight turn — but the rehydrated spawn record stayed `running` (the pod idles at sleep-infinity with no agent process) until the reconciler's ~65-minute deadline backstop failed it, and a workflow-runtime `Resume` polling the spawn id hung the whole window. Restart recovery now classifies every recovered non-terminal spawn (`classifyInterruptedSpawn`, pure + table-tested): **keyed** spawns re-run the full spawn flow — the image build is cached, pod creation `AlreadyExists`-adopts the live pod, and the turn re-executes in the same workspace, so spawn id/pod/worktree stay stable while turn execution remains at-least-once; **unkeyed** spawns (which the AlreadyExists backstop deliberately refuses to re-attach) fail immediately with an honest driver-lost error instead of holding a pool slot. Also hardens `failSpawn` against a nil agent bridge. This unblocks the scoped S1c spawn/journal gate re-run (`docs/runbooks/mills-workflow-s1c-killtest.md`). Pinned by `TestClassifyInterruptedSpawn` and `TestRecoverInterruptedSpawns_RedrivesKeyedAndFailsUnkeyed`.

### Added
- **Council external dependency incident runbook now covers repeated Renovate and GitLab CI clusters** (`docs/council-external-dependency-incidents.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`): documents how council summaries should group repeated external failure signatures, capture operator evidence, and limit follow-up proposals to actionable in-repo changes.
- **Mills escalation payloads now emit grouped classification metadata** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate_test.go`, `internal/contracts/escalation_test.go`, `internal/contracts/testdata/escalation_failure_record.golden`): `FailureRecord` includes a structured `classification` object with escalation class, policy failure class, retryability, and external dependency fields while preserving the legacy top-level fields for existing consumers.
- **S1c dual-crash kill-test harness for the Mills imperative workflow runtime** (`pkg/mills/workflow/killtest/`, `cmd/mills-workflow-killtest`, `docs/runbooks/mills-workflow-s1c-killtest.md`; plan `.loom/134` §3). The harness drives the documented procedure against the real deployed pods: fail-closed preflight, caller-owned idempotent launch, continuous exact-name Kubernetes watch, target-bound crash leases, CRASH A (operator) + CRASH B (mobile-hud), terminal/all-record polling, exact replacement Loki attribution, and pure unit-tested PASS-1/2/4/5 verdicts. PASS-1 requires a single pod UID incarnation and durable-path re-attach/AlreadyExists evidence; one point-in-time pod is insufficient. The harness never flips policy itself. Its scope is spawn/journal identity, not exactly-once agent-turn effects.
### Changed
- **Factory loom sounds upgraded to a py-chiptune sound pack** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/assets/sfx/{clack,chime,klaxon}.wav`, `utils/factorySounds.ts`, `scripts/gen-sfx.sh`; plan `.loom/181-iteration-plan-factory-chiptune-sfx-2026-07-08.md`). The three loom voices are now authored with `libs/py-chiptune` (flexinfer-chiptune: `sfx land` → reed clack, `sfx success` → bolt chime, `sfx error` → spark klaxon) and shipped as ~40 KB of bundled WAVs, fetched + decoded lazily inside the enable gesture; per-voice gain trims into the existing master bus. The original hand-synthesized oscillator voices remain as per-voice fallback, so a failed fetch/decode degrades to the old sound rather than silent errors. `scripts/gen-sfx.sh` records the exact regeneration commands. The audio honesty contract is unchanged: sound only when a real row is laid, opt-in, off by default.

### Added
- **Mills budget fuel gauge — the rolling 24h window on the operator wire and the Factory panel** (`pkg/mills/budget.go` `WindowUsage` + tests, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_status.go`, HUD `mills.svelte.ts` `BudgetWindowUsage`, `factoryHelpers.ts` `fuelReading` + tests, FactoryPanel gauge; research `.loom/174` thread F, deferred from !1015; plan `.loom/180-iteration-plan-mills-budget-fuel-gauge-2026-07-08.md`). New informational `Budget.WindowUsage(tier)` projects the enforcer's own rolling-24h spend/run queries (the SAME `spentSince`/`runsSince` that gate runs — no second aggregation path that could disagree) as `{spent_usd, cap_usd, runs, runs_cap}`; `/api/mills/status` carries it under `budget.{pipeline,council}`, omitting a tier whose read failed. The Factory gauges gain a fuel tank for the pipeline tier: green above half, amber above a quarter, red below; an uncapped policy shows spend with "no cap" (an uncapped tank, not an empty one); an absent payload renders an em dash — the gauge never guesses a level.

### Added
- **HUD Factory panel: pattern books — the Pattern Loom catalog on the loom's shelf** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/PatternShelf.svelte`, `utils/patternBooks.ts` + tests, FactoryPanel wiring, `BacklogItem.PlanID` TS surface; research `.loom/174` concept 8 / thread C, plan `.loom/179-iteration-plan-factory-pattern-books-2026-07-08.md`). Jacquard card chains were interchangeable programs; the shelf renders every approved catalog pattern as a labeled book with a mini punch-card chain, and a book whose pattern has a run weaving right now visibly *feeds* (its chain animates; static under reduced-motion). Attribution is derived, never guessed: stamped plans embed their pattern slug in the plan id (`plan-stamp-<slug>-<primary>`, `svc_pattern_stamp.go:134`), the operator's backlog list already serializes `PlanID` (untagged struct → on the wire; now declared on the TS interface), and runs carry `BacklogID` — longest-slug prefix matching disambiguates `go-rest` vs `go-rest-service`. Shuttle pick labels name the book (`· book «Go REST Service»`) when the advancing run was stamped. One-shot catalog fetch on mount (no new pollers — the patterns poller has no owner refcount); empty catalog renders no shelf, not placeholder theater; clicking a book opens the Pattern Loom panel. The research doc's "verify catalog on HUD API first" gate was run in-code: `/api/patterns` route + full attribution chain confirmed in source before any UI work.

### Added
- **HUD Factory panel: garnish quartet — departure board, creel, gauge motion, loom sounds** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/DepartureBoard.svelte`, `utils/departureHelpers.ts` + tests, `widgets/RollingNumber.svelte`, `utils/factorySounds.ts` + tests, FactoryPanel; research `.loom/174` concepts 4/5/7/9, plan `.loom/178-iteration-plan-factory-garnish-2026-07-08.md`). (4) The floor log's CSS marquee becomes a split-flap departure board — one row per run, cells flap only when a value changes, active shuttles read EN ROUTE or DELAYED (delayed = *observed* sitting in the same stage past a fuse: 12 m default, 25 m for ci_watch — client-observed, not a p90 guess), history reads ARRIVED/DIVERTED/HELD; `floorLogLines` removed. (5) The creel: logical fleet agents drawn as bobbins at the top of the stage — active agents wind (orbiting pirn dot), idle sit dim, a stale feed freezes the lot; the "weavers on the floor" gauge switches to `liveAgentCount` so gauge and creel show the same honest root-collapsed number. (7) Count gauges roll via a shared `RollingNumber` widget and the pass-rate gauge gains a spring-overshoot needle dial (budget fuel gauge deferred — no rolling-window budget on the HUD payload yet). (9) Opt-in synthesized loom sounds (clack per laid pick, chime per bolt, low horn per spark) behind a 🔇/🔊 toggle persisted in localStorage; the AudioContext is created inside the toggle gesture, and the reed beating air stays silent — audio obeys the same only-real-events rule as the canvas. All new motion is static under `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Fixed
- **HUD Factory paused state could never fire** (`FactoryPanel.svelte`, `AndonMode.svelte`): both read `status.Enabled`, a field the operator never sends — `/api/mills/status` is snake_case (`policy_enabled`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_status.go:38`) — so the "paused" badge and the andon PAUSED lamp were unreachable. Now uses the status→policy fallback OverviewPanel already uses; verified end-to-end in a browser with a `policy_enabled: false` payload.

### Added
- **HUD Factory panel: andon mode — the fullscreen office-TV board** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/AndonMode.svelte`, `utils/andonHelpers.ts` + tests, FactoryPanel wiring; research `.loom/174` concept 6 / threads B+F, plan `.loom/177-iteration-plan-factory-andon-mode-2026-07-08.md`). An andon board exists to be glanced at from 3–10 m, so the Factory panel gains a `⛶ andon` header action that opens a full-viewport board: one lamp with a safety-first priority order (FEED STALE outranks paused outranks escalation-storm outranks weaving/idle — the board never glows green on a dead feed; stale gets hazard stripes, not just a hue), a giant north-star odometer on bolts merged · 24h with mechanically rolling digit reels (static under reduced-motion), four supporting gauges, and a live "updated Ns ago" freshness line that turns urgent after three missed poll ticks. The storm rule is explicit and unit-pinned: ≥3 sparks in 24 h AND sparks at half the bolt rate or worse. Mode state IS the router's third hash segment, so `#mills/factory/andon` deep-links from a TV bookmark, survives reload, and Escape restores `#mills/factory`; the overlay mounts inside FactoryPanel so the existing 15 s poll is the only data source. Optional browser-fullscreen toggle for chrome-free TVs. Helpers rune-free, pinned by `andonHelpers.test.ts` (state-priority matrix, storm boundaries, odometer padding, freshness buckets).
- **HUD Factory panel: bolt archive — the "tartan of the week"** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/BoltArchive.svelte`, `utils/tartanHelpers.ts` + tests, `stores/mills.svelte.ts` `fetchArchiveRuns`; research `.loom/174` concept 3, plan `.loom/176-iteration-plan-factory-bolt-archive-2026-07-08.md`). The live loom's cloth evaporates as rows scroll off the beam, but every row is a real merged MR — so a new header button re-weaves the last 7 days of terminal runs into one persistent strip: day bands (empty days show honest "no cloth" dashes), each run woven with the same `seededPattern` the loom uses (it IS the same fabric), per-run hover titles, and a caption with the week's bolts/sparks/cost totals. Exportable as self-contained SVG or 2× PNG (colors resolved from live theme tokens at build time — no CSS vars in the file) for standups or the office TV. The archive fetches via a new non-storing `fetchArchiveRuns` — deliberately not `pipelineHistory`, which the loom diffs into weave events (parking 500 older runs there would flood the shuttle with phantom rows). Helpers rune-free and pinned by `tartanHelpers.test.ts` (day-boundary grouping, byte-identical determinism, XML escaping).

### Changed
- **HUD Factory panel: the loom is now an instrument, not theater** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/FactoryPanel.svelte`, `utils/factoryHelpers.ts` + tests; research `.loom/174-research-factory-view-inspiration-2026-07-08.md`, plan `.loom/175-iteration-plan-factory-honest-loom-2026-07-08.md`). Four honesty upgrades: (1) a shuttle pass lays cloth only for a real event — a terminal run (bolt/spark) or an active run advancing a stage (new `diffStagePicks` poll differ); with no event the reed beats air, and the 30%-random stage labels are gone. (2) Woven rows are deterministic (`seededPattern`, mulberry32 over the run ID — the same history always weaves the same fabric) and inspectable: hover shows the backlog/run and kind, click opens the existing `PipelineRunDetail` drawer with the run's stages and gates. (3) The punch-card tape is a pure function of the live policy (`policyTapeSeed`/`tapeHole`); a version bump or kill-switch flip visibly re-punches the chain and floats a "policy spliced · vN" label. (4) A stale poll or fetch error halts the loom mid-pick with an explicit caption and freezes the tape — the panel can no longer animate a healthy factory on a dead feed.

### Fixed
- **mills: preserve retryability and dependency metadata on partial escalation metadata updates** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline_escalation_class_test.go`): `SetEscalationMetadata` now treats omitted fields as unchanged so later classification stamps cannot erase persisted retryability or external dependency context.

### Changed
- **Scope-gate directory envelope includes descendants of slice-declared directories** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`): the first live run under the same-dir-only envelope (`PIPE-…emit-classification…-1783538288`, 2026-07-08) cleared `nonempty_diff` via the resurrected cumulative capture but failed scope on a REQUIRED numbered SQL migration in `pkg/mills/store/migrations/` while its slice declared `pkg/mills/store/store.go` — a file the agent could neither omit nor relocate, so the run could only escalate. The council cannot predict a package's internal layout any more than its basenames; descendants of a declared directory are now in scope (parents and unrelated packages still violate). Pinned by `TestScope_DirectoryEnvelopeIncludesDescendants`.
- **govulncheck job gets the vet-class k8s memory profile** (`.gitlab-ci.yml` `security:govulncheck` `KUBERNETES_MEMORY_REQUEST: 4Gi`/`LIMIT: 8Gi`): symbol-mode scanning typechecks + builds SSA for the whole module — the same working set as `go vet`, whose job already carries this override — and the runner's default limit OOM-killed the scan on main pipeline 17805 (`Killed`, exit 1), blocking the deploy stage.
- **CI + image toolchains: Go 1.26.4 → 1.26.5; govulncheck gates on reachable symbols instead of module presence** (`.gitlab-ci.yml` `GO_VERSION` + `security:govulncheck`, `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.custom-server`, `Dockerfile.custom-server.local`, `Dockerfile.local`, `Dockerfile.loom-mills-operator`, `internal/hud/spawn.go` `goVersion`). Main went red on 2026-07-08 (pipeline 17801) when the vuln DB gained GO-2026-5856/GO-2026-4970 (stdlib, fixed in go1.26.5) and GO-2026-5932 (`x/crypto/openpgp`, unmaintained, "Fixed in: N/A") — with `security:govulncheck` a hard gate on the deploy stage, every image build was blocked. The toolchain bump clears the stdlib pair; the scan switches from `-scan=module` (fails on mere dependency presence — unfixable for no-patch advisories; nothing in the repo imports openpgp) to symbol mode over `./...`, which fails only when an affected symbol is reachable and demotes the openpgp advisory to informational. The spawn-runtime `goVersion` tracks the bump so in-spawn `go` never lags CI (drift-guard `TestGoVersionCoversGoMod`).

### Fixed
- **Workflow definitions can now declare `map_reduce` and `parallel` steps through the public define path** (`pkg/agentcontext/service_workflows.go`, `workflow_expr.go`). `HandleWorkflowDefine`'s hand parser silently dropped `map_input_key`, `map_step_template`, `reduce_tool_name/server_name/tool_args`, `max_concurrency`, `parallel_steps`, and `approval_timeout_seconds` — all fields the executor fully supports — so a `map_reduce` step registered via `agent_workflow_define` (or the `.agents/workflows` YAML bootstrap) failed at runtime with `map_step_template is required`. Step parsing is now a recursive `parseWorkflowStepMap` covering the full `WorkflowStep` schema. Also fixed the companion gap in variable resolution: `resolveVariables` didn't recurse into `[]any`, so `${...}` templates nested inside arrays (e.g. an `agent_context_add` entries array) passed through unresolved. Pinned by `TestParseWorkflowSteps_*` and `TestResolveAny_ArrayRecursion`.
- **Two `.agents/workflows` definitions no longer call a tool deleted in SIMP-4** (`.agents/workflows/long-context-analysis.yaml`, `recursive-context.yaml`). Both `record` steps invoked `agent_reasoning_chain_add`, removed from the MCP surface in SIMP-4 — the step failed on every run. Findings now record via `agent_context_add` entries, per the SIMP-4 note ("agents use context entries instead"). `recursive-context.yaml`'s `map_phase` additionally becomes definable thanks to the parser fix above.
- **Weaver honors `max_tokens` instead of silently ignoring it** (`pkg/weaver/responses_client.go`, `router.go`). `QueryRequest.MaxTokens` was plumbed through the MCP tool schema, HUD handler, and daemon dispatch but never read — subagent completions hardcoded 4096 and synthesis hardcoded 1024. The request-level value now bounds the synthesized answer (its documented semantics), and the per-domain YAML `max_tokens` (parsed but unused since the field landed) bounds each subagent completion. `TestFlexInferResponsesClient_MaxTokens` pins the wire format.
- **Weaver auto-compose "refused" metric no longer counts the whole registry** (`pkg/weaver/router.go`, `auto_compose.go`). The refused-outcome bookkeeping incremented once per query whenever ANY write-domain existed in the registry, regardless of whether the query would have touched it. It now counts only Write=true domains whose description matched the query's tokens — the domains actually refused for this query. Pinned by `TestCountRefusedWriteDomains`.
- **`policy.squads.enabled` actually gates squad routing now** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `handlers_squads.go`). The router's `PolicyGate` was designed and documented as "production wires this to PolicyManager" but never wired, so the flag had no effect and squads always routed. The operator now installs a live-PolicyManager gate on both the reconciler's router and the `/route-test` handler (whose candidate ranking stays visible while the flag is off). Behavior note: with the default `policy.squads.enabled = false`, squad routing/attribution now genuinely pauses until the 8.3-1 flip — previously it ran unconditionally.
- **`hud.weavertion` SSE event renamed to `hud.shuttle`, and the "weaverted"/"weavertion" rename artifacts cleaned up** (`internal/hud/embed.go`, frontend `events.svelte.ts`, `cmd/mcp-weaver/main.go`, `internal/daemon/weaver_tools.go`, `internal/hud/domain/weaver/`). The shuttle-monitor broadcast carried an event type produced by a bad global rename ("orchestration" → "weavertion"); no consumer keyed on the old name. The `weaver__gather` tool description and the weaver domain package comments read correctly again, and the standalone server's instructions block now lists all seven compound tools (fleet_status, deploy_status, incident_triage, codebase_overview were registered but undocumented).

### Added
- **Squad confidence now learns from failures: `failed` outcomes recorded on real escalations** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go` new `OnEscalated` hook, `pkg/mills/squads/outcome_recorder.go`). Squad success rate was structurally ~1.0 — merges wrote `merged_clean` and failures wrote nothing, so the router's 30-outcome confidence window carried no signal. A new runner hook (symmetric with `OnMerged`; auto-retried transient escalations excluded) drives `OutcomeRecorder.OnEscalated`, which attributes a `failed` squad outcome via the same routing event lookup. Pinned by `TestOutcomeRecorder_OnEscalated*` and `TestRunner_EscalateFiresOnEscalatedHook`.
- **Squad working memory gets its first production writer + the documented pruner** (`pkg/mills/squads/outcome_recorder.go`). `squad_memory` was read by the HUD detail panel and the squad planner but nothing ever wrote it. Each first-time merge attribution now appends a `merge` memory entry (title/backlog/run refs, importance 0.5) and opportunistically enforces the documented retention policy (drop importance < 0.3 older than 30 days — the "weekly pruner" that never existed). Best-effort: memory failures never affect the merge path. Pinned by `TestOutcomeRecorder_OnMergedAppendsMemory`.
- **Squads `in_flight` stat is real** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_squads.go`). The HUD rendered `outcome_stats.in_flight` as a headline metric but the backend always sent 0 ("slice fills 0; left for follow-up"). The list + detail endpoints now count active pipeline runs per squad via the same attribution-event lookup the outcome recorder uses. Pinned by `TestInFlightBySquad_CountsActiveAttributedRuns`.
- **Standalone `cmd/mcp-weaver` reaches feature parity with the daemon-embedded router** (`cmd/mcp-weaver/main.go`, preflight moved to `pkg/weaver/preflight.go` with `internal/daemon` delegating). The deployed k8s weaver server skipped YAML domain/behavior overrides, tool validation, and — critically — the model preflight, so it could never report `degraded`/`missing_models` when the FlexInfer catalog drifted (the exact failure mode the preflight was built for). The standalone server now loads both YAML override files, validates domain tool references against the hub, runs the boot-time preflight, and merges the result into `loom/weaver/status` with the same field names the daemon path uses. Preflight logic is shared (`weaver.RunPreflight`), covered by `TestRunPreflight_*`.

### Fixed
- **Cumulative diff capture actually works in production — retried runs no longer escalate finished branch work on `nonempty_diff`** (`pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/repo_root.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`; issue #224, observed live on the 2026-07-08 scope-fix kill-test run `PIPE-…add-mills-classification…-1783526902`: attempt 1 pushed tested work, the retry found the branch up to date, telemetry reported 0 files, and the run escalated with the finished 207-line change sitting on origin). Three stacked defects killed `attachGitContext` in prod: (1) **no post-boot git auth** — `ensureRepoRoot` authenticates via a `.netrc` in a temporary HOME deleted on return, so every later `git fetch` died with "could not read Username" (verified in-pod); new `installRepoGitAuth` persists a git-credential-store file inside the clone's `.git` dir (0600, token URL-encoded, same pod trust boundary as the `GITLAB_TOKEN` env) wired via repo-local `credential.helper`, installed on every boot so token rotation heals on the next roll; (2) **single-branch shallow clone** — the boot clone is `--depth=1 --branch main`, so `remote.origin.fetch` covers only main: a bare `git fetch origin <branch>` landed in FETCH_HEAD without creating `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>`, and depth-1 history held no merge-base for the `base...head` diff; the capture now fetches with an explicit `+refs/heads/<ref>:refs/remotes/origin/<ref>` refspec and `--depth=100` (recipe proven live in the operator pod against the stuck branch before shipping); (3) **gate bypass on carried diffs** — scope/path_policy judge `FilesChanged`, which telemetry leaves empty in this shape, so both passed trivially; the capture now fills it via `git diff --name-only` when the session reported none. The temp git-HOME moves off `os.TempDir()` (the pod's root fs including /tmp is read-only) to the PVC beside the repo. Tests: `TestInstallRepoGitAuth_PersistsCredentialStore`, `TestInstallRepoGitAuth_NoCloneNoop`, `TestRun_FillsFilesChangedFromCumulativeDiff`, updated fetch-refspec expectations in `TestRun_PopulatesDiffPatchAndCommitMessages`.
- **Scope gate accepts sibling basenames inside slice-declared directories** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`; 8 of the 19 Mills escalations on 2026-07-08). The council decomposes work into slice `files` before those files exist, and its guessed basenames are systematically near-misses of what implement actually writes — the slice said `pkg/mills/pipeline/escalation.go`, the agent (correctly) edited `escalate.go`; the slice said `pkg/mills/council/classifier.go`, the agent created `ci_incident_classifier.go`. `SanitizeProposalSlices` grounds parent *directories* against the real tree but by design cannot pin a basename, so the gate's exact-filename match false-failed every code slice, burned 3 implement attempts each, and escalated — a meta-loop, since the council's response was to mint more classification plans that then escalated the same way (the day's only merged run was docs-only, saved by the docs carve-out). The enforceable envelope is now the directory: a changed file whose parent directory equals a slice-declared file's parent directory is in scope (same directory only — subdirectories, parents, and unrelated packages still violate; a repo-root slice file does not open the root). Also: `testdata/` paths count as test collateral (a golden fixture added next to an in-scope test escalated `PIPE-…-propagate-failure-classification…`), and duplicate spawn-telemetry paths are deduped before rendering (every 2026-07-08 scope reason listed each violation twice). New tests pin the live failure paths: `TestScope_DirectoryEnvelopeAllowsSiblingBasenames`, `TestScope_DirectoryEnvelopeDoesNotCrossDirectories`, `TestScope_TopLevelSliceFileDoesNotOpenRepoRoot`, `TestScope_TestdataFixturesAllowed`, `TestScope_DuplicateChangedPathsRenderedOnce`.

### Changed
- **HUD: Mills tab joins the UX convention + the palette gets a `--mills` token** (pass 3 of the normalization; 15 files). New semantic token `--mills: #96BEFA` + `--mills-rgb` (theme.css) names the Mills lavender that 10 sites across 6 files hardcoded as `rgb(150,190,250)`; all swept onto the token, and the Mills panels' slate-blue chrome (`rgba(120,144,200,…)` hovers/borders) now rides `--mills-rgb` too. **Correctness**: PolicyProposalsCard's Apply/Reject failures were swallowed to `console.warn` — they now toast, Reject gained the same confirm() guard as Apply, and the card wires `loading`/error-aware empty states; BacklogPanel and mills/WorkflowsPanel passed no `emptyTone`, so a failed fetch rendered as a *pulsing healthy* idle surface — both now show the error tone; all nine Mills panels (Overview, Pipelines, Backlog, Workflows, Council, Eval, Squads, Audit, Cross-Repo) render `ErrorBanner` over stale content on poll failure instead of silently serving old rows. **Identity/affordances**: PatternsPanel's off-family hand-rolled header (15px span title) → shared `PanelHeader` with count pill + `aria-pressed` filters; EvalPanel's inert loop pills became real Loop A/B/C filters (aria-pressed, toggle-to-clear); Pipelines/Workflows inert count-pills demoted to plain stat text (no filter state exists to wire). **Tokens**: ~150 off-palette color literals across the panels and all three detail drawers (the copy-pasted `rgba(64,144,240)`/`(72,200,128)`/`(220,80,80)`/`(220,140,60)`/VS-Code-ish `(78,201,176)`/`(215,160,58)`/`(224,108,117)` state sets) → `--info/--success/--error/--warning/--mills` triplets; purples → the existing `--tier-short` violet; every `ui-monospace` stack → `var(--font-mono)`; Overview's ad-hoc glow spreads → `--glow-shadow-*`; SpawnDetailPanel migrated off the foreign `--color-*` alias namespace and its `rgba(129,240,254,…)` off-brand cyans onto canonical tokens. `vitest` 75/75, `vite build` clean, panels verified rendering in browser preview with zero console errors. HUD source-only — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **HUD: extend the panel-identity/failure-state/affordance convention to every remaining tab** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/` — 19 files, net −94 lines; pass 2 of the UX normalization started with the Operations/Work overhaul). **Identity**: `PanelHeader` adopted by Timeline, Traces, Stream, Sandbox (SandboxLive), Projects, Servers (via ServersHeader), Weaver, Knowledge, Memory, Graph (GraphFullView), and Reasoning — seven of these previously had no visible panel title at all, and three used off-scale hand-rolled titles. **Correctness**: two fetch-failures-masquerading-as-empty fixed — TracesPanel now renders `traceStore.error` (was "No traces matched" on a failed poll) and ReasoningPanel renders `reasoningStore.error` (was "No reasoning chains"); SpawnList surfaces list-poll failures; WeaverPanel no longer blanks the whole panel on every refresh (loading gate is cold-start-only) and its bare error div is a proper banner; TasksGroupedView no longer claims "No tasks match filters" before first fetch. **Deep links that kept their context**: Lifecycle's risky-namespace/relation cards now navigate to `#agents/dispatch/<namespace>` and DispatchPanel reads the detail segment as a roster namespace focus with a clearable chip (previously both CTAs dropped their argument and landed on the bare dispatch view); Plans' slice-agent button routes through `navigateToAgentSessionOrTraces` instead of ignoring its agent id. **Affordances**: Servers' inert status spans became clickable `aria-pressed` filter pills wired to `healthStore.setStatusFilter`; Projects' two header badges became pills deep-linking to Plans/Tasks; Timeline's hover-on-inert rows removed; GraphFullView's invalid nested-interactive entity header (button-in-button) restructured into sibling expand + name buttons. **Dedupe**: seven hand-rolled copies of the error banner (Servers, Catalog, Knowledge, Memory, Graph, Projects, SandboxLive) replaced by `shared/ErrorBanner.svelte`. **Tokens**: the `rgba(0,200,255,0.06)` header underline and all remaining accent/success/warning/error rgba literals + `0 0 Npx` glow geometry across these panels moved onto `--*-rgb`/`--glow-shadow-*` (Projects' mills-lavender `rgb(150,190,250)` deliberately left — no token exists yet). **A11y**: `role="group"`/`aria-pressed` on every segmented control (Stream type chips, Traces status chips, Memory tier tabs, Catalog chips + view toggle, Stream pause), `aria-label` on glyph-only buttons (Catalog ☰/▣, Memory ↑/↓/✕, Graph ✕, SandboxLive ■, Projects ✕), labels on filter inputs. Verified live in a browser preview (Servers pill filtering, Traces/Projects/Sandbox/Graph headers, Projects pill deep-links, Dispatch namespace focus round-trip); `vitest` 75/75, `vite build` clean. HUD source-only — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.

### Added
- **mills: persist and emit escalation classification metadata** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/store/types.go`, `pkg/mills/store/migrations/010_pipeline_escalation_metadata.sql`). Escalated pipeline runs now carry the runner error class, policy-facing failure class, retryability, and recognized external dependency identifiers through the store, API JSON, issue body, and handoff failure record.
- **Mills council dependency-update CI classification** (`pkg/mills/council/ci_incident_classifier.go`, `pkg/mills/council/ci_incident_classifier_test.go`): classify Renovate/dependabot/dependency-update branch CI failures as `dependency_update_ci_incident` when the failure is branch-owned, while preserving shared external dependency incidents as `external_dependency_incident`.
- **HUD: Mills → Factory panel — a live Jacquard-loom visualization of the dark factory** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/FactoryPanel.svelte`, `src/lib/utils/factoryHelpers.ts` + `.test.ts`, registry + router entries; route `#mills/factory`, key `7k`). A canvas loom where every element is real Mills state, not decoration: warp-thread density scales with backlog items strung on the beam (queued/ready/running/escalated/paused via `backlogByState`); the orange shuttle weaves while pipeline runs are active (speed scales with run count) and visibly parks with a "floor idle" caption when the floor is quiet — Mills paused renders "jacquard halted"; each run reaching a terminal state in `pipelineHistory` weaves a row into the cloth (green bolt = done/merged, amber spark = escalated) via a seen-set diff (`diffTerminalRuns`), with floating labels naming the backlog id; active runs surface their `CurrentStage` as loom vocabulary (`implement` → "laying weft", `ci_watch` → "under the inspection lamp"). Gauges are KPI-window truth: shuttles in flight (active runs), bolts merged · 24h (`pipeline_merged_runs`, the north star), sparks · 24h (`pipeline_escalated_runs`), threads on the beam, inspection pass rate (`gate_pass_rate`), weavers on the floor (live fleet agents). A floor-log marquee narrates active runs @ stage plus recent terminal runs. Reuses the millsStore 15s poll + `historyActive` gating (no new endpoints, no new pollers); colors come from theme `--*-rgb` tokens so the loom follows the design system; honest states throughout (ErrorBanner on feed failure, "factory floor is dark" EmptyState when the operator isn't configured, reduced-motion renders a static frame). The data→weave mapping is rune-free and pinned by 10 vitest cases (`factoryHelpers.test.ts`). Verified against the live cluster operator (via `HUD_API_TARGET`): real gauges (1 merged / 23 escalated / 68 threads / 87% gate pass), real `psl-plan-*` escalations in the floor log, zero console errors.

### Changed
- **HUD: unify panel identity, failure states, and affordances across the Operations and Work tabs** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/` — 10 panels + 2 new shared primitives). A code+visual audit of the two tabs found three header languages, four empty/error idioms, and several dishonest affordances; this slice normalizes them onto the existing token system. New `shared/PanelHeader.svelte` (mirrors PanelShell's title/count language for panels that own their layout) gives Fleet, Presence, Topology, Lifecycle, Tasks, Workflows, and Plans the same identity row Dispatch already had — Fleet/Presence/Topology previously had **no visible panel title at all**, and Lifecycle's was body-text-sized. New `shared/ErrorBanner.svelte` (extracted from PresencePanel's bespoke banner) now surfaces poll failures on Fleet, Lifecycle, Tasks, and Workflows, which previously degraded silently to empty-looking or stale content. Affordance fixes: Tasks header status badges are now clickable filters with hover/aria-pressed (same behavior as Plans' phase pills — the identical visual pattern was inert in one panel and interactive in its sibling); Tasks priority cycling no longer also opens the row drawer (missing `stopPropagation`); Workflows' "Cancel Workflow" is disabled with an explanatory tooltip on terminal workflows; Dispatch drops hover highlights on non-clickable roster rows and relation cards, wraps its 8 metric cards instead of clipping them in an affordance-less horizontal scroll, and stops repeating the blocker/conflict counts in both the headline and the MetricCards; Plans' five ad-hoc `.empty` text divs become proper `EmptyState`s with actions (Clear filters / + New Plan). Token sweep: the copy-pasted `rgba(0,200,255,…)` cyan literals, `rgba(34,224,118,…)` greens, the off-palette `rgba(0,170,255,0.2)` selected-card glow, and hardcoded `0 0 Npx` glow geometry now use `--info-rgb`/`--success-rgb`/`--glow-accent`/`--glow-shadow-*`. A11y: aria-pressed + role="group" on all segmented toggles, aria-labels on glyph-only buttons (resolve ✓, card/table view toggles, attention ⚠), labels on the Plans create-row inputs. Verified live against the running daemon in a browser preview (all 8 panels, pill-filter interaction, terminal-Cancel gating, filtered-empty actions); `vitest` (65) green, `vite build` clean. HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.

### Added
- **Human re-run of an escalated Mills backlog item via `?requeue=1`** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`). `POST /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{backlog_id}/start?requeue=1` flips an item found in `state=escalated` back to `queued` before starting — escalation parks an item for human review, and an admin-authenticated start IS that review. Previously a plan hand-off whose prior run escalated dead-ended on 409 with no sanctioned recovery (observed on the `services/procmodel` plan: all 7 slice items escalated, so re-handing the plan to Mills could only 409). Requeue is escalated-only: running/merged items still refuse, and `paused` remains a deliberate operator hold. The HUD passes `requeue` from the Plans "Run in Mills" confirm gate and from the backlog drawer, whose Start button now stays available on escalated items as "Re-run (requeue)" (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `PlansPanel.svelte`, `mills/BacklogDetail.svelte`). Pinned by `TestReconciler_StartQueuedItemRequeuesEscalated` and `TestHandlePipelineStart_RequeueQueryReRunsEscalatedItem`.
- Documented council handling for repeated GitLab CI failures classified as external dependency incidents, including allowed repo-local follow-up in `docs/council-external-dependency-incidents.md`.
- **Council planning rules now codify external dependency incidents** (`pkg/mills/council/external_incident_rules.go`, `pkg/mills/council/external_incident_rules_test.go`): GitLab CI and similar third-party failures classify as `external_dependency_incident`, carry the `external-dependency-incident` label, and use conservative in-repo-only follow-up guidance with an explicit no-action omit reason when no repository-owned fix exists.

### Fixed
- **spawn-driver: live SDK spawns resolve the vendor CLI from PATH — the 0.3 SDK's native-binary lookup can never succeed in a spawn pod** (`tools/spawn-driver/src/exec-path.ts` new, `src/claude-driver.ts`, `src/codex-driver.ts`, `internal/hud/spawn_driver_bundle.js`): a live (non-dry-run) kill-test of the just-merged agent-sdk 0.3 bundle failed immediately with `Native CLI binary for <platform> not found` — agent-sdk 0.3.x locates the Claude CLI via optional platform npm packages resolved relative to the SDK module, which cannot exist next to the standalone esbuild bundle the orchestrator injects at `/opt/loom/spawn-driver.js` (no `node_modules`). codex-sdk has the same shape (`findCodexPath()` only searches vendored `@openai/codex` binaries), so bundled codex spawns were **already** broken at 0.118 — pre-existing, same fix. Both drivers now resolve the CLI from PATH (pods guarantee `claude`/`codex` there via the `command -v`-guarded install layer in `spawn.go`) and pass it explicitly (`pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` / `codexPathOverride`), with env overrides `LOOM_SPAWN_CLAUDE_CLI` / `LOOM_SPAWN_CODEX_CLI` and fall-through to the SDK's own lookup when PATH has nothing (dev setups with real `node_modules`). Also restores the compact wire format: 0.3 reports hook execution as `system` subtypes `hook_started`/`hook_response` (previously a dedicated, already-dropped type), so pod-injected hooks would have emitted two ignored events per tool call — the forwarder now drops `system:hook_*`. Verified by a live local kill-test: the SDK resolves the PATH CLI, handshakes (init → assistant → result telemetry; driver exits 1 on `is_error`, which 0.3 now surfaces by throwing — the existing catch handles it), and the stream carries exactly `system:init`/`assistant`/`result`. Affects the opt-in SDK-driver spawn path (`UseSDKDriver`/multi-turn); the default legacy CLI spawn path never used the SDK. First in-pod SDK spawn remains the cluster-side confirmation.
- **Terminal config escalations no longer burn the daily run cap** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go` `CountBudgetedSince`). A run escalated on a first-sight terminal verdict (gate fail marked Terminal — e.g. the scope gate's "no slices" — or an unmergeable MR config, stamped `escalation_class=config` since 2ec38e41) parks its item as escalated and is never auto-retried, so there is no loop for `MaxRunsPerDay` to bound — yet each one consumed a run slot. The 2026-07-07 bootstrapped-plan scope-gate storm (19 escalated runs) froze the cap at 20/20 for a full rolling day and 409'd the operator's own recovery hand-off. `CountBudgetedSince` now discounts `state=escalated AND class=config` alongside the existing `$0 transient_quota` no-ops; code/infra/unclassified escalations still count, and the dollar cap (`MaxUSDPerDay`) is unaffected. Extends `TestPipeline_CountBudgetedSince`.
- **Pipeline start 409s now say why** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go`). A deferred/skipped `StartQueuedItem` returned the opaque `reason: "not started"` — the concrete gate verdict (e.g. `budget: pipeline daily run count 20 reached cap 20`, `blocked by dependency X (not merged)`, `cross_repo disabled: …`) was written to the event store but never surfaced to the caller, so the HUD hand-off toast gave operators nothing to act on (the procmodel hand-off 409 was a full daily run cap, indistinguishable from a state conflict). `tryStart` now returns the deferral/skip reason and `StartQueuedItem` carries it into the HTTP response body. Pinned by `TestReconciler_StartQueuedItemSurfacesDeferralReason`.
- **Council stops re-minting a theme while its first attempt is still unresolved** (`pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go`). The flat dedup's hard 0.7 Jaccard threshold let reworded re-mints through: "Add external CI incident classification for GitLab pipeline failures" (!970) vs "Add GitLab CI external dependency incident classification to Mills" (!978, re-minted again as !980 the same day) scores exactly 0.6. Lowering the threshold globally would false-positive on legit near-twins ("… for Mills runs" vs "… for Weaver runs"), so a new **gray band** `[0.55, threshold)` blocks a proposal only when its lookalike is *recent* (≤ 7 days) **and** *unresolved* (any state except `merged`): don't mint a theme's twin while the first attempt is in flight or escalated. A merged or stale lookalike never gray-blocks — a similar successor to shipped work is legitimate follow-up, and a week-old wedge shouldn't suppress fresh attempts forever. Skips surface in the existing `DuplicatesSkipped` audit trail plus a dedicated log line. New tests pin the live !970/!978 pair and all three non-blocking cases (`TestApply_GrayBandBlocksRecentUnresolvedReMint`).
- **Spawn agents get a working `go` on PATH again** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`; escalations [#272](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/272)–[#278](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/278) side-symptom). Two defects in the k8s spawn-runtime image: (1) `agentRuntimeDockerfile` now symlinks `go`/`gofmt` into `/usr/local/bin` — the golang image exposes them only via its `ENV PATH` (`/usr/local/go/bin`), which vendor agent CLIs drop when they sanitize the environment for spawned shells, so implement agents reported "go is not installed" while `/usr/local/go/bin/go` sat right there (the harvester-vm installer already used the same symlink trick); (2) the `goVersion` const lagged the toolchain at 1.25.11 — since the golang images pin `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, the runtime image being *older* than the repo's `go.mod` directive (1.26.x after !979/!983) made every in-spawn `go` command fail outright; now 1.26.4. New drift guard `TestGoVersionCoversGoMod` fails the suite whenever `goVersion` falls behind the go.mod directive.

### Changed
- **spawn-driver: Claude Agent SDK 0.2.92 → 0.3.203, Codex SDK 0.118.0 → 0.142.5, esbuild 0.24 → 0.28** (`tools/spawn-driver/package.json`, `tools/spawn-driver/src/claude-driver.ts`, `internal/hud/spawn_driver_bundle.js`): lands the three bumps dropped from `renovate/all-minor-patch` on 2026-07-07 — claude-agent-sdk 0.3.143 moved `@anthropic-ai/sdk` (>=0.93.0) and `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` to peerDependencies, so the lockfile regen ERESOLVE'd; `@anthropic-ai/sdk ^0.110.0` is now a direct dependency and `package-lock.json` is regenerated from scratch (npm auto-installs the zod/MCP-SDK peers). Of the three flagged 0.3.142 breaking changes, none touch the driver: the v2 session API was never used (`query()` only), no MCP servers are configured, and the TodoWrite→Task-tools rename doesn't matter because the Go spawn parsers don't consume tool names. One contract tightening applied: `allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true` is now set alongside `permissionMode: "bypassPermissions"`, which the 0.3.x `Options` docs mark as required. The `go:embed` bundle was resynced via `make sync-spawn-driver` (739k → 1.0M). Verified: `npm run typecheck` (which also proves codex-sdk 0.142 added no unhandled `ThreadEvent`/`ThreadItem` variants — the drivers' exhaustive `never` switches would fail to compile), `npm run build`, both `smoke:claude`/`smoke:codex` dry-runs emitting the expected JSONL, and `go test ./internal/hud`.
- **CI and image builders upgraded to Go 1.26.4** (`.gitlab-ci.yml` `GO_VERSION`, `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.custom-server`, `Dockerfile.custom-server.local`, `Dockerfile.local`, `Dockerfile.loom-mills-operator`). The official golang images pin `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, so the 1.25.11 CI image hard-failed any dependency whose own go.mod requires go ≥ 1.26 (`kubernetes v0.36.x` in !630, `kkdai/youtube v2.10.6` in the all-minor-patch group) with `go: go.mod requires go >= 1.26.0 (running go 1.25.11; GOTOOLCHAIN=local)`. The repo's `go.mod`/`go.work` directives stay at 1.25.11 — a newer toolchain builds them unchanged, and dependency bumps can now raise the directive up to 1.26.4 without hitting the wall. The devbox baseimage registry intentionally gains no `go:1.26` entry yet: `baseimage.Lookup` fails open to a from-scratch build, whereas registering an image that isn't pushed to Harbor would break `FROM` pulls (add via `scripts/build-base-images.sh` when a repo actually needs it).

### Fixed
- **Slice-less items on bootstrapped repos no longer wedge the scope gate** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/gates.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`; loom-core [#272](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/272)–[#278](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/278)). All seven items of the first plan-bootstrapped project escalated with `backlog item has no slices; no scope to enforce`: a plan authored before its repo existed cannot declare file paths, so its emitted items land slice-less by construction — and the deterministic gate fail burned 3 implement attempts (~$0.60) per item before escalating. Two-part fix: (1) the scope gate now **passes with a recorded warning** when the item's `TargetProject` is in the `bootstrapped_projects` registry (`StageInput.ProjectBootstrapped`, stamped by the runner via `RepoBase` match; a freshly-seeded repo has nothing for the scope envelope to protect, and sliced items are still enforced); (2) gate outcomes gained a `Terminal` flag — the no-slices fail on a *non*-bootstrapped project is marked terminal and the runner **escalates on first sight** with `[class=config]` instead of rewinding to `RetryFrom`, since re-running implement can never mint slices onto the item. New tests: `TestScope_NoSlicesBootstrappedProjectPasses`, `TestScope_SlicedItemStillEnforcedOnBootstrappedProject`, `TestRunner_TerminalGateFailEscalatesWithoutRetry` (implement dispatched exactly once), `TestRunner_GateInputStampsProjectBootstrapped`.
- **Mills 405 merge recovery falls back to close+reopen when the supersede pipeline can't be created** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`; loom-core [#279](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/279)). The permission-safe supersede path POSTs a fresh branch pipeline as the merge identity — but a bot that isn't a member of the private cross-project include sources (`platform/gitops`, `services/tech-radar`) gets `400 "Project platform/gitops not found or access denied"` and the recovery died, re-escalating the exact class the supersede path was built to clear. On a create failure `recoverDetachedHead` now falls back to close+reopen (the proven !768 recipe): GitLab recomputes `head_pipeline` against the branch's existing green push pipeline with no new pipeline and no delete. If the reopen doesn't stick the retried merge 405s and escalates as before — never a merge of red code. Durable fix is granting the merge identity access to the include projects (tracked separately). New regression test `TestMerge_SupersedeCreate400FallsBackToCloseReopen`.
- **Mills autonomous-merge recovers the 405 detached-head block without pipeline-delete permission** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`; loom-core [#271](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/271)/[#269](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/269)/[#265](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/265)/[#259](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/259) — the largest recurring escalation class). The merge stage's detached-head recovery deleted the spurious failed `merge_request_event` head pipeline, but pipeline deletion is **Owner-only** and the Maintainer merge identity (`svc-loom-mills`) got `403 Forbidden`, so every recovery aborted and the merge escalated. `recoverDetachedHead` now has two paths: an **Owner fast-path** (delete + close/reopen, unchanged) and a **permission-safe supersede path** — on a delete `403` it POSTs a fresh branch pipeline on the MR source branch (loom-core's `workflow.rules` run any `CI_COMMIT_BRANCH` pipeline, source `api` included, but never a `merge_request_event` one), which becomes the newest pipeline for the head SHA so GitLab re-points `head_pipeline` to it; recovery polls it to `success` (wrapping `ErrPipelinePollTimeout` → `ClassInfra`) then merges. Degrades gracefully: a failed/stuck superseding pipeline escalates as before — never a merge of red code. New regression test `TestMerge_RecoversDetachedHeadWithoutDeletePermission` (delete 403 → supersede → merge, no successful DELETE); the existing Owner-path `TestMerge_RecoversDetachedHeadOn405` still passes.
- **HUD "Hand off → Mills" no longer 400s and now routes minted repos to the right target** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/plansHelpers.ts`): running a plan/slice in Mills from the Plans panel built the `POST /api/mills/backlog` body with snake_case top-level keys (`plan_id`/`spec_doc`/`created_by`), but the operator decodes into `store.BacklogItem` — which carries *no* top-level json tags, so the canonical wire shape is PascalCase (the same shape `writeJSON` emits and the frontend `BacklogItem` type reads) — with `json.Decoder.DisallowUnknownFields()`. Go's case-insensitive field match can't bridge the underscore, so those keys were rejected with `400 unknown field`, surfaced in the UI as "Mills run failed: /api/mills/backlog: 400" — most visibly when handing off a freshly bootstrapped repo (`services/procmodel`) from the Spinning Room. The body is now built by a new pure `buildMillsBacklogItem()` helper that emits PascalCase keys and, critically, carries the plan's `project` through as `TargetProject`, so a plan minted into a non-home repo routes cross-repo instead of silently running against the operator's home repo. Nested slices keep their tagged snake_case shape (`name`/`files`). New regression tests: `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/plansHelpers.test.ts`.
- **Mills council external-dependency escalation blocks now include explicit summary and local-action routing** (`pkg/mills/council/escalation_render.go`, `pkg/mills/council/escalation_render_test.go`): recognized GitLab and registry incidents render non-speculative operator guidance that points humans to the local runbook path.
- **Mills escalation issues now label known external dependency incidents and route them to local runbook guidance** (`pkg/mills/council/escalation_render.go`, `pkg/mills/council/escalation_render_test.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate_test.go`): recognized external dependency signatures render a non-speculative incident block with dependency, disposition, evidence, and `docs/mills-escalation-and-dependency-failures.md`; unmatched code/config failures keep the existing escalation body.
- **Mills auto-closes an item's open escalation issue once a later run for that item succeeds** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`; DEBT-073 / [#167](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/167) criterion e, auto-close half): the complement to dedup — dedup stops *new* duplicates, auto-close *reaps* the issue when the item finally goes green, so stale escalations don't linger open. `Runner.markDone` (the success terminal) now calls a new optional `EscalationResolver.ResolveOnSuccess`, which reuses `FindOpenEscalation` to locate the item's open `mills-escalation` issue, appends a resolution note, and closes it via the new `ClosableIssueClient.CloseIssue` (`PUT /issues/:iid` `state_event=close`). Both are **optional interfaces** the concrete `*GitLabClient` satisfies (activates on deploy); a client lacking them is a no-op. **Best-effort**: any resolve error is logged and never undoes the run's `done` state or fails the merge, and the resolution comment is soft (a comment failure still closes). New metric `mills_escalation_issues_auto_closed_total` — with created/deduped it closes the loop (open → don't multiply → reap). New unit tests: `TestEscalator_ResolveOnSuccess{ClosesOpenIssue,NoOpWhenNoOpenIssue,NoOpWithoutDedupClient,ClosesDespiteCommentError}` and client-level `TestCloseIssue_{PutsStateEventClose,RejectsZeroIID}`.
- **HUD: toasts now render above modal dialogs, and a failed "Spin up a repo" shows a persistent inline error** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/Toast.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/action/ActionToast.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/BootstrapRepoDialog.svelte`): two related defects. (1) **Toasts rendered behind inputs/dialogs** — the toast stacks were `z-index: 1000` (primary) and `950` (action), but every modal backdrop is `9999`, so any toast fired while a dialog was open (e.g. an error from the bootstrap dialog) was painted *behind* it and never seen. Both toast stacks are raised to `10001`, above the modal layer and the `body::after` noise overlay (`10000`); since dialogs are `position: fixed` and escape to the viewport, a higher z-index is sufficient (no portal needed). (2) **BootstrapRepoDialog swallowed failures** — a failed mint surfaced *only* as a transient `toastStore.error(...)` that auto-dismissed in 5s **and** rendered behind the dialog, so the user "couldn't see the error and it didn't persist." The dialog now holds a persistent inline `errorMsg` state rendered as a `role="alert"` banner that stays until the operator retries or edits a field (cleared on submit, on input, and on re-open for a new plan); the transient error toast is dropped. Verified in a browser preview: the inline error banner shows the server/token message persistently inside the dialog, and a toast renders over the open dialog (computed `z-index` 10001 > 9999). No component-DOM test added — the HUD's vitest runs in a `node` environment (no jsdom); the existing `bootstrapActions` error-mapping unit tests are unchanged and green. HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **Mills escalations now dedup onto one open issue per backlog item instead of filing a duplicate every run** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/escalate.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`; DEBT-073 / [#167](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/167) criterion e): the escalator's `Handle` unconditionally called `CreateIssue`, so a backlog item that kept failing filed a fresh `mills-escalation` issue on every run — the root of the "67 of 100 open issues are bot-filed escalations" pileup. Escalation issue bodies now embed a stable, invisible dedup marker (`EscalationDedupMarker` → `<!-- mills-escalation:backlog=<id> -->`), and before filing, the escalator (when its issue client implements the new optional `DedupIssueClient`) looks up the existing OPEN escalation issue for the item and appends a **recurrence note** to it instead of creating a duplicate. The concrete `GitLabClient` gains `FindOpenEscalation` (built on the existing `ListIssues`, matched on the marker over the newest ≤100 open `mills-escalation` issues) and `CommentIssue` (`POST /issues/:iid/notes`). The path is **fail-open**: any lookup error falls back to filing a fresh issue, so an escalation is never silently dropped, and it performs no destructive action (auto-close on a later success is a separate follow-up). New metric `mills_escalation_issues_deduped_total`. New unit tests: `TestEscalator_DedupReusesOpenIssue` / `…CreatesWhenNoOpenIssue` (asserts the created body carries the marker) / `…FailsOpenOnLookupError`, and client-level `TestFindOpenEscalation_*` + `TestCommentIssue_*`. Dedup is per backlog item (not per class) for this slice — the dominant pileup is one item failing repeatedly; class-granular dedup and the one-time bulk triage of the stale markerless backlog remain tracked on #167.
- **Mills `ci_watch` pipeline-poll timeout is now classified `infra` and surfaces the stuck pipeline's URL** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`; DEBT-073 / [#167](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/loom-core/-/issues/167) class a): when an MR's branch pipeline never reached a terminal state within `PollDeadline`, the GitLab client's timeout error fell through `Classify` to the default `ClassCode`, so a stuck/slow CI pipeline was reported as a real code bug and buried among genuine build/test breaks (escalations #149/#153). A new `ErrPipelinePollTimeout` sentinel (mirroring `ErrSpawnPollTimeout`) is now wrapped by `PollPipeline` and mapped to `ClassInfra` via `errors.Is`, so the escalation-class metric attributes it to the CI/cluster layer where the fix lives. Because `Infra` shares `Code`'s retry accounting (both count against `MaxAttempts`, neither is a free transient retry), total wall-clock stays bounded at `MaxAttempts × PollDeadline` and a genuinely-slow pipeline can still go green on a re-poll — only the class label and actionability change. The timeout error and poll log tail now embed the branch pipeline's `web_url`, so the escalation the runner builds from the error is directly actionable. New unit tests: `TestClassify_PipelinePollTimeoutIsInfra` (sentinel → infra, not free-retry, not terminal) and `TestPollPipeline_TimeoutWrapsSentinelAndSurfacesURL` (timeout wraps the sentinel and embeds the pipeline URL).
- **HUD Plans compare/merge editor: close seven UX gaps found in a post-merge audit of the new surface** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansComparePanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/planCompareHelpers.ts` + `.test.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`): (1) **stale-pick leak** — the cherry-pick selection was never cleared when the compared id set changed and never pruned when a reload dropped a plan/slice, so a compose could silently include slices from a previously-viewed comparison; picks now reset on id-set change and are pruned against the freshly loaded plans. (2) **Misleading CTA copy** — the button said "Advance merged plan" but the endpoint deliberately authors a `draft` (never advanced); it now reads "Compose merged plan" and the rail footer says "Composing authors one new plan (phase draft) for review". (3) **Uncontrollable merged-plan title** — the title was derived silently; the rail now has a "Merged plan title" input (brief-derived default as placeholder) that flows into `POST /api/plans/compose`. (4) **No cross-column theme linkage** — `alignSlices` now exposes the reconciled `themeKey` on each shared `AlignedSlice`; hovering or keyboard-focusing a shared slice highlights its counterpart(s) in the other columns, and the shared tag names them inline ("● shared · also in flyer"). (5) **Silent duplicate-theme picks** — picking the same theme from more than one frame put near-duplicate slices in the merged plan with no signal; the rail now warns ("⚠ N shared themes picked from more than one frame — the merged plan will carry both variants", `--warning` tokens). (6) **Escape while typing killed the surface** — App.svelte's global Escape → `router.back()` computed `isInput` but never applied it to the Escape branch, so Escape inside any detail-view input (including the new title field) yanked the surface away and lost the selection; Escape now yields to focused inputs app-wide. (7) **Stacked-layout blindness** — under 900px the merge rail sits below all columns so "+ add to merge" gave zero visible feedback; a mobile-only floating "⚙ N picked ↓" chip mirrors the count and scrolls the rail into view. Tests: 3 new `planCompareHelpers` vitest cases (counterpart slices share one `themeKey`, unique slices carry none, a 3-plan theme converges on one key); full frontend `vitest` (56) green, `vite build` clean. Verified end-to-end in a browser preview against mocked competing drafts: hover/focus cross-highlight, dup-theme warning, custom title reaching the compose body (`title`, `source_plan_ids`, per-slice `source` frames), navigate-to-merged, Escape-in-input retention, and the mobile tracker jump — no console errors. HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.

### Changed
- **Go toolchain 1.25.11 → 1.26.4** (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `.gitlab-ci.yml` `GO_VERSION`, all five Dockerfiles' `golang:1.26.4-alpine` builder stage): unblocks the Renovate MRs held behind the go-1.26 wall (k8s.io v0.36, youtube v2.10.6) and the deferred image bumps, and keeps govulncheck green on the current toolchain. Paired with the `platform/gitops` `ci/templates/go.yml` shared-template image bump (1.25.5 → 1.26.4) and warming the Harbor Docker Hub pull-through cache with `golang:1.26.4`/`golang:1.26.4-alpine` so runner prepare doesn't 404 on the new tag.
- **HUD: migrate the remaining 30 data-polling stores off hand-rolled `setInterval` onto the shared `createPoller` primitive** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/*.svelte.ts` — 30 stores): completes the follow-up promised by !885 (which introduced `createPoller` + migrated the `catalog`/`health`/`memory` exemplars). Every remaining store that hand-rolled `setInterval`/`clearInterval` now uses `createPoller`, so all HUD data polling shares one implementation of **tab-visibility pause** (ticks skip while `document.hidden` and a catch-up tick fires on return, so a backgrounded HUD stops generating backend load without going stale), an **overlap guard** (a tick never fires while the previous async tick is still in flight), and leak-free timer lifecycle. Store-specific policy is preserved exactly: SSE-watchdog stores keep their `!eventStore.connected || isStale` guard (`pollTimer === null` → `!poller.running`); multi-owner stores (`fleet`/`otel`/`traces`) keep their owner-scoped `startPolling(owner)`/`stopPolling(owner)` API wrapping the poller; two-timer `sandbox` uses separate `poller` + `execPoller`; the `liveSessions` reaper routes through the primitive (visibility-pause is harmless — `visibleSessions` filters by retention independently). `events`/`staleness`/`toasts` deliberately keep their own timers (SSE reconnect countdown, global reactive clock, self-clearing auto-dismiss — not data polls). No behavior change beyond the shared visibility-pause + overlap guard. `vitest` (60) green, `vite build` clean; every migrated store verified to instantiate and start/stop cleanly at runtime in a browser preview. Reconciles the stale !889 by rebuilding it on current main (the 3 already-migrated exemplars are correctly left untouched, and the 4 stores main changed since the fork — `mills`/`patterns`/`projects`/`sandbox` — were re-migrated against their current versions). HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.

### Added
- **Mills: spin up a new project/repo from a Spinning Room plan (`POST /api/mills/projects/bootstrap`)** (`pkg/mills/bootstrap/{bootstrap,seed}.go` + tests, `pkg/mills/clients/{gitlab_projects,plan_project}.go` + tests, `pkg/mills/store/dao_bootstrap.go` + migration `009_bootstrapped_projects.sql` + tests, `pkg/mills/store/store.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go` + test, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go` + test, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{server,main,handlers_bootstrap}.go` + test, `internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/{BootstrapRepoDialog.svelte,bootstrapActions.ts}` + test): a competitive spin (or the compare/merge editor) can author a draft plan for a product that has **no repo yet** — it lands with `project=""` and the plan-slice emitter can never source it (the business-process-analyzer spin is the motivating example). Bootstrap closes that gap in one admin action: `Service.Bootstrap` validates the plan (must be `draft`/`planned` and unscoped), resolves the target group via a new `GitLabClient.LookupNamespaceID`, creates the project with `GitLabClient.CreateProject` (the operator's **group-scoped** token authorizes `POST /projects`), seeds a single root commit (README from the plan `spec_doc`, a **self-contained green `.gitlab-ci.yml`** with no cross-project includes so merge-bot pipelines don't config_error, `.gitignore`, and an `AGENTS.md` pointing back at the live plan), records the project in a new `bootstrapped_projects` registry, and re-scopes the plan onto the new path (`PlanClient.RescopePlan` → `agent_plan_update`) into the emitter's namespace gate. Ordering is crash-safe: every fallible read runs before the externally-visible create, and the registry row lands before the plan re-scope (a re-scope failure surfaces a `warning` with the manual-fix hint rather than hard-failing a repo that already exists). **Demand wiring**: `PlanSliceEmitterConfig` gains a `DynamicDemandProjects func(ctx) []string` resolved every tick and unioned (deduped) with the static `demand_projects` allowlist, wired to the registry so a just-minted repo dispatches without an operator restart. **Two-key activation** (ships inert): both the mint and the dynamic demand are gated on `cross_repo.enabled` AND a new `cross_repo.allow_bootstrapped` (`Policy.CrossRepoBootstrapEnabled`), so a stray flag can't mint or source while cross-repo is off; the endpoint is admin-gated at the operator and double-gated through the HUD admin token. **HUD**: a "🧵 Spin up repo…" action on the Plans drawer (shown only for project-less draft/planned plans) opens `BootstrapRepoDialog`, which POSTs through `submitBootstrap`/`adminFetch` (Labs access-bar token) and reloads the board onto the plan's new project on success; the bootstrapped-project registry is an open read (`GET /api/mills/projects/bootstrapped`). Tests: bootstrap service happy-path + guards (scoped-plan/advanced-phase/invalid-path/re-mint-conflict/seed-failure-fatal/re-scope-warning), GitLab `LookupNamespaceID` exact-full-path-match + `CreateProject` payload, `BootstrapDAO` insert/get/list + conflict, emitter dynamic-demand union + dedup + nil-provider no-op, the policy two-key gate, operator handler (admin-gate/503-unwired/503-policy-off/201-success/409-remint/open list), and `submitBootstrap` admin-token + error mapping (4 vitest); `go test ./pkg/mills/... ./cmd/loom-mills-operator/` and frontend `vitest` (60) green, `vite build` clean. Ships in the loom-mills-operator image (inert until the two-key policy is enabled in gitops); HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite).
- **HUD Plans: compare competing draft plans + a merge editor that composes one plan from cherry-picked slices** (`internal/hud/bridge/agent_plans.go`, `internal/hud/domain/plans/{handler_plans,plans,plans_test}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/{planCompareHelpers,plansHelpers}.ts` + `planCompareHelpers.test.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{PlansComparePanel.svelte,PlansPanel.svelte}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{shared/SpinningRoomTray,mills/SpinningRoomCard}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/{App.svelte,lib/panelRegistry.ts,lib/stores/router.svelte.ts}`): a competitive spin authors 2–3 sibling **draft** plans from one brief, but the board could only open them one at a time — the operator had no way to see where the frames agreed vs diverged, or to keep the best of each. Adds a **compare/merge editor**: a "⚖ Compare all N" affordance (on the competing-sibling section of the Plans drawer, and on the succeeded-competitive rows of both the Spinning Room tray and the Mills-overview card) opens a side-by-side view via `router.navigateCompare([...ids])` → `#tasks/plans/id1+id2`; App.svelte routes a `+`-bearing plans detail to the new `PlansComparePanel` instead of the board drawer (a single-id detail stays on the normal drawer). Each competing draft renders as a column; a slice reads **shared** (teal `--success`, "● shared theme") when a similarly-named or file-overlapping slice exists in another frame, or **unique** (accent-orange, "◆ only in <frame>") when only one frame proposed it — accent is otherwise reserved for the single merge action. A diff-summary strip counts distinct shared themes + per-frame uniques; columns stack under 900px. The merge rail cherry-picks slices (with a provenance chip naming the source frame) and **Advance merged plan** POSTs the new `POST /api/plans/compose` — which authors ONE `draft` plan carrying the picked slices (`name`/`goal`/`files`) and a `spec_doc` that opens with a provenance note naming the source drafts, leaving the sources untouched for the operator to abandon later; on 201 it toasts and opens the merged plan. Backend mirrors the open (not admin-gated) create path: `PlanCreateParams` gains `Slices []PlanSliceInput` (passed to `agent_plan_create` as `slices:[{name,goal,files}]`, proven accepted by the mills spin path), and `handlePlanCompose` requires `len(source_plan_ids) >= 2` (else 400), stamps `agent_id:"hud:plan-merge-editor"`, and reuses the `planStoreUnavailable` degrade. All slice classification lives in a pure, unit-tested `planCompareHelpers.ts` (normalized-name similarity with a distinctive-token rule + file-overlap fallback, `diffSummary`, frame resolution) so the component stays presentation-only; `PlanSlice` gains the optional `goal` the API already returns. Tests: `TestPlanCompose_Success` (asserts the merged slices + source-id-bearing spec_doc reach `agent_plan_create`, 201 with the plan_id) + `TestPlanCompose_RejectsFewerThanTwoSources` (400); 12 new helper `vitest` cases (shared/unique classification, diff counts, frame resolution, no-overlap/single-plan edges); full `internal/hud/domain/plans` + `internal/hud/bridge` Go suites and frontend `vitest` (53) green, `vite build` clean. Verified end-to-end in a browser preview against mocked drafts + a competitive spin: shared/unique borders + diff strip render correctly, cherry-pick populates the merge rail with the accent CTA, compose fires and navigates, columns stack under 900px — no console errors. HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **Mills S6: multi-repo demand — the plan-slice emitter sources an allowlist of foreign repos and stamps `TargetProject`** (`pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `pkg/mills/policy_test.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter_test.go`): completes the demand half of the cross-repo execution keystone. S1–S5 proved a *manually-injected* foreign-repo `BacklogItem` runs implement→tests→mr→merge against a non-home repo (flexdeck!244, autonomous cross-repo merge 2026-07-05); S6 lets demand itself target foreign repos. The `PlanSliceEmitter` previously scanned a single `cfg.Project`+namespace and left every item target-less (home repo). It now *additionally* scans each repo in a new `cross_repo.demand_projects` allowlist and stamps the emitted item's `TargetProject` with that repo's path, so the existing S2 `effectiveProject` / S3 `ForProject` / S4 reconciler routing carries the run cross-repo — no new execution surface. **Two-key activation**: the allowlist is consulted ONLY when `cross_repo.enabled` is also true (enforced in one place, `Policy.CrossRepoDemandProjects`), so a stray allowlist can't source foreign demand while cross-repo execution is off, and the reconciler's fail-closed gate still guards execution of any stamped item. Default empty allowlist ⇒ home-only (pre-S6 behavior) — the code ships **inert**; activation is a gitops `demand_projects` edit + operator roll. A foreign entry that resolves to the home repo (`store.SameRepo`) is skipped; a per-repo demand error is logged and never stalls the home lane. Tests: foreign demand stamps `TargetProject` while home stays target-less, empty allowlist stays home-only, and the two-key gate (disabled cross-repo ⇒ nil allowlist); `go test ./pkg/mills/ ./pkg/mills/intake/` green. Ships in the loom-mills-operator image.
- **Mills council: plan-level dedup so demand-sourcing stops re-minting already-served themes** (`pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_reader.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_sliced.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator_plan_dedup_test.go`): the scheduled council (`schedule_cron 0 */6`, `triggers.on_incident`) had **no dedup on the plan lane**. The existing Jaccard dedup only compares proposals against `backlog_items`, but demand-sourcing proposals author sliced **Plans** (via `AuthorSlicedPlan`) into the `mills/demand-sourcing` namespace and never create a backlog item — so nothing deduped them. Every 6h tick re-minted a near-duplicate Plan for the same over-served theme (36 council plans clustered on a handful of themes; CI-failure-classification alone already had 7 merged implementations on main), which `plan_slice_emitter` then fanned into ~9 conflicting `psl-plan-council-*` MRs (curbed as a stopgap by disabling the emitter in gitops!327). The mutator now snapshots the target namespace's Plans once per `Apply` (new optional `PlanLister` interface, satisfied structurally by `*clients.PlanClient.ListExistingPlans` → `agent_plan_list`, **all phases**) and skips authoring a proposal whose title crosses the same 0.7 Jaccard cutoff against an existing Plan — cross-run (a `merged` plan the take-up reconciler advanced still blocks a re-ask, so a theme already served on main is covered) and within-batch (a just-authored Plan blocks its own near-dup later in the same run). **Fail-open**: a Plan Store read blip logs a warning and authors without dedup rather than dropping legit work; the deterministic plan id already collapses *identical* re-asks, so only near-dups can slip through on a failed-snapshot run. Skips are recorded on `MutationResult.PlanDuplicatesSkipped`, surfaced in `Summary()` (`plan_deduped=N`), and counted by a new `mills_council_plan_dedup_skipped_total`. No behavior change when the plan lane is off or the author isn't a `PlanLister`. Tests: namespace-dup skip, within-batch dedup, distinct-themes-both-authored, lister-error fail-open, no-lister no-op, plus the `findDuplicatePlan` predicate; `go test ./pkg/mills/council/ ./pkg/mills/clients/` green. Ships in the loom-mills-operator image; the emitter re-enable (gitops) is gated on this image being live.
- **HUD Mills Overview: the Spinning Room, inline on the Mills page** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/SpinningRoomCard.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`): the Spinning Room lived only under Work → Plans (a header button + popover tray), but the iOS companion surfaces it on the **Mills** screen — so the web Mills Overview was missing the same at-a-glance view. Adds a `SpinningRoomCard` section to the Mills Overview (right below the KPI grid, matching the mobile layout order): a "⟳ Spin a plan" action plus the live + recent spins read from the durable `spinRunsStore` — model frame(s), a status badge with derived **slow/stuck** escalation, live elapsed, the brief, links to the resulting draft plan(s), and a **Retry** for failed/timed-out/stuck spins. Competitive spins show as **one entry that fans out to its sibling drafts**; opening a draft routes to Work → Plans (`router.navigate('tasks','plans',id)`) since the drafts live in the Plan Store. Reuses the shared store + pure helpers (`spinRuns.svelte.ts` / `spinRunsHelpers.ts`) — only the dashboard-flavoured card presentation is local, so phase/elapsed/grouping logic stays single-sourced with the Plans tray. Degrades with the rest of the overview: the operator-not-configured state hides the card, and an undeployed async-spin endpoint shows a calm "still deploying" note. Verified end-to-end in a browser preview against a mock operator (card renders under the KPI grid with running/stuck/succeeded-competitive/failed rows; "Spin a plan" opens the dialog with all frames; a competitive draft link routes to the Plans board — no console errors); full frontend `vitest` (41) green; `vite build` clean.
