HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **HUD Spinning Room: a durable status tray for async spins — per-spin status, stuck detection, and competing-draft grouping** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/spinRunsHelpers.ts` + `.test.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/spinRuns.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/SpinningRoomTray.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/SpinPlanDialog.svelte`): the async-spin backend has been fully durable since !925/!926 (`POST /api/mills/spin/async` → 202, status in the SQLite `spin_runs` table read back via `GET /api/mills/spin/runs[/{id}]`, proxied through the HUD), but the Plans panel ignored it — it tracked spins in **ephemeral component state** and rendered only a bare "`N spinning…`" chip, so the operator couldn't see per-spin status or tell a wedged spin from a slow one, the tracking died on refresh, showed nothing from other sessions/tabs, and competitive spins landed as two disconnected draft cards. Now a clickable header indicator ("`N spinning · ⚠ M stuck`") opens a **Spinning Room tray** backed by a new `spinRunsStore` that polls the durable endpoint (fast while a spin is live, slow when idle, stops when there's nothing to watch) — so it survives a reload and reflects every recent spin regardless of which tab started it. Each row shows the model frame(s), a status badge with derived **slow** (>2m) / **stuck** (>8m, near the operator's 10m budget) escalation the wire status doesn't carry, live elapsed time, the brief, links to the resulting draft plan(s), and a **Retry** action for failed/timed-out/stuck spins (re-seeds the Spin dialog with the original brief + frames). A **competitive spin now reads as one entry that fans out to its sibling drafts** instead of two disconnected cards: the tray groups them, board cards carry a "`⚔ competing N`" badge, and each draft's detail drawer links its competing siblings ("compare + advance the winner"). Completion toasts still fire only for spins started in the current session. All grouping/phase/elapsed semantics live in a pure, unit-tested `spinRunsHelpers.ts` (mirrors the mobile Kit's `MillsSpinBoard`); the components stay presentation-only. Tests: 19 new helper cases + full frontend `vitest` (41) green; `vite build` clean; verified end-to-end in a browser preview against a mock operator (running/stuck/succeeded-competitive/failed rows, stuck warning, grouped draft links, retry, drawer siblings — no console errors). HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **iOS companion: a separate HUD admin token so Mills mutations (spin / escalate) work from the phone** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/Networking/{TokenStore.swift,Endpoint.swift,APIClient.swift}`, `.../ViewModels/ConnectionViewModel.swift`, `.../Navigation/DeepLink.swift`, `.../Views/Connection/{LoginView.swift,ConnectionDiagnosticsView.swift}`, tests): Mills mutations from the app always 401'd because the app only ever sent the **pairing bearer** (which must match `HUD_MOBILE_OPERATOR_TOKEN`), while the HUD gates spin/escalate on `HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` — a *different* secret (`internal/hud/api_agent.go` `extractAdminToken` reads `X-Admin-Token` then falls back to the bearer). There was no way to supply the admin token, so it was effectively always empty and the Spinning Room board degraded to read-only. Now the app stores a **separate admin token** in the Keychain (`TokenStore.{save,load,delete}AdminToken`, account `hud-admin-token`, distinct from the bearer) and `APIClient` attaches it as `X-Admin-Token` **only** on the two admin-gated endpoints (`Endpoint.requiresAdminToken` → `millsSpinAsync`, `millsPipelineEscalate`), so the admin secret never rides on the `/api/mobile/v1` surface or on the un-gated plan advance/priority calls. It can be entered at pairing (a new optional field on the Connect screen), set/replaced/cleared any time from Connection settings without re-pairing (`ConnectionViewModel.saveAdminToken`, surfaced in `ConnectionDiagnosticsView`), or seeded over USB via `loom://configure?...&admin=<token>` (new `ConfigureSpec.adminToken`), which `make mobile-app-run-device`/`-sim` now inject automatically — `scripts/mobile/build_configure_url.sh` resolves `HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN` (env → `~/.config/loom/hud.env` → the `loom-hub/loom-secrets` k8s secret) and appends `&admin=`, so a freshly installed dev build lands able to spin/escalate rather than read-only (`run_on_device.sh` masks it in the echoed URL). This also unblocks the widget's escalate-confirm path (it builds its client via `buildAPIClient()`). Reads and the calm-empty degradation contract are unchanged; a missing admin token still yields the existing "needs the HUD admin token" copy rather than a bare 401. Tests: `requiresAdminToken` flag coverage, deterministic `X-Admin-Token` attach/omit assertions (`APIClient.authorizedRequest`), and `loom://configure` admin round-trip — full Kit `swift test` (398) green; app target `xcodebuild` (iOS Simulator) compiles clean. iOS-only; ships in the next companion build.
- **HUD Sandbox (Labs): quality-gate + environment-detect surfaces, and a project-keyed workbench that matches what devbox actually does** (`internal/hud/domain/sandbox/{handlers,sandbox,deps,handlers_test}.go`, `internal/hud/{app_routes_operations,domain_adapters}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/sandbox.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/sandboxHelpers.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/sandbox/{SandboxLive,SandboxOffline,EnvironmentCard,QualityGateCard}.svelte`): the Labs → Sandbox panel drove only a subset of the devbox/sandbox backend and mislabeled what it drove. Two of the most-used devbox tools had **zero UI** — `devbox_quality_gate` (the workspace's canonical fmt → lint → test gate) and `devbox_detect` (the environment fingerprint that determines a project's image) — and the "Start Sandbox" button actually called `devbox_build` while discarding its result (`image`/`hash`/`languages`/`cached`/`build_duration_ms`) and broadcasting a phantom `build_id` that was never in the response. This wires the panel to the real feature set: (1) new `POST /api/sandbox/quality-gate` → `devbox_quality_gate` (admin-gated) returns the structured per-check result (name, passed, exit code, duration, output/stderr tail) rendered as a hero **Quality gate** card with an overall verdict and inline failure output; (2) new open `GET /api/sandbox/detect/{project}` → `devbox_detect` (read-only, cached 30s, hoists the `fingerprint` wrapper to a flat shape) powers an **Environment** card + inline language chips showing the exact toolchain (languages/versions/tools, system deps, image hash) baked into the sandbox — so the operator sees what a build/exec/gate will run against before provisioning; (3) `handleSandboxCapabilities` now reports the full action set (`detect`, `build`, `exec_async`, `exec_poll`, `quality_gate`, `status`, `stop`) with `notes.quality_gate`/`notes.detect`, replacing a stale 4-action list that rendered as a debug dump; (4) the build path surfaces the real `image`/`hash`/`cached` fields in its activity broadcast + action banner instead of the phantom `build_id`. The frontend is reframed from a sparse status dashboard into a **workbench keyed to one active project**: a single project selector (datalist of known sandboxes) replaces the two disconnected project inputs and drives Build image, Run command (async exec), and Quality gate, each with accurate labels and helper copy; clicking a project in the list makes it active. Preserves the existing dark/mono design system (accent chips, rail cards, status tones) and covers loading/empty/error/disabled states, admin-gating, and mobile (single-column stack, no horizontal overflow — verified 375px). Tests: `internal/hud/domain/sandbox` gains `TestHandleSandboxDetect_{Success,NotAdminGated,MissingProject}`, `TestHandleSandboxQualityGate_{Success,RequiresAdminToken,MissingProject}`, and `TestHandleSandboxCapabilities_ReportsFullActionSet`; full `internal/hud/...` Go suite + frontend `vitest` (22) + `vite build` green; live-rendered the workbench + gate-results states with seeded data (no console errors). HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **CI guardrails: document deterministic Renovate and image-build gate triage** (`docs/ci-renovate-and-image-gate-guardrails.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`): added a repo-local runbook for classifying Renovate and image-build failures, required evidence, retry limits, and closeout notes so operators can handle gate failures without changing external systems.
- **Mills incident triage runbook** (`docs/mills-incident-triage.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`): documents operator-facing classification and repo-local recovery steps for CI failures, external dependency incidents, local Mills configuration failures, and branch hygiene issues.

### Fixed
- **Mills Spinning Room: async spins no longer wedge in `running` forever with no reason — a watchdog enforces the budget even when a dependency ignores context** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/{handlers_spin_async.go,server.go,handlers_spin_async_test.go}`): the HUD showed spins "stuck" running **past the 10-minute budget** (20m+) with no indication why, and the only thing that ever cleared them was an operator restart (surfaced as "orphaned: operator restarted before the spin finished"). Root cause: `runSpinAsync` called `o.spinner.Spin/SpinAll(ctx, …)` and *blocked on it*, and the spin's per-phase timeouts (45s author, 10m budget) are only effective if every downstream call honors `ctx` — but `libs/mcp-go` `WebSocketTransport.Recv` blocks on a raw gorilla `ReadMessage()` and **never consults `ctx`**, so when the MCP hub goes silent (connection open, no reply) the draft-plan write (`AuthorDraftPlan` → hub) hangs indefinitely, defeating the 45s author timeout *and* the 10m budget. The goroutine never returned, `finishSpin` never ran, and the `spin_runs` row stayed `running` until a restart orphaned it — the live evidence: both competing frames logged `spin editor produced output` at the ~90s mark, then the spin logged nothing for 18+ minutes. Fix: `runSpinAsync` now runs the spinner in a monitored goroutine and **selects on completion vs. the budget context**, so it ALWAYS records a terminal status at the budget — `SpinTimeout` with an explanatory error ("spin exceeded 10m budget: a dependency … stopped responding and ignored cancellation") — even when the worker is still blocked. The row can never wedge in `running`, the HUD always shows why, the orphaned worker settles on hub recovery/restart, and the semaphore slot is released regardless. The async budget is now an injectable operator field (`spinAsyncBudget`, default 10m) so the watchdog is unit-tested with a short budget. Tests: `TestHandleSpinAsync_WatchdogTimesOutWhenWorkerIgnoresContext` (a ctx-ignoring author that blocks forever ⇒ the row still reaches `SpinTimeout` with a budget-breach reason); full `cmd/loom-mills-operator` suite green. **Follow-up (root cause)**: `libs/mcp-go` `Recv` should honor `ctx` (set a read deadline from the context) so the 45s author timeout fails *fast* instead of at the 10m outer bound, and the same hub-stall hang stops wedging the plan-slice emitter / devbox calls too. Lands on the next loom-mills-operator redeploy.
- **Mills operator: pick up the mcp-go `Recv` ctx-honoring fix so a silent hub fails fast instead of wedging** (`go.mod`, `go.sum`): bumps `gitlab.flexinfer.ai/libs/mcp-go` to `…-c5a240709f8c` (mcp-go!9), where `WebSocketTransport.Recv` now derives a read deadline from the context instead of blocking forever on a raw gorilla `ReadMessage()` when a peer accepts the connection but stops replying. This is the **root-cause half** of the async-spin wedge fix (paired with the operator watchdog): with it, a stalled MCP hub makes the spin's 45s author budget — *and* the plan-slice emitter / devbox hub calls — **fail fast with a clear error** instead of hanging until the 10m outer bound (or, before the watchdog, forever until an operator restart). loom-core builds locally against the same lib via go.work; this bump is what the CI-built operator image consumes. No loom-core code change — a dependency bump only.
- **Mills spawn/devbox git-clone: right-size + make configurable the init-container memory so larger-history repos stop OOM-killing the clone** (`internal/devbox/backend/{k8s.go,k8s_objects.go,k8s_test.go}`, `cmd/mcp-devbox/{main.go,manager.go}`): the `git-clone` init container that seeds both the spawn pod and the devbox tests-stage sandbox in git-clone mode carried a **hardcoded 256Mi memory limit** — the only spawn resource that wasn't env-configurable (unlike `SPAWN_DEFAULT_MEMORY_MB` / `SPAWN_BUILD_MEMORY_*`). The clone is deliberately a **full clone** (no `--depth 1`, so Mills keeps a merge base for its `git diff <base>...HEAD` rubric capture), so `git index-pack` delta resolution spikes memory on larger-history repos and 256Mi was too tight a margin: during the 2026-07-05 cross-repo kill-test the `services/flexdeck` clone was **OOMKilled (exit 137) on all 8 `pr_self_review` attempts** before a retry happened to land under the ceiling (the same run's *implement* + *tests* stages cloned it fine — the OOM is intermittent, keyed to peak delta-resolution memory). The init-container memory request/limit are now backend-config fields (`GitCloneMemoryRequest`/`GitCloneMemoryLimit`) defaulting to a safer **256Mi request / 1Gi limit** (4× the old limit; the init container is short-lived, so the higher ceiling is cheap and only consumed during the delta-resolution burst), applied on **every** path since both the spawn orchestrator and the devbox server build the backend through `NewK8sBackend`. The devbox entrypoint also exposes `DEVBOX_K8S_GIT_CLONE_MEMORY_REQUEST`/`_LIMIT` so the tests-stage sandbox is tunable per-deployment without a rebuild. CPU (100m/500m) is unchanged — it was never the fault. Tests: `TestGitCloneInitContainer` asserts the container carries the configured request/limit; `TestNewK8sBackend_DefaultsAndOverrides` covers the 256Mi/1Gi defaults plus a 512Mi/2Gi override. Ships in the loom-core (spawn) and custom-server (devbox) images; live on the next roll of mobile-hud and the loom-hub `devbox` deployment.
- **HUD: the mobile companion token can now reach the Mills / Plans / Weaver read+board surfaces — "Couldn't reach Mills" on the phone** (`internal/hud/api_mobile.go`, `internal/hud/api_mobile_scope_test.go`): the iOS companion's Mills screen showed "Couldn't reach Mills / Pull to retry" (and the Plans/Weaver screens were equally dead) over the gateway. Root cause was NOT a proxy outage or the admin token — it was an authorization scope: the HUD's `withCORS` middleware calls `mobileTokenOutsideMobileAPI`, which **hard-restricted the mobile operator token to `/api/mobile/v1/*`** and 403'd everything else with `"mobile_operator token is restricted to /api/mobile/v1 endpoints"`. But the companion renders Mills/Plans/Weaver directly off the HUD proxy (`/api/mills/*`, `/api/plans[...]`, `/api/weaver/*`, `/api/aimodels/roles` — see the app's `Endpoint.swift`), so every one of those requests 403'd **before** auth even mattered (this sits upstream of the separate `X-Admin-Token` admin gate, so the companion admin-token work is necessary but was insufficient on its own). Live-confirmed against `https://hud.flexinfer.ai`: `/api/mobile/v1/ping` → 200, but `/api/mills/pipeline/runs`, `/api/mills/kpis`, `/api/mills/spinning-room/frames`, `/api/plans` all → **403 "restricted…"**. Fix: an explicit allowlist (`mobileCompanionExtraPrefixes` + `mobileCompanionExactPaths`) lets the mobile token also **reach** those read/board surfaces. It grants no admin power — mutations under `/api/mills` (spin, escalate, backlog, kill-switch, council) stay behind their own `requireAdminToken`/`X-Admin-Token` gate; plan advance/priority stay open exactly as the HUD web frontend already allows (bare fetch). Agent/labs/spawn and every other admin surface remain denied for the mobile token (regression-guarded). Tests: `api_mobile_scope_test.go` pins the allowed set (mobile v1 + mills/plans/weaver/aimodels incl. subpaths), the denied set (`/api/agent/*`, `/api/labs/*`, `/api/spawn`, bare `/api/mills`, `/api/plansx`, `/api/weaver`), and that non-mobile/absent tokens are unaffected; full `internal/hud` suite green. Goes live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **Mills: a SIGKILLed/OOMKilled sandbox exec (exit 137) no longer escalates as a fake code failure** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class_test.go`): a real escalation (`PIPE-psl-plan-council-add-a-mills-incident-triage-runbook-…-1783261156`, 2026-07-05) showed the `tests` stage's `fmt` check hang ~3m17s then die with **exit code 137** (SIGKILL — an OOM/deadline/pod-kill), three times, and the run escalated at cost $0 with no verdict. Root cause: the failure classifier had **no pattern for exit 137**, so `"command terminated with exit code 137"` fell through to the default `ClassCode` ("real bug — counts against `MaxAttempts`"), burning all attempts and escalating as if the code had failed a test. A killed exec produced *no verdict* — it's a transient sandbox death (same family as the k8s pod-GC / "backend unavailable" cases the classifier already treats as transient), where a fresh exec/pod usually lands healthy. `Classify` now maps `exit code 137` / `exit status 137` / `signal: killed` / `oomkilled` → **`ClassTransient`**, so it retries *free* (off the code/`MaxAttempts` budget) instead of escalating; a sandbox that is killed *every* time still escalates via the transient hard cap (labeled transient, not a code bug). Deliberately narrow: an in-process `runtime: out of memory` and a test that merely prints "137" stay `ClassCode` (regression-guarded). Tests: `TestClassify_KillTestFixtures` gains the exit-137 k8s-remotecommand + os/exec + OOMKilled cases plus the two false-positive guards; full `pkg/mills/pipeline` suite green. Lands on the next loom-mills-operator redeploy.
- **HUD Spinning Room: "spin failed: invalid admin token" — the spin dialog now attaches the HUD admin token** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/{spinActions.ts,spinActions.test.ts,SpinPlanDialog.svelte}`): the sibling miss to the Run-in-Mills 401 fix below. That fix routed the *store's* `postJSON` through `adminFetch`, but the Spinning Room dialog fires its **own** `fetch` and was left behind — `SpinPlanDialog.submit()` POSTed `/api/mills/spin/async` with a bare `globalThis.fetch` (only `Content-Type`), so the request reached the HUD's `requireAdminToken` double-gate with **no `X-Admin-Token`** and 401'd "invalid admin token" (the operator was healthy and `GET /api/mills/spinning-room/frames`, an open read, loaded fine — so frames appeared but the spin failed). Entering the token in the Labs access bar didn't help because the dialog never read it. The spin POST is extracted into a unit-testable `submitAsyncSpin()` helper that routes through `adminFetch({ requireToken: true, action: 'Spinning a plan' })`, so the Labs-bar token rides along, an **empty bar fails fast client-side** ("Spinning a plan requires an admin token") with no tokenless request, and a real 401/403 surfaces "admin token missing or invalid — set it in the Labs access bar" instead of the raw gate body. The frames GET and the spin-status poll stay bare (open reads, correctly). Tests: `spinActions.test.ts` (token attached + spin_id returned; empty bar rejects with the hint and fires no request; 401→actionable copy; 404→still-deploying); full frontend `vitest` (17) + `vite build` green. HUD source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **HUD Mills mutations no longer 401 — the "Run in Mills" slice handoff attaches the HUD admin token** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/shared/millsActions.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.adminToken.test.ts`): handing a plan's slices off to the mill from the HUD (`PlansPanel` "Run in Mills" → `millsStore.createBacklog` → `POST /api/mills/backlog`) returned **401 on the backlog endpoint**, and so did every other Mills mutation from the main store (start pipeline, escalate, council run/dryrun, kill-switch, policy proposal apply/reject). Root cause: Mills mutations are **double-gated** — the HUD's own `handleProxyAdminPost` → `requireAdminToken` gate (checks `X-Admin-Token`/`Authorization: Bearer` against `HUD_ADMIN_TOKEN`) runs FIRST, and only then does the proxy inject the *operator's* admin bearer (`internal/hud/domain/mills/proxy.go`). The store's `postJSON` issued a bare `globalThis.fetch` with **no admin token**, so the HUD gate rejected it before the proxy ever ran — while a stale comment in `millsActions.ts` ("the browser never handles a token") encoded exactly that wrong mental model. `postJSON` now routes through the existing `adminFetch` helper (attaches `X-Admin-Token` from the Labs access bar) with `requireToken: true`, so an empty bar fails fast with a clear "This Mills action requires an admin token" message instead of firing a tokenless request that 401s, and a real 401/403 from the gate surfaces "admin token missing or invalid — set it in the Labs access bar" rather than a bare status code. This matches how `mills_crossrepo.svelte.ts` `abort()` and the spawn-telemetry tabs already authenticate; the operator-side gate and in-cluster token wiring were correct (operator `loom-mills-admin` == HUD `LOOM_MILLS_OPERATOR_TOKEN`, live-verified 2026-07-04). Tests: `mills.adminToken.test.ts` (createBacklog sends `X-Admin-Token`; an empty bar rejects with the hint and fires no tokenless request). HUD source-only (`dist/` is gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite) — goes live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **custom-server graceful drain — rollouts no longer reset in-flight proxied requests** (`cmd/custom-server/main.go`, `k8s/base/kustomization.yaml`): the shared `custom-server` WS/SSE bridge backs ~46 loom-hub MCP-server Deployments, so any server-code change rolls the whole fleet; each terminating pod previously exited on a bare 5s `srv.Shutdown()` with **no readiness flip and no clean close** of its hijacked WebSocket/SSE connections, so proxied clients saw resets and killed sessions on every roll (even with `maxSurge:1`/`maxUnavailable:0` from gitops!311). On SIGTERM the server now fails `/ready` (503 → the Service deregisters the pod; `/health`/liveness stays green) and rejects new sessions, waits `MCP_SHUTDOWN_DRAIN` (default 3s) for in-flight requests to settle and endpoint removal to propagate, then closes every tracked WS/SSE session cleanly and concurrently — WebSocket close 1012 *service restart* / SSE stream end — so clients reconnect to the already-Ready surged-in replacement, before a bounded `srv.Shutdown(MCP_SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT, default 10s)`. A `k8s/base` patch adds `preStop: sleep 5` (endpoint-dereg propagation before SIGTERM) + `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 40` to the `component=mcp-server` fleet (gateway/mcpo/mobile-hud excluded — different images). Both waits are env-tunable. Tests: readiness flip, `drainRegistry` Add/Remove/Len/DrainAll, env-duration parse/fallback; `kubectl kustomize k8s/base` renders preStop+grace on the 46 fleet Deployments and none of the excluded three. Goes live as pods cycle onto the new custom-server image.
- **HUD Mills proxy: dial deadline so a rolling operator can't hang the Pipelines page** (`internal/hud/domain/mills/proxy.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/PipelineRunDetail.svelte`): the run-detail drawer showed an apparently infinite spinner whenever the operator was mid-deploy. Root cause: the operator ships with `strategy: Recreate` (single SQLite writer), so every rollout briefly blackholes the old pod IP — SYN packets drop with no RST — and the HUD's reverse-proxy transport had **no `DialContext` deadline**, so each `/api/mills/*` request waited out the kernel's ~2-minute SYN-retry window while the browser spun (the frontend's own 12s timeout fired, but every retry hung the same way; live-verified 2026-07-04 — the endpoint answers in ~25ms when the operator is up). The transport now dials with a 3s deadline (+ 5s TLS handshake timeout), so a rolling operator produces a fast, retryable 502 instead of a hang; the drawer names the situation ("operator may be restarting — retries automatically") instead of showing a bare status code, and its background poll self-heals once the operator is back. Also removes the runs-list N+1: `GET /api/mills/pipeline/runs` issued **9 sequential per-state SELECTs** per HUD poll (racing state transitions mid-call); a new single-query `PipelineDAO.ListActive` preserves the progression-grouped order and shares `CountActive`'s predicate. Tests: `TestProxy_TransportHasDialDeadline`, `TestPipeline_ListActive` (ordering, terminal exclusion, CountActive agreement).

### Changed
- **mcp-devbox: cross-repo tests stage — resolve un-staged services repos in git-clone mode instead of hard-failing** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/manager.go`): completes the Mills cross-repo keystone (loom-core!944). The live cross-repo kill-test (2026-07-05, target `services/flexdeck`) proved routing end-to-end through *implement* — the spawn cloned flexdeck and pushed a branch there via the group token — but **escalated at the `tests` stage** with `devbox_quality_gate: project 'services/flexdeck' not found under /app`. Root cause: the devbox server's `resolveProject` requires the repo to exist under the local workspace (`/app`, which only stages loom-core), even though in **git-clone mode the sandbox's init container clones the repo itself** and the on-disk copy is used *only* to fingerprint the toolchain image — and `Fingerprint`+`generateSandboxDockerfile` already degrade a missing/empty dir to the generic git-clone sandbox image (`devbox-base/go:1.25` + node + python, so `gofmt`/`go test` still run). So the hard-fail was the *only* blocker. `resolveProject` now falls back to a lexical path (`lexicalProjectDir`: bare name → `services/<name>` matching the git-clone base-URL group; bucket-qualified verbatim; abs/`..` rejected) in git-clone mode when the repo isn't staged locally — mirroring the spawn orchestrator's !941 fix — so the tests stage can clone+check any services-group repo without pre-staging it. nfs/tar-pipe keep the hard-fail (there the local copy *is* the source). Tests: `TestResolveProject_GitCloneFallbackForUnstagedRepo` (staged repo still resolves on disk; unstaged bare/qualified names resolve lexically; unsafe inputs rejected) and `TestResolveProject_NonGitCloneStillHardFails`. Ships in the **custom-server** image (the loom-hub `devbox` deployment); goes live on that roll — after which a re-run of the cross-repo kill-test should clear `tests` → `mr`/`merge` in the target repo.
- **Mills spawn capacity + multi-repo readiness — raise the concurrency ceiling and stop hard-failing on un-staged repos** (`k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/project_resolver.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`, `docs/MILLS_RUNBOOK.md`): two changes that let Mills workers scale out across more repos. (1) `SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT` raised **6 → 10** on the mobile-hud Deployment (the spawn orchestrator is embedded in mobile-hud; the gitops override patch doesn't touch this env var, so the base manifest is authoritative). Each spawn is 0.5 CPU + 4 GiB, so 10 = 5 CPU + 40 GiB against ~1.25 TiB cluster allocatable (16 nodes) — well under 4% of capacity; image builds stay throttled to 1 (`SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT_BUILDS`). Capacity was never the constraint — demand is — but the higher ceiling removes serialization when work fans out over multiple repos. (2) In **git-clone mode**, a target repo that is *not* staged on the `loom-hub-workspace` PVC no longer hard-fails project resolution (`runSpawn` previously `failSpawn`'d at the first `resolveProjectPath` miss). Because the spawn pod's init container clones the repo fresh as its source of truth and the on-disk copy is used *only* to fingerprint the Dockerfile (which already degrades to a generic runtime image on fingerprint failure), the orchestrator now falls back to a lexical `services/<name>` path (`lexicalProjectRel`) and proceeds — letting Mills target additional **services-group** repos without pre-staging them on the RWO workspace volume. nfs/tar-pipe modes keep the strict hard-fail (there the local copy *is* the source of truth); the gate is a new `SpawnOrchestrator.gitCloneMode()` fed from `SPAWN_SYNC_MODE`. Staging a repo on the PVC still yields a better-fingerprinted image, so build-heavy repos should prefer it (documented in the runbook's new "Spawn pool capacity & multi-repo" section, alongside the services-token/git-group scope for cross-repo). Tests: `TestLexicalProjectRel` (bare name → services bucket, bucket-qualified verbatim, unsafe inputs rejected) and `TestGitCloneMode` (only `git-clone` opts into the fallback). Concurrency change goes live on the next mobile-hud roll; the code path ships in the loom-core image.
- **CI: gate the loom-core/custom-server image builds on changed inputs** (`.gitlab-ci.yml`): `build:image:loom-core` and `build:image:custom-server` published a fresh `:YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS` tag on *every* default-branch commit, so unrelated merges (docs, Mills, CI config, other services) rolled the whole shared-image loom-hub fleet via Flux Image Automation — 7+ agent-context rollouts in 24h, each dropping HUD SSE connections and killing in-flight proxied requests. The existing feature-branch `changes:` list is now mirrored onto the `$CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH` rule for both jobs, so a deployable tag is published only when the image's real inputs change (`cmd/**`, `internal/**`, `pkg/**`, `go.mod`/`go.sum`, the Dockerfile, the build script, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `.dockerignore`). Release-tag builds (`$CI_COMMIT_TAG`) stay unconditional, and the rule fails safe — pipelines with no comparison base (schedule/API/first push) still build. A per-server image split was ruled out: 60-day history shows ~91% of build-triggering commits touch shared `internal/`/`pkg/` and would roll everything regardless.
- **Mills canary heartbeat fixture** (`testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md`): update the deterministic canary Run ID to `MILLS-CANARY-AUTOPILOT-20260704-090043`.

### Added
- **mills/audit: deterministic external-dependency incident classifier** (`pkg/mills/audit/{incident_classifier.go,incident_classifier_test.go}`, `internal/contracts/incident.go`): map recurring raw workspace failure messages to the structured `incident-classifier` contract with stable dependency/reason/retryability labels for audit follow-up and reporting.
- **HUD: LAN trusted-network admin — no token on the internal (Cloudflare-bypassing) path** (`internal/hud/{trusted_network,trusted_network_test}.go`, `internal/hud/{app.go,embed.go,api_agent.go,domain/sandbox/{deps,handlers}.go}`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `cmd/loom/hud.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{stores/labsAuth.svelte.ts,components/shared/LabsAccessBar.svelte}`, `k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`): the companion to the Cloudflare-Access-SSO admin below, closing the **on-LAN** gap. Split-horizon DNS points `hud.flexinfer.ai` at the internal nginx ingress for LAN clients, so the request never transits Cloudflare and carries **no** Access JWT — the SSO path can't fire and the operator was stuck pasting a token (the "respin requires an admin token" report; confirmed via nginx logs: the browser reaches the HUD from LAN IP `192.168.50.153`, never Cloudflare). Now the admin gate also authorizes a request whose **real client IP** is in an operator-configured CIDR allowlist (`HUD_ADMIN_TRUSTED_CIDRS`, e.g. `192.168.50.0/24`), so LAN browsers are admin with no token. `adminIdentity` resolves in order **Access SSO → trusted network (`via:"network"`) → token**; `clientIP` reads `X-Real-IP` then the leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` then `RemoteAddr`, and `/api/labs/auth-check` now also returns `client_ip` so the operator can see what IP the HUD attributes to them when tuning the CIDR. The frontend generalizes: any tokenless `auth-check` 200 (Access **or** network) drops the token requirement and the access bar shows a "✓ Trusted network" / "✓ Cloudflare Access" chip. **Trust model** (documented in `trusted_network.go`): trusts the ingress to set the client-IP headers and assumes a single-tenant cluster — the allowlist must be the LAN subnet only, never the pod/service CIDRs (10.x), so an in-cluster pod hitting the ClusterIP (its own 10.x IP, no forwarding headers) is *not* trusted and still needs its token (`TestTrustedNetworkAdmin` pins this). Off unless `HUD_ADMIN_TRUSTED_CIDRS` is set. Tests: `internal/hud` (`TestParseCIDRs`, `TestClientIP_Precedence`, `TestTrustedNetworkAdmin`, `TestAdminIdentity_TrustedNetwork`, `TestRequireAdminToken_TrustedNetworkPasses`), frontend `labsAuth.access.test.ts` (+ `via:network` grant); HUD Go suite + daemon config test green; frontend `vitest` (22) + `vite build` green. Live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **HUD: Cloudflare Access SSO → admin — no manual token when signed in via Gmail** (`internal/hud/cfaccess/{verifier,verifier_test}.go`, `internal/hud/{app.go,embed.go,api_agent.go,domain_adapters.go,domain/sandbox/{deps,handlers}.go}`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `cmd/loom/hud.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/labsAuth.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/LabsAccessBar.svelte`, `k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`): when an operator reaches `hud.flexinfer.ai` through Cloudflare Access (Google/Gmail login), the HUD now treats them as admin automatically — the Labs token bar becomes optional. Cloudflare validates identity at the edge and injects a signed `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion`; the new `cfaccess.Verifier` (built on the already-vendored `coreos/go-oidc/v3`) **verifies that JWT** against the team's JWKS (`https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com/cdn-cgi/access/certs`) plus issuer / expiry / (optional) `aud`, then checks the `email` claim against an allowlist. **Security**: it verifies the *signature* — it never trusts the bare `Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email` header — because in-cluster callers can reach the HUD's ClusterIP directly and forge headers; a forged header carries no valid Cloudflare signature (regression-tested by `TestVerifier_RejectsBadSignature`). The single `requireAdminToken` gate now authorizes via **either** a verified Access admin email **or** the existing token (the token stays the LAN/CLI/non-browser fallback); `GET /api/labs/auth-check` reports `{valid, via, email}` so the frontend detects SSO-admin with a tokenless probe on load, satisfies `adminFetch({requireToken})` without a local token (Cloudflare injects the identity server-side), and the access bar shows a "✓ Cloudflare Access (email)" chip. Config is env-driven on the mobile-hud deployment and **off unless set**: `HUD_CF_ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN` + `HUD_ADMIN_EMAILS` (inline) enable it; `HUD_CF_ACCESS_AUD` (optional `loom-secrets` key) scopes tokens to the HUD app — unset logs a warning and skips only the aud check (signature+issuer+expiry+email still gate). Tests: `cfaccess` (9 — allowlisted-admin accept, non-admin/wrong-aud/expired/bad-signature/no-header reject, aud-optional, disabled-when-unconfigured, issuer/email parsing), frontend `labsAuth.access.test.ts` (4 — tokenless grant, 401 no-grant, `requireToken` satisfied by SSO, still-throws without either); HUD Go suite + daemon config test green; frontend `vitest` (17) + `vite build` green. **Riskiest assumption** (to kill-test after deploy): the tunnel actually forwards a verifiable Access JWT for the Gmail user to the origin — verify by loading the HUD via `hud.flexinfer.ai` and confirming `GET /api/labs/auth-check` returns `via:"access"`. HUD frontend source-only (`dist/` gitignored; CI rebuilds via Vite); live on the next mobile-hud redeploy.
- **Mills cross-repo execution keystone — per-item repo targeting (default-OFF, fail-closed)** (`pkg/mills/store/{types,dao_backlog,migrations/008_backlog_target_project.sql}.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `docs/MILLS_RUNBOOK.md`, `.loom/170-plan-mills-cross-repo-execution-keystone-2026-07-05.md`): the code half of letting the default Mills pipeline run a backlog item against **any services-group repo**, not just the operator's home repo (loom-core). Previously every stage hardwired the home repo — `BacklogItem` had no target field, spawn stages defaulted to `loom-core`, and the GitLab client was single-project-pinned across ~20 `projectPath()` sites. Now: **(S1)** `BacklogItem.TargetProject` (migration 008, DAO round-trip; the REST intake auto-accepts it since `handleBacklogCreate` decodes straight into the struct) — empty targets the home repo, so every existing item is unchanged. **(S2)** the implement/plan_slice/pr_self_review spawn stages and the tests (devbox) stage resolve the effective project from `item.TargetProject` via `effectiveProject`; a cross-repo item also skips the operator-local `RepoRoot` git-capture (that clone is the home repo — the spawn pod captures the freshly cloned target instead). **(S3)** a new `GitLabClient.ForProject(project)` returns a shallow client copy scoped to another project (shared token/http), wired through a `GitLabWorker.ForProject` hook so mr/ci_watch/merge/cleanup target the item's repo. **(S4)** a **fail-closed reconciler gate**: an item whose `TargetProject` names a repo other than `Reconciler.HomeProject` is *skipped* (never run against the home repo) unless `cross_repo.enabled` is true — so a misrouted or premature cross-repo item can't land changes in the wrong place; `HomeProject` empty leaves the gate inert (pre-cross-repo behavior). Repo identity compares by bare name ignoring bucket/case (`store.SameRepo`/`RepoBase`), so `loom-core` == `services/loom-core`. Cross-repo reuses the existing services-group merge token + services-pinned git base + the !941 spawn fallback — **no new credential for services repos**; cross-*group* (libs/, platform/) stays out (needs a token grant). Tests: `TestBacklog_TargetProjectRoundTrip`, `TestRepoBaseAndSameRepo`, `TestEffectiveProject`, `TestSpawnWorker_TargetProjectRoutesCrossRepo`, `TestDevboxWorker_TargetProjectRoutesCrossRepo`, `TestGitLabWorker_ForProjectRoutesCrossRepo` (+ nil-ForProject fallback), `TestGitLabClient_ForProject`, and reconciler-gate cases (disabled-skips / home-not-gated / enabled-runs / inert-without-home). The live end-to-end kill-test (inject a `services/loom-flightdeck` item → verify it merges *there*) is the deploy-gated next step (needs the operator image + a token authorized on target repos); plan + riskiest-assumption in `.loom/170-…`. Ships inert (default-OFF) until `cross_repo.enabled` is flipped.
- **iOS companion: interactive widget button — escalate a running pipeline from the home screen** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/{Networking/Endpoint.swift,Mills/MillsControlAPI.swift,Navigation/DeepLink.swift}`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionWidget/MillsFactoryWidget.swift`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanion/{ContentView.swift,Views/Mills/MillsScreen.swift,Views/Dashboard/DashboardView.swift}`): the Mills Factory widget's in-flight pipeline rows (systemMedium/Large) are now tappable — each row `Link`s to a new **`loom://pipeline/{id}/escalate`** deep link (a small ⚠ glyph telegraphs the action). **Grounding note**: the operator's `pause`/`resume` are still `501` stubs ("4.x pipeline runner"), so the one *real* per-run intervention is **escalate** — it pulls a run out of the autonomous loop and holds it for human review (it won't auto-merge). **Auth-safe by design**: the widget extension cannot read the operator token (`TokenStore` Keychain items have no shared access group), so rather than widening credential storage + forcing every paired user to re-pair, the button **opens the app** — which already holds credentials — where `MillsScreen` shows a confirm sheet and performs the admin-gated `POST /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{id}/escalate` (double-gated behind the HUD admin token; a non-admin pairing 401s into actionable copy via `millsMutationFailureMessage`, not a silent no-op). The confirm sheet also guards against a lock-screen mis-tap on an irreversible action; success shows a dismissible banner and reloads. New Kit surface: `Endpoint.millsPipelineEscalate(id:reason:)` (source-tagged default reason "escalated from iOS companion"), `MillsControlAPI.escalatePipeline(id:reason:)` → `MillsPipelineEscalateAck`, and `DeepLink.pipelineEscalate(id:)` (unknown pipeline actions like `/pause` deliberately fail to parse — no silent mis-route). Tests: `MillsPipelineEscalateTests` (10 cases — endpoint shape + body default/explicit reason, control-API success + 401/404 surfaced, ack decode, deep-link round-trip + escaping + unknown-action rejection); Kit suite 393 green; `xcodebuild … -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator'` (app + widget extension) succeeds. Builds on the Mills Factory widget (!936) and control surface (!935).
- **iOS companion: Mills Factory home/lock-screen widget** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/{Models/MillsWidgetData.swift,Services/MillsWidgetPublisher.swift,Navigation/DeepLink.swift}`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionWidget/{MillsFactoryWidget.swift,LoomWidgetBundle.swift}`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanion/{ContentView.swift,Views/Dashboard/{DashboardView,NextActionCard}.swift}`): oversee the autonomous factory from the home screen without opening the app. New **`MillsFactoryWidget`** across six families — `systemSmall` (north-star merges/24h + running/queued/escalated chips + floor line), `systemMedium` (adds the top-3 in-flight pipeline rows + a live-spin line + auto-merge/cost/drafts strip), `systemLarge` (KPI tiles + up-to-5 depth-indented pipeline rows + Spinning-Room rows + plan-review-pressure footer), and lock-screen `accessoryCircular`/`accessoryRectangular`/`accessoryInline`. Data rides a dedicated App-Group snapshot (`MillsWidgetData`, `millsWidgetData` key — the SpawnBudget-widget pattern, separate from the main `WidgetData` blob) written by **`MillsWidgetPublisher`**, the sole writer: `publish(using:)` fans out the four Mills reads (`latestKPI`/`pipelineRuns`/`spinRuns`/`plans`) off the DashboardViewModel's reliable foreground cadence — each degrades independently, and all-four-nil flips `operatorReachable=false` so the widget shows "operator offline" instead of misleading zeros. Derivation (`MillsWidgetData.from`) is pure + unit-tested: pipeline rows ranked live-roots-first and capped, spins via the shared `MillsSpinBoard.visibleRuns` semantics, plan pressure counted by phase (drafts awaiting review + active plans). Timeline refresh tightens to 5min while a spin is live (a draft can land within minutes), else 15min. Taps deep-link to the Mills tab via a new **`loom://mills`** route (threaded through `DeepLink` parse/build/`destinationGroup`/`shareTitle`, `ContentView.handleDeepLink`, and the two `DashboardNavAction` switches + `NextActionCard` CTA label). Tests: `MillsWidgetDataTests` (11 cases — KPI/run-fallback derivation, live-root ranking + cap, plan-pressure counts, spin board mapping, `floorLine` per state, Codable round-trip, tolerant older-schema decode, `loom://mills` round-trip); Kit suite 383 green; `xcodebuild … -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator'` (app + widget extension) succeeds.
- **iOS companion: Spinning Room + Plans board on the Mills tab** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/{Networking/Endpoint.swift,Mills/{MillsSpinModels,MillsPlanModels,MillsControlAPI}.swift}`, `apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanion/Views/Mills/{MillsScreen,SpinPlanSheet,PlansScreen,PlanDetailView}.swift`): the mobile Mills tab gains its first *control* surfaces, porting the HUD's Spinning Room (Live Beam slice 3 + async spins, plan `.loom/166`) and the Plans lifecycle board (incl. respin lineage, commit 6e678e88). New **Spinning Room** section on MillsScreen shows in-flight/recent async spins (via the open reads `GET /api/mills/spin/runs[/{id}]`) with a 5s poll that only runs while a spin is live, plus a **Spin a plan** sheet mirroring the HUD dialog: brief (roving), 1–3 policy frames from `GET /api/mills/spinning-room/frames` (2+ = competitive, cap mirrored from `spin.maxCompetitiveFrames`), priority, optional project/namespace scope — fired **async-always** (`POST /api/mills/spin/async` → 202 + spin_id) so a minutes-long frontier frame never holds a mobile connection open. New **Plans** screen (toolbar link) renders the Plan Store board over `GET /api/plans[/{id}]`: phase-grouped cards with priority, slice-progress segments (from `slice_summary`), respun-from lineage, shipped/abandoned hidden behind a toggle; the detail view adds **Advance** (server-legality, 422 surfaces as copy), **Respin** for the plan or a single slice (briefs built by `MillsRespinBrief`, byte-matching the HUD's builders, `respun_from` threaded), and a confirm-gated **Abandon** (the supersede companion). Mutations require the paired bearer to BE the HUD admin token (`RequireAdminToken` accepts the same `Authorization: Bearer` header); a non-admin pairing keeps a fully useful read-only board and 401s fold into actionable copy (`millsMutationFailureMessage`). Reads keep the established Mills degradation contract (bare 502/503/404 → calm empty state; `available:false` renders "plan store not deployed"). Semantics (spin status buckets + board filtering `MillsSpinBoard`, plan phase tones/terminality/ordering `MillsPlanPhases`, `MillsSliceProgress` rollup) live in LoomCompanionKit so they're unit-tested and shared. Tests: `MillsControlAPITests` (26 cases — operator-shaped wire decoding incl. RFC3339 dates + minimal pending rows, single-vs-competitive `{frame}`/`{frames[]}` body shapes + `respun_from` threading, 401 surfaced-not-swallowed, degradation paths, board ordering, respin brief content, endpoint wiring); full Kit suite green (374 tests). Requires `make mobile-ios-project-sync` consumers to rebuild the Xcode project (already regenerated here).
- **Mills council CI incident classifier** (`pkg/mills/council/{ci_incident_classifier.go,ci_incident_classifier_test.go}`): adds a deterministic, test-covered classifier that turns branch ownership and GitLab pipeline failure evidence into structured incident classes for repository regressions, CI configuration failures, runner incidents, external dependency incidents, flakes, and branch/plan hygiene.
- **Respin plans/slices from the HUD — redo an old, sparse plan on a model frame, linked + supersedable** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema_plan,svc_plans,svc_plan_render}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_plan.go`, `pkg/mills/spin/spin.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_spin.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{handlers_spin,handlers_spin_async}.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_plans.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{utils/plansHelpers.ts,components/PlansPanel.svelte,components/shared/SpinPlanDialog.svelte}`): the Plans drawer gains **⟳ Respin…** next to *Hand off* (and per-slice), for redoing older/pre-spinner plans that are too thin. Respin is non-destructive: it seeds the (async) Spin dialog's brief + scope from the plan/slice, spins a **fresh draft** on a chosen frame, and the new draft is **linked** back to the source via a new immutable `respun_from` plan field — threaded end-to-end (spin request → `AuthorDraftPlan` → `agent_plan_create`; surfaced through the HUD bridge + rendered into the plan mirror). The drawer shows the link both ways: a **respun from** pointer on the new draft, and a **respun into** list + a **Supersede this plan** button on the source (the "review, then 1-click" deprecation — it advances the old plan to `abandoned`, reversibly). To keep the board from filling up when redoing a batch, **abandoned plans are now hidden by default** behind a `Show archived (N)` toggle (an explicit `phase=abandoned` filter still reveals them). `respun_from` mirrors `priority`'s plumbing (Qdrant payload round-trip, no migration — schemaless), immutable like `created_by`. Tests: `TestSpin_HappyPath` asserts `respun_from` threads to the author; `TestPlan_PayloadRoundTrip` asserts the field survives the payload round-trip; frontend `vite build` + `vitest` green. Goes live once mcp-agent-context, the operator, and the HUD redeploy.
- **Async spins (Spinning Room slice 3) — lifecycle metric + operator runbook** (`pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_spin_async.go`, `docs/mills-async-spins.md`): closes out the async-spins plan (`.loom/166`) with observability + docs. New counter **`mills_async_spins_total{outcome}`** tracks the async lifecycle — `accepted` on the `202`, then `succeeded`/`failed`/`timeout` when the background spin resolves (`accepted` minus the terminal sum is the current in-flight count; a rising `timeout` share flags a frame outgrowing the 10m request budget). Outcome is a **closed set**, so cardinality stays bounded — per-**frame** async reliability is already covered by the existing `mills_spin_total{frame,outcome}` (async reuses the same `Spinner` + editor-reached `OnSpinDone` hook, which never mints labels from arbitrary request strings). The runbook (`docs/mills-async-spins.md`) documents the endpoints + status lifecycle, how to trigger a spin (HUD or curl with the `loom-mills-admin` token) and read its status, recovering a stuck/orphaned run (the durable artifact is the Plan Store draft, not the `spin_runs` row — the startup orphan-sweep fails the row but the plan survives), the `LOOM_MILLS_SPIN_MAX_CONCURRENT` knob, and example PromQL for success-rate + per-frame reliability. Tests: `mills_async_spins_total` (and the previously-untested `mills_spin_total`) added to `metrics_test.go`'s registration/label-accept/gatherable lists.
- **Async spins (Spinning Room slice 1) — 202 + background goroutine so slow frontier frames never time out** (`pkg/mills/store/{migrations/007_spin_runs.sql,dao_spin.go,types.go,store.go}`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{handlers_spin_async.go,server.go,main.go}`): the durable fix for the slow `jacquard`/`claude-opus-4-8` frame (plan `.loom/166-plan-mills-async-spins-2026-07-04.md`). `!924` made synchronous spins *return or fail-fast, never hang*, but a synchronous POST held for the whole minutes-long synthesis is still cut by the client-facing proxy timeout (Cloudflare 524 at ~100s) — unreachable from the operator. `POST /api/mills/spin/async` accepts the **same** request shape as `/api/mills/spin` (single `frame` or competitive `frames[]`), persists a `pending` status row, launches the existing `Spinner` in a **detached goroutine rooted at the operator lifetime** (not the request — a client disconnect can't cancel an in-flight spin; operator shutdown can), and returns **`202 {spin_id, status, status_url}`** in well under a second. The draft plan lands in the Plan Store via the unchanged `AuthorDraftPlan` path; the client polls `GET /api/mills/spin/runs/{id}` (and `GET /api/mills/spin/runs`) — both open reads mirroring the council-run endpoints — until `succeeded`/`failed`/`timeout` instead of holding a connection open. Modeled on the scheduled-council fire-and-forget pattern; reuses `Spin`/`SpinAll` untouched. New durable `spin_runs` table (migration 007, mirrors `council_runs`) so status survives an operator restart, with a **startup orphan-sweep** (`SpinDAO.MarkOrphaned`) that fails any `pending`/`running` row left by the previous process — the singleton operator's goroutines died with the old pod, but the draft plan, if authored before the crash, stays durable in the Plan Store. Concurrency is bounded by a semaphore (default 2 — frontier frames are slow + costly — overridable via `LOOM_MILLS_SPIN_MAX_CONCURRENT`), and a per-request queue bound (`spin_runs` `pending+running` ≥ 32 → `429`) stops a burst piling up. The synchronous `/api/mills/spin` is unchanged (kept for fast frames + CLI/tests). Tests: `pkg/mills/store` (`TestSpinDAO_RoundTripCountAndOrphanSweep` — round-trip, `CountActive`, newest-first `List`, idempotent orphan-sweep, `ErrNotFound`) and `cmd/loom-mills-operator` (`TestHandleSpinAsync_{AcceptedThenSucceeds,CompetitiveSucceeds,503WhenNoSpinner,503WhenDisabled,400OnMissingBriefOrFrame}`, `TestHandleSpinRunGet_404`, `TestHandleSpinRunsList_EmptyIsJSONArray`), all green under `-race`. HUD dialog wiring (fire-and-forget + status polling) and observability are the next slices; goes live once the operator image redeploys.
- **Async spins (Spinning Room slice 2) — HUD dialog fires-and-forgets + polls the draft in** (`internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/SpinPlanDialog.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`): the HUD half of the async-spins feature (backend was slice 1, `POST /api/mills/spin/async`). The mills proxy domain now forwards `POST /api/mills/spin/async` (double-gated through the HUD admin token, same as `/spin`) and the open status reads `GET /api/mills/spin/runs[/{id}]`. The **Spin a plan** dialog goes **async-always**: it POSTs `/api/mills/spin/async` (same request shape — one `frame` or competitive `frames[]`), gets a `202 {spin_id}` back, toasts "Spin queued — tracking…", clears the form and **closes immediately** instead of blocking on a minutes-long frontier synthesis (a `jacquard`/`claude-opus-4-8` spin would otherwise exceed the ~100s client-facing proxy timeout). The Plans panel takes the `spin_id` via the dialog's new `onQueued` callback and polls each in-flight spin with the shared `createPoller` (4s, visibility-paused, overlap-guarded): on `succeeded` it toasts the draft plan id(s) and reloads the board, on `failed`/`timeout` it toasts the typed reason, and it gives up after 12 min (the draft, if it landed, still surfaces on the board's own 30s refresh). A compact pulsing "N spinning…" indicator sits next to the Spin button while spins are in flight; the poller stops when none remain and on unmount. A `404` on the async POST (operator not yet updated) surfaces a clear "still deploying — try again shortly" toast. Tests: `internal/hud/domain/mills` (`TestProxy_SpinAsyncRequiresAdmin`, `TestProxy_ForwardsSpinRunsReadsWithoutAdmin`) + frontend `vite build` and `vitest` green. Observability (a dedicated async-spin metric + runbook) is slice 3; HUD source-only (dist/ is gitignored, CI rebuilds via Vite).
- **Anthropic backend: prompt caching on the stable editor prefix** (`pkg/mills/clients/anthropic.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council_anthropic.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council.go`): the Anthropic council/Spinning-Room editor now caches the invariant portion of its prompt per the [Anthropic prompt-caching guidance](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching). `buildCouncilEditorPrompt` is split into `buildCouncilEditorPromptParts` → `(stable, volatile)`: the **stable** half (instruction preamble + editor guardrails + repo-layout grounding + approved-pattern catalog) is identical across every council run and spin against the same repo, and now rides a `cache_control: {type: "ephemeral"}` **system block** (5-minute TTL); the **volatile** half (this run's brief + reviewer notes) stays in the user message. Render order is `tools → system → messages` and the thinking config is held constant, so nothing silently invalidates the system-cache prefix between calls — repeated spins on the same frame within the TTL read the (often large) prefix at ~0.1× instead of full input price. `stable + volatile` is byte-identical to the previous single-string prompt, so the flexinfer and OpenAI editors are **completely unaffected**. `AnthropicClient.CreateMessage` takes a request struct with a `System` field, puts one breakpoint on the system block, and now surfaces `cache_creation_input_tokens` / `cache_read_input_tokens` in the result → the editor records `cache_read`/`cache_write` in its sidecar notes, so cache effectiveness is a queryable signal (a persistent `cache_read=0` across repeated spins means a silent invalidator or a prefix below the model's minimum cacheable length — 1024 tokens on Opus 4.8, 512 on Fable 5). Streaming already carries the cache usage fields, so no change to the streaming path. Tests: `TestCouncilEditorPromptParts_StableVolatileSplit` (concatenation identity + brief only in the volatile half + repo-layout in the stable half) and the extended `TestAnthropicEditor_ParsesDocsAndProposals` (system prefix carries the instructions but not the brief; cache tokens flow into the notes).
- **OpenAI Responses editor: prompt-cache routing key + real usage/cache reporting** (`pkg/openairesponses/{contracts,client}.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council_openai.go`): brings [OpenAI prompt caching](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/prompt-caching) to the OpenAI council/Spinning-Room editor, the peer to the Anthropic-caching change. OpenAI caching is **automatic** for prompts ≥1024 tokens and keys on the prompt *prefix* — and the editor prompt is already stable-first (`buildCouncilEditorPrompt` leads with instructions + repo-layout grounding + pattern catalog, then the brief), so the prefix was already cacheable. Two gaps are now closed: (1) the Responses request carries a `prompt_cache_key` (`"loom-mills-council-editor"`, a new `TurnRequest.PromptCacheKey` field) so every editor call sharing that long prefix routes to the same cache and lifts hit rate; and (2) **the client never parsed the response `usage` block at all** — `TurnResponse.PromptTokens`/`CompletionTokens` were dead zeros, so the editor's `(in=0 out=0)` notes were meaningless. `normalizeAPIResponse` now parses `usage`, coalescing the Responses shape (`input_tokens` / `output_tokens` / `input_tokens_details.cached_tokens`) with the chat-completions shape (`prompt_tokens` / `completion_tokens` / `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`) so an OpenAI-compatible proxy returning either family still reports usage. The editor now records `(in=… out=… cached=… tokens)` in its sidecar notes, making cache effectiveness a queryable signal (a persistent `cached=0` on repeated editor runs means the prefix is below the 1024-token threshold or a silent invalidator moved it). `prompt_cache_key` is `omitempty` (only sent when set). Tests: `TestAPIClientCreate_{SendsCacheKeyAndParsesUsage,OmitsCacheKeyWhenUnset}`, `TestResponsesUsage_ChatCompletionsFallbackShape`, and the extended `TestOpenAIEditor_ParsesDocsAndProposals` (asserts the cache key is sent + cached tokens surface in notes).
- **Native Anthropic Messages API council/Spinning-Room editor backend** (`pkg/mills/clients/anthropic.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council_anthropic.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `go.mod`): adds `backend: anthropic` (alias `claude`) as a third editor backend alongside `flexinfer` and `openai`, so a council ensemble editor or a Spinning-Room frame can run on a frontier Claude model (`claude-opus-4-8`, `claude-fable-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`, …) over the official Anthropic Go SDK (`github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-go`). `AnthropicCouncilEditor` is a native peer to `OpenAIResponsesCouncilEditor`: it reuses the exact same shared prompt builder (`buildCouncilEditorPrompt` — pattern catalog + repo-layout grounding), section splitter, and `## Backlog Proposals` diagnostic parser, so it emits the identical three markdown artifacts + structured proposals and the writer/mutator/eval flow is unchanged — only the synthesis model differs. The SDK client (`AnthropicClient.CreateMessage`) drives a single stateless streaming turn with **adaptive thinking** (the supported on-mode for every current Claude model; `budget_tokens`/`temperature` are deliberately omitted since they 400 on those models) and streams the response so a large, minutes-long plan decomposition never hits the SDK's non-streaming HTTP timeout. A safety-classifier **refusal** (`stop_reason == "refusal"`) is treated as an unusable run (`EditorOutput.Empty = true`, reason recorded in the sidecar notes), so the existing `FallbackCouncilEditor` transparently degrades to the local flexinfer editor — the same resilience the OpenAI path already has for timeouts/5xx. `buildEditorForAgent` wires the `anthropic`/`claude` branch identically to the OpenAI branch (primary + flexinfer fallback), reading the key from `LOOM_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` then the SDK-standard `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`AnthropicAPIKeyFromEnv`), with an optional `LOOM_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` override; an absent key logs a warning and degrades rather than failing, so shipping this is inert until a key + `backend: anthropic` frame land in policy. Tests: `pkg/mills/clients` (`TestAnthropicEditor_{ParsesDocsAndProposals,EmptyOutputMarksEmpty,RefusalMarksEmpty,NilBriefErrors}`, `TestNewAnthropicClient_RequiresKey`, plus a live `TestAnthropicEditor_Live` skipped unless `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set). Activation is a GitOps step: create the `loom-mills-anthropic` secret, add `backend: anthropic` frames to `spinning_room.frames` (and/or the council editor) — the operator image must carry this change.
- **mcperror: external dependency incident classifier** (`pkg/mcperror/external_incidents.go`, `pkg/mcperror/external_incidents_test.go`): adds deterministic metadata for known container-registry blob storage and GitLab authentication failure signatures.
- **Competitive spinning (Live Beam / F2 close-out) — spin the same roving on up to 3 frames, keep the better yarn** (`pkg/mills/spin/spin.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_spin.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{main,handlers_spin}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/SpinPlanDialog.svelte`): the last deferred item of the Spinning Room framing in `.loom/brainstorm-mills-steering-preparation-line-2026-07-03.md`. `POST /api/mills/spin` now accepts `frames: []string` alongside the legacy `frame`; the new `Spinner.SpinAll` merges + dedupes the two, resolves **every** frame against policy before any model spend (an off-policy name fails the whole request), then runs each frame over the same brief **concurrently**, authoring one `phase=draft` plan per frame. Each draft's spec_doc audit gains a `Competing frames:` line naming its siblings, so a reviewer knows to compare before advancing one — the operator keeps the better yarn (advance to `planned`) and leaves the rest in `draft`. Spend is bounded by a hard cap of 3 frames per request (mirrored in the HUD dialog) and the existing 10-minute handler deadline, which now covers the whole fan-out since frames run in parallel. Partial success is success: per-frame failures ride along in the new `CompetitiveResult{results, failures}` response, and only an all-frames failure maps to an error status. Response-shape contract keys off field **presence**: requests without `frames` keep the original single-`Result` body, so pre-competitive clients (and the dialog's single-pick path, which deliberately sends the legacy shape) are unaffected. The HUD dialog swaps the frame `<select>` for checkbox chips ("pick 2+ to spin competitively"), labels the submit button `Spin N competing drafts`, and toasts per-frame plan ids + failed frames. Observability: new counter **`mills_spin_total{frame,outcome}`** via an injectable `Spinner.OnSpinDone` hook that only reports attempts that reached the editor — pre-spend rejections never mint label values from request strings, so cardinality stays bounded by policy. Tests: 8 new `spin` cases (`TestSpinAll_*`: per-frame drafts + competitor audit + metrics hook, partial failure, all-fail error, unknown-frame fails before spend, Frame/Frames merge-dedupe, frame-count cap, single-frame-no-competitors) + 3 handler cases (`TestHandleSpin_CompetitiveFramesReturnsOneDraftPerFrame`, `_SingleEntryFramesListIsComposite`, `_CompetitiveUnknownFrameIs400`) + the `AuthorDraftPlan` spec_doc assertion. Frames go live via the `spinning_room:` policy block in platform/gitops (hot-reloads, no redeploy needed for frame-list edits); competitive requests need the operator image carrying this change.
- **Spinning Room (Live Beam slice 3) — operator spins model-selectable draft plans from the HUD** (`pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/spin/spin.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_spin.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{server,main,handlers_spin}.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{PlansPanel,shared/SpinPlanDialog}.svelte`): third slice of the Live Beam (`.loom/brainstorm-mills-steering-preparation-line-2026-07-03.md`), the demand source the beam feeds on. Spinning turns prepared fiber into yarn on a chosen frame; here the operator picks a **frame** — claude-opus, gpt-5.4, or a local flexinfer model — from the HUD's Plans panel, hands it a brief (roving), and it spins a **draft** plan + slices directly into the agent-context Plan Store for review before dispatch. The F2-vs-GitOps-governance line is drawn in the schema: the **set** of allowed frames is GitOps policy (new `spinning_room.{enabled,frames[],default_priority}`, each frame a `CouncilAgent`-shaped `{name,model,backend}`; default-off + validated for name/model/uniqueness), while the frame **chosen per spin** is run-scoped and recorded on the resulting draft plan for audit (spec_doc header + `mills:spinning-room` creator + the model/backend that actually ran). Reuses the existing council editor clients (`FlexInferCouncilEditor`/`OpenAIResponsesCouncilEditor`) via a new `buildEditorForAgent` factory rather than new inference plumbing: the `spin.Spinner` gates on policy, resolves the frame, runs one `Edit` over the brief, decomposes the output's backlog proposals into deduped draft slices (flat proposals synthesize one slice; capped at 24 with a log line), and authors a `phase=draft` plan carrying the warp-beam `priority`. Draft plans are **deliberately not emitted** — the plan-slice emitter (slice 1) only picks up `planned`/`in_progress`, so the operator reviews and advances the draft before the beam runs it. Surface: `POST /api/mills/spin {brief,frame,priority,project,namespace}` behind the operator admin-token gate (double-gated through the HUD proxy) + open `GET /api/mills/spinning-room/frames` for the selector, and a "⟳ Spin a plan" dialog on the Plans panel. Wired only when the MCP hub is reachable (draft plans author over it); leaves 503 otherwise. Tests: `spin` package (12 cases — happy path, disabled/fail-closed, invalid/unknown-frame, editor+author error propagation, empty output, flat-proposal synthesis, default-priority, actual-model-overrides-frame, slice cap/dedup), `TestPlanClient_AuthorDraftPlan_*`, `TestSpinningRoomPolicy_{AccessorsAndFailClosed,Validate,ParsesFromYAML}`, `TestHandleSpin_*`/`TestHandleSpinningRoomFrames_*`, and HUD proxy `TestProxy_{ForwardsSpinningRoomFramesWithoutAdmin,SpinRequiresAdmin}`. Goes live once the operator image redeploys AND a `spinning_room:` block with frames lands in the policy ConfigMap.
- **Take-up motion (Live Beam slice 2) — MR reality drives plan/slice lifecycle** (`pkg/mills/takeup/reconciler.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/{plan_takeup,plan_reader,gitlab}.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): second slice of the Live Beam (`.loom/brainstorm-mills-steering-preparation-line-2026-07-03.md`). Plan/slice phases previously advanced only when an agent remembered to call `agent_plan_lifecycle_advance`, so the Plan Store drifted stale the moment an MR merged outside the recorded flow — exactly the staleness that makes HUD steering untrustworthy. On a loom the take-up roller winds finished cloth and advances the warp automatically; this reconciler does the same: it polls active plans in a policy-gated project+namespace and trues them to GitLab — a slice whose MR merged advances to `merged` (via new `PlanClient.UpdateSlicePhase`, attributed `mills:take-up`) and its emitted backlog item (`psl-…`, resolved via the now-exported `intake.BacklogIDForSlice`) closes to `merged` when still queued/paused/escalated (a **running** item is the pipeline's to finish and is never touched); a slice whose MR **closed without merging** gets ONE deduped orphan decision note (dedupe reads the slice detail first and skips on fetch failure rather than risk append-spam every tick); a plan whose slices are ALL merged rolls forward hop-by-hop along the phase DAG to `merged`, each hop recorded in `phase_history`. `ParseMRIID` tolerates the free-form refs agents record (`!912`, bare IID, full URL, `/diffs` suffix, trailing annotations). Fail-closed like the plan-slice emitter: `policy.intake.takeup.enabled` default-off AND an empty `namespace` makes every tick a no-op; needs both the MCP hub and a GitLab client. New `GitLabClient.MRState` (read-only GET by IID) and `PlanSliceSummary.{MRRef,Decisions}` projections. Tests: 9 reconciler cases (merge/close/open/running-item/escalated-item/orphan-dedupe/fail-closed/rejected-hop/IID parsing) + `TestTakeupPolicy_AccessorsAndFailClosed`.
- **Mills Pattern Loom — catalog expansion: Go MCP server, Go CLI tool, Python FastAPI service** (`pkg/agentcontext/schema_patterns_builtin.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/schema_engrams_builtin.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_pattern_stamp.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_patterns.go`, `internal/hud/api_patterns.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{stores/patterns.svelte.ts,components/mills/PatternsPanel.svelte}`, `docs/PATTERN_LOOM.md`): the builtin catalog grows from one pattern to four. Each new pattern earned `approved` the same way the Go REST seed did — a context-free follower agent executed the real stamped manifest alone and an independent gauge (written before any follower output existed) scored it: **`pattern-go-mcp-server`** (stdlib-only newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio; initialize/tools/list/tools/call; declarative tool registry with generated inputSchema; gauge 18/18), **`pattern-go-cli`** (stdlib `flag.FlagSet` subcommands, pinned exit-code table, `-ldflags` version injection; gauge 17/17, zero architecture-class gaps), and **`pattern-python-fastapi-service`** (FastAPI single-entity CRUD mirroring the Go REST external contract — same error envelope, status-code table, 16-hex ids; `app/` package pinned entity-independent so engram proofs resolve; uv-managed; gauge 15/15). Architecture-class gaps the followers surfaced are pinned back into the patterns (JSON-Schema type mapping; exact endpoint set incl. no-update + plural derivation; app-wide 500 handler; ValidationError as the scaffolded business-rule seam) — the S1 feedback loop, evidence in `.loom/kill-test-pattern-expansion-2026-07-03.md`, manifests regenerable via `pkg/agentcontext/manifest_dump_test.go`. Ships with: **7 new builtin tier-1 engrams** (`go-jsonrpc/stdio-line-protocol`, `go-mcp/tool-registry-inputschema`, `go-cli/flagset-subcommand-dispatch`, `go-cli/ldflags-version-injection`, `py-fastapi/app-factory-di`, `py-fastapi/error-envelope-handlers`, `py-config/env-dataclass-config`) with the shared `go-build/stdlib-only-gomod` engram now tagged for all three Go patterns; a **language/shape-neutral stamp path** (the `target_dir` confinement note no longer hardcodes `go.mod`, and the deterministic plan-id primary accepts `tool_name` alongside `service_name` — proven by `plan-stamp-go-cli-sprockctl`); and the **HUD Patterns panel now renders the instruction book** — a collapsible pins/gauge/engrams/deploy-contract/provenance section per selected pattern (bridge `PatternInfo` previously dropped those fields). Tests: `TestPattern_SeedBuiltins_CatalogWellFormed` (every builtin resolves, pins ≥8, pattern↔engram non-drift, every slice-template placeholder derivable from its materials schema), `TestStamp_GoMCPServer_ExpandsPlan`, `TestStamp_GoCLI_UsesToolNamePrimary`, `TestStamp_PyFastAPI_ExpandsPlan`. The expanded catalog goes live on the next mcp-agent-context redeploy (SeedBuiltins upserts on startup).
- **loom CLI: CI log classification command** (`cmd/loom/main.go`, `cmd/loom/internal/ci_classify.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_ci_classify_test.go`, `cmd/loom/internal/ci_classify_test.go`): adds `loom ci classify` to read CI logs from a file or stdin and emit human-readable or JSON failure-taxonomy results.
- **Warp Beam (slice 1) — plan priority steers Mills dispatch order end-to-end** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema_plan,svc_plans,svc_plan_render}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_plan.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_reader.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_plans.go`, `internal/hud/domain/plans/{plans,handler_plans}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{components/PlansPanel.svelte,utils/plansHelpers.ts}`): first slice of the Live Beam from `.loom/brainstorm-mills-steering-preparation-line-2026-07-03.md`. The kill-test found the Mills dispatcher already picks queued backlog items `ORDER BY priority ASC, created_at ASC` (`pkg/mills/store/dao_backlog.go`) — but nothing upstream could steer that order: plans had no priority at all and the plan-slice emitter stamped every emitted item with one config default (P2). Now: (1) the Plan entity carries a validated, clearable `priority` bucket (`P0`..`P3`, aligned with `store.Priority`), settable via `agent_plan_create`/`agent_plan_update` and rendered in the plan mirror; `agent_plan_list` returns deterministic beam order (priority bucket first, unset last, newest-updated within a bucket). (2) The emitter propagates the plan's bucket onto emitted backlog items (unknown/unset falls back to the config default) and **resyncs still-queued items when a plan's priority changes after emission** — so a HUD reorder re-orders autonomous dispatch on the next tick instead of freezing at emit time; in-flight/terminal items are never touched. (3) The HUD grows the reorder knob: `POST /api/plans/{id}/priority` (empty string clears), priority passthrough on plan create, priority badges on plan cards/drawer, and a priority selector next to "Advance to". Tests: `TestPlan_PriorityBeamOrder`, `TestPlan_PayloadRoundTrip` (priority round-trip), `TestPlanSliceEmitter_{PropagatesPlanPriority,ResyncsQueuedPriority,NoResyncOnceInFlight}`, `TestPlanSetPriority`, `TestPlanCreate_PassesPriority`. Take-up reconciler (MR-merge → plan/slice phase sync) is the next slice.
- **mills/hud: escalations-by-fault-class breakdown in the operator console** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/kpi_writer.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`): completes the fault-class visibility trifecta (Prometheus + Grafana already shipped) by surfacing it in loom-core's own HUD. New DAO query `CountEscalationsByClassSince` GROUPs escalated runs by `pipeline_runs.escalation_class` (NULL/empty → `unclassified`, so counts sum to `pipeline_escalated_runs`); the KPI writer adds an `escalations_by_class` map to the snapshot, which flows through the existing `GET /api/mills/kpis` endpoint (no REST change). The Mills Overview panel renders a compact per-class breakdown, colour-coded by what the class demands of an operator (code/config = human fix, infra = cluster fix, transient/transient_quota = self-correcting). Tests: `TestPipeline_CountEscalationsByClassSince` (grouping, unclassified bucket, window bound, never-nil empty map) and an assertion in `TestKPIWriter_FrontendKeys_PopulatedWhenDataAvailable` pinning the DAO→snapshot→`metrics_json` round-trip. Completes the HUD half of council plan `surface-classified-faults-in-alerting-and-visibility-outputs`.
- **mills: surface escalation fault class as a Prometheus metric** (`pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`): the terminal fault class stamped on every escalated run (`pipeline_runs.escalation_class`) was persisted but never emitted, so Grafana/Alertmanager could not tell escalations driven by real code bugs from infra flake, quota walls, or config — the visibility gap behind the 2026-07-02 budget no-op escalation DoS. New counter **`mills_pipeline_escalation_class_total{class}`** (`transient` / `transient_quota` / `infra` / `code` / `config` / `unclassified`) is incremented **co-located** with `mills_escalations_total` at both escalation sites (`escalateWithItem` + `maybeAutoRetry`) so the two series share one population (`sum-by-class == sum-by-reason`). Label cardinality is bounded to the closed `ErrorClass` taxonomy plus `unclassified` via new `ErrorClass.Valid()`/`AllErrorClasses()`; the `FailureClass` spellings (`infrastructure`/`configuration`) deliberately fall through to `unclassified` since the `[class=…]` marker carries `ErrorClass` values. Tests: `TestEscalationClassLabel`, `TestRunner_EscalateIncrementsClassMetric`, `TestErrorClass_ValidAndAll`, and the `metrics_test.go` registration/label-accept/gatherable lists. Advances council plan `surface-classified-faults-in-alerting-and-visibility-outputs`; the Grafana panel + Alertmanager rule that consume the series land in platform/gitops.
- **CI triage runbook** (`docs/ci-triage-runbook.md`): documents repeated GitLab pipeline failure classification, preflight checks, evidence collection, external dependency incident handling, retry policy, and closeout dispositions.
- **mills: closed failure taxonomy and retry-aware escalation policy** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/failure_classification.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/failure_classification_test.go`, `pkg/mills/council/escalation_policy.go`, `pkg/mills/council/escalation_policy_test.go`): adds policy-facing failure classes, stable mappings from the runner classifier, and retry/auto-retry/human-escalation decisions with unit coverage.
- **mills: deterministic autonomy breaker evaluator** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/breaker.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/breaker_test.go`): added a policy/KPI/infrastructure health evaluator that returns stable fail-closed autonomy decisions before Mills starts or continues autonomous execution.
- **Mills infrastructure health gates** (`pkg/mills/gates/health.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/preflight.go`, `pkg/mills/council/planning_policy.go`, `pkg/policy/stability_first.go`, `internal/hud/{monitor,alerting,fleetview}/health_gates.go`, `docs/mills-infrastructure-health-gates.md`): adds a fail-closed health-gate decision model, blocks Mills pipeline dispatch when configured preflight health gates fail, prioritizes remediation in stability-first planning, and exposes the gate verdict to HUD operators.
- **Mills health gate hardening** (`pkg/mills/gates/health.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/preflight.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/health_gates.go`): fail-closes on stale critical component checks, health-gate provider errors, and incomplete HUD status payloads.
- **Mills operational guardrails operator guide** (`docs/mills-operational-guardrails.md`): documents breaker thresholds, fail-closed safe state, and remediation workflow for telemetry, storage, and auth failures.
- **mills: a real-work north-star gauge that excludes heartbeat canaries** (`pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `pkg/mills/kpi_writer.go`): the headline `mills_autonomous_merges` counts *every* merged pipeline run — including the deterministic `mills-canary` fixtures the autopilot enqueues daily for liveness.
