During the 2026-06-30 audit it read a healthy 7/7d while **every one was a trivial canary** and zero real council/importer/plan-slice work landed (every council item escalated) — the headline gauge actively *hid* the demand-starvation. New gauge **`mills_autonomous_merges_real{window=1d|7d|30d}`** counts merged runs EXCLUDING canary-labeled backlog items (`countRealMergedRunsSince` joins `pipeline_runs`→`backlog_items` and filters `labels_json` against the `mills-canary` label via a quoted-token `LIKE` that won't snag a superstring). Wired into both `Record` (per-tick refresh, decays as merges age out) and `SeedDurableGauges` (restart-safe seed at boot), and surfaced in the KPI snapshot as `pipeline_merged_real` for the status/HUD path. A persistent `real==0 while autonomous_merges>0` is now an alertable signal that the loop is idle on real demand instead of an invisible failure. Tests: `kpi_writer_test.go` (`TestKPIWriter_RealMergedRuns_ExcludesCanary` — canaries excluded, no-label + superstring counted as real, escalated excluded, gauge + snapshot agree).
- **Mills Pattern Loom — taste gate (B2)** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_pattern_taste.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_pattern.go`): a Pattern's `status` (`candidate → approved → deprecated`) now controls whether the factory will stamp it (the rails + front door offer `approved` by default). New tools `agent_pattern_promote` (human curation — approve/deprecate, `force` to override the threshold) and `agent_pattern_record_instance` (the stamp/merge hook that increments `instances_shipped_green` and **auto-promotes** a candidate to approved at `PatternApprovalThreshold`, default 1). Taste is both editorial and empirical. See `docs/PATTERN_LOOM.md` §"Authoring patterns".
- **Mills Pattern Loom — Pattern catalog + stamp verb + council rails + front door** (`pkg/agentcontext/{schema_pattern,svc_patterns,svc_pattern_stamp}.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_pattern.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/pattern.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council.go`, `internal/hud/api_patterns.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_patterns.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_mills_patterns.go`): a first-class, worktree-resilient **Pattern** catalog (`agent_patterns_v1`) — the software analog of a textile pattern. A Pattern **pins the load-bearing architecture** (plus the five "stampability" seams the S1 kill-test surfaced — error envelope, per-endpoint bodies, wiring convention, error model, full status-code table) so only **Materials** vary, then is **stamped** (`agent_pattern_stamp`) into a Plan the Mills pipeline executes. Ships:
  - the seed pattern `pattern-go-rest-service`, formalized from the `go-service-scaffold` skill and validated by the S1 kill-test (independent black-box gauge 10/10, zero unrequested architecture);
  - MCP tools `agent_pattern_add` / `_get` / `_list` / `_search` / `_stamp` (cross-agent, Qdrant-backed, mirroring the Plan/Engram services);
  - **Track A** — the Mills council editor now injects the approved-pattern catalog into its prompt and records a `pattern_id` per proposal (both the FlexInfer and gpt-5.4 editors);
  - **Track B** — a stamp front door: `loom mills patterns` / `loom mills stamp` CLI plus `GET /api/patterns` and `POST /api/patterns/stamp` HUD endpoints.

  Plan `plan-pattern-loom-mills` (S0+S1+A1+B1); see `.loom/164-brainstorm-pattern-loom-mills-2026-06-28.md`, `.loom/165-plan-pattern-loom-mills-2026-06-28.md`, `.loom/kill-test-pattern-go-rest-service-2026-06-28.md`, and `docs/PATTERN_LOOM.md`. The new `agent_pattern_*` tools require an mcp-agent-context redeploy to go live; the Svelte front-door page, live tools-manifest probing, and the Mills BacklogItem projection + pipeline run are deferred sub-slices.

### Changed
- **hud/frontend: complete the phantom-token alias block and drop now-redundant hex fallbacks** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css` + 36 components/utils): the theme.css "Phantom Token Aliases" block was incomplete — 28 tokens referenced across panels (`--accent-blue/green/amber/red`, `--status-error`, `--danger-text`, `--text-link`, `--text`, `--fg-default/tertiary`, `--font-size-lg`, `--leading-relaxed`, `--surface(-subtle)`, `--bg-default/deep/hover/row(-hover)`, `--bg-warning/success/danger-soft`, `--border-default/strong`, `--space-xs/sm/md/lg`) were undefined, so components silently rendered their foreign hex fallbacks (GitHub-Primer palette in LiveSessionsCard status dots, Tailwind hues in Mills panels). All 28 now alias to canonical brand tokens (soft backgrounds via `rgba(var(--*-rgb), var(--opacity-*))`; `--danger-text` via `color-mix`) — an intended visual change from GitHub/Tailwind hues to brand hues. With every referenced token defined, a sweep dropped 467 `var(--token, fallback)` fallbacks across 37 files, taking hardcoded hex colors in components from 390 to 0 — panels can no longer drift from the theme. Dynamic per-element tokens (`--accent-color`, `--tone-color`, `--gauge-color`, `--budget-fill`, etc.) keep their semantic `var()` defaults. Verified: vite build + vitest green, live computed-style probe of all 28 tokens non-empty, Fleet view visual spot-check with zero console errors.
- **hud/frontend: data-driven lazy panel registry, shared visibility-aware poller, design-token gap-fill** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/panelRegistry.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/LazyPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/poller.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{catalog,health,memory}.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/{theme,layout}.css`): App.svelte's 31-branch view->panel chain (14 branches carrying duplicated `{#await import(...)}` scaffolding) is replaced by a registry of lazy import thunks rendered through a single `LazyPanel` — every panel now code-splits into its own chunk (main bundle 734 KB -> 280 KB, gzip 211 KB -> 82 KB; App.svelte 1143 -> 936 lines) and adding a panel is a registry entry, not an App.svelte edit. New `createPoller` primitive owns the timer lifecycle, tab-visibility pause (a hidden tab stops generating backend poll load; a catch-up tick fires when the tab returns), and an overlap guard so slow responses cannot pile up requests; adopted by the catalog/health/memory stores as exemplars (the remaining ~30 stores hand-rolling `setInterval` migrate mechanically in a follow-up). `theme.css` gains the type-scale steps components kept hardcoding (`--text-12/14/20`), an opacity scale (`--opacity-faint..heavy`), RGB channel triplets for tokenized alpha fills (`--accent-rgb` etc.), and glow-shadow geometry tokens (`--glow-shadow-sm..xl`); `layout.css` gains `.btn-xs`/`.btn-lg` and a `.btn:disabled` treatment. Directory naming normalized: `components/Mills/` -> `components/mills/`, `components/SpawnTelemetry/` -> `components/spawn-telemetry/`. The vite dev proxy target is overridable via `HUD_API_TARGET` for daemons not on :9800. Tests: `poller.test.ts` (7 cases: interval, stop, restart-no-stacking, hidden-skip, visible-catch-up, overlap guard, manual refresh). Verified live against a local daemon (fleet/mills/memory/spawn views + 375px mobile reflow, zero console errors).
- **mills: enforce the autonomy circuit breaker inside pipeline continuation** (`pkg/mills/council/autonomy_gate.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/autonomy_gate.go`): blocked autonomy decisions now normalize to stable reason codes and halt/escalate an in-flight Mills run before the next autonomous stage can proceed.
- **mills: the council slice-grounding guard flags brand-new-directory slices instead of dropping them (flag-never-drop)** (`pkg/mills/council/slice_grounding.go`, `backlog_mutator.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`) — verified item #0 from the 2026-06-30 gauntlet audit. The guard (MR !848/!850) dropped any proposal slice whose `files` ALL lived under a not-yet-existing directory, to stop the editor's fabricated paths (`pkg/planning/`, `pkg/pipeline/`) from landing empty-diff items. But a legitimate **new package** (`pkg/mills/newpkg/x.go`, whose parent `pkg/mills` exists) is *structurally indistinguishable by path* from a fabricated one — both are "all files under a new dir" — so real new-package work was dropped, its proposal removed, and the item escalated on an empty diff. There is no path-only signal separating the two, so per an explicit policy decision the guard now **keeps** the wholly-new-directory slice and records it as a new `speculative` class (`SlicesSpeculative`, metric `mills_council_slices_guard_total{action="speculative"}`) rather than dropping it; the council editor's repo-layout prompt grounding plus the downstream build/tests/CI gates catch genuine fabrication, and the speculative count keeps every new-directory creation observable. Nothing is dropped on grounding grounds anymore (the `dropped`/`FictionalProposalsDropped` counters are retained for metric-shape compatibility and stay zero). Tests: `slice_grounding_test.go` (`TestSanitizeProposalSlices_AllNewDirSliceKeptAsSpeculative`, `TestSanitizeProposalSlices_LegitNewPackageKept`, `TestApply_SliceGuard_KeepsNewDirProposalAsSpeculative`, `TestApply_SliceGuard_KeepsRealAndNewDirSlices`).
- **mills: the council editor now always emits a `## Backlog Proposals` block and records *why* a run created zero proposals** (`pkg/mills/clients/council_proposals.go`, `council.go`, `council_openai.go`): the council is the loop's demand source, but a run could complete with `created: 0` — three good markdown docs and no runnable backlog items — and nothing recorded why. Two causes: (1) the editor prompt gave the model an over-broad off-ramp (*"omit the section if you are unsure, or if you cannot fill every field"*), so a conservative model omitted the proposals block by default; and (2) a nil parse was opaque — `created: 0` looked identical whether the model correctly judged the brief a single unit, malformed the JSON, or simply ignored the instruction. Fix: (a) the prompt now asks the editor to **always** emit the `## Backlog Proposals` section — one proposal per independently-shippable unit when the plan decomposes, or an explicit `{"proposals": [], "omit_reason": "…"}` for a single-unit/unmappable brief — while keeping the path-grounding guard (emit an empty list rather than invent fictional paths); and (b) `parseCouncilProposalsDiag` classifies the outcome (`emitted` / `marker_absent` / `no_json_object` / `decode_error` / `empty_declared` / `empty_titles`) and the run's Sidecar `Notes` records the reason, so `created: 0` is diagnosable in the `council_runs` record and the HUD Council tab. Both the FlexInfer and gpt-5.4 (OpenAI Responses) editors share the prompt + parser, so both paths are covered. The prompt's effect on emission rate is confirmable only by a live council run on the redeployed operator (deferred kill-test). Tests: `council_proposals_test.go` (`TestParseCouncilProposalsDiag_Statuses`) + updated `council_test.go`.
- **infra(mills): raise the mobile-hud spawn-pool concurrency cap from 3 → 6** (`k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`): the spawn orchestrator runs in the mobile-hud process with a default `SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT` of 3 (`internal/hud/spawn.go` `DefaultSpawnConfig`). On a working loop the daily council, daily canary, plan-slice emitter, and manual runs routinely overlap, so a cap of 3 serialized real work — and before the saturation classifier fix (!857) made over-limit requests back off + retry, a 4th concurrent run escalated instantly with `class=code`, `$0` cost, zero stage progress (observed live 2026-06-30 on `MILLS-VERIFY-CODE-…2002`). Set `SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT=6`; each spawn is 0.5 CPU + 4 GiB, so 6 = 24 GiB spread across the ~16-node cluster, and image builds stay throttled to 1 via `SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT_BUILDS`, so the higher run cap does not trigger a buildah thundering herd.

### Fixed
- **mills: Spinning-Room spins no longer hang — extend the write deadline for the spin route + bound the editor and plan-store phases** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_spin.go`, `pkg/mills/spin/spin.go`): a live spin held the HTTP connection for the whole model synthesis + the plan-store write over the MCP hub, and two things made that hang instead of returning. (1) The operator's `http.Server` sets `WriteTimeout: 30s` (server.go), so a handler running past 30s — which every spin does — failed silently when it finally tried to write the response: the connection closed with **no HTTP response** (the client saw `HTTP 000`, reading as a hang even on a *successful* spin). `handleSpin` now extends this connection's write deadline via `http.NewResponseController(w).SetWriteDeadline(...)` to just over the request budget, so the response is actually delivered. (2) `AuthorDraftPlan` (the `agent_plan_create` write over the hub → `agent_context`) was unbounded, so a stalled hub — the observed live failure, where `agent_context` plan ops hung and the take-up reconciler's own 2-min tick timeout was firing — hung the spin until the outer budget. The `Spinner` now bounds each phase independently: `EditorTimeout` (default 6m) on the model synthesis and `AuthorTimeout` (default 45s) on the plan-store write, each failing fast with a typed error (`ErrEditorTimeout` / `ErrAuthorTimeout` → HTTP 504) and a clear "retry" message instead of hanging; an outer-context cancellation (client disconnect / request budget) is passed through as-is, not misreported as a phase timeout. Per-phase timing (`edit_seconds`, `author_seconds`) is logged so a slow spin's bottleneck is visible. Tests: `TestSpin_{EditorTimeoutFailsFast,AuthorTimeoutFailsFast,OuterCancelNotMisreportedAsPhaseTimeout}`. Note: this makes spins *robust* (return or fail-fast, never hang), but a frontier model that legitimately takes minutes still strains a synchronous HTTP spin through the Cloudflare/proxy chain — async spins remain the durable fix for slow frames.
- **mills/hud: the Spinning-Room frames endpoint is now uncacheable so the "Spin a plan" picker can't show a stale/empty frame list** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_spin.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/SpinPlanDialog.svelte`): `GET /api/mills/spinning-room/frames` reflects **live policy** (the frame set changes whenever `spinning_room.frames` is edited) but sent no `Cache-Control` header, so a browser (heuristic caching) or the Cloudflare edge could serve a stale response — most visibly an empty list rendering as "— none —" in the picker after a frame was just activated. The handler now sets `Cache-Control: no-store` (the HUD reverse-proxy forwards it to the browser), and the dialog fetches with `{ cache: 'no-store' }` as belt-and-suspenders, so the picker always reflects current policy. Test: `TestHandleSpinningRoomFrames_DefaultDisabled` now asserts the `no-store` header. Goes live on the next operator + mobile-hud image roll.
- **mills: take-up reconciler can no longer wedge silently — per-tick deadline + unconditional outcome logging + Prometheus visibility** (`pkg/mills/takeup/reconciler.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): the take-up reconciler (`!913`) shipped enabled in prod (gitops `intake.takeup`, namespace `mills/demand-sourcing`) yet was a runtime no-op — the pod logged "started" then never reconciled, never errored, and never logged again across multiple poll intervals and a fresh restart. Root cause: `Run` executed the first `r.Tick(ctx)` **synchronously and unbounded** before starting the `time.Ticker`; a stalled hub (`agent_plan_list`/`agent_plan_slice_list`) or GitLab (`MRState`) call there blocked the goroutine forever, so the ticker never started and the loop went dark while the metrics-port readiness probe kept the pod `Ready`. Fixes: (1) every pass — the immediate startup pass AND each periodic one — now runs through a new `runTick` helper that wraps `Tick` in a `context.WithTimeout` (`Config.TickTimeout`, policy `intake.takeup.tick_timeout_seconds`, default **2min**, well under the 5min poll), so a stalled dependency is a bounded, logged timeout and the loop keeps going. (2) `runTick` records the outcome (`ok`/`timeout`/`error`/`cancelled`) **unconditionally** to logs — an `ok` tick with `plans_scanned=0` now distinguishes "namespace gate matched nothing" from a wedge, where the old code logged only when work happened. (3) New Prometheus series `mills_takeup_ticks_total{outcome}` (a flat counter on an enabled operator = alertable stalled loop), `mills_takeup_tick_duration_seconds`, and `mills_takeup_{plans_scanned,slices_merged,plans_merged,items_closed,orphans_flagged}_total`. (4) `PlanClient.Hub` decoupled behind a new `clients.HubCaller` interface so tests can inject a fake hub returning production-shaped TOON. Tests: `TestTakeup_StalledTickDoesNotWedgeRun` (a hanging `ListPlans` no longer wedges `Run` — the exact regression), `TestTakeup_RealHubTOONDecodePathEndToEnd` (merged MR → slice/plan advance driven through a real `PlanClient` over `mcp.JSONResult`-encoded TOON, closing the untested live decode + initial-tick gap that let this ship), `TestNew_AppliesTickTimeoutDefault`, plus extended `TestTakeupPolicy_AccessorsAndFailClosed` and `metrics_test.go` registration/label lists. Fail-closed behavior is unchanged; the fix goes live when the operator image auto-updates (Flux IUA) with no further gitops change.
- **lint: `.golangci.yml` migrated to the v2 schema so its settings and exclusions actually apply** (`.golangci.yml`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Makefile`): the config declared `version: "2"` but used v1 keys (`linters-settings`, `issues.exclude-rules`, `issues.exclude-dirs-use-default`); golangci-lint v2 (the repo pins v2.8.0) does not recognize them, so `golangci-lint config verify` rejected them and `run` silently ignored them, leaving the effective linter set drifted to the v2 default (issue #240). Migrated to the v2 schema via the official `golangci-lint migrate` (`linters.settings`, `linters.exclusions` + the `legacy`/`common-false-positives`/`comments`/`std-error-handling` presets to preserve the v1 default-exclude behavior the broken config had lost, `formatters.settings.goimports`). `govet`'s `shadow` check is intentionally left disabled — it was inert under the broken config (so *not* in the current lint baseline) and enabling it now surfaces 50+ idiomatic `if err :=` shadows in `pkg/mills`+`internal/hud` alone, so loom-core aligns with the canonical fleet config (shadow off). Added `golangci-lint config verify` as a hard gate in the CI `lint` job (no `allow_failure`), `make lint`, and `ci-lint-strict`, so this class of schema drift fails at push time instead of being silently ignored. Verified: `config verify` exit 0 and `golangci-lint run ./cmd/... ./internal/... ./pkg/...` reports 0 issues.
- **mills: no-op capacity escalations no longer burn the daily `MaxRunsPerDay` budget** (`pkg/mills/store/migrations/006_pipeline_escalation_class.sql`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `pkg/mills/store_adapter.go`, `pkg/mills/budget.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`): a saturated spawn pool (`400 max concurrent spawns reached (N)`) makes a pipeline run escalate at its very first spawn call — class `transient_quota`, cost $0, zero stage progress, ~9ms — but the budget enforcer counted every `pipeline_runs` row toward the rolling-24h `MaxRunsPerDay` cap regardless of outcome. On 2026-07-02 a spawn-pool wedge produced 18/20 no-op escalations that exhausted the whole day's run budget, starving 3 queued+ready items for hours until the rows aged out — a transient, self-correcting capacity limit turned into a full-day throughput DoS. Migration 006 adds a nullable `pipeline_runs.escalation_class` column (stamped by the runner from the `[class=…]` marker it already emits, on both the real-escalation and auto-retry-divert paths); the new `PipelineDAO.CountBudgetedSince` — which the budget reader now uses in place of `CountSince` — discounts only `state='escalated' AND cost_usd = 0 AND escalation_class = 'transient_quota'` runs. Real `code`/`infra`/`config` failures, generic transients, and any run that did billable work still count, so the cap stays protective against runaway loops; `CountSince` is unchanged for KPI telemetry. This is the budget-accounting substrate the in-flight Mills failure-classification / fault-taxonomy backlog work extends. Tests: `dao_pipeline_escalation_class_test.go`, `pipeline/escalation_class_test.go`.
- **spawn: reap empty-metadata zombie spawns via an absolute-age backstop** (`internal/spawn/controller.go`, `internal/spawn/controller_test.go`, `internal/spawn/reconciler_test.go`): the restart-durable deadline reaper (!859) never bounded a non-terminal spawn whose `Request.TimeoutMinutes <= 0` — `spawnDeadlineExceeded` short-circuited to `false` before ever computing a deadline. States the reconciler rebuilds from pod labels alone (the discovered-untracked-pod path drops `TaskDescription`/`TimeoutMinutes`) carry no timeout, so on 2026-07-01 six codex spawns with blank request metadata survived 22–30h `status:"running"` in `loom-spawn-state`, pinning the Mills spawn pool at its cap of 6 and forcing every pipeline stage to escalate with `400 max concurrent spawns reached (6)`. The reaper now fires at `StartedAt + max(TimeoutMinutes, 60m) + grace(5m)`, so any non-terminal spawn with a real `StartedAt` is bounded regardless of request completeness; explicit longer timeouts are still honored and only a zero-`StartedAt` entry (no age anchor) is skipped. The empty-metadata reconstruction is documented in-code as the smell, with a `loom.dev/timeout-minutes` pod-label follow-up noted. Tests: `TestReconcileReapsRunningSpawnWithBlankRequest`, `TestSpawnDeadlineExceeded` (table), `TestReconcileReapsUnboundedZombieViaStore`.
- **mills: council editor external-dependency guardrails** (`pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails.go`, `pkg/mills/council/editor_guardrails_test.go`, `docs/mills-escalation-and-dependency-failures.md`): label external dependency incidents, preserve file-backed in-repo follow-ups across council slice formats, and drop speculative external-only remediation proposals that have no actionable in-repo file changes.
- **hud: codebase monitor stops failing every poll — `codebase_stats` gets a repo-agnostic fleet mode** (`pkg/codebase/repo_handlers.go`, `pkg/codebase/qdrant/serialize.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/codebase.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/codebase.go`): the HUD `CodebaseMonitor` polls `codebase_memory__codebase_stats` with no `repo_id`, but `HandleStats` required one (or `CODEBASE_REPO_ID`), so the loom-hub codebase-memory pod logged `tool call failed name=codebase_stats error="repo_id is required (or set CODEBASE_REPO_ID)"` on every poll forever (the `BaseMonitor` was already at max backoff, ~2.5 min). Two defects: (1) the call failed 100% of the time, and (2) even on success the bridge/monitor DTOs (`total_files`/`total_symbols`/`languages`/`last_indexed`/`index_status`) never matched the handler's real output (`total_chunks`/`by_language`/`by_chunk_type`), so the panel would have shown zeros. Fix: `HandleStats` now enters an **aggregate (fleet) mode** when no repo is configured — it summarizes the whole collection across every indexed repo using repo-agnostic `Count` filters (new `qdrant.FilterAcrossRepos`, which omits the `repo_id` condition; index-free, so it works on the un-indexed `repo_id` payload) and returns `{aggregate:true, total_chunks, by_language, by_chunk_type}` with zero buckets omitted. A configured `repo_id`/`CODEBASE_REPO_ID` still yields the per-repo path unchanged. The bridge `CodebaseStatsInfo` and monitor `CodebaseSnapshot` are realigned to the true response shape (`RepoID`/`Collection`/`Aggregate`/`TotalChunks`/`ByLanguage`/`ByChunkType`). Tests: `pkg/codebase/repo_handlers_test.go` (aggregate-when-no-repo-id succeeds and never filters by `repo_id`; per-repo path still scopes; `CODEBASE_REPO_ID` default still selects single-repo mode), `pkg/codebase/qdrant/serialize_test.go` (`FilterAcrossRepos` never scopes repo, nil for match-all), plus updated bridge/monitor/domain codebase tests.
- **hud: weak panels surface fetch errors + cold-start states instead of a blank or false-empty** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/projects.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{ProjectsPanel,ServersPanel,PresencePanel,WeaverPanel}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/graph/GraphFullView.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/servers/ServersTable.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/sandbox/SandboxLive.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/vite.config.js`): a 2026-07 UX audit found HUD panels the prior state-coherence pass (`fix/hud-state-coherence`, `feat/hud-empty-state-tones`) didn't reach that rendered blank on cold start or swallowed fetch errors, showing an empty state indistinguishable from a failed load. The `projects` store now throws a rejected/non-OK `/api/plans` into its `error` state (was silently ignored → a daemon-down failure read as "No projects yet"; `/api/sessions` stays best-effort). ProjectsPanel, GraphFullView, ServersPanel/ServersTable, and PresencePanel gain the CatalogPanel/MemoryPanel `.error-banner` (`role="alert"`) plus cold-start error/loading states: Servers no longer spins an endless DataTable skeleton on a failed first `/api/health`, Graph no longer flashes "No entities" during the first fetch, and Presence no longer reads a failed fleet poll as "No registered agents". WeaverPanel keeps its last-known sections under a non-blocking banner instead of blanking the whole panel on a transient 5s-poll blip; SandboxLive's existing error banner gains `role="alert"`/`aria-live`. `vite.config.js` honors `HUD_API_TARGET` so the documented `HUD_API_TARGET=… npx vite` dev-preview proxy points at the daemon (was hardcoded to a dead `:9800`). Deliberately keeps the inline `.error-banner` + `EmptyState` idiom rather than converting 6 custom-layout panels to `PanelShell`. Verified live in the vite dev preview against both a healthy daemon and a dead-port proxy (all five error states + normal render, no console errors).
- **deps: fi-mcp-kit bumped so `Manager.Stop` bounds its post-SIGKILL wait for every caller** (`go.mod`, `go.sum`): fi-mcp-kit `v0.2.1-0.20260412164817` → `v0.2.1-0.20260702142828` picks up upstream commit `4a3d85f` (fi-mcp-kit MR `fix/stop-bound-sigkill-wait`, merged 2026-05-25), which bounds the post-SIGKILL `cmd.Wait()` in `pkg/process.stopProcess` (2s) so an orphaned grandchild holding the child's stdout pipe can no longer pin `Stop()` callers indefinitely. Complements the caller-side `stopServerProcBounded` reaper bound from !524 (see the stuck call-lock entry below) — that guard stays as defense in depth, but every other `procMgr.Stop` call site is now protected too. Upstream regression test: `TestStopProcess_PostKillBoundUnsticksHangingWait`.
- **codebase: durable watches no longer accumulate as immortal duplicates that re-embed every file change forever** (`pkg/codebase/{watch,watch_store,watch_state,watch_claim_unix,watch_claim_other,config,service}.go`, `cmd/mcp-codebase-memory/tools.go`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`): the durable-watcher work (!854) made `codebase_watch_*` survive process restarts but gave watches no lifecycle, so they burned embedding tokens (paid Morph API) four compounding ways. **(1) No dedup** — every `codebase_watch_start` minted a fresh immortal watch (`hash(repo_id+now)`); the documented agent pattern is "start a watch per exploration session" and nothing ever stopped them, so N duplicate watches on the same root each re-embedded every file change (concurrent processing races the qdrant preflight content-hash check, so the unchanged-skip never fires). Start is now idempotent per `(repo_id, absolute root)` — checked under the same lock as the insert — and returns the live watch with `reused:true`. **(2) One watch per *process*** — the hub's `custom-server` spawns a child `mcp-codebase-memory` per WebSocket connection and every child ran `ResumeWatches` over the shared state dir, so each persisted watch ran once per connected client. A per-watch run-claim (exclusive `flock` on `<id>.claim`; kernel-released on process death, no staleness heuristics) makes exactly one process own a watch; `codebase_watch_poll` reports claim-held watches as `remote:true` and **adopts** orphaned ones (owner died) instead of returning the `found:false` that pushed agents to start duplicates. **(3) No expiry** — start/reuse/poll now record activity (persisted at most every 5m); watches idle beyond `CODEBASE_WATCH_TTL` (default `72h`, `0` disables) are expired at resume time and by a periodic janitor sweep. **(4) Zombie roots** — descriptors whose root no longer exists (deleted worktrees) are dropped at resume/adopt instead of resuming a watch over nothing, and `codebase_watch_start` rejects a missing root outright. New `codebase_watch_list` tool exposes local watches (all statuses + stats + last-activity) and persisted watches owned elsewhere, so leaked watches are findable at all. Tests: `watch_lifecycle_test.go` (idempotent start, missing-root rejection/cleanup, TTL expiry at resume + sweep, claim exclusivity, orphan adoption, poll hint, list), existing persistence tests unchanged.
- **Stuck per-server MCP call lock + silent server-side session auto-end** (`internal/daemon/daemon_loops.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_end.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_sessions_reaper.go`): two independent defects. **(1) Lock leak** — the idle reaper took a server's `callLock` then called `stopServerProc`, whose `procMgr.Stop` has an unbounded `<-done` wait after SIGKILL; if `Cmd.Wait()` never returned (orphaned child holding the stdout pipe) the reaper goroutine pinned the lock forever, bricking that server (every later call failed with `acquire call lock … after 0s: context deadline exceeded`) until daemon restart. The reaper now uses `stopServerProcBounded` with a 5s `reaperStopTimeout`; a hung Stop goroutine is intentionally leaked but the call lock is always released. **(2) Silent auto-end** — `End()` published `EventTypeSessionEnd` but the server-side auto-end paths `EndActiveForAgent` (agent timeout) and `EndStale` (reaper) did not, so sessions ended server-side never emitted the SSE event to subscribers. Both paths now emit via a shared `publishSessionEnd` helper. (The HUD live-sessions store reconcile that originally accompanied this fix was superseded by !694's snapshot reconcile and is not included.) Regression tests in `daemon_loops_test.go` + `pkg/agentcontext/publisher_test.go`. (Original branch `fix/stuck-call-lock-and-session-end`, 2026-05-25, rescued 2026-07-02.)
- **mills: council roadmap parser reads the workspace roadmap convention** (`pkg/mills/council/roadmap.go`): the roadmap-refresh MR !882 rewrote ROADMAP.md to the workspace template (libs/STANDARDS.md "Roadmap & Backlog") — horizon sections `## Now`/`## Next`/`## Later` and plain-text task bullets — but the council extractor only matched `- [ ] **bold**` bullets and knew nothing of the horizon sections, so it extracted **0 intents** from main's ROADMAP.md. That both starved the council's demand source in production and turned the pre-push gate red on a clean main checkout (`TestExtract_RealROADMAP` failed in `go test -short ./...`, forcing `--no-verify` pushes; !882's docs-only pipeline skipped the Go jobs that would have caught it). Bold wrappers on bullet titles are now optional and Now/Next/Later map to priorities 1/2/3 (`Backlog` is link-only and excluded, exact-match on horizon names so "Next Steps"-style headings keep the unknown-section bucket); the old tiered format still parses unchanged. Tests: `roadmap_test.go` (`TestExtract_ParsesWorkspaceConventionRoadmap` + the existing `TestExtract_RealROADMAP` smoke now passes against main's real ROADMAP.md).
- **hooks: pre-commit/pre-push Go hooks wedged on nested Go modules** (`scripts/ci/go_package_filter.sh`, `scripts/ci/go_build_hook.sh`, `scripts/ci/go_changed_packages_hook.sh`): the go-build, go-vet, and golangci-lint hooks enumerate changed packages by dirname and hand them to the main-module toolchain, so any staged set or push range touching `examples/sprocket` — its own Go module since the Pattern Loom stamp (1c85bad6) — failed with `main module (github.com/crb2nu/loom) does not contain package …`, which forced a `--no-verify` push during the MR !703 rescue. A shared sourced filter (`filter_main_module_packages`) now drops package dirs that live inside a nested module (any parent below the repo root with its own go.mod) and dirs deleted/renamed out of the working tree. Also applied the pending `goimports -local` grouping fix to `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer_test.go` so hooks pass clean on main again. Verify: `PRE_COMMIT_FROM_REF=$(git rev-parse 1c85bad6~1) PRE_COMMIT_TO_REF=$(git rev-parse 1c85bad6) bash scripts/ci/go_build_hook.sh` (skips instead of erroring).
- **hud: unified the per-agent staleness threshold across the web HUD and the mobile API** (`internal/hud/fleetview/fleetview.go`, `internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mobile/handler_agents.go`): the 90s live-presence horizon lived as an unexported constant in the fleet monitor while `fleetview.TelemetryStatus` and the mobile `/agents` fallback both used a divergent hardcoded 300s — so an agent with a 2-5 minute-old heartbeat showed "offline" on the web HUD but "live"/"stale" on mobile. The constant is now exported as `fleetview.LivePresenceStaleAfter` (single source of truth), the active/idle→offline downgrade runs inside `fleetview.Join` (after `markOrphan`, before `TelemetryStatus`, so `Status` and `TelemetryStatus` in a row always agree and re-joining a snapshot is idempotent), and both `TelemetryStatus` and `mobileAgentTelemetryStatus` derive their "stale" branch from the shared constant. Tests: `fleetview_test.go` (`TestJoin_DowngradesStalePresence`, `TestJoin_DowngradeIsIdempotent`, `TestTelemetryStatus_StaleBranchUsesLiveThreshold`).
- **mills(flexinfer): chat calls walk the aimodels fallback chain on model-not-found** (`pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`): escalation #230 — the research stage burned 3 attempts on `flexinfer chat: status 404: Model 'gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq' not found` and escalated, while `gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq-5930k`, the registry's FIRST declared fallback for the role, sat Ready on the cluster. `pkg/aimodels` has declared per-role fallback chains since 2026-05-23 (`ResolveWithFallbacks` was even documented as "reserved for future plumbing") but nothing in the Mills chat path ever walked them: `NewFlexInferClient` resolved only the primary, so any model drift (undeployed, renamed, mid-rollout) was a hard stage failure. Now: registry-resolved Judge/Weaver models carry their role's fallback chain (`JudgeModelFallbacks`/`WeaverModelFallbacks`; explicitly pinned models get NO automatic fallbacks — pinned means pinned), `chat` marks the proxy's model-not-found 404 with a sentinel, and `chatFallback` — used by the rubric judge, the research path, and the public `Chat` — walks the chain in order, logging each hop. Non-not-found failures return immediately (fallbacks fix drift, not outages); an exhausted chain returns an error naming every tried candidate, which classifies as code → escalate, the right outcome for real config drift. Tests: `flexinfer_test.go` (`TestChatFallback_WalksChainOnModelNotFound` — exact #230 error shape, `_AllCandidatesGone`, `_NonNotFoundErrorReturnsImmediately`, `_AdHocModelHasNoFallbacks`, `TestNewFlexInferClient_ResolvesFallbackChains`).
- **mills(pipeline): spawn-pool saturation now backs off on a slot-release timescale instead of burning the attempt cap in minutes** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`, `runner.go`): the HUD spawn API's 400 `"max concurrent spawns reached (N)"` is correctly classified `transient_quota` (free retry), but the shared `quotaBackoff` schedule is rate-limit-scaled — 32s cap, ~95s total across the 7 free retries of a run. A saturated pool frees a slot only when a running spawn *finishes* (10–30 min), so a run burned all 8 attempts and escalated while the pool was still busy; even with `escalation_auto_retry_cap: 2` the item human-escalated within ~6 minutes (escalations #220 pre-classification-fix, #235 post). New `saturationBackoff` (1m·2ⁿ⁻¹ capped at 5m, ~27 min across one run's free retries) selected by `retryBackoff` when the error matches the shared `spawnSaturationNeedle`; real 429/rate-limit errors keep the seconds schedule, non-quota classes still retry immediately. Drive deliberately blocks for the wait: an idling in-flight run is cheaper than an escalation and holds a `MaxConcurrentRuns` slot so the reconciler doesn't pile more runs onto the saturated pool. Also corrected the `RetryPolicy.EscalationAutoRetryCap` doc comment (code default is 0/disabled — `Default()` never set the claimed "default 2"; the deployed cluster policy opts in explicitly). Tests: `error_class_test.go` (`TestRetryBackoff_SpawnSaturationUsesSlotReleaseSchedule` — exact #235 error shape routed to the minutes schedule, `TestSaturationBackoff_SpansSlotRelease` — ≥20 min invariant across the free-retry budget, `TestIsSpawnSaturation`).
- **mills(audit): pipeline_merge audits now score the real merged diff instead of a metadata stub** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): the v2.0 `stubMergedDiffLoader` returned only run/item metadata (run id, backlog title, slices, MR iid, cost) — never a diff — so EVERY pipeline_merge audit scored survival 0.00 with a critical "diff unreadable" finding and auto-opened an advisory issue (#225/#227/#233/#234): a pure noise generator that buried real audit signal. New `GitLabClient.MRDiff` assembles the MR's unified diff from `GET /projects/:id/merge_requests/:iid/diffs` (re-adds per-file `diff --git`/`---`/`+++` headers, paginates, truncates at a prompt-safe cap — the artifact is substituted verbatim into the LLM rubric with no downstream truncation and the bulk auditors are ~26B quantized models). `gitlabMergedDiffLoader` wires it behind the existing trigger: metadata header for grounding + `## Unified diff` body to score; returns empty (trigger skips with a warn) when the run has no MR iid, GitLab returns no diff, or no GitLab client is configured — no audit beats a junk 0.00 audit. Tests: `gitlab_test.go` (`TestMRDiff_AssemblesUnifiedDiff`/`_TruncatesAtMaxBytes`/`_Paginates`/`_Errors`), `main_test.go` (`TestGitlabMergedDiffLoader` — real-diff artifact shape + all three skip paths).
- **mills(pipeline): the operator's cumulative branch-diff capture is wired up — gates now judge the true branch-vs-main diff instead of per-attempt spawn telemetry** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`): `clients.attachGitContext` computes `git diff <base>...origin/<branch>` after every spawn reaches terminal state, but it early-returns unless `WorkingDir`, `BaseBranch`, AND `Branch` are all non-empty — and in the deployed operator the implement/plan_slice/pr_self_review `SpawnWorker` routes set no `BaseBranch` while `Run.WorktreePath` is never populated on the standard (non-integrator) path (`NeedsWorktree` is an unread marker), so `DiffPatch`/`CommitMessages` were **always nil** and gates ran on per-attempt spawn telemetry only. This is the exact `attempt-1-errored-after-push` escalation shape (issue #224) that the `carryForwardDiff` fix (!876) cannot cover: an errored attempt records no successful StageOutput to carry forward. Fix: **(1)** the three spawn routes now set `BaseBranch: "main"` (matching the HUD spawn server's own default, so the spawn wire is unchanged) plus a new `SpawnWorker.RepoRoot` = `cfg.RepoRoot` — the operator-local clone `ensureRepoRoot` bootstraps at startup (the runtime image ships `git`; `Dockerfile.loom-mills-operator` installs it for exactly this class of use). `SpawnWorker.Run` prefers the run's own worktree and falls back to `RepoRoot`, and resolves an empty `BaseBranch` to `"main"` so an unwired field can no longer silently disable the capture. The hub-based `WorktreeAllocator` was deliberately NOT used here: it materializes worktrees in the agent-context pod's filesystem, which the operator cannot read in the split-pod topology. **(2)** `attachGitContext` now also fetches the base branch and diffs `origin/<base>...origin/<branch>` — the operator clone is fetched only at boot, so its local `main` goes days-stale between rolls and a stale base would fold every unrelated main commit into the captured diff. The capture is cumulative by construction (`origin/<branch>` holds everything ever pushed), so a retry that does nothing still surfaces attempt 1's pushed work to `post_implement_gate`. Tests: `spawn_test.go` (`TestRun_CumulativeDiffCoversErroredPushAttempt` — the #224 regression: attempt 1 fails after pushing, attempt 2 completes with empty telemetry, `nonempty_diff` passes on the captured cumulative diff; existing capture tests updated for the origin-qualified base), `dispatcher_test.go` (`TestSpawnWorker_GitCaptureFallbacks`).
- **mills(pipeline): a do-nothing gate-fail retry no longer erases the prior attempt's recorded work and escalates on `nonempty_diff`** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`): the implement-retry empty-diff wedge behind escalations #218/#221–#224/#226/#228/#231/#232 (2026-06-30 → 2026-07-01). Attempt 1 pushes real commits; a gate fails (`RetryFrom: implement`); the fresh-clone retry finds the branch already up to date, reports an empty per-attempt diff ("Everything up-to-date"), and its output *overwrote* `prior["implement"]` — so `nonempty_diff` failed every retry round and the run escalated with finished work sitting on the branch. Root cause: gates only ever see per-attempt spawn telemetry (what THAT agent session touched); the cumulative `git diff base...origin/<branch>` capture in `clients.attachGitContext` is skipped in the deployed operator because the implement `SpawnWorker` sets no `BaseBranch` and nothing populates `Run.WorktreePath` on the standard path. Fix: `carryForwardDiff` — when a retry reports no work of its own and the previous successful attempt did, the runner carries the previous attempt's diff evidence (`FilesChanged`, line counts, `DiffPatch`, `CommitMessages`) onto the retry output before gating (logged + `pipeline.stage.diff_carried_forward` event), and `loadPriorOutputs` applies the same preservation so a Drive resumed after an operator restart doesn't re-enter the wedge. A run whose attempts ALL report no work still escalates — the empty-MR guard is untouched. The real gate verdicts (scope, secret_scan, …) now re-judge the actual branch work, so a resolved transient failure proceeds to MR/merge and a persistent one escalates naming the REAL failing gate instead of the knock-on empty diff. Complements the retry-context prompt fix below (which tells the agent to redo work; this handles the agent correctly concluding there is nothing left to redo). Follow-up (not this MR): wire `BaseBranch` + an operator-readable worktree so `attachGitContext`'s cumulative capture actually runs, covering the attempt-1-errored-after-push case (#224's shape) that has no recorded prior output to carry. Tests: `runner_test.go` (`TestRunner_RetryEmptyDiffCarriesForwardPriorWork` — verified failing pre-fix with the exact live escalation reason, `TestRunner_AllAttemptsEmptyStillEscalates`, `TestLoadPriorOutputs_EmptyRetryRowKeepsPriorDiff`, `TestCarryForwardDiff`).
- **mills(pipeline): a gate-fail retry no longer spawns a blind agent that skips the work and masks the original failure** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `dispatcher.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): when a post-implement gate failed with `RetryFrom: implement`, the retry dispatched a FRESH spawn in a fresh clone with the *identical* prompt — no signal that it was a retry, which gate failed, or that attempt 1's commits were discarded. For a plan-linked item the prompt says the plan store is canonical (`agent_plan_get`), so the retry agent found the slice already claimed/advanced by the discarded attempt, rationally did nothing, and failed `nonempty_diff`; the run then burned all attempts and escalated with a reason that named only the gate stage — the original failure (e.g. `scope`) was invisible. Observed live 2026-07-01 on `PIPE-pattern-stamp-go-rest-service-{widget,gadget}-…` (original gate failure fixed separately by the intake slice hydration, !872). Three-part fix: **(1)** the runner now records a `StageRetryContext` per `RetryFrom` stage (first + latest failing gates with reasons, seeded from persisted `gate_outcomes` so it survives an operator restart) and threads it to the `WorkerDispatcher` via context onto `JobContext.RetryContext`; **(2)** `implementPromptFor` appends a RETRY CONTEXT block on retries: names the failed gate + reasons, states the prior attempt's commits were discarded, warns that the plan-slice status is STALE, and orders a full redo ending in a non-empty pushed diff; **(3)** the retry-cap escalation reason now carries the failing gates of both the latest round and the FIRST failure (`gate post_implement_gate failed (nonempty_diff: …); implement exceeded 3 attempts; first failure (gate post_implement_gate): scope: …`), so triage lands on the real defect instead of the knock-on empty diff. Reusing attempt 1's worktree/branch on gate-only failures was considered and deferred — it needs spawn-service changes and re-derives dirty state; the redo instruction addresses the observed wedge without it. Tests: `runner_test.go` (`TestRunner_GateFailEscalationCarriesFirstFailingGate`, `TestRunner_GateFailRetryThreadsRetryContextToDispatcher`, `TestRunner_ResumeRehydratesRetryContextFromGateOutcomes`), `main_test.go` (`TestImplementPromptForAddsRetryDiscipline`).
- **mills(intake): plan-linked backlog items now hydrate their slice scope from the plan store at enqueue** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_backlog.go`): the HUD-side slice materialization (!871) turned out insufficient — slice `files` are lost in transit across the MCP hub (TOON tabular encoding drops array columns, the same gotcha the plan-slice emitter works around via `GetSlice`), so the round-2 gadget stamp arrived with a named slice and an EMPTY file list, which the scope gate treats as everything-out-of-scope. `handleBacklogCreate` now hydrates: for an item with `PlanID` set and no slice files, it fetches the plan's slices (`ListSlices` + per-slice `GetSlice` for the files the list view omits), materializes `item.Slices`, and pre-declares protected-path touches for the `path_policy` gate — at the single choke point every REST enqueue passes through. Best-effort: a plan-store failure logs and leaves the item as-is (scope gate still fails closed, the correct under-specified signal). Tests: `TestHandleBacklog_CreateHydratesPlanSliceScope`, `TestHandleBacklog_CreateHydrationSkips`.
- **mills(pattern-loom): stamped backlog items escalated at the scope gate because the HUD projection omitted `Slices`** (`internal/hud/api_patterns.go`): the live 2026-07-01 widget kill-test run (`PIPE-pattern-stamp-go-rest-service-widget-1782942288`) implemented cleanly on attempt 1 (nonempty_diff + diff_size passed) but failed `scope` with "backlog item has no slices; no scope to enforce" — `buildStampBacklogItem` deliberately carried only `PlanID` ("canonical, agent resolves via agent_plan_get"), but the operator's scope gate reads `item.Slices` directly and fails closed on a slice-less item. Retries then produced empty diffs (the plan slice was already implemented) and the run escalated. The projection now materializes the stamp result's expanded slices (name + files) into `BacklogItem.Slices`, matching the convention every other intake path (council mutator, GitLab importer, plan-slice emitter) already follows. Test: `TestBuildStampBacklogItem_MaterializesSlices`.
- **mills(pattern-loom): a stamp enqueued against the operator's own repo would have clobbered the host's root `go.mod`/`Makefile`/`README.md`** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_pattern_stamp.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/schema_pattern.go`): pattern slice templates are written repo-root relative for a fresh, dedicated repo, but Mills executes stamps against its single configured repo (loom-core) — and the plan-slice protected-path pre-declaration would have waved the root-file writes through the gate. `pattern-go-rest-service` v0.2 adds an optional `target_dir` material; the stamp core sanitizes it (repo-relative only, rejects absolute and `..`-escaping paths), prefixes every expanded slice file, and grounds the slice goal so the implement agent stays inside the subtree. Empty `target_dir` keeps the fresh-repo behavior. Unblocks the live queued-proof kill-test for `plan-pattern-loom-mills`. Tests: `TestStamp_TargetDir_PrefixesFiles`, `TestStamp_TargetDir_NoPrefixWhenAbsent`, `TestStamp_TargetDir_RejectsEscapes`.
- **mills: a resumed run wedged active-but-idle forever, and a manual escalate did not survive an operator restart** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`): on operator startup the resume path re-drives a run stuck mid-stage by re-attaching to its persisted spawn. Two defects turned a reaped/hung spawn into a permanent wedge — observed live 2026-07-01 on `PIPE-psl-plan-council-enforce-autonomy-circuit-breaker-…-1782864313`, which logged `pipeline start skipped; run already active in this operator` every minute with no spawn pod, no progress, and no escalation. **(1)** `runStage` only converted a stalled spawn to an errored attempt on `ErrSpawnPollTimeout`; a resumed spawn whose poll returned a *non-timeout* error (a GC'd/reaped pod → `pod not found`/404) re-parked pending with the stall counter **unchanged**, so the reconciler re-attached the same dead spawn every tick forever — never re-dispatching a fresh spawn, never escalating. The counter now tracks consecutive *non-terminal poll failures* (timeout **or** error): a single failure still parks (resume-safe across a genuine restart-mid-poll), but past tolerance it converts, re-dispatches fresh, and ultimately escalates. **(2)** a still-running `Drive` goroutine (stale in-memory `run.State`) clobbered an out-of-band terminal row back to a non-terminal stage when its poll finally returned, so a manual `/escalate` was resurrected by the next restart's resume (`ListInFlight` re-activated it). New `runTerminatedExternally` re-reads the persisted head at the top of each stage and immediately after each dispatch; if an out-of-band actor moved the run to `done`/`escalated`/`paused`, `Drive` stops cleanly (`errRunTerminated`) instead of re-persisting its stale head. Tests: `runner_test.go` (`TestRunner_ReattachErrorEscalatesInsteadOfLoopingPending`, `TestRunner_OutOfBandEscalationSurvivesResumeDrive`, `TestRunner_DriveBailsWhenRunEscalatedOutOfBand`) and `reconciler_test.go` (`TestReconciler_DoesNotResumeOrReDriveTerminalRuns`).
- **mills(gates): two gate blind-spots that mis-judged legitimate spawn work** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/diff_size.go`) — surfaced by a verified pass over the 2026-06-30 gauntlet audit (16/23 items confirmed against live code, 6 already-fixed, 1 false positive): **(1) scope gate, glob patterns vs absolute spawn paths** — spawn stages report ABSOLUTE in-pod paths (`/workspace/services/loom-core/cmd/x.go`) while a slice's `files` globs are repo-relative (`cmd/*.go`); `filepath.Match` never crosses `/`, so a repo-relative glob could never match an absolute path and a legitimate change false-failed "outside slice scope" (the literal-path case already had an absolute-suffix fallback; globs did not). `matchGlobMaybeAbs` now matches a glob against each path-segment suffix of an absolute path, mirroring the literal fallback; an out-of-scope path still fails. **(2) diff_size gate failing OPEN on Codex telemetry** — the Codex spawn parser hardcodes `0/0` line counts (`internal/hud/spawn_codex_parser.go`), so an oversized Codex diff arrived with `total==0` and sailed past the 800-line blast-radius cap. `Evaluate` now recovers real counts from `DiffPatch` (populated by `attachGitContext`) when the telemetry is zero but a patch is present; a genuinely empty diff carries no patch and still passes, so the fallback only ever makes the gate stricter. Tests: `TestScope_GlobSlicePatternsMatchAbsoluteSpawnPaths`, `TestDiffSize_RecoversCountFromPatchWhenTelemetryZero`.
- **mills: plan-slice items escalated at the path_policy gate for protected paths the plan declared** (`pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/path_policy.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`) — verified item #4 from the 2026-06-30 gauntlet audit: a plan-slice item whose declared `files` touch a protected path (`**/*auth*.go`, `**/secret*.yaml`, …) hit the post-implement `path_policy` gate, which fails on any protected-path touch not pre-declared in `item.Policy.ProtectedPathsTouched` — a field nothing actually populated. The gate is in the post-implement set with `RetryFrom: implement`, so the item burned its retries re-running implement (same diff) and escalated. Two parts: **(1)** the `PlanSliceEmitter` now pre-declares the protected paths among a slice's *declared* files (`SetProtectedPathHitter` → `pm.Current().ProtectedPathsHit`), so a plan-declared protected touch is treated as intended — while an *undeclared* protected touch the implement stage introduces still fails the gate. **(2)** the audit's own suggested fix wouldn't have worked: `ProtectedPathsHit` returns matched *paths* and spawn stages report ABSOLUTE in-pod paths, but the gate subtracted pre-declarations by exact string match, so a repo-relative pre-declaration never matched an absolute hit; `protectedHitDeclared` now suffix-matches an absolute hit against a repo-relative declaration (mirrors the scope gate). Per an explicit policy decision, a plan-*declared* protected touch auto-proceeds (no human gate); unplanned protected touches still escalate. Tests: `TestSliceToBacklog_PreDeclaresProtectedTouches`, `TestPathPolicy_DeclaredTouchPassesForAbsoluteSpawnPaths`.
- **mills: a spawn that outlived a mobile-hud restart became an immortal spawn-pool slot-holder** (`internal/spawn/controller.go`): the spawn deadline is enforced by two mechanisms that both live *in the mobile-hud process* — the per-spawn liveness watchdog (`runLivenessWatcher`, 15-min stall) and the agent exec's `TimeoutSec` (= `Request.TimeoutMinutes`). When mobile-hud restarts, a spawn that was mid-flight is recovered from the spawn-state store as `running` with a live pod, but neither mechanism is re-attached (`resumePreRuntimeSpawns` only re-launches *pre-runtime* spawns that have no pod yet), and `Reconcile`'s phase update keeps a `running` spawn alive for as long as its pod exists. So the spawn ran forever, holding one of the (then 3) `SPAWN_MAX_CONCURRENT` slots and 4 GiB with no watcher and no deadline. Observed live 2026-06-30: a codex `implement` spawn for a real plan-slice item (`psl-…-circuit-breaker-…-1`) ran **79 min** — 19 min past its 60-min timeout — after a mobile-hud restart 6 min into its life, with zero stdout. Fix: a restart-durable deadline backstop in `Reconcile` — a non-terminal spawn whose `StartedAt + TimeoutMinutes + 5-min grace` has passed is marked failed and queued for terminal cleanup (which deletes the pod), independent of the in-process watchers. Skips spawns with no usable bound (zero `StartedAt`/`TimeoutMinutes`, e.g. a pod discovered only from labels) so in-flight work within its deadline is never touched. Tests: `reconciler_test.go` (`TestReconcileReapsDeadlineExceededSpawn`, `TestReconcileKeepsHealthyRunningSpawn`).
- **mills: the plan-slice emitter emitted scope-less items because the slice LIST view omits `files`** (`pkg/mills/clients/plan_reader.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`): with the council demand path unblocked, the *other* demand source — the plan-slice emitter (S2) — was found escalating every item at `post_implement_gate/scope` with *"backlog item has no slices; no scope to enforce"*, even though `implement` produced a real diff (live: `psl-…-2d158d-5` S4, escalated after 3 successful implement attempts). Root cause: the emitter reads `agent_plan_slice_list`, whose token-efficient TOON **tabular** form omits array columns — so `PlanSliceSummary.Files` was *always* empty and `sliceToBacklog` left `Slices` nil → the scope gate fail-closed. The Plan Store slice model **does** carry `files` (confirmed via `agent_plan_slice_get`, which returns e.g. `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/crossrepo/registry.go`); only the list projection drops it. Fix: new `PlanClient.GetSlice` (`agent_plan_slice_get`) and the emitter now recovers a ready slice's declared files from the **detail** view when the list omits them (best-effort — on error or a genuinely file-less slice it falls through and the gate still fail-closes, the correct under-specified signal). Pairs with the scope-gate docs carve-out below so a plan-slice item carries a real scope **and** the mandated CHANGELOG passes. Tests: `plan_slice_emitter_test.go` (`RecoversFilesFromSliceDetail`, `DetailAlsoFilelessStaysScopeless`).
- **mills: a saturated spawn pool escalated legitimate items instantly instead of waiting for a slot** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`): the HUD spawn API caps concurrent spawns and rejects over-limit requests with `400 {"code":"spawn_error","message":"max concurrent spawns reached (N)"}`. The error classifier matched no transient pattern for this, so it fell through to `ClassCode` (a real-code defect) and the run **escalated immediately** — `cost $0`, zero stage progress, on the very first `plan_slice` attempt. So whenever Mills' own spawn pool was briefly busy (overlapping council + canary + manual runs — the steady state on a working loop), a perfectly good item was killed. Observed live 2026-06-30 (`MILLS-VERIFY-CODE-…2002` escalated in 24 ms with `class=code` while the pool sat at 3/3; the doc verify item had merged through the same path hours earlier when the pool was free). This is a robustness defect a static gauntlet audit can't see — it only surfaces under live concurrency. Fix: classify `"concurrent spawns"` as **`ClassTransientQuota`**, so the run backs off (`quotaBackoff`, capped 32 s) and retries when a spawn finishes and frees a slot, rather than escalating. A multi-minute spawn releasing its slot is well within the retry window. Test: `TestClassify_KillTestFixtures/spawn_pool_saturated`.
- **mills: the scope gate escalated legitimate items for the toolchain-managed go.sum/go.mod the build writes** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`): a proactive audit of the whole pipeline gauntlet (`.loom/mills-gauntlet-audit-2026-06-30.json`) found that *any* legitimate item adding or removing a Go import escalates at the scope gate. The implement spawn's `go build`/`go test` runs `go mod tidy`, which rewrites `go.sum`/`go.mod` — files a council/plan slice's `files` list never declares — so the gate flags them as *"outside slice scope"* even though the in-scope source change is correct (the verify item that proved the pipeline was a doc-only change, so it never hit this; almost any real feature would). Fix: an always-on **auto-managed-dependency carve-out** (`isAutoManagedDepFile`) treats package-manager manifests/lockfiles (`go.mod`, `go.sum`, `go.work`, `package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `Cargo.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `uv.lock`, …) as always in scope, keyed by basename so the absolute spawn paths the gate sees match too. Mirrors the existing canary-fixture / test-file / docs-guardrail carve-outs; an out-of-scope *source* file still fails. Tests: `TestScope_AutoManagedDepFilesAlwaysAllowed`, `TestScope_DepCarveOutDoesNotMaskCodeViolation`.
- **daemon: stateful upstream servers (agent_context, codebase_memory) are no longer idle-reaped, and legitimately slow long-poll calls no longer tear down a server** (`internal/daemon/daemon_loops.go`, `internal/daemon/config.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_errors.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline_stages.go`, `internal/daemon/callpipeline.go`): two daemon teardown paths killed server *processes* with no awareness they hold working state, causing the observed `agent_context` recv-churn and `gitlab` teardowns. **(1) Idle reaper:** `reapIdleServers` terminated any upstream idle >5 min (call-activity only), so `agent_context` — whose entire job is continuous background reconciliation (session reaper, presence cleanup, compaction) — and `codebase_memory` (file watches) were reaped every idle cycle, forcing reload/respawn churn. A new `resources.resident_servers` set (default `agent_context` + `codebase_memory`; override via config or `LOOM_RESIDENT_SERVERS`, `none` to disable) is now exempt from idle-reaping; genuine wedges are still recovered by the health monitor. **(2) Recv-timeout breaker:** a call that opted into an extended timeout budget (`_timeout` / `timeout_seconds`, or a per-server config override) that then times out on recv is now kept alive and **not** counted toward the 3-strike transport-teardown breaker — a slow tool (e.g. `gitlab poll_pipeline` on a long CI run) is not a wedged transport, and a genuinely wedged transport is still caught by ordinary default-budget calls. Complements the durable-watch fix below (Track 1 of the loom-MCP stability work; the hub-transport residual is tracked separately). Tests: `resident_servers_test.go` (`TestGetResidentServers`, `TestShouldReapIdleServer`) and `callpipeline_recv_breaker_test.go` (`TestTransportFailure_ExtendedBudgetSkipsBreaker` — 5 extended-budget recv timeouts never tear down the server nor touch the streak).
- **codebase: watches were silently killed on MCP reconnect / idle reap and never recovered; they now persist and resume across process restarts** (`pkg/codebase/watch.go`, `pkg/codebase/watch_store.go`, `pkg/codebase/service.go`, `pkg/codebase/config.go`, `cmd/mcp-codebase-memory/main.go`): a `codebase_watch_start` runs as a detached goroutine *inside* the shared `mcp-codebase-memory` upstream child process. The daemon's idle reaper (`internal/daemon/daemon_loops.go`) terminates any upstream whose `LastActivity` exceeds the idle timeout (**default 5 min**), and `LastActivity` is refreshed **only by inbound tool calls** (`procMgr.MarkActivity`, called solely in the call pipeline) — a running watcher generates no calls between polls. So ~5 min after `watch_start`, with no intervening `codebase_*` call, the reaper killed the child (and the watcher); the next `codebase_watch_poll` re-spawned a fresh child with an empty in-memory `watchJobs` map and returned `found:false`. The same loss happened on any transport-storm respawn or crash. This is the "watcher was killed; loom MCP reconnecting" failure. Fix: running watches are now persisted as durable descriptors (one atomic-write JSON file per watch under `~/.loom/codebase/watches`, override `CODEBASE_WATCH_STATE_DIR`) capturing everything needed to relaunch — the **absolute** root (the respawned process may have a different cwd), resolved languages, debounce, git-metadata/embeddings flags. `Service.ResumeWatches` runs at server startup (before the first request is served) and re-launches every persisted watch under its original `watch_id`, so a poll after a reap/respawn transparently re-attaches instead of returning not-found. `HandleWatchStop` removes the descriptor first (authoritative cleanup — a stopped watch never resumes, even if no longer in memory). Watch goroutines now parent on a service-lifetime context (`baseCtx`) rather than the per-request handler context that dies on every reconnect. Path-traversal-safe watch ids; persistence failures are non-fatal and logged (`slog`). Tests: `watch_persist_test.go` (store round-trip + remove idempotency, disabled/no-op store, unsafe-id rejection, **`TestResumeWatchesReattachesPersistedWatch`** — a watch persisted by one process is re-launched by a fresh process over the same state dir, and **`TestWatchStopRemovesPersistedDescriptor`** — stop prevents resume).
- **mills: the scope gate escalated council MRs for adding the CHANGELOG entry the docs guardrail requires (gate deadlock)** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`): the docs-guardrail fix below got the implement spawn to add a `CHANGELOG.md` entry, and the live 06:00 council re-run of `MILLS-2026-06-30-001` on the fully-fixed operator **proved the grounding + docs fixes work** — slices targeted *real* packages (`pkg/mills/gates/`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/`, `internal/hud/...`, `docs/`), implement added the CHANGELOG and passed `check_docs_guardrails.sh` locally. But it still escalated — now at `post_implement_gate/scope`: *"1 file(s) outside slice scope: CHANGELOG.md"*. The council slice `files` lists never include the CHANGELOG entry, so the `scope` gate (which fails any changed file outside the slice envelope) rejected exactly the file the downstream `ci_watch` docs guardrail **mandates** — a deadlock the docs fix introduced. Fix: a docs-guardrail carve-out in the scope gate (`isDocsGuardrailFile`, always-on, mirroring the existing canary-fixture and test-file carve-outs) treats the guardrail's allowlist — root `README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md`/`ROADMAP.md`/`AGENTS.md` or anything under `docs/` — as always in scope, matching `scripts/ci/check_docs_guardrails.sh`'s `docs_pattern` and handling the absolute spawn-style paths the gate sees. A genuinely out-of-scope *code* file still fails (the carve-out is docs-only). Tests: `TestScope_DocsGuardrailFilesAlwaysAllowed`, `TestScope_DocsCarveOutDoesNotMaskCodeViolation`.
- **mills: real council work never merged because the implement spawn produced doc-less MRs that failed the docs guardrail** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): tracing the escalation of `MILLS-2026-06-30-001` showed it ran the *full* pipeline — `plan_slice` → `implement` (non-empty diff, gates passed) → opened MR !847 — then escalated at `ci_watch` because its CI (pipeline 16005) failed on a **single** job: `guardrails:docs-cli` (*"code-facing changes detected without doc updates … expected at least one change in README.md, CHANGELOG.md, ROADMAP.md, AGENTS.md, or docs/"*). (The HUD's "0 runs" was a red herring — its detail panel queries the active-only runs endpoint and hides terminal/escalated runs.) Root cause: the `implement` stage prompt (`stagePromptFor`) asks the agent for "code + tests" and is **silent on the repo's docs guardrail**, so every code-changing run shipped a docs-less diff that fails CI — which is precisely why only doc-only heartbeat **canaries** merged (they touch `testdata/.../heartbeat.md`, never code) while every real council MR escalated. Fix: new `implementDocsDiscipline` + `implementPromptFor()` wrapper (mirrors `researchPromptFor`/`researchPathDiscipline`) appends a CI-docs-guardrail instruction to the implement prompt — add a `## [Unreleased]` CHANGELOG entry as part of the diff, do not bypass with `[skip-docs-check]` — and `routes["implement"]` now uses it. Complements the council-editor grounding + output-guard fixes below (the editor no longer invents `pkg/planning/`/`pkg/pipeline/` paths the implement agent was materializing as new packages). Test: `TestImplementPromptForAddsDocsGuardrailDiscipline`.
- **mills: council slice file paths now validated against the real repo (output guard, symmetric with the research stage)** (`pkg/mills/council/slice_grounding.go`, `pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go`, `pkg/mills/metrics.go`): the next fix below grounded the council editor **prompt** in the real package layout so gpt-5.4 stops inventing fictional slice paths (`pkg/planning/`, `pkg/pipeline/` — loom-core code lives under `pkg/mills/...`), but nothing validated the editor **output**: a slice whose `files` all referenced a non-existent directory still flowed to `implement` and escalated on an empty diff. This adds a defense-in-depth output guard mirroring the research stage's `SanitizeResearchNotes`. `SanitizeProposalSlices` validates each proposal's `PlanSlices` `files` against the operator's `cfg.RepoRoot` checkout via an injected directory-existence predicate (`repoDirChecker`, which rejects `..` escapes and requires a real directory — new files are expected, so the file's *parent directory*, not the file, must exist). A **real** slice (all dirs exist) is kept; a **mixed** slice (some real, some fictional) is kept but **flagged**; a **wholly-fictional** slice (no file's dir exists) is **dropped**; a proposal left slice-less by the drop is **dropped entirely** (a slice-less item is exactly what trips the scope gate and escalates). `BacklogMutator.Apply` runs the guard before persistence using the `RepoRoot` already threaded through `MutationOptions` (no `main.go` change — the runner already passes `RepoRoot: r.RepoRoot`), **fail-open** gated on `repoRootIsDir` so an unset/missing root leaves the guard inert rather than dropping legitimate work (a checker over an absent root would read *every* slice as fictional). New Prometheus counters mirror the research guard: `mills_council_slices_guard_total{action="dropped"|"flagged"}` + `mills_council_slice_paths_dropped_total`, and `MutationResult` surfaces `SlicesDropped`/`SlicesFlagged`/`FictionalProposalsDropped` in its `Summary()`. Tests: `slice_grounding_test.go` (all-fictional dropped, real kept, mixed flagged, within-proposal drop+keep, no-`PlanSlices` passthrough, nil predicate, empty input, `repoDirChecker`/`repoRootIsDir` helpers, and `Apply` wiring with Prometheus counter-delta assertions including the empty-`RepoRoot` no-op).
- **mills: the council editor invented fictional file paths, escalating every council item** (`pkg/mills/clients/council.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council_openai.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): live backlog (2026-06-30) showed every council-created item (`MILLS-2026-06-{28,29,30}-001`) `escalated` while every trivial heartbeat canary `merged` — so the autonomous-merge north-star was entirely canaries and **zero real council work ever landed**. The newest council item carried slices (the slice-drop fix landed) but scoped them to **files that do not exist**: `pkg/planning/workspace_signals.go`, `pkg/pipeline/preflight.go`, `pkg/policy/guardrails.go` — `pkg/planning/` and `pkg/pipeline/` are not loom-core packages (its code lives under `pkg/mills/...`). Root cause: `buildCouncilEditorPrompt` instructs the model to emit each slice's `files` with **zero repo grounding**, so gpt-5.4 invents a plausible clean architecture; the implement worker then can't act on the fictional paths → empty diff → retries → escalation (burning ~$1–2.50/run). The earlier research-grounding fix grounded only the **research** stage, one stage downstream of where the paths are born. Fix (symmetric prompt grounding): thread the operator's `cfg.RepoRoot` checkout into `buildCouncilEditor` → both the FlexInfer and OpenAI (gpt-5.4, prod) editors → a new `## Repository layout — ground EVERY file path in this` prompt section listing the real package directories, plus an explicit "do NOT invent directories absent from this tree … OMIT the proposals block rather than emit a fictional path" instruction. The listing comes from the new `repoPackageLayout` (top-level dirs + two levels beneath `pkg/`/`internal/`/`cmd/`) **instead of** the research stage's `RepoTreeDigest`: a whole-tree sort+truncate drops `pkg/` entirely on loom-core (it first sorts at entry ~1100 of ~1700, past any sane cap), so the digest is rooted at the code dirs to guarantee the real packages show (verified: 198 entries, full `pkg/mills/*` list present, no truncation). Empty `RepoRoot` ⇒ no layout section (back-compat; grounding inert, never panics). Tests: `council_grounding_test.go` (prompt includes/omits the layout section; `repoPackageLayout` surfaces real packages, omits invented ones, survives an alphabetically-early `apps/` subtree that would bury `pkg/` in a flat digest, bounds depth, and no-ops on a missing root).
- **mills: the `implement` spawn on the harvester-vm substrate couldn't `go build`/`go test` (`go: not found`)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): the explicit out-of-scope follow-up from the next fix below. !843 gave the implement spawn a working Go build env on the **k8s** backend, whose spawn-runtime image is golang-based (`agentRuntimeDockerfile`). The **harvester-vm** substrate has a no-op `Build` against one stock Ubuntu cloud image and relies on `agentCLIInstallShell` (run over SSH at Start) for tooling — but that snippet installed only **node/npm**, so a Go spawn on harvester-vm still hit `go: not found` and shipped unverified Go changes. Fix: prepend a guarded Go install (`ensureGo`) to each known agent's snippet — it downloads the official linux tarball to `/usr/local/go` and symlinks `go`/`gofmt` into `/usr/local/bin` (on the agent's default PATH for later execs). `curl` is bootstrapped the same way `ensureNPM` bootstraps npm, with the same `DPkg::Lock::Timeout=300` wait against cloud-init's racing first-boot apt; arch comes from `dpkg --print-architecture` (amd64/arm64 map directly to Go's tokens), and the `command -v go` guard keeps it a fast no-op on a curated image or reused VM. The pinned version is a new `goVersion` const (`1.25.11`) matching `go.mod` + `.gitlab-ci.yml` `GO_VERSION`, so a spawned `go build`/`go test` never triggers a `GOTOOLCHAIN` auto-download; the const is wired into both the install snippet **and** `agentRuntimeDockerfile`, which had drifted one patch behind at `golang:1.25.10-alpine`. Tests: `TestAgentCLIInstallShell_InstallsGoToolchain` (each known agent installs pinned Go + wires it onto PATH); `TestGenerateDockerfile_UsesLeanAgentRuntime` now tracks `goVersion`.
- **mills: the `implement` spawn couldn't `go build`/`go test` loom-core, so it shipped unverified code** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/k8s_objects.go`): the Mills `implement` spawn (HUD spawn API, `agent_type=codex`) runs in **git-clone mode** — a single-repo emptyDir clone with **no `../../libs` siblings** and **no Go env**. loom-core's committed `go.work` `use`s three **private** `gitlab.flexinfer.ai/libs/*` modules, so the spawned agent could never build/test its own changes (run `PIPE-MILLS-2026-06-29-001-1782734575`: three implement attempts, three different toolchain excuses — no private-module auth, missing sibling modules, broken go.work overlay). Fix replicates the canonical single-repo build recipe already proven by `Dockerfile` + `.gitlab-ci.yml` ("authenticate direct Git module fetches … without recloning sibling repositories"): `buildSpawnPodEnv` adds `GOWORK=off`, `GOFLAGS=-buildvcs=false`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, and `GOPRIVATE`/`GONOSUMDB`/`GONOPROXY=<host>/*` via the new `spawnGoModuleEnv()` (host from `resolveSpawnGitPrivateHost()` — `SPAWN_GIT_PRIVATE_HOST`, default `gitlab.flexinfer.ai`, empty disables); `injectAgentConfig` configures `git config --global url."https://token:${GIT_TOKEN}@<host>/".insteadOf …` (guarded on `$GIT_TOKEN`, so a no-op on NFS-mode devbox / harvester-vm); and the k8s backend `buildPodSpec` now exposes `GIT_TOKEN` on the **runtime** container (not just the git-clone init container) when `gitEnabled()`. The token is already baked into the clone's remote URL — loom-core is hosted on the **same** `gitlab.flexinfer.ai` as its private libs, so the clone token authorizes the module fetches. `CGO_ENABLED=0` mirrors CI (the lean agent image has no fi-accel C headers, so the pure-Go fallback is used and `GOWORK=off` does not hit the cgo wall). The harvester-vm "`go` not on PATH" symptom is a separate gap (that substrate installs only node/npm) — out of scope; the k8s base image is `golang:alpine`. Tests: `spawnGoModuleEnv`/`resolveSpawnGitPrivateHost`, the `buildSpawnPodEnv` Go-env case, the `injectAgentConfig` `insteadOf` command, and `GIT_TOKEN` present on the runtime container in git-clone mode / absent in NFS mode.
- **proxy: the Mills Pattern Loom was invisible to the LLM-core tool profile** (`cmd/loom/proxy_tool_filter.go`): the autonomous Mills council (codex/gemini), squad agents, and the stamp path run under the proxy's capped LLM-core profile, but `agent_pattern_*` and `agent_engram_*` were **absent from the cross-vendor allow-list** (`requiredPatterns`) — so A1 rails couldn't cite an approved pattern, B1/stamp couldn't fire, and A2's green-stamp engram hook was unreachable. The Pattern Loom was invisible to the very agents that run the factory. Fix: add the pattern (`list`/`get`/`search`/`add`/`stamp`/`promote`/`record_instance`) + engram (`recall`/`list`/`verify`) tools at the **tail** of `requiredPatterns` — the LLM-core profile (limit 140) has headroom so codex/gemini/claude get them, while the tighter **antigravity** ceiling (hard 100-tool cap) truncates before reaching them, leaving its tool set unchanged (antigravity isn't a Mills vendor). Mirrors the plan-store cross-vendor rationale. Test: `proxy_tool_filter_test.go` asserts the new tools survive the codex profile.
- **mills: council-decomposed slices were dropped before the scope gate, escalating every council item** (`pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_reader.go`): every council-created backlog item escalated at `implement` 2026-06-28/29 (`MILLS-2026-06-28-001/002`, `-29-001`) — burning ~$1–2.50/run with zero MRs — while every sliced canary merged. Root cause: `parseCouncilProposals` only ever fills `BacklogProposal.PlanSlices`, but `persistOne` copied the always-empty `Slices` field onto the item; with the S2 plan lane off in prod (`plan_slice_emitter.namespace` empty) every decomposed proposal took the flat-item path and landed with `Slices: null`. A slice-less non-canary item trips the `scope` gate (*"backlog item has no slices; no scope to enforce"*) on every implement attempt → retries in fresh worktrees until it escalates with an empty diff. **Two fixes** so council-decomposed work always carries scope on both intake paths: (1) `persistOne` resolves item slices via the new `proposalItemSlices` helper — prefer explicit `Slices`, else convert `PlanSlices` (name + files; goal dropped, `store.Slice` has none); (2) the S2 plan-slice emitter's `sliceToBacklog` now stamps a single-slice scope `{name, files}` onto each emitted item (and `PlanSliceSummary` decodes the `files` field `agent_plan_slice_list` already returns) — without this, enabling the S2 lane would bypass fix (1) and re-introduce the escalation via the emitter path. An empty file set leaves `Slices` nil so the gate still fail-closes on an under-specified slice. Tests: `backlog_mutator_sliced_test.go` (`TestApply_NoNamespace_FlatItemCarriesPlanSlices`, `TestProposalItemSlices`), `plan_slice_emitter_test.go` (`TestPlanSliceEmitter_EmittedItemCarriesSliceScope`, `TestSliceToBacklog_NoFilesLeavesScopeless`).
- **mills: research stage hallucinated a non-existent codebase, poisoning the implement worker** (`pkg/mills/clients/research_grounding.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/flexinfer.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): in pipeline run `PIPE-MILLS-2026-06-29-001` (backlog `MILLS-2026-06-29-001`, a Go repo) the FlexInfer research stage (model `gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq`, `research_mode=off` → legacy single-prompt chat) returned notes referencing **Python files that do not exist** — `mills/core/council/orchestrator.py`, `mills/core/agent/execution_engine.py`, `mills/telemetry/metrics_collector.py`, `mills/state/transition_manager.py` (loom-core is Go: `pkg/mills/...`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/...`). Those fabricated paths flowed into the downstream implement worker's context as `research_notes`.
