Root cause: `stagePromptFor("research")` asks the model to "summarize relevant code paths" with **zero codebase grounding**, so it invents plausible ones; `research_mode=off` does **not** disable research — it only selects the ungrounded legacy chat. Two defenses, both anchored on the operator's `cfg.RepoRoot` checkout (present at research time, before any worktree is allocated): (1) **prompt grounding** — `researchPromptFor` prepends the real top-of-tree layout (`RepoTreeDigest`, depth 2, ≤80 entries, dotfile/noise-pruned) plus an explicit "do NOT invent file paths" instruction; (2) **output validation** — `WeaverClient.groundNotes` (runs in every mode) checks each path the model cites against the run's worktree (`LOOM_MILLS_WORKTREE`) or `RepoRoot` via `SanitizeResearchNotes`: a wholesale hallucination (≥2 referenced paths, ≥50% absent — the observed case) **withholds** the notes entirely; a partial one keeps them with an "⚠ Unverified paths" footer; dropped paths are logged, recorded in `Citation["paths_dropped"]`, and counted in new Prometheus metrics `mills_research_notes_guard_total{action="withheld"|"flagged"}` + `mills_research_paths_dropped_total` so the guard's firing is observable in prod rather than silently green. Degrades safely (no RepoRoot ⇒ guard inert, path-discipline instruction still ships; worktree path preferred over the stale checkout when present). Tests: `research_grounding_test.go` (extractor, sanitize wholesale/partial/single-stray/all-real, repo-path checker, tree digest depth/truncation) and `flexinfer_test.go` (`TestWeaverClient_Research_{WithholdsHallucinatedPaths,KeepsRealPaths,NoRepoRootSkipsGuard,WorktreeEnvOverridesRepoRoot,IncrementsGuardMetric}`).
- **mobile + mills: iOS Mills/Weaver screens showed "Couldn't reach Mills" because they decoded the wrong wire format** (`apps/loom-companion-ios/Sources/LoomCompanionKit/{Networking/APIClient.swift,Mills/MillsAPI.swift,Weaver/WeaverAPI.swift}`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`): the `/api/mills/*`, `/api/weaver/*`, and `/api/aimodels/*` HUD endpoints return **bare operator JSON** (reverse-proxy passthrough / `App.writeJSON`), not the `/api/mobile/v1/*` `APIEnvelope<{ok,data,meta}>`. The mobile client routed them through `request(_:)`, which forces envelope decoding (and a default epoch-number date strategy that also chokes on Go's RFC3339 timestamps), so every Mills/Weaver response failed to decode. The old `MillsScreen` masked this with `(try? …) ?? []` — it had never actually decoded live Mills data; the screen rebuild that surfaces errors exposed the latent bug. Fix: new `APIClient.requestRaw`/`decodeRaw` decode bare bodies with an ISO-8601 (`LoomFormat.date`) strategy and the shared `mapHTTPError` for non-2xx; `MillsAPI` + `WeaverAPI` use it. `pipelineRuns()` decodes `[MillsPipelineRun]?` so an empty-set `null` body coalesces to `[]`, and `notFound`/`notConfigured` stay swallowed to a calm empty state (genuine network/auth/upstream errors still surface). Separately, the operator's `handlePipelineRunsList` active branch encoded an empty set as JSON `null` (its `mr_iid`/terminal siblings already guarded this) — it now returns `[]`. This pairs with the iOS Mills screen rework (Mills promoted to a first-class tab; north-star + canonical KPI grid reading the keys the operator actually publishes). Tests: `MillsRawDecodeTests` (bare array, `null` body, bare KPI object, RFC3339 dates, 404/503 mapping), `MillsAPI resilience` (swallow vs. propagate), and `TestHandlePipelineRuns_EmptyIsJSONArrayNotNull`.
- **agent-context: a mis-scoped plan's `project`/`namespace` couldn't be corrected without a destructive re-create** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_plans.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-context/tools_plan.go`, `pkg/projectmeta/projectmeta.go`): `PlanSvc.Update` patched `title`/`spec_doc`/refs but had **no branch for `project` or `namespace`**, so a plan minted with a bare project id (`loom-core` instead of canonical `services/loom-core`) could only be fixed by a re-`Create` — which upserts by id and is therefore lossy. The bad project string then rendered as its own ungrouped card in the web HUD, which groups by the *exact* project string. Fix: (1) `PlanSvc.Update` now patches `project`/`namespace` in place; (2) the `agent_plan_update` MCP tool exposes both params (the live `mcp-agent-context` daemon needs a rebuild+restart to surface them); (3) new `projectmeta.LooksLikeBareRepo` drives a **non-fatal** warning on `Create`+`Update` when a project id lacks a path segment, so the fragmentation risk is surfaced without breaking existing callers. Tests: `svc_plans_test.go` (`TestPlan_UpdateProjectNamespace` — re-scope persists, lists under the canonical project not the stale bare one, omitted fields untouched) + `projectmeta_test.go` (`TestLooksLikeBareRepo`).
- **hooks: `with-stashed-worktree.sh` race leaked stashes and corrupted rendered files across concurrent worktrees** (`scripts/hooks/with-stashed-worktree.sh`, `scripts/hooks/with-stashed-worktree_test.sh`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Makefile`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`): the pre-commit/pre-push stash wrapper used a fixed stash message (`loom-hook-temporary`) plus a bare `git stash pop` (implicit `stash@{0}`). Because `refs/stash` is **shared across every linked worktree of a repo**, concurrent agents committing in sibling `.claude/worktrees/*` would pop **each other's** stashes — leaking **69** `loom-hook-temporary` stashes into the shared object store, cross-contaminating WIP, and injecting committed merge-conflict markers into a rendered `.loom` plan (cleaned in !832). The !586 design for this never actually landed on `main`. Fix: the wrapper now **never touches `refs/stash`** — it captures the dirty state as a *dangling* commit via `git stash create` (no ref), discards only the changed tracked files (`git diff --name-only` → `git checkout-index`, avoiding whole-tree mtime churn), moves untracked files aside, then restores by applying that specific SHA. `restore()` runs `set +e` and `return 0` so the EXIT trap can't corrupt the wrapper's exit status, and no git output is piped to an early-exiting consumer (the `pipefail` SIGPIPE trap). The existing concurrency integration test is now hermetic (unsets inherited `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`) and wired into CI as a `test:hooks` job, a `make test-hooks` target, and a files-gated pre-commit hook so it can't silently regress.
- **mills: gpt-5.4 council editor timed out (90s default) + had no run-time fallback** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council_openai.go`): the first scheduled council run fully on the new OpenAI gpt-5.4 editor (2026-06-28T12:00) failed with `responses request failed: context deadline exceeded (Client.Timeout exceeded while awaiting headers)`. gpt-5.4 is a reasoning model and a full council synthesis (3 docs + a `## Backlog Proposals` decomposition, with reviewer notes) exceeds the `openairesponses` `DefaultRequestTimeout` of **90s** — and `buildCouncilEditor` constructed the API client without overriding it. Worse, the OpenAI editor was wired with **no run-time fallback**, so a single timeout *hard-failed the whole council run* (the construction-time flexinfer fallback only covers a missing key). Fix: (1) `buildCouncilEditor` now sets a **5-minute** `APIClientConfig.Timeout` (`openAICouncilEditorTimeout`) — ample headroom over the observed >90s; (2) new **`FallbackCouncilEditor`** wraps the OpenAI editor + the local flexinfer editor and, on any error OR empty output, falls back to flexinfer *for that run* — so a transient OpenAI timeout / 429 / 5xx degrades to the local editor instead of failing the council. Tests: `council_openai_test.go` (`Fallback…` primary-error-falls-back, primary-empty-falls-back, primary-success-no-fallback).
- **mills: audit reviewer pool was hardcoded + drifted off the FlexInfer cluster (404 on every run)** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `platform/gitops k3s/mills/configmap-policy.yaml`): the adversarial audit council recorded `survival=0.65 escalated=true` on every artifact while logging three `404 model_not_found` reviewer failures — it was running blind. Root cause: `buildAuditSubsystem` **hardcoded** the reviewer pool, so `policy.yaml`'s `audit.pool_default` / `audit.pool_escalation` were parsed into the struct but had **zero consumers** (the configmap audit section was dead config). The hardcoded models had drifted off the cluster (verified via proxy `/v1/models`, 2026-06-27): `gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq-5930k` (bulk[1]) has no serving pod, and `claude-opus-4-7` / `codex-gpt5` (escalation) are spawn-driver CLIs, not flexinfer-served — all 404. The lone working bulk reviewer scored 0.65, landing in the escalation band `(0.40,0.70)`, so every audit escalated into the dead frontier pool → `survival = max(0.65, 0) = 0.65, escalated=true`. Fix: `buildAuditSubsystem` now builds the pool from `policy.Audit` (new `auditPoolPolicy` / `auditPoolMembers` helpers), **closing the long-standing `// v2.1 will load the pool from policy.yaml` TODO** — model drift is now a configmap edit + pod roll, not a recompile. The corrected built-in fallback (when `pool_default` is omitted) is the two FlexInfer chat models verified Ready — `gemma4-26b-a4b-gptq` + `qwen36-35b-mtp-uncensored-5930k` — with **no escalation pool** (flexinfer serves no stronger model; the spawn frontier isn't registered in the dispatcher's reviewer map, so escalating would 404 or silently skip). The matching gitops MR reconciles `audit.pool_default` to the same two Ready models and empties `pool_escalation`; the operator image auto-rolls via Flux IUA and reads the reconciled configmap at boot. Tests: `cmd/loom-mills-operator/audit_pool_test.go` (pool-from-configmap, empty→fallback, drops half-specified rows).
- **agentcontext: best-effort embed on the session-summary write path** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_context_summary.go`): `GenerateSummary` returned the embedder error straight to the caller, so a provider outage (Morph HTTP 429 "service overloaded" / flexinfer HTTP 503 "model parked behind a higher-priority GPU primary" / circuit breaker open) hard-failed `agent_context_summarize`. It was the last remaining hard-fail embed path after the `!725`/`!803`/`!804` resilience work — every other write path (context_add, task_add, annotations, plans) already persists with a deterministic fallback vector and search/recall degrade to keyword. The summary embed now routes through the shared `embedQueryBestEffort` helper, so a failed embedder logs + increments `EmbeddingErrors` but still persists the summary with a correctly-sized fallback vector. Regression test: `TestGenerateSummary_EmbedFailureStillPersists`.
- **build: stop committing the HUD frontend bundle; build it in CI/Docker** (`internal/hud/app.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/.gitkeep`, `.gitignore`, `Dockerfile`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Makefile`): the content-hashed vite bundle under `internal/hud/frontend/dist/` was tracked in git, so every concurrent HUD MR rebuilt it and conflicted — `index.html` rewrites the hashed entry-chunk reference on every build, and two frontend MRs can never 3-way-merge a minified bundle regardless of filename scheme. The bundle is now gitignored (only `dist/.gitkeep` is tracked) and built where it is consumed: a new `build:frontend` CI job produces it as an artifact for the Go build jobs (`build:binaries` + the tag-only cross-platform release builds, which now fail loudly if the artifact is missing rather than embedding a stub), and the Docker image builds it in a `node:20-alpine` frontend stage. The embed is now `//go:embed all:frontend/dist` so a plain `go build`/`go test` on a fresh checkout still compiles (serving a stub HUD until vite runs). Frontend-source MRs now merge at the `.svelte` level and the bundle is regenerated post-merge, eliminating the `dist/` merge-conflict class entirely.
- **hud: Now-page "TODAY · sessions" stuck at 0 + Mills KPI row all "—"** (`internal/hud/monitor/fleet.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/MillsKPIRow.svelte`): two Now-page counter fixes. (1) `sessions_today` was an in-memory `FleetMonitor` counter that resets to 0 on every daemon restart — and the cluster `mobile-hud` pod rolls on each loom-core image build — so the chip chronically read 0 right after a roll even with dozens of sessions. It's now recomputed durably each fleet refresh from the fetched session list (`countSessionsStartedToday`, `started_at >= midnight`, monotonic `max()` so a transient/active-only fetch can't regress it). (2) The Mills KPI row showed five derived ratios that are all `—` when Mills is idle (no merges/gates in 24h). It now leads with **Merges 24h** (`pipeline_merged_runs` — the north-star), which is a real number (incl. 0) even when the ratios are idle. Tests: `TestCountSessionsStartedToday`.
- **hud: Plans branch links 404 on branches with slashes** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/plansHelpers.ts`): `gitlabBranchUrl` ran the whole branch name through `encodeURIComponent`, turning `feat/foo` into `feat%2Ffoo` — GitLab's `/-/tree/` route does not resolve a percent-encoded slash (its own UI links branches with literal slashes). It now encodes each path segment and keeps the slashes literal, so `feat/foo` → `…/-/tree/feat/foo`. (A link to a branch that was deleted after merge or never pushed still 404s — that is stale plan data, not a URL-builder bug.)
- **weaver: model preflight `degraded` 401 ("No api key passed in") — route through the keyless FlexInfer proxy when no gateway key is set** (`pkg/flexinfer/client.go`, `internal/daemon/weaver_embed.go`, `cmd/mcp-weaver/main.go`, `k8s/base/servers/weaver/deployment.yaml`): the embedded weaver (and the standalone `mcp-weaver`) built its FlexInfer client from `FLEXINFER_URL`, which on the canonical cluster resolves to the **keyed LiteLLM gateway** (`loom-secrets`). No `FLEXINFER_API_KEY` is provisioned anywhere, so every weaver call — the preflight `GET /v1/models` *and* live completions — got LiteLLM's `401 {"error":{"message":"Authentication Error, No api key passed in.",...}}`, surfacing in the HUD as a permanent **"Model preflight: degraded — showing all configured models as missing"** banner. The keyless **FlexInfer proxy** (`FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL` → `flexinfer-proxy.flexinfer-system`) serves the same router/subagent/domain models and is already the catalog source-of-truth the aimodels domain uses. New `flexinfer.ResolveBaseURL(gateway, apiKey, proxyURL)` prefers the keyless proxy when no gateway key is set (and honors the gateway when a key *is* configured); both weaver entrypoints use it, and the standalone weaver Deployment now wires `FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL`. Tests: `TestResolveBaseURL` (all six gateway/key/proxy combinations, including the exact bug scenario).
- **hud: Now page Live Sessions showed barren rows ("0 calls / no captured activity")** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/liveSessions.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/LiveSessionsCard.svelte`): the card only read the fleet snapshot's `sessions` array (empty descriptions, no activity) and ignored the `agents`/presence array entirely — so every active session rendered as an opaque 8-char hash with "no captured activity", even though the daemon had rich presence for it. The store now joins `FleetSnapshot.agents` (presence) onto each session by `session_id`/`agent_id` and the card surfaces the session **description** (e.g. "Claude Code · services/flexinfer"), **current_task** + **branch** + **active-file count** (when the agent reports them), and **heartbeat freshness** ("15s ago"). Tool-call telemetry still shows when captured; the prominent "No captured activity" line is replaced by a soft idle/telemetry-status note. Enrichment is live via the `hud.fleet` SSE (which already carried `agents`) and the `/api/fleet` seed.
- **weaver: model preflight `degraded` 401 ("No api key passed in") — route through the keyless FlexInfer proxy when no gateway key is set** (`pkg/flexinfer/client.go`, `internal/daemon/weaver_embed.go`, `cmd/mcp-weaver/main.go`, `k8s/base/servers/weaver/deployment.yaml`): the embedded weaver (and the standalone `mcp-weaver`) built its FlexInfer client from `FLEXINFER_URL`, which on the canonical cluster resolves to the **keyed LiteLLM gateway** (`loom-secrets`). No `FLEXINFER_API_KEY` is provisioned anywhere, so every weaver call — the preflight `GET /v1/models` *and* live completions — got LiteLLM's `401 {"error":{"message":"Authentication Error, No api key passed in.",...}}`, surfacing in the HUD as a permanent **"Model preflight: degraded — showing all configured models as missing"** banner. The keyless **FlexInfer proxy** (`FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL` → `flexinfer-proxy.flexinfer-system`) serves the same router/subagent/domain models and is already the catalog source-of-truth the aimodels domain uses. New `flexinfer.ResolveBaseURL(gateway, apiKey, proxyURL)` prefers the keyless proxy when no gateway key is set (and honors the gateway when a key *is* configured); both weaver entrypoints use it, and the standalone weaver Deployment now wires `FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL`. Tests: `TestResolveBaseURL` (all six gateway/key/proxy combinations, including the exact bug scenario).
- **hud: Plans drawer can't be closed (infinite re-open)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`): when a plan was opened via a deep-link (or with a stale `#tasks/plans/<id>` hash), closing the detail drawer cleared `selected` but left `router.detail` set, so the deep-link `$effect` immediately re-opened it — an infinite popup (and a retry loop if the id failed to resolve). Selection is now route-driven: open/close mutate `router.detail` and a single effect syncs `selected` to it (clearing it when the detail segment is gone). Card clicks, deep-links, and close now share one source of truth, so the drawer closes and stays closed; opens also reflect in the URL (shareable).
- **ci: feature-branch image builds no longer auto-deploy to the prod HUD** (`scripts/ci/buildkit-build.sh`): the loom-hub `ImagePolicy` filter (`^[0-9]{8}-[0-9]{6}$`) is branch-agnostic and selects the newest timestamp tag, while `build:image:*` runs on feature branches too — so an unmerged feature-branch image could win the newest-tag race and deploy to `hud.flexinfer.ai` before merge (observed 2026-06-27). The Flux-deployable `:YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS` tag is now minted only on the default branch and release tags; feature branches still publish `:<short-sha>` for validation/manual pulls but never a deployable tag. Applies to the `loom-core` and `custom-server` images built by this script.
- **hud/mills: pipeline-detail drawer hung forever on a stalled operator (couldn't even close it)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelineRunDetail.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/{vitest.config.ts,package.json}`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`): opening a pipeline run issued a bare `fetch()` through `getJSON` with **no timeout and no abort**, so a slow/unreachable Mills operator pinned the drawer in *"Loading…"* indefinitely while the 15s poll kept firing more un-cancellable requests — the panel froze and even the ✕ appeared dead (the HUD proxy's only guard, a 30s `ResponseHeaderTimeout`, covers just time-to-first-byte). Fix: (1) `getJSON` now bounds **every** Mills request with a 12s `AbortController` deadline (under the 15s poll interval) and relays an external cancel onto the live fetch — timeouts surface as a retryable `request timed out after 12s` instead of an opaque `AbortError`, which also hardens the whole `fetchAll` poll that previously hung and blanked the panel; (2) `openRunDetail`/`closeRunDetail` (and the mirrored backlog drawer) abort the in-flight detail fetch on close so the drawer dismisses instantly even mid-request, swallowing the close-abort so it never leaves a phantom error on reopen; (3) `PipelineRunDetail` clamps a stage log to the trailing 20k chars (a multi-MB `<pre>` can itself block the main thread) with a "Showing the last 20k of 500k characters." note, and replaces the bare loading text with a spinner + an explicit "Press Esc or ✕ to close" hint. Adds the frontend's first unit-test harness (vitest, run in the `build:frontend` CI job): `mills.timeout.test.ts` covers the deadline, the caller-abort re-tag, and that closing mid-flight leaves no error state.

### Added
- **mills: GPT-5.4 council editor — OpenAI Responses backend for frontier-quality decomposition (.loom/163 S3, default flexinfer)** (`pkg/mills/clients/council_openai.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): an alternative council editor that drives a frontier OpenAI model (e.g. `gpt-5.4`) via the existing `pkg/openairesponses` Responses-API client, for higher-quality roadmap decomposition than the local gemma/qwen flexinfer editor. `OpenAIResponsesCouncilEditor` makes one stateless Responses turn per `Edit` and **reuses the exact same prompt + parsers** as the flexinfer editor (`buildCouncilEditorPrompt`, `splitCouncilSections`, `parseCouncilProposals`), so the writer/mutator/eval flow — and the S3 `## Backlog Proposals` decomposition path — is identical; only the synthesis model differs. `buildCouncilEditor` selects it **only** when `policy.council.ensemble.editor.backend` is `openai`/`openai-responses` AND a key is present (`OPENAI_API_KEY`/`LOOM_RESPONSES_API_KEY`); otherwise it falls back to the flexinfer editor, so a missing key never breaks council. **Live-validated** against the real API: gpt-5.4 decomposed a 3-improvement brief into 3 independent proposals, each a named slice with goal + concrete files. Tests: `council_openai_test.go` (fake-client doc/proposal parse, empty-output handling, request shape; plus a `OPENAI_API_KEY`-gated live decomposition test skipped in CI). Activate by wiring the key env into the operator + setting the editor backend in the gitops ConfigMap.
- **mills: council decomposition — editor emits structured sliced proposals → S2 plan lane (.loom/163 S3, fail-safe)** (`pkg/mills/clients/council.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/council_proposals.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_sliced.go`, `pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): turns the dormant council→backlog path into a roadmap-decomposition source for the S2 plan-slice emitter. The FlexInfer council editor previously emitted only three markdown docs and **zero** structured proposals; its prompt now optionally asks the model to decompose work into independently-shippable slices and append a `## Backlog Proposals` JSON block, which `parseCouncilProposals` lifts into `EditorOutput.BacklogProposals` (each with `PlanSlices{name,goal,files}`). The parse is **deliberately lenient/fail-safe**: a missing or malformed block yields nil, so a model that ignores the instruction can't break council runs (preserving today's markdown-only behavior). The backlog mutator then routes any proposal carrying slices to a **sliced Plan in the plan-slice-emitter's namespace via the new optional `SlicedPlanAuthor` interface** (`*clients.PlanClient.AuthorSlicedPlan` → `agent_plan_create` with a deterministic `plan-council-<slug>` id) **instead of a flat fan-out backlog item** — so each slice ships as its own MR (S2 emits them) rather than the whole proposal fanning into one. The routing is gated on `BacklogMutator.PlanSliceNamespace` (wired from `policy.intake.plan_slice_emitter.namespace`): empty ⇒ off (flat-item behavior unchanged); on any authoring error it falls back to the flat item so work is never dropped. The `SlicedPlanAuthor` extension is type-asserted, so existing `PlanAuthor` implementations and test fakes are unaffected. Pairs with bumping the council editor model from gemma-26b to the larger qwen36-35b (gitops policy ConfigMap) for higher-quality decomposition. Tests: `council_proposals_test.go` (valid/absent/malformed/defaults+skips), `backlog_mutator_sliced_test.go` (routes-to-plan-lane, no-namespace-falls-to-flat, author-error-falls-back). Implements `.loom/163` (plan-mills-demand-sourcing) S3.
- **mills: plan-slice emitter — the Plan Store → backlog bridge (.loom/163 S2, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/intake/plan_slice_emitter.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/plan_reader.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): a new in-operator intake source that makes **planning feed the autonomous loop with real work** instead of only hand-filed GitLab issues or no-op canaries. `PlanSliceEmitter` polls the agent-context Plan Store (new `PlanClient.ListPlans`/`ListSlices`, with the JSON-or-TOON response decode used by the devbox client) for `pending` slices belonging to `planned`/`in_progress` plans in a configured project + namespace, and emits one deterministic, plan-linked `BacklogItem` per slice (id `psl-<slice>`; `goal` + `acceptance_criteria` → `SpecDoc`; `PlanID` stamped for convergence). Idempotent on re-run via read-then-insert dedup (mirrors the GitLab importer), so the reconciler's state is never clobbered. **Triple-gated for safety**: default-OFF (`policy.intake.plan_slice_emitter.enabled`), **fail-closed on an empty namespace** (never scoops up arbitrary planning-scaffold slices), and a configurable ready-phase filter; new nil-safe policy accessors `PlanSliceEmitter{Enabled,Namespace,Project,ReadyPhase,Label,Priority,PollInterval}`. Scoped to loom-core — no credential change (the S0 cross-repo credential kill-test passed separately). To activate: set `intake.plan_slice_emitter.{enabled: true, namespace: <ns>}` in the gitops policy ConfigMap after the operator image deploys. Tests: `plan_slice_emitter_test.go` (mapping/linkage, re-run dedup, fail-closed-without-namespace, deterministic + collision-safe id), `plan_slice_emitter_policy_test.go` (parse + defaults + nil-safety). Implements `.loom/163` (plan-mills-demand-sourcing) S2.
- **loom: unified `loom worktree gc` to stop agent-worktree sprawl** (`cmd/loom/cmd_worktree.go`, `cmd/loom/main.go`): one command that garbage-collects the git worktrees created by every coding agent — Claude (`<repo>/.claude/worktrees/`), Codex (`~/.codex/worktrees/<hash>/<repo>`), and manual/loom trees (`<repo>/.worktrees/`) — consistently. The unification works because all of them register in their canonical repo's `.git/worktrees/` regardless of where the working dir physically lives, so one `git worktree list` sweep per canonical repo surfaces them all. Each linked tree is classified (agent, dirty, on-remote, merged, idle) and removed **only when unambiguously safe**: branch merged into the repo's default branch, or a clean working tree whose commits are all on a remote. Dirty trees, trees with unpushed/at-risk commits, the main tree, the current tree, and recently-active trees (HEAD touched within `--min-idle`, default 30m) are always kept. Dry-run by default; `--execute` to remove, `--json` for machine output, `--policy merged` for the conservative variant, `--repo`/`--workspace` to scope. Idle is read from the gitdir `HEAD` mtime (unaffected by the tool's own `git status` dirtiness probe). On the live workspace this classified 206 worktrees and flagged 104 (93 merged + 11 clean+pushed) as safely reclaimable while keeping 80 dirty + 22 at-risk. Tests: `cmd_worktree_test.go` (decision-policy matrix, parse/classify with a fake git, agent-path inference) + an end-to-end execute validation.
- **mills: daily canary-autopilot scheduler (W1.2 — A3 sustain, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/canary_scheduler.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `pkg/mills/store/canary_item.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `cmd/loom/cmd_mills_pipelines.go`): closes the A3-sustain gap that drops the north-star to 0 on any day nobody hand-runs `loom mills pipelines canary` (observed 2026-06-26 ×0). The autonomous-merge loop is proven green on k8s (first unattended merge 2026-06-24, `.loom/126`) but had **no automatic demand source** — `scheduler_min.go` notes canaries launch only via the manual admin path. New `mills.CanaryScheduler` (modeled on `CouncilScheduler`, reusing its `cronMatches`) wakes every minute and, when `policy.intake.canary_autopilot.enabled`, enqueues + starts one deterministic heartbeat canary per `schedule_cron` match (default `0 9 * * *`, daily 09:00 UTC) so `autonomous_merges_24h` ticks ≥1/day unattended. The operator's in-process run-fn applies the **same 24h canary dedupe** the manual path uses (`findRecentCanary`), so a still-in-flight or escalated canary suppresses the enqueue rather than piling on — and `StartQueuedItem`'s autonomy gate still guards it. Default-OFF (omitted `canary_autopilot:` block = zero behavior change), self-gating on the policy flag like the Workflows runtime; flip via the gitops policy ConfigMap (hot-reloaded) **after** the operator image carrying this reader deploys. The canary's `BacklogItem` shape now lives in one place — `store.CanaryHeartbeatItem`, shared by the CLI's manual-enqueue path and the autopilot — eliminating the prior "kept in sync by hand" drift between the CLI builder and the operator. New policy accessors `CanaryAutopilot{Enabled,Cron,Priority,FixturePath}` (nil/omission-safe, store-owned path/priority defaults). Tests: `canary_scheduler_test.go` (fires-on-match, per-minute dedup, disabled-by-default, kill-switch-honored, accessor defaults), `store/canary_item_test.go` (label/path/default-fallback regression). Implements `.loom/126` "Next waves" W1.2 (A3 sustain).
- **hud: hand plans/slices off to a session, an agent, or Mills from the Plans drawer** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/PlanDispatchDialog.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/plansHelpers.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`): a single **⤳ Hand off…** button on the plan detail drawer (and a **⤳ hand off** button on each slice row) opens a chooser dialog (`PlanDispatchDialog`) with three destinations: **Spawn a new session** (POST `/api/agent/spawn` via `spawnStore`, tagging `plan_id`/`slice_id` in metadata, then jumps to Labs → Spawn); **Hand to an existing agent** (POST `/api/handoffs` via `createHandoff` to a live agent picked from `fleetStore.liveAgents`, with a self-contained brief built by the new `plansHelpers.dispatchInstructions` — the backend auto-provisions the source session); and **Run in Mills** (confirm-gated autonomous pipeline — for a whole plan it reuses/creates the born-linked backlog item via `millsStore.createBacklog`, for a single slice it creates a slice-scoped backlog item with a deterministic id, then starts the pipeline). Slices can now reach Mills and existing agents too (previously a slice could only spawn a fresh session). No new backend endpoints — reuses `/api/agent/spawn`, `/api/handoffs`, `/api/mills/backlog`, and `/api/mills/pipeline/runs/{id}/start`.
- **hud: Plans board polish — drawer detail, slice-progress cards, pill filters, interactive slices** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/PlansPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/plansHelpers.ts`, `pkg/agentcontext/{schema_plan,svc_plans}.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_plans.go`): follow-up to the Plans panel from a live-UX review. Plan detail now opens in a right-edge `DetailDrawer` (was an orphaned bottom strip far from the clicked card); board cards gain a segmented **slice-progress bar** + age + Mills/kill-test/MR meta; the phase stat-pills double as one-click phase filters; and the slice→task rollup has explicit "no slices"/"no tasks linked" empty states. **Slices are now interactive**: each slice's branch links to its GitLab tree, its MR ref to the MR, its assigned agent jumps to Fleet, and its files/decisions render when present. Backend: `agent_plan_list` now attaches a per-plan `slice_summary` (phase→count) via one batched scroll of the slice collection — so the board renders progress without a detail fetch per card — surfaced through the HUD bridge `PlanInfo.SliceSummary`. Tests: `TestPlan_ListSliceSummary`.
- **hud: desktop `GET /api/blocked` — flightdeck stall signal for the VS Code extension (loom-extension flightdeck plan S1)** (`internal/hud/api_blocked.go`, `internal/hud/routes.go`): a desktop-readable endpoint serving the sessions currently waiting on a human — the flightdeck "blocked on a human" stalls the `flightdeck-hud-bridge` already folds into the daemon's `blockedStore` via `agent.blocked`/`agent.unblocked` events (`IngestDaemonEvent`) — so the Loom VS Code extension's planned Flightdeck view can poll it without a mobile-operator token. `GET /api/blocked` (wrapped in the existing `withCORS`) returns `{"blocked": [BlockedSessionInfo…], "count": N}`, longest-wait-first, `blocked` always an array (never `null`); backed by the existing `App.BlockedSessions()`. No new auth code — `withCORS` already scopes the mobile-operator token to `/api/mobile/v1` (→ 403 here) while ordinary desktop clients (no token) are served normally; this is the desktop counterpart of the mobile dashboard's `blocked` array. Riskiest-assumption kill-test (of the loom-extension flightdeck plan) **PASSED 2026-06-27**: a unit test drives the real `IngestDaemonEvent` fold path (`agent.blocked` event → `blockedStore` → `GET /api/blocked`) plus empty / longest-wait ordering / `unblock` / TTL-prune cases; live, a synthetic `agent.blocked` injected into the running daemon (`POST :9876/events/publish`) was observed on the live `:3333 /api/events` SSE stream with the exact `BlockedSessionInfo` fields, confirming the deployed daemon folds the bridge's signal (`flightdeck-hud-bridge` running). Unblocks the extension's Flightdeck-in-the-IDE slices (S3/S4). Tests: `internal/hud/api_blocked_test.go`.
- **hud: Work/Mills UX — Plan Store linking, Project lens & Mills polish** (`internal/hud/bridge/agent_dto.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{TasksPanel,PlansPanel}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/tasks/TaskDetail.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/plansHelpers.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/tasks.svelte.ts`): makes the new Plan Store (S7b) usable and visible in the HUD instead of a set of disconnected lists. Plan doc: `.loom/plan-hud-work-mills-ux-2026-06-26.md`.
  - **Slice 1 — task↔plan linking**: `TaskInfo` now passes `plan_id`/`slice_id` through to the frontend (covers `/api/tasks`, the `hud.fleet` SSE snapshot, and mobile handlers). `TaskDetail` gains a "Plan" section that deep-links a task to its plan, and `blocked_by` chips become buttons that open the blocking task. `PlansPanel` honors `#tasks/plans/<plan-id>` route deep-links.
  - **Slice 2 — Plans panel upgrade**: new `plansHelpers.ts` (clickable GitLab MR/pipeline URL builders, phase/project grouping, filters). `PlansPanel` gets a FilterBar (search + project + phase), a By-Phase / By-Project view toggle, plan → slice → task rollup, clickable MR/pipeline refs, a deep-link to the born-linked Mills backlog item, and kill-test status.
  - **Slice 3 — Mills ↔ Plan Store wiring**: `BacklogDetail` surfaces the born-linked plan (`PlanID`) as a deep-link into Work → Plans, completing bidirectional Mills↔Plan navigation (plan → backlog from Slice 2, backlog → plan here).
  - **Slice 4 — Projects lens**: new first-class top-level nav surface (`projects` view, `ProjectsPanel.svelte`, `projects.svelte.ts`) that federates plans + tasks + active sessions per GitLab project into a per-project rollup card grid, with deep-links out to Plans, Tasks, Fleet, and the Mills backlog. No new backend — aggregates the existing `/api/plans`, `/api/tasks`, and `/api/sessions` endpoints client-side (an optional `pm_project_status` enrichment can layer on later).
  - **Slice 5 — Mills operator polish**: Mills `OverviewPanel` shows a `⚠ stale` indicator in the status meta when data is >90s old (`millsStore.isStale`). `BacklogDetail` shows a top "needs attention" banner for escalated/failed items that deep-links to the most-likely culprit run + stage, so triage doesn't require drilling run → stage → log.
- **mills: heuristic dispatch ranker (W3.2, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/ranker.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`): replaces the reconciler's FIFO-within-priority dispatch order with a deterministic estimate of expected merge probability when `policy.pipeline.ranker_enabled` is set. Pure `Rank(items, escalations, now)` scores each queued item by priority (dominant base) − a recent-escalation penalty (chronically-failing items yield their dispatch slots to fresher work) + a small age bonus (anti-starvation). Penalty + bonus are capped strictly below the priority gap, so the ranker only reorders WITHIN a priority band — it never promotes a lower-priority item above a higher one, and with zero escalations it reproduces FIFO-within-priority exactly (a strict, safe-to-flip refinement). The reconciler reads recent escalations via the existing `ListByStateSince` (no new SQL) and falls back to FIFO on any read error — no LLM in the hot path. Default-off (`ranker_enabled` unset = current behavior). Tests: `ranker_test.go` (priority dominance, escalation deprioritization within band, band preservation, age tie-break, FIFO-refinement, nil-safe). Implements `.loom/126` "Next waves" W3.2 (heuristic-first; an LLM ranker can layer on later).
- **mills: GitLab-CI-failure signals in the council brief (W3.1, second half)** (`pkg/mills/clients/ci_signals.go`, `pkg/mills/council/signals.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): completes W3.1 by adding recent FAILED CI pipelines as a workspace signal alongside the Loki error clusters. `*clients.GitLabClient` now satisfies `council.WorkspaceSignalSource` — `RecentErrorClusters` lists failed pipelines (`GET /projects/:id/pipelines?status=failed&updated_after=…`, reusing the same `requestJSON`/`projectPath` plumbing as the MR/poll/merge stages) and clusters them by branch ref, top-N by count. A new `council.CompositeSignalSource` (`NewCompositeSignals`) fans the brief's signal fetch across both Loki + CI and concatenates the results, best-effort per source (one source erroring never suppresses the other). The operator composes both into `councilRunner.Signals`. Tests: `clients/ci_signals_test.go` (pipeline clustering, nil-safe), `council/signals_test.go` (composite collapse + error-skip). Finishes `.loom/126` "Next waves" W3.1.
- **mills: workspace-signals council brief (W3.1)** (`pkg/mills/clients/loki.go`, `pkg/mills/council/brief.go`, `pkg/mills/runner/runner.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/{config,main}.go`): the council brief now surfaces recent real failures so the council proposes grounded work instead of synthetic canaries. New `clients.LokiClient` queries Loki's `query_range` for error-class logs over the last 24h (plain HTTP to `LOKI_URL`, default `http://loki.logging.svc.cluster.local:3100`; no MCP hub), then clusters them in-code by a normalized message signature (timestamps/ids/hex/quoted-values collapsed) and returns the top clusters by volume. `council.BriefSources` gains `Signals` (a `WorkspaceSignalSource` interface, consumer-side so the brief stays decoupled) + `SignalWindow`; `Compile` renders a "Workspace signals (recent errors)" section when signals are present. The operator wires the Loki client into `councilRunner.Signals` when `LOKI_URL` is set. Best-effort: a nil client, a fetch error, or an empty result simply omits the section — never fails the brief. Tests: `clients/loki_test.go` (clustering + ranking, signature normalization, disabled/HTTP-error paths). Implements `.loom/126` "Next waves" W3.1 (the Loki half; GitLab-CI-failure clusters are a follow-up).
- **mills: handoff-inbox merge notifications (W2.1, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/notify/handoff.go`, `pkg/mills/policy.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): an in-cluster alternative to the webhook notifier. When `policy.notify.handoff_inbox` is set, every merge posts a "Mills merged X" record to the **agent_context handoff inbox** (`agent_handoff_create` over the MCP hub, addressed to `notify.handoff_target`, default `mills-merges`) instead of an external HTTP webhook — no Slack/external dependency, routed through the hub the operator already uses for escalations. The new `notify.HandoffHook` reuses the existing `clients.HandoffClient` and is late-bound into the `pipelineRunner.OnMerged` chain via the same nil-safe-closure pattern as tick-on-merge (the handoff client is built later, with the escalator). Best-effort: a `CreateHandoff` failure is logged and swallowed so it never blocks the merge-hook chain. Default-off (unset = zero behavior change); requires a reachable MCP hub. Tests: `notify/handoff_test.go` (posts to inbox with the right target/links/context, swallows creator errors, nil-creator + nil-receiver disabled). Implements `.loom/126` "Next waves" W2.1.
- **mills: restart-durable autonomous-merges gauge (W1.1 — A3 north-star)** (`pkg/mills/metrics.go`, `pkg/mills/kpi_writer.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`): adds `mills_autonomous_merges{window="1d|7d|30d"}`, a Prometheus gauge recomputed from the SQLite store — `KPIWriter.SeedDurableGauges()` at operator startup (so `/metrics` is correct the instant it is scraped after a pod roll, not 0 until the first scheduler tick up to 5min away) and `KPIWriter.Record()` each tick (so it decays as merges age out of the window). Reuses `countPipelineStateSince(PipelineDone)`, so the gauge equals the `pipeline_merged_runs` KPI for that window. Fixes the counter-reset trap: the pre-existing `mills_pipeline_runs_total{state="done"}` is an in-memory counter that resets to 0 on every operator roll (the operator rolls on each loom-core image build via Flux image automation), chronically mis-framing the north-star `autonomous_merges_24h` as 0 even while canaries merge. `window="1d"` is the north-star; this complements (does not replace) the counter. Tests: restart-durability proof (a fresh `KPIWriter` recomputes the gauge from the store alone; escalated runs excluded; 1d/7d windowing) + registration/label/gather assertions. Implements `.loom/126` "Next waves" W1.1 ([!798]).
- **mills: council born-linked plan authoring (plan store S7b-γ, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/council/backlog_mutator.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): completes inline plan authoring at the second create site. S7b-β born-linked the GitLab importer; the council editor's `BacklogMutator` was still creating backlog items unlinked between boot backfills. `BacklogMutator` gains optional `PlanAuthor` + `Project` + `Logger` fields (with a locally-declared `PlanAuthor` interface so `council` stays decoupled from `intake`; `*clients.PlanClient` satisfies both structurally); `persistOne` now authors a Plan for each created item *before* its `Put` (`maybeAuthorPlan`), so the item is persisted already carrying `plan_id` in one write. Best-effort: a nil author or any authoring failure is a no-op — the item still persists unlinked and the boot backfill links it later, so authoring never blocks the council from creating backlog. The operator reaches into `councilRunner.Mutator` to wire the inline `clients.PlanClient` **only** when `LOOM_MILLS_PLAN_AUTHORING` is set, the MCP hub is reachable, and the council runner is non-nil (the runner is built before the hub is established, so the wiring is post-hub). Default-off (unset flag = zero behavior change), shares the exact gate + client as the importer. With γ landed, **both** Mills create sites born-link new backlog and the boot backfill (S7b-α) covers the historical migration. Tests: `backlog_mutator_test.go` (`TestApply_InlinePlanAuthoring_BornLinked`, `_NilAuthorNoop`, `_AuthorErrorStillPersists`, `_EmptyPlanIDLeavesUnlinked`). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **mills: born-linked GitLab import (plan store S7b-β, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/intake/gitlab_importer.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): the S7b-α backfill only links backlog at operator boot, so items imported between boots stay unlinked until the next pass. `GitLabImporter` gains optional `PlanAuthor` + `Project` fields; when an inline `clients.PlanClient` is wired (operator sets it when `LOOM_MILLS_PLAN_AUTHORING` is set **and** the MCP hub is reachable), `Tick` authors a Plan for each newly imported item *before* its first `Put` (`maybeAuthorPlan`), so the item is persisted already carrying `plan_id` in one write. Best-effort: a nil author or any authoring failure is a no-op — the item still imports unlinked and the boot backfill links it later, so plan authoring never blocks intake. Default-off (unset flag = zero behavior change); off the reconciler hot path. The other create site (`council.persistOne`) shares the same client + gate and remains S7b-γ. Tests: `gitlab_importer_test.go` (`TestTick_BornLinked_AuthorsPlan`, `TestTick_AuthorError_StillImportsUnlinked`). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **mills: Plan-store backlog backfill (plan store S7b-α, default-off)** (`pkg/mills/clients/plan.go`, `pkg/mills/intake/plan_backfill.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/main.go`, `docs/PLAN_STORE.md`): S7 added the `BacklogItem.PlanID` link + read-through prompt but nothing *populated* it — existing backlog (which predates the Plan store) stayed unlinked, so a spawned Mills agent still fell back to the stale `.loom` SpecDoc. New `clients.PlanClient.AuthorPlan` authors a first-class Plan from a `BacklogItem` via `agent_plan_create` over the MCP hub (the same `agent_context` server backing `HandoffClient`/`WorktreeAllocator`); the pure `backlogItemToPlanArgs` mapping round-trips the linkage fields (`mills_backlog_id`, `gitlab_issue_iid`, slices, dependencies, state→phase) and mints a **deterministic** id (`plan-mills-<backlog-id>`) so a re-run upserts rather than duplicating. `intake.PlanBackfiller` lists the backlog, authors a Plan for each item with no `plan_id`, and stamps the returned id back onto the item (skip-already-linked; best-effort per item — an author/Put failure is logged and the loop continues, mirroring the importer). The operator runs one boot-time pass **only** when `LOOM_MILLS_PLAN_BACKFILL` is set and the MCP hub is reachable; unset (the default) is **zero behavior change** and the pass is off the reconciler hot path, so the freshly-working autonomous merge pipeline is untouched. The live canary (set the flag, restart the operator, verify Plans authored + `plan_id` stamped) is operator-gated. Authoring plans *inline at create time* (`council.persistOne` + `GitLabImporter.Tick`) so new backlog is born linked remains S7b-β. Tests: `pkg/mills/clients/plan_test.go` (mapping, deterministic id sanitization, state→phase, JSON+YAML decode, AuthorPlan happy/YAML/failure paths), `pkg/mills/intake/plan_backfill_test.go` (links-unlinked-skips-linked, best-effort-on-author-error, put-failure-doesn't-count, nil-deps no-op). Spec/plan: `.loom/160`/`.loom/161`.
- **spawn: wire Mills spawn pods to the Plan Store over MCP** (`cmd/loom/{proxy_wsbackend,cmd_proxy}.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`): Mills-spawned agent pods had **zero MCP tools** — the generated `~/.codex/config.toml` carried no `[mcp_servers]` block and no `loom` binary shipped in the pod — so a spawned codex agent could not call `agent_plan_get`, blocking plan-store ↔ Mills unification (plan-store S7). The store **is** reachable from spawn pods, but it speaks **MCP over WebSocket at `/ws`** (`mcp-agent-context` deploys with `MCP_TRANSPORT=websocket`; svc port named `websocket`, health `/health`, ready `/ready`) — **not** HTTP/streamable, so a plain codex `url=` transport can't reach it; and the in-cluster gateway's `agent_context` `always_allow` filters out **every** `agent_plan_*` tool (the gateway analog of the !785 proxy-profile fix). Fix: a new `loom proxy --ws-backend <ws-url>` mode (`wsBackendPump`, also `LOOM_WS_BACKEND`) — a thin **single-backend** MCP pass-through bridging the agent's stdio MCP client straight to one upstream MCP server over WebSocket, with **no daemon, no aggregation, and no tool-name namespacing** (so `agent_plan_get` stays un-prefixed and the gateway's plan-tool filter is bypassed). The default proxy handlers speak daemon-only `loom/tools`/`loom/call` and would not work against a raw MCP server, hence a dedicated pump. The generated spawn Dockerfile now bundles the `loom` binary (`COPY --from=<loom-core image> /usr/local/bin/loom`, `SPAWN_LOOM_IMAGE` override) before the `USER agent` switch, and `injectAgentConfig` writes an `[mcp_servers.loom]` stanza for codex (`config.toml`), claude-code (project `.mcp.json` + `enableAllProjectMcpServers`), and gemini (`settings.json` `mcpServers`), each launching `loom proxy --ws-backend ws://mcp-agent-context.loom-hub.svc.cluster.local:8080/ws`. WS URL via `SPAWN_PLAN_STORE_WS_URL` (`disabled`/`off` opts out **and** drops the loom COPY layer). Closes the long-standing TODO in `injectAgentConfig`. Kill-test (load-bearing transport assumption) **PASSED** live against the deployed `mcp-agent-context` (port-forwarded): both the raw mcp-go WS transport and the built `loom proxy --ws-backend` mode — driven through a real stdio MCP handshake (`initialize` → `notifications/initialized` → `tools/call agent_plan_get`) — return the byte-identical kill-test plan (marker `KILLTEST-20260624`, id `plan-plan-store-live-kill-test-2026-06-24-c5d64f`). Deploy verification (a real Mills pod's codex calling `agent_plan_get`) follows the loom-core image build + operator redeploy. Tests: `TestResolvePlanStoreWSURL`, `TestResolveSpawnLoomImage`, `TestLoomBinaryCopyLines`, `TestGenerateDockerfile_BundlesLoom`, `TestLoomMCPServer{TOML,JSON}` (internal/hud); `TestWSBackendPump_ForwardsRequestsAndMatchesIDs`, `TestJSONRPCIDEqual` (cmd/loom).

### Changed
- **mills: document the council repo layout digest bounds** (`pkg/mills/clients/council.go`, `CHANGELOG.md`): expanded the `repoPackageLayout` GoDoc to state that `maxEntries` is a safety cap, not the expected layout size, and that the digest lists top-level directories plus two levels beneath `pkg/`, `internal/`, and `cmd/`.
- **docs: reconcile Loom Plan Store planning artifacts to shipped reality** (`.loom/160`, `.loom/161`, `ROADMAP.md`): the spec/plan still read "Draft / S0 done, S1 code-complete" while the epic had in fact shipped S0–S7 to `main` (`81d50941`→`4578ec3b`) plus the cross-vendor reachability hardening (`69768b7e`/[!785], `feba82e5`/[!786]) that made S1's live legs pass. Updated both docs to "Shipped", added a per-slice landing table with commit evidence to `.loom/161`, recorded the riskiest-assumption kill-test as PASSED live (codex + Mills-pod WS transport), confirmed S8 cross-platform sync is propagated (registry plan-aware, `platform/gitops` mirror in sync, HOME skills current for claude/codex/gemini/kilocode), and queued S7b (council/importer plan write-path + backlog backfill) + the end-to-end in-pod deploy verification as the explicit remaining follow-ups. Added a "Loom Plan Store" entry to the ROADMAP "Recently Shipped" section. Docs-only; no code or behavior change.
- **HUD: IBM Plex typography — reserve monospace for data** (`internal/hud/frontend/{index.html, src/lib/styles/{theme,layout}.css, src/lib/components/fleet/{EconomicsPanel,FleetStatsGrid,FleetTable}.svelte, src/lib/widgets/AgentCard.svelte}`): swapped the UI typeface from Outfit to **IBM Plex Sans** and JetBrains Mono to **IBM Plex Mono** — a cohesive instrument-panel pairing — loaded via an `index.html` `preconnect` + stylesheet instead of a render-blocking `@import`, with `kern/liga/calt` enabled on `body`. Pulled prose out of monospace so mono now signals data only (numbers, IDs, timestamps, code): economics captions/ratio-hints/legend, the fleet-table grouping toggle + evidence/ungrouped labels, stat sub-captions, and agent-card status/evidence/telemetry chips move to IBM Plex Sans (with `tabular-nums` where they carry figures); ratio values, agent IDs, and counts stay mono. Rebuilt the `go:embed` dist via `pnpm build` (CI does not rebuild it). Verified: `document.fonts` confirms both Plex faces load + apply, `pnpm build` compiles clean, and the built `dist/index.html` references `IBM+Plex+Sans`.

### Fixed
- **agent-context: `agent_context_add` and code-annotation writes survive an embedder outage instead of dropping the write** (`pkg/agentcontext/svc_context_add.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/svc_context_annotations.go`): `storeContextEntries` (backing `agent_context_add`), `LinkCodebase` (`agent_code_annotate`), and `AnnotationAdd` hard-returned an error on any embedder failure, so during an embedder outage (Morph 522 / `gte-qwen2-1.5b` HTTP 500 / circuit-breaker-open) agents could not record any decision, finding, or annotation — while the already-decoupled task/plan write paths kept working. Ported the best-effort pattern from `svc_tasks.go`: on embed failure, log + increment the embed-error metric + persist with a deterministic non-zero fallback vector (Qdrant rejects all-zero vectors under cosine distance) via a shared `embedQueryBestEffort` helper, resolving the vector size as real-embedding → shared discovered size → live collection → embedder default (1536). Also fixes a latent bug where `storeContextEntries`'s old zero-vector branch would have failed the Qdrant upsert. Embedding is enrichment for semantic search, never a correctness gate for persistence; the search path already degrades to keyword fallback (!725). Tests: `TestStoreContextEntries_EmbedFailureStillPersists`, `TestAnnotationAdd_EmbedFailureStillPersists`.
- **mills: deflake `pkg/mills/pipeline` under `-race` + stop the reconciler same-tick double-count (DEBT-079, #176)** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/{runner,adapter}.go`, `pkg/mills/reconciler.go`, tests, `.gitlab-ci.yml`): the package failed ~75–100% of `go test -race` runs and was quarantined from CI `test:race`. Two root causes. (1) **`Started:2`** — within one `Reconciler.Tick`, the queued-item loop started a run, then `pickupInFlightRuns` (same tick) re-found that still-non-terminal run and re-invoked `Starter.Start`, double-counting `res.Started`. `Runner.Start`'s `active`-guard always prevented an actual second drive, so it was a **count/re-entrancy artifact, not double agent-spend** — but flaky and wrong. Fixed by threading a `startedThisTick` set so the in-flight re-driver skips runs launched earlier in the same tick (they re-drive on the next tick if their goroutine has since exited). (2) **DATA RACE / leaked goroutines** — `Runner.Start` and `RunnerStarter.driveFanOut` run detached goroutines; `Integrator.Run` does `*run = parentRun` on the shared run pointer while tests polled it, and `t.Cleanup` closed the store while drive loops still ran ("database is closed" noise). Fixed by adding deterministic `Runner.Wait()` (`sync.WaitGroup`; fan-out goroutines register on the same group) + `RunnerStarter.Wait()`; tests now join before reading shared state / closing the store, replacing fragile poll loops. The `pkg/mills/pipeline` quarantine is removed from `.gitlab-ci.yml` `test:race`. Verified: `go test -race -short -count=10 ./pkg/mills/pipeline` 10/10 (was 0/4). Regression test: `TestReconciler_TickDoesNotDoubleStartFreshlyQueuedItem`.
- **mills: a stalled-but-alive spawn re-spawns and escalates instead of looping pending forever** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/{runner,dispatcher,error_class}.go`, `pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`): when a spawn pod stayed alive but never reached a terminal status, `HUDSpawnClient.pollSpawn` returned a non-terminal error with `SpawnID` set; `runStage` parked the stage **pending** and `Drive` treated `errStagePending` as a no-op, never burning the retry budget. Every reconciler tick (`pickupInFlightRuns`) re-attached to the same dead spawn (`resumeSpawnID`), re-polled to the 30-minute `PollDeadline`, and re-parked — so the run looped pending forever (observed 2026-06-25: a run sat `pending@implement` ~13h on `spawn-2ab680e9275a` until the orphan pod was deleted by hand). The HUD-side `livenessStallTimeout` didn't catch it: `runLivenessWatcher` is process-local to the exec stream, only trips on output *silence*, and dies with its goroutine on HUD restart — it never re-attaches to an orphaned pod, so the durable backstop belongs operator-side. Fix: a new sentinel `pipeline.ErrSpawnPollTimeout` is wrapped on every `pollSpawn` deadline-exceeded return; `Classify` maps it to `ClassTransient` (free retry, but every attempt still counts toward the `maxAttempts+transientRetryCap` hard cap so a persistently hung substrate escalates rather than looping); and `runStage` counts consecutive poll-timeouts per attempt (persisted in the pending row's artifacts, JSON round-trip safe) — below `maxConsecutiveSpawnPollTimeouts=2` it keeps the resume-safe "check again next tick" behavior, and at/above it falls through to the error path so a fresh spawn re-dispatches and the run ultimately escalates. Non-timeout interruptions (operator restart mid-poll, transient GET error) still park pending unchanged. Tests: `TestRunner_StalledSpawnEscalatesInsteadOfLoopingPending` (drives the reconciler loop against a perpetually-stalling spawn; asserts escalation + fresh re-spawns rather than unbounded pending), `TestRunner_SinglePollTimeoutStillParksPending` (legit slow-but-progressing spawn still parks, no budget burn), and `TestRun_PollDeadlineExceeded` extended to pin `errors.Is(err, ErrSpawnPollTimeout)`.
- **proxy: expose the `agent_plan_*` tools in the `llm-core`/`antigravity-core` profiles so codex/antigravity can resolve plans** (`cmd/loom/proxy_tool_filter.go`): the loom proxy shapes tools/list per client — codex and antigravity get a curated `selectCoreDeveloperTools` set (`--tool-profile llm-core --max-tools 140`, antigravity 100). The `requiredPatterns` allowlist had the full agent_context session/recall/task/handoff/worktree/presence family but **none of the plan-store tools**, so `agent_plan_get` and the slice tools were filtered out for every profile-limited vendor. A live cross-vendor kill-test (2026-06-24) caught it: a plan created from Claude was byte-identical-resolvable from a Claude subagent, but a `codex exec` call **could not see `agent_plan_get`** and silently fabricated an approximate answer from fuzzy `agent_context_recall` instead of erroring — exactly the worktree-resilient, cross-agent resolution the plan store exists to provide, broken for codex/Mills. Added `agent_plan_get/list/create/update/lifecycle_advance` + `agent_plan_slice_get/list/update/claim` to `requiredPatterns` (placed right after the agent_context block so they land within even antigravity's 100-tool window). Re-ran the codex leg after rebuilding the binary: `loom/agent_context__agent_plan_get` now resolves byte-identical (`TITLE=Plan Store live kill-test 2026-06-24 CREATEDBY=claude-code`). Deploy: takes effect for new codex sessions after the `loom` binary refresh (`~/go/bin` + `~/.local/bin`). Tests: `TestFilterProxyTools_{AntigravityCore,LLMCore}Defaults` extended to assert the plan tools survive both profiles.
- **mills: scope the devbox `tests`-stage quality gate to `fmt` for all items so real (non-canary) work isn't false-failed** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/dispatcher.go`): the hub-spawned devbox sandbox clones only `services/loom-core`, but loom-core's `go.work` `use`s sibling repos (`../../libs/fi-accel/go/fiaccel`, `fi-mcp-kit`, `mcp-go`) and fi-accel is cgo — so whole-module `go vet ./...` / `go test ./...` fail immediately (~79ms, `directory ../../libs/... does not exist`). Canary items already scoped to `fmt` for this reason; non-canary items used the gate default (`fmt+lint+test`), so the **first real autonomous-merge attempt** (`MILLS-DEBT-TICKLABEL-20260624`, a unit-test addition) escalated at the `tests` stage on `PASS fmt / FAIL lint (79ms)` despite the change being correct (GitLab `test:unit` passed, all 6 post-implement gates passed). `devboxScopeFor` now returns the sandbox-safe `[fmt]` scope for every item; the authoritative lint/vet/test/build runs in GitLab CI, which the `ci_watch` stage already gates before merge, so no merge safety is lost. A richer pre-flight that runs the changed packages with `GOWORK=off` is a follow-up. Test: `TestDevboxWorker_NonCanaryScopesToFmt` (with `TestDevboxWorker_CanaryScopesChecks` still green).
- **HUD: codex worktree-canonicalization also covers the hooks.json mint path (completes the prior fix)** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`): the earlier fix canonicalized codex's `WS_HASH` in the `config.toml` notify mint and the Go proxy, but codex's `hooks.json` events mint a *scoped* `codex-<WS_HASH>-<SESSION_SCOPE>` id through the shared `hookAgentIDBootstrap`, which still hashed the raw `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (worktree) path. In a worktree that left the scoped hooks.json id keyed on the worktree hash while the scopeless notify/proxy ids used the canonical-repo hash — so codex would **split into two conversation rows within a single worktree** (`conversationId` keeps the `WS_HASH` for codex). `hookAgentIDBootstrap` now injects `worktreeCanonRootShell` before the `WS_HASH` cksum **only for `agentID == "codex"`**, so all three codex mint paths agree on the canonical hash and fold across (and within) worktrees; conversation-scoped vendors (claude/gemini) are byte-for-byte unchanged and never receive the canon snippet (it is unsafe for Gemini's `settings.json` `${...}` interpolator). Test: `TestHookAgentIDBootstrap_CodexCanonicalizesWorktreeRoot` (string + ordering + `sh -n` parse, asserts claude/gemini untouched).
- **HUD: codex agent identity is stable across worktrees of one repo (no more per-worktree orphan rows)** (`pkg/generator/{configs_hooks,configs_codex}.go`, `cmd/loom/proxy_heartbeat.go`): codex is workspace-anchored — its lifecycle hook mints `codex-<WS_HASH>` with no `SESSION_SCOPE`, and `conversationId`/`ConversationID` keep the `WS_HASH` for codex (`WORKSPACE_ANCHORED_BASES`). `WS_HASH` was the `cksum` of `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, i.e. the **worktree** path, so one codex app working across a repo's main checkout and its linked worktrees (`<repo>/.worktrees/<b>`, `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/<b>`) fragmented into a separate, unjoinable conversation row per worktree — and the proxy/session base-match couldn't reconcile across the divergent hashes (the open "worktree divergence → different `WS_HASH`" limitation noted in the `IsBaseOf` fix below). The namespace derivation (`hookNamespaceVars`) already collapsed those two worktree layouts to the main repo, but the identity hash did not. Fix: a shared `worktreeCanonRootShell` snippet rewrites `$WS_ROOT` to the main repo root **before** the `WS_HASH` cksum in the codex notify mint, and the Go proxy mirror (`workspaceProxyAgentIDFor`, gated on `agentType == "codex"`) applies the existing `stripWorktreeFromRepoRoot` to the same effect — so codex folds all worktrees of one repo to a single id while conversation-scoped vendors (claude/gemini) stay worktree-specific to keep matching their unchanged shell bootstrap. Namespace output is unchanged (nsVars reads the now-canonical `$WS_ROOT` and yields the same `NS_PROJECT`). Deploy: takes effect after `loom sync codex --regen` + a new `loom` binary; existing worktree-hashed codex sessions age out. Tests: `TestWorktreeCanonRootShell_StableHashAcrossWorktrees` and `TestCodexNotifyCommand_CanonicalizesWorktreeRoot` (shell-exec proof + wiring/ordering guard), `TestWorkspaceProxyAgentIDFor_CodexFoldsWorktree` (codex folds, claude/gemini do not).
- **mills: reconciler increments the pipeline_run attempt on re-queue so escalation auto-retry isn't wedged** (`pkg/mills/reconciler.go`): `tryStart` hardcoded `Attempts: 1` when creating a `pipeline_run`. The Slice-3d escalation auto-retry path (`pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go` `maybeAutoRetry`) marks a transiently-escalated run `escalated` but leaves the backlog item `queued` so the reconciler spins up a fresh run on the next tick — that fresh insert collided with the prior run on the `pipeline_runs` `UNIQUE(backlog_id, attempts)` constraint (migration `001_initial.sql:74`), so `PutRun` failed **every tick** and the item wedged `queued` forever. Observed live 2026-06-24: `mills_reconciler_ticks_total{outcome="errored"}` was the only label emitted (climbing ~1.4/min) and two stale canary backlog items (`MILLS-CANARY-20260619-135827`/`-134843`) had been erroring on every tick for 5 days across operator restarts (state persisted on the PVC) — silently disabling the entire auto-retry resilience feature. `tryStart` now computes the next attempt = `max(existing attempts) + 1` via `PipelineDAO.ListByBacklog`; the retry ceiling stays enforced upstream in `maybeAutoRetry` (`EscalationAutoRetryCap`), so attempt numbers remain bounded. Test: `TestReconciler_RequeuedItemStartsNextAttempt` (seeds an escalated `attempts=1` run on a queued item, asserts the next tick starts a fresh `attempts=2` run instead of erroring).
- **HUD: reconcile background proxy/mirror identity to the real session; unstick the trace drawer** (`internal/hud/fleetview/{identity,fleetview}.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_session.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/SessionDetail.svelte`): the session-detail drawer's "Recent Traces" stayed pinned on "Loading recent traces…" forever because the loading state read the **global** `traceStore.loading` poll flag, which is decoupled from the per-session trace fetch — a session with zero traces never fell through to "No recent traces" while the global poll was reloading; now gated on the per-session fetch (`loadingEntries`). Separately, the background proxy heartbeat (`cmd/loom/proxy_heartbeat.go`) and federation mirror (`internal/hud/mirror/mirror.go`) register a **scopeless workspace-base** identity `<type>-<WS_HASH>` while the CLI hooks own a **scoped** session `<type>-<WS_HASH>-<SCOPE>`. Exact-only matching (`fleetview.Join`, `GetActiveSession`) surfaced the base id as a bogus "NO ACTIVE SESSION MATCH" orphan and, with `ensure_session=true`, forked a phantom `agents/<id>` session — a background wakeup of the wrong session. New directional `fleetview.IsBaseOf` (base must be the shorter, scopeless id, so two distinct scoped conversations in one workspace never merge) lets `Join` reconcile the base presence to the most-recent active scoped session before flagging an orphan, and `ensureHeartbeatSession` base-matches and reuses an existing active session instead of bootstrapping a twin (truly hook-less agents still bootstrap). Known limitation: when proxy and hooks resolve different git toplevels (worktree divergence → different `WS_HASH`) there is no shared sub-id to link them, so reconciliation can't fire. Rebuilt the `go:embed` dist via `pnpm build`. Tests: `TestIsBaseOf`, `TestJoin_ProxyBaseReconcilesToScopedSession`, `TestJoin_DifferentWSHashStaysOrphan`.
- **mills/spawn: liveness watchdog fails a zombie spawn pod instead of waiting out the operator's poll deadline** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/spawn_telemetry.go`): a spawned agent (codex) sometimes dies on its first `implement` attempt while its pod stays in `Phase=Running` — a "zombie pod" (container PID 1 alive, agent process dead). The spawn controller maps `Running → status=running` indefinitely (`internal/spawn/controller.go`), so the Mills operator's `pollSpawn` (`pkg/mills/clients/spawn.go`) never saw a terminal status: it blocked up to its 30-minute `PollDeadline` holding the run's `active` lock (so `pickupInFlightRuns` no-op'd it), and only an **operator restart** (`ResumeInFlightRuns`) re-spawned — the manual nudge every autonomous run needed ~2 of 3 times, gating the `autonomous_merges_24h` north-star. Root cause: nothing checked agent **liveness**, only pod **existence**. Fix: the `SpawnTelemetryAccumulator` now tracks `lastActivity`, stamped via `Touch()` from the streaming exec's per-line callback (every flight-deck JSONL line proves the process is alive); a new `runLivenessWatcher` (modeled on the existing budget watcher) cancels the exec context with `errSpawnStalled` when no output arrives for `LivenessStallTimeout` (default **15m**, env override `LOOM_SPAWN_LIVENESS_STALL_TIMEOUT`). The finalize path reads the cancellation cause (`context.WithCancelCause`) and calls `failSpawn`, which already cleans the pod → the operator's poll sees a terminal failure and auto-retries instead of wedging. Streaming (K8s) path only — the buffered harvester-vm path has no mid-flight telemetry and keeps its own `Exec` `TimeoutSec`. Tests: `TestRunLivenessWatcher_{TripsOnStall,StaysAliveWhileActive,ExitsOnDone}`, `TestSpawnTelemetryAccumulator_{LastActivityInitialised,TouchAdvancesLastActivity}`.
- **mills: `scope` gate exempts slice-less canaries before the empty-slices check** (`pkg/mills/gates/scope.go`): the `post_implement_gate` `scope` check failed with `backlog item has no slices; no scope to enforce` and escalated deterministic canaries whose backlog item carried **zero slices** (e.g. when `plan_slice` fails to persist one) — observed on `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-20260623-235142`, where the implement work was correct (branch pushed, diff = only `testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md`) yet the run looped to max attempts and escalated because retrying `implement` can never add a slice. Root cause: the `CanaryLabel` carve-out that seeds `canaryAllowedPaths` (added for escalations #151/#163) ran **after** the `allowed.empty()` guard, so a slice-less canary failed before the carve-out could exempt the fixture path. The carve-out now runs **before** the empty check, so a slice-less canary enforces the narrow `canaryAllowedPaths` allowlist instead of escalating; non-canary slice-less items still fail (blast radius unchanged). Test: `TestScope_CanaryNoSlicesHeartbeatAllowed` (with `TestScope_NoSlicesFails` and `TestScope_CanaryHeartbeatStillFailsForNonCanaryItems` still green).
- **mills: merge stage recovers the detached `merge_request_event` head pipeline (autonomous-merge `405`)** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`): the `405` survives the no-MWPS-at-create and branch-poll fixes below. GitLab attaches a spurious 0-job `failed` `merge_request_event` pipeline as the MR `head_pipeline` on MR-open **even with** `workflow.rules: merge_request_event → never` and no `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds` — the rule suppresses the jobs but GitLab still records the failed pipeline and pins it as head, so under `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` the merge `405`s forever (`detailed_merge_status=ci_must_pass`). Verified live on canary MR !770 (2026-06-23): the run reached a green `ci_watch` yet the merge stage still escalated. `Merge()` now probes once, and on a `405` inspects `head_pipeline.source`: a persistent **detached-head** `405` triggers `recoverDetachedHead` (delete the `merge_request_event` pipeline for the head SHA, then close+reopen the MR so head re-points to the green push pipeline), then retries; a **benign timing** `405` (normal head pipeline still settling) is polled away without close/reopen. The delete is the load-bearing precondition — close+reopen alone re-attaches the same failed pipeline. Tests: `TestMerge_RecoversDetachedHeadOn405`, `TestMerge_BenignTiming405DoesNotCloseReopen`.
- **mills: `ci_watch` polls the branch pipeline for the MR SHA, ignoring the spurious `merge_request_event` placeholder** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`): `PollPipeline` previously polled `mr.head_pipeline`. When an MR is opened on a repo whose `workflow.rules` block `merge_request_event` pipelines (loom-core runs branch pipelines), GitLab briefly attaches a 0-job `merge_request_event` pipeline as the head and immediately fails it (then deletes it) — the `ci_watch` poll raced that transient and reported `failed`, escalating the run (the long-standing autonomous-merge blocker #147/#148/#150). `PollPipeline` now resolves the most recent **non-`merge_request_event`** pipeline for the MR's head SHA (`GET /pipelines?sha=`) and polls that, so the placeholder is ignored and a still-only-placeholder state keeps waiting rather than failing. Pairs with disabling the project's Auto DevOps (which made the placeholder transient instead of persistent). Tests: `TestPollPipeline_TerminatesOnSuccess` (ignores a listed placeholder), `…_IgnoresMergeRequestEventPlaceholder` (placeholder-only → wait, not fail), `…_FailedTerminal`, `…_TimeoutWhenNoSHA`.
- **mills: don't arm `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds` at MR-create so the autonomous merge isn't blocked by an empty MR pipeline** (`pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`): the operator's `mr` stage created the MR with `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds: req.AutoMerge`. Opening an MR with MWPS armed makes GitLab spin up a detached merge-request pipeline; on a repo whose `workflow.rules` block `merge_request_event` pipelines (loom-core runs branch pipelines), GitLab empties it into a **`failed`, 0-job** placeholder that becomes the MR `head_pipeline` and blocks merge with **HTTP 405** under `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` — the long-standing autonomous-merge `405` blocker (#147/#148/#150). Operator-created (bot) MRs hit this; human MRs created plain (then armed separately) never did. `CreateMR` now omits MWPS entirely, so the MR `head_pipeline` is the green branch pipeline; the operator's explicit `merge` stage (`runMerge → Merge`, already a 405-retry immediate merge) performs the autonomous merge after `ci_watch` confirms green. Test `TestCreateMR_AutoMergeDoesNotArmMWPSOnCreate` (was `…SetsMergeWhenPipelineSucceeds`) now asserts the create body omits the flag even when `AutoMerge=true`. (The earlier `.gitlab-ci.yml` `test:race` rule removal was necessary cleanup but not the root cause — the empty pipeline is created by the MWPS-on-create, not job rules.)
- **ci: drop the dead `merge_request_event` rule from `test:race` so MR merges aren't blocked by an empty MR pipeline** (`.gitlab-ci.yml`): the top-level `workflow.rules` blocks all `merge_request_event` pipelines (this repo runs branch pipelines), but `test:race` still carried `rules: - if: $CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE == "merge_request_event"`. That made it the one MR-pipeline-eligible job, so opening an MR spawned a *merge request pipeline* that the workflow then emptied into a **`failed`, 0-job pipeline** — which became the MR's `head_pipeline` and blocked merge with a **HTTP 405** under `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` (the green branch pipeline was ignored). This is the long-standing Mills autonomous-merge `405` blocker (#147/#148/#150): the canary reached `mr` then `ci_watch`/merge always failed on the spurious pipeline. The rule was vestigial — the job only ever ran on `main` because the workflow blocks MR pipelines — so removing it is a no-op for when `test:race` runs, but stops the empty MR pipeline from being created. MR head pipelines are now the branch pipeline, so `only_allow_merge_if_pipeline_succeeds` gates on real CI.
- **mcp-devbox: quality gate awaits the async sandbox build instead of failing instantly** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/quality_gate.go`): on the K8s backend a cold/stale sandbox triggers an asynchronous image build that returns a `buildInProgressError` immediately (so quick `devbox_exec` calls don't hang). `handleQualityGate` surfaced that as `ensure sandbox: ...`, which the Mills operator's `tests` stage recorded as an opaque `devbox quality gate failed: 0 checks` and retried within **~0.1s** — exhausting its 3 attempts long before a cold build (`go mod download`, apk installs, image push; minutes) could finish. The Mills A2 canary consequently passed `plan_slice → research → implement` and every post-implement gate but always died at `tests`, blocking the `autonomous_merges_24h` north-star. The gate now calls `ensureRunningAwaitBuild`, which polls `ensureRunning` with bounded exponential backoff (up to 8m) past the build-in-progress signal — so the first gate call waits for the build, then runs real checks; a genuine build/start failure is surfaced (no longer masked as `0 checks`). Wait/backoff/timeout logic extracted to `awaitSandboxBuild` with tests (`TestAwaitSandboxBuild`: completes-after-building, passthrough of non-building errors, timeout-while-building, context cancellation).
- **codebase-memory: revert workspace volume to `emptyDir` to unblock the `loom-hub-servers` Flux reconciliation** (`k8s/base/servers/codebase-memory/deployment.yaml`): a prior incomplete fix pointed codebase-memory's workspace volume at the `loom-hub-workspace-nfs` PVC, but that NFS export (`storage-vm-linux-nas-pilot`, `192.168.50.211`) was halted in the 2026-06-21 storage-fault incident. Worse, the live pod still had an `emptyDir` volume of the same name co-owned by a stray `kubectl-patch` field manager, so Flux's server-side apply could not replace it with the PVC (`volumes[0]: may not specify more than 1 volume type`) — the dry-run failed and **aborted the entire `loom-hub-servers` Kustomization**, freezing it at revision `8463b1fe` and blocking *every* loom-hub server deploy (including the mobile-hud workspace fix above). Reverted the volume to `emptyDir` (matching the live pod) so the same-type apply succeeds and reconciliation resumes. codebase-memory keeps indexing an empty workspace exactly as it does today; repointing it at a healthy Longhorn workspace volume is a follow-up.
- **mobile-hud: mount a workspace volume so Mills spawns can resolve projects** (`k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`): the spawn orchestrator's project resolver (`internal/hud/spawn.go` → `resolveProjectPath`, `wsRoot = $HOME/workspace = /home/mcp/workspace`) reads a repo on disk to fingerprint + generate the Dockerfile **before** the spawn pod git-clones, but the mobile-hud Deployment never mounted a workspace volume — the live pod had a read-only empty `emptyDir` at `/home/mcp/workspace`. Every Mills spawn stage (`plan_slice`/`implement`) then failed with `project "services/loom-core" not found under /home/mcp/workspace (searched services, libs, platform, private, labs)`. Now mounts the `loom-hub-workspace` PVC (read-only) at `/home/mcp/workspace`. This is the **healthy Longhorn replacement** (storageClass `loom-ws-1r`) for the prior `loom-hub-workspace-nfs` export on `storage-vm-linux-nas-pilot` (`192.168.50.211`), which was halted during the 2026-06-21 storage-fault incident (two dying SAS disks) — a fresh pod doing a `hard` NFS mount against the down server would hang on mount. Because the Longhorn volume is `ReadWriteOnce`, the Deployment also switches to the `Recreate` strategy so the old pod releases the volume before the new one attaches (a `RollingUpdate` surge pod would deadlock on the RWO mount). Volume-only change (no image rebuild); Flux rolls the pod.
- **weaver: record metrics in the embedded daemon + instrument tool-response compression so the F8 economics card shows real ratios** (`internal/daemon/weaver_embed.go`, `pkg/weaver/{metrics,telemetry,router,tokens}.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/economics.go`): two root causes left the Token Economics card full of `STUB`/`INSUFFICIENT DATA`/degenerate values even after the unreachable fix. (1) `startEmbeddedWeaver` never called `router.SetMetrics()`, so the daemon's weaver Router had `metrics == nil` — `loom/weaver/metrics` always returned all-zeros (so "Local util." stuck at 0 and lifetime counters never accumulated). Now wired with `weaver.NewMetrics(nil)`, mirroring the standalone `cmd/mcp-weaver`. (2) The `token_savings`, `compression`, and `context_waste` ratios depended on `ToolResponseTokens`/`WeaverResponseTokens` that **nothing ever populated**. Weaver now counts raw tool-response tokens in the subagent telemetry sink (`RecordToolCall`, previously discarded the result) and compressed-answer tokens per successful query, exposes them via `raw_tool_response_tokens`/`weaver_response_tokens` in the metrics summary, and the HUD threads them into the economics inputs. `context_waste` now reports `insufficient_data` when no tool tokens have accrued instead of a misleading `0.00`.
