Two-layer fix: (1) **first-class generation** — a new `platform_permissions.<platform>.settings.flightdeck_capture` registry gate renders the capture entries as generated hooks: Claude gets the installer's 9-event MVP set (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure, PermissionRequest, Notification, Stop, SessionEnd; matcher `"*"` on the three tool events) running `~/.loom/flightdeck/bin/flightdeck-capture`, Gemini the 7-event set (SessionStart, BeforeAgent, BeforeTool, AfterTool, AfterAgent, Notification, SessionEnd) running `flightdeck-capture -platform gemini-cli` — sets mirror `edge/internal/install/{install,gemini}.go` in services/loom-flightdeck. Gemini hooks now get the same event-name validation Claude gained in !686 (new pinned `geminiHookEventBaseline`, Gemini CLI 0.43.0 enum), since one unknown event name can silently disable a platform's hooks. The gate is **on** in this workspace's registry and must stay off on machines without the flightdeck edge binary (e.g. cluster code-server pods — the gitops registry mirror is intentionally left without it). (2) **foreign-entry preservation** — `MergeSettingsForHome` now merges regenerated hooks entry-by-entry: canonical entries win, home entries survive when their command neither appears in the canonical block (after `~`/`$HOME`/absolute-home-path normalization, so installer-written absolute flightdeck paths dedupe against the generated `~` form instead of double-firing) nor matches a loom-managed marker (`HOOK_SESSION_ID`, `loom agent `, formatter/guardrail snippets — stale generated variants are dropped, not accumulated). Tests: `flightdeck_hooks_test.go` (9/7-event sets, matcher placement, gate-off default, sets ⊆ per-platform baselines), `gemini_hook_events_test.go` (unknown-event filtering, emitted-events-accepted), `merge_foreign_test.go` (foreign hook survives regen — the incident regression — absolute-path dedupe, idempotency).

### Removed
- **Dead HUD panel `AlertsPanel.svelte`** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/AlertsPanel.svelte`): not imported anywhere in `internal/hud/frontend/src`, absent from the router (`lib/stores/router.svelte.ts`) and `App.svelte`'s panel map, and shipped in no dist chunk. Added in 026a7a8c (2026-03-24) alongside ContextHealthPanel and OrchestrationPanel — none of the three were ever wired into `App.svelte`; the other two were removed in 6116e2a1 (chore/hud-remove-dead-panels), and AlertsPanel was deferred to keep that MR small. Self-contained: imports only `shared/EmptyState.svelte` (40 other consumers) and defines its types inline, so no stores or shared code are orphaned. Rebuilt dist is byte-identical. Verify: `grep -rn 'AlertsPanel' internal/hud/frontend/src` returns nothing.
- **Dead HUD panels `ShuttlePanel.svelte` + `ContextHealthPanel.svelte` and the orphaned `contextHealth` store** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/contextHealth.svelte.ts`): neither component was imported anywhere or reachable from the router (`lib/stores/router.svelte.ts`), and their CSS shipped in no dist chunk. Both date to 026a7a8c (2026-03-24), which added the panels alongside their Go backends but never wired them into `App.svelte`; 115a4f68 (2026-04-03) renamed OrchestrationPanel → ShuttlePanel during the weaver/shuttle rebrand and routed the new WeaverPanel instead, while shuttle data surfaced through DispatchPanel/OverviewPanel via `shuttleStore` (which stays — it has live consumers). The `contextHealth` store and its types were only consumed by the dead panel. Flagged by the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`, slice 9 / MR !681). Verify: `grep -rn 'ShuttlePanel\|ContextHealthPanel\|contextHealthStore' internal/hud/frontend/src` returns nothing.

### Changed
- **Daemon honors `loom catalog disable` for proxy-connected platforms** (`internal/daemon/runtime_registry.go`, `daemon_new.go`, `daemon_dispatch_ops.go`, services/loom-core!691): `loom catalog disable <server>` previously only filtered direct per-server config generation (`pkg/generator/configs_targets.go`) — a no-op for every current platform, since they all connect via `loom proxy` → daemon and the daemon loaded the full registry into proxy `tools/list`. The daemon now passes its normalized registry view through `applyCatalogState()` (filters `~/.config/loom/catalog-state.yaml` `disabled_servers`) at startup and on registry reload; the reload path's removed-server diff stops freshly-disabled servers live. Fail-open: a missing or unreadable state file serves all servers, so in-cluster hub deployments (no catalog state) are unaffected. Motivated by loom-flightdeck's Context Ledger (prod, 2026-06-10): 28 of 53 servers had zero tool calls in 90 days across 372 sessions (~460k est tokens of dead catalog weight). Tests: `TestApplyCatalogState_*` in `internal/daemon/runtime_registry_test.go`.
- **HUD panel empty states gain semantic tones + filled centering** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/PanelShell.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`, services/loom-core!635): the shared `PanelShell` empty state read as broken/missing — an off-palette cyan radial glow (a color in no design token) on a small dashed card pinned to the top of a tall black void. `PanelShell` now takes an `emptyTone` prop (`idle | ready | error | disabled`) driving the glow, border, and icon-ring color from `--tone-color`/`--tone-glow` tokens (accent/success/error), stretches and vertically centers the empty card across the full content height (`is-empty` flex), and adds a slow standing-by pulse on idle/ready only (never errors), gated by `prefers-reduced-motion`. `PipelinesPanel` wires `emptyTone()` (disabled/error/autonomy-blocked/ready/idle) and drops the redundant "queue/active" hint that duplicated the readiness banner in favor of forward-looking copy.
- **Agent-context session reaper retention default dropped 168h → 72h, and `agent_sessions_v1` gains datetime payload indexes** (`pkg/agentcontext/config.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/qdrant_indexes.go`, services/loom-core!341): two small independent slices ported from the long-closed !341 (its headline `session_list` fix already landed via !610; these two never did). (1) `SessionReaperMaxAge` default for ended/summarized sessions is now **72h (3 days)** instead of 168h (7 days) — cluster median session age is ~3 days, so 168h kept ~700 stale points warm for no listener; the `AGENT_CONTEXT_SESSION_REAPER_MAX_AGE_HOURS` env override is unchanged. (2) `EnsureCollection` now registers **datetime** payload indexes on `started_at`/`ended_at` for the sessions collection (new `datetimeIndexesByKind` + `EnsureDatetimeIndex`/`EnsureDatetimeIndexes`, applied idempotently alongside the existing keyword indexes in `ensureRegisteredIndexes`), so the time-windowed `session_list`/reaper queries use Qdrant range filters instead of a full scroll + post-filter in Go. Tests: `config_test.go` pins the 72h default + env override; `qdrant_indexes_test.go` captures the PUT bodies to assert sessions get keyword **and** datetime indexes while non-session kinds get keyword only.

### Fixed
- **mcp-gitlab `merge_merge_request` no longer feeds agents into blind 405-retry loops; `pipeline_summary` failures are actionable** (`cmd/mcp-gitlab/merge_requests.go`, `cmd/mcp-gitlab/pipeline_summary_polling.go`, `cmd/mcp-gitlab/utils.go`, `cmd/mcp-gitlab/merge_requests_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): the loom-flightdeck Stall Board (prod, 7-day pareto, 2026-06-10) showed `gitlab__merge_merge_request` (3 stalls, p95 ~19 min) and `gitlab__pipeline_summary` (2 stalls, p95 ~110 min) as the worst MCP repeated_tool_failure loops. Trigger (recorded in agent context, jobsearch-app session 2026-06-04): GitLab returns **405 Method Not Allowed** when merge/auto-merge is requested before the head pipeline is far enough along (and **406 Not Acceptable** while the async mergeability check runs or on a real conflict), and agents retried the opaque error in a loop. Now, when `auto_merge=true` gets a 405/406, the handler waits briefly for the MR's `head_pipeline` to exist (poll every 2s, up to 8s per attempt) and retries the merge request up to 2 times with backoff (~20s worst case); on persistent rejection it returns a structured `AUTO_MERGE_NOT_READY` error carrying the head pipeline id/status and explicit guidance — poll the pipeline with `poll_pipeline`/`pipeline_summary` until green and merge once, rebase on persistent 406 — instead of the raw status. Immediate (non-auto) merges keep the raw error path, single attempt. `pipeline_summary`'s audit found two opaque-failure modes: a 404 (pipeline id stale or not created yet — pipeline creation lags the push) surfaced as a bare API error, now a structured `PIPELINE_NOT_FOUND` error pointing at `list_pipelines` → `poll_pipeline`; and a jobs-fetch failure failed the whole call even when the pipeline fetch succeeded, now returned as a partial result (`partial: true`, `jobs_error`) with the pipeline payload intact (transient 5xx on either leg was already retried 3× in `doRequest`). Tests pin the 405-retry-success, persistent-405, 406-with-head-pipeline, no-auto-merge single-attempt, 404, and partial-jobs paths; retry timings are package vars so the tests run in milliseconds.
- **HUD shared widgets now use semantic palette tokens instead of legacy cyan/orange literals (Slice 10a)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/{MetricCard,DataTable,ConfirmDialog}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/{AgentCard,EntityGraph,ErrorBanner,Toast}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{MemoryPanel,graph/GraphFullView}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): narrows Slice 10 of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`) to the shared/widget layer so the higher-risk Presence/SpawnDetail palette pass can happen with its own visual review. Replaces old hardcoded status fills (`rgba(1,135,153,...)`, `rgba(34,178,85,...)`, `rgba(231,179,18,...)`, `rgba(230,30,63,...)`, `rgba(129,240,254,...)`) with `--info-dim`, `--success-dim`, `--warning-dim`, `--error-dim`, `--accent-dim`, or `color-mix()` off the semantic token; moves the toast stack from magic `top:52px` to `--header-height`; and derives EntityGraph's type ramp from the HUD token set instead of a private 10-hex palette. Also migrates the last two imports of the older `widgets/ConfirmDialog.svelte` (`MemoryPanel`, `GraphFullView`) to the focus-trapped shared dialog and deletes the stale wrapper. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt.
- **Generated Gemini CLI settings drop the invented `agentConfig` key; `loom sync gemini --regen` is schema-warning-free** (`pkg/generator/configs_gemini.go`, `pkg/generator/configs_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): every gemini sync logged `WARN [gemini] upstream schema: / - additionalProperties 'agentConfig' not allowed`. The key was introduced by commit 809562b6 (2026-04-08, a Gemini-CLI-agent-authored commit claiming to "migrate to agentConfig and hooksConfig" for deprecation warnings) — `hooksConfig` is real, but `agentConfig` (with `enabled`/`allowParallelSlices`/`worktreeManagement`) exists in **neither** the vendored upstream settings schema (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/main/schemas/settings.schema.json, `additionalProperties: false`) **nor** the installed binary: gemini-cli 0.43.0's bundled `SETTINGS_SCHEMA` was extracted from `/opt/homebrew/Cellar/gemini-cli/0.43.0/libexec/.../bundle/chunk-4IXP3BQP.js` and accepts exactly 24 top-level keys, none of them `agentConfig` (the string appears in the bundle only as the `/agents` config **dialog** id). A live probe (workspace `settings.json` containing the key, `gemini mcp list`) confirmed the CLI **silently ignores** it — exit 0, no warning — i.e. the key never did anything; the vendored schema was right and the generator was wrong (the `feedback_vendor_specs_first` failure class: agent commit-message claims about vendor behavior, unverified against the installed binary). The validator deliberately classifies unknown-key violations as warnings (schemas drift vs installed binaries), and `TestGeneratedGeminiSettingsValid` only failed on errors — so the bug was invisible to CI. That test now fails on warnings too, and a new `TestGeminiSettingsNoUnknownTopLevelKeys` asserts every top-level key in the registry-driven config exists in the vendored schema's `properties` (plus an explicit `agentConfig`-absent regression check); both verified to fail against the pre-fix generator. Verified live: rebuilt `loom` (both `~/.local/bin` and the PATH-shadowing `~/go/bin` copy, via `scripts/install_atomic.sh` — an in-place `cp` invalidates the macOS code signature and the binary is SIGKILLed) now logs `[gemini] settings.json validated against upstream schema` and the regenerated workspace + `~/.gemini/settings.json` contain zero `agentConfig` occurrences.
- **HUD live-event subscriptions now attach named SSE listeners dynamically, so Live Sessions receives `session.*`, `tool.call*`, and `agent.status.change` events again** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/events.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`): the browser `EventSource` API does not deliver named events through `onmessage`, but the HUD event store only registered a fixed allowlist that omitted the canonical daemon lifecycle/tool-call events consumed by `liveSessionsStore` (and could drift from other stores such as `hud.cost`, `hud.otel`, or `access.denied`). `eventStore.on(...)` now registers its event name with the active EventSource immediately, and reconnects reattach both the core documented events and all currently subscribed store names. This fixes Live Sessions rows/tool-call activity disappearing when the backend emits canonical named events instead of the older `agent.session.*` aliases. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **HUD state coherence: Mills disabled-chrome gate, truthful workflow section label, Now hero/inbox dedupe, dim zero instruments** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{OverviewPanel,WorkflowsPanel,DispatchPanel}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): four fixes from a live-render UX review (frontend-ux-craft pass, desktop 1280 + mobile 375 against the local daemon), all under one thesis — *no two elements on a view may disagree about system state*. (1) **Mills Overview contradicted itself when the operator is not configured**: the body showed the "Mills operator not configured" empty state while the header still rendered an actionable "Run council now →" idle banner, a "Pause autonomy" kill-switch, and a "Checking autonomy" chip — all of which would just error. The header snippet is now gated on `!disabled`, so the empty state is the single voice. (2) **Work › Workflows grouped every instance under a hardcoded "Running" heading** — live render showed a "RUNNING" section whose members were all `cancelled`. The label is now "Instances" (each row's status badge carries the state; the `cancelled` badge was already semantically `muted` — it merely *reads* lighter since 6b's AA lift of `--fg-muted`). (3) **The Now screen stated its top incident twice**: the hero ("Server health needs attention" → *Check servers*) and inbox card #1 ("4 servers down" → *Open Servers*) were the same fact with two differently-labelled CTAs. Each hero alert branch now declares the inbox-card kind it consumes (`file_conflict`/`server_down`/`blocked_task`/`pending_approval`) and the inbox filters that kind out — the inbox is "everything else". (4) **Dispatch's instrument strip rendered six zeros at full `--fg-primary` brightness** directly under a "2 agents need attention" headline — idle counters shouting as loudly as live signal. Zero values across all eight tiles now render `--fg-dim` (extending the !679 idle-tile pattern); non-zero keeps its semantic color. **Proof (headless live-render):** Mills disabled view contains none of the three chrome strings; section labels probe to `["Instances","Definitions"]`; "Server health needs attention" appears exactly once with only the hero CTA (`Open Servers` gone, pressure-point count excludes the hero's item); Dispatch tiles compute to `rgb(104,155,168)` (= `--fg-dim` #689BA8) for all six zeros and `--fg-primary` for the live `7`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **Generated Gemini CLI hooks no longer emit nested shell parameter defaults that Gemini's settings loader mangles** (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`, `pkg/generator/templates/extras/post_session_end_retrospective.json.tmpl`, services/loom-core!682): observed live on Gemini CLI 0.43.0 (2026-06-09) — every hook fire logged `mkdir: /Users/<user>}: Permission denied` and the agent-id cache file was never written. Root cause: Gemini expands `${VAR}` / `${VAR:-default}` in **settings.json strings at load time** with a non-brace-aware regex (`resolveEnvVarsInString`, [envVarResolver.ts](https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/blob/main/packages/cli/src/utils/envVarResolver.ts)), so `${HOME:-${TMPDIR:-/tmp}}` matched only to the first `}` and substituted to `/Users/<user>}` with a stray brace. The same pass replaces `${shellvar:-default}` with the *default* when the variable isn't in the CLI's environment, so `HOOK_INPUT="${INPUT:-}"` loaded as `HOOK_INPUT=""` — session-scoped agent IDs silently never worked on Gemini. `hookAgentIDBootstrap` (shared by Claude/Gemini/Antigravity; replacement is POSIX-equivalent everywhere) now derives the cache base sequentially (`AGENT_CACHE_BASE="$HOME"; [ -n … ] || AGENT_CACHE_BASE="${TMPDIR:-/tmp}"`), reads `$INPUT` without a brace-default, and bakes the scope dash into `SESSION_SCOPE` instead of the nested `${SESSION_SCOPE:+-${SESSION_SCOPE}}` (agent-id format unchanged). Same construct fixed in the session-retro extras template. Codex's `${INPUT:-${1:-}}` notify command is untouched (codex does not pre-interpolate). Guarded by `TestGeminiHooksConfig_NoNestedBraceDefaults`, which walks every generated gemini hook command and rejects nested `${…${…}…}` and `${INPUT:-…}`. Verified live: after `loom sync gemini --regen` with the fixed binary, a headless `gemini -p` run is clean and both `~/.cache/loom/agent-id-gemini-cli-<hash>` and the session-scoped `session-cwd-…-<scope>` stamp are written. Existing user-level configs stay broken until regenerated with a rebuilt `loom` binary.
- **HUD mobile breakpoints: Modal viewport clamp, Mills table scroll-wraps, audit/ensemble/role row reflows, Memory/BacklogDetail grid collapses (WCAG 1.4.10)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/Modal.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{MemoryPanel,WeaverPanel,ShuttlePanel,ContextHealthPanel}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/{AuditPanel,CouncilPanel,PipelinesPanel,BacklogPanel,EvalPanel,WorkflowsPanel,BacklogDetail}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): Slice **9** of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`) — every change is additive (`@media` blocks + overflow wrappers); desktop rendering is untouched. (1) **Modal** `min-width: 380px` overflowed a 360px phone; now `min(380px, calc(100vw - 32px))` (max-width clamped the same way) so every Modal consumer fits any viewport by CSS construction. (2) **Mills tables** (`Backlog`/`Council`/`Eval`/`Pipelines`/`Workflows`): the 6–10-column `.mills-table`s can't reflow, so each is wrapped in a `.mills-table-wrap { overflow-x: auto }` — the table scrolls inside the wrap instead of stretching the page. (3) **AuditPanel** `.audit-row-btn`'s 6-track grid (`1.2rem … 9rem 5rem 1fr`) folds at ≤720px into three explicit lines (subject·score / severity·cost / timestamp); **CouncilPanel** `.ensemble-row`'s fixed `11rem 5rem` rails fold to two lines (role·backend / model·status). (4) **MemoryPanel** tier overview (3-col) and compression strip (6-col) collapse at 800/480px; **WeaverPanel** `.role-row` (200px+220px rails) and `.status-grid` stack at 720px; **BacklogDetail**'s meta grid goes single-column at 480px. **Proof (real-render gate satisfied):** the working-tree frontend was served against the live local loomd (vite proxy → `:3333`) and driven headlessly at **360×640** — a programmatic probe asserted **0px horizontal page overflow** on every modified view, with live data for Memory/Weaver and **fixture-fed real components** for the Mills panels (local daemon has no Mills operator): audit row computed to the 3-track layout (`16.8/201/45px`, timestamp on grid-row 3), the Council table (527px) scrolled inside its 312px wrap. **Negative control:** at 1280×800 the original grids return (6-track audit row, 3-col tiers/roles) — the breakpoints gate both ways. Honest scope note: `ShuttlePanel.svelte` and `ContextHealthPanel.svelte` turned out to be **dead code** (imported nowhere, never bundled — their selectors don't ship in any dist chunk), so their `@media` additions are inert; kept because they're correct if the components are revived, and their removal is filed as follow-up cleanup. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **Embedded HUD "Request Metrics" card no longer 503s — `buildEmbeddedHUDConfig` now wires the daemon `MetricsAddr`** (`internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `internal/daemon/config.go`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): from the live-HUD triage. The Infrastructure › Servers "Request Metrics" card showed `Request metrics unavailable: Daemon metrics: 503`. Root cause: `handleDaemonMetrics` (`internal/hud/api_metrics.go:18`) returns **503 only** when `a.config.MetricsAddr == ""`, and the **embedded** HUD path (`loomd --hud-port` / the cluster `mobile-hud` deployment → `daemon.startEmbeddedHUD` → `buildEmbeddedHUDConfig`) built the entire `hud.Config` literal **without ever setting `MetricsAddr`** — so the daemon-metrics proxy had no upstream and 503'd on every embedded deployment. (The standalone `loom hud` path *does* wire it, `cmd/loom/hud.go:262` — the embedded path simply omitted the field.) Verified end-to-end against the live cluster: `mobile-hud` runs `loomd --metrics-addr 127.0.0.1:9876 --hud-port 3333` (ports `9876/metrics`, `3333/http`), so the daemon's `/metrics` **is** served on loopback — only the HUD's knowledge of the address was missing. Fix: a new `EmbeddedHUDConfig.MetricsAddr` (`yaml: metrics_addr`) plumbed through `buildEmbeddedHUDConfig` as `firstNonEmpty(cfg.MetricsAddr, $LOOM_DAEMON_METRICS_ADDR, "127.0.0.1:9876")` — the loopback default matches the address loomd always serves (it appends `127.0.0.1:9876` as a compat endpoint even under a custom `--metrics-addr`). This is **proxy-only**: it does **not** start the standalone SSE event consumer (that path is `Run()`-exclusive; the embedded HUD ingests events in-process via `IngestDaemonEvent`, confirmed at `runtime.go:45` vs `hud_embed.go:60`). Tests: `hud_embed_test.go` pins default/env/file-config precedence. Backend-only (no `dist` change); takes effect on the next `loom-core` image rebuild + `mobile-hud` rollout.
- **Mills Overview "Pipeline runs" tile reads the KPI 24h window count; idle KPI tiles read as idle, not broken** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/MetricCard.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): from the live-HUD triage. The "Pipeline runs" tile bound `millsStore.pipelineRuns.length` — the **active-only** `/api/mills/pipeline/runs` list — which duplicated the adjacent "Active runs" tile and ignored the authoritative window count; it now reads `metrics.pipeline_runs` from the KPI snapshot (runs in the last 24h). Separately, the "Gate pass" and "Cost / merged" tiles rendered a bare `-` whenever their ratio was absent, which reads as *broken* when it actually means *idle*: the operator only emits `gate_pass_rate` / `cost_per_merged_*` once their denominator is non-zero (`kpi_writer.go`: `if gateTotal > 0` / `if mergedRuns > 0`), so on a 24h window with 0 gate evaluations / 0 merges the fields are correctly omitted. Verified against the live operator (`/api/mills/kpis?window=1d` → `gate_evaluations:0`, `pipeline_merged_runs:0`) — the dashes were honest. Those tiles now distinguish **idle** (snapshot present, ratio absent → dimmed `—` + a tooltip "No gate evaluations / merges in the last 24h (idle, not an error)") from **unknown** (no snapshot → "KPI snapshot unavailable"), via a new optional `hint` (title tooltip) prop on `MetricCard`. No operator change; HUD-only. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **Mills › Workflows list now carries a real `step_count` per run, so the table renders the actual step count instead of a permanent `—`** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_workflow.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_workflow.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): the backend follow-up the Work › Workflows fix (!TBD, the `0/0 steps` slice) filed. The run-LIST payload (`GET /api/mills/workflow/runs`) was "deliberately a flat summary" that omitted `step_count`, so every Mills › Workflows row showed `STEPS —` even when the run had a populated step journal (the detail endpoint already reported the count). A new batch DAO method `WorkflowDAO.CountStepsByRun(ctx, runIDs)` runs a single `GROUP BY` over the bounded id set the list already fetched (no N+1 scan of `workflow_steps`), and `handleWorkflowRunsList` joins it into each `workflowRunSummary`. `step_count` drops its `omitempty` so a run with no steps reports an honest `0` rather than being indistinguishable from an old operator build that omitted the field — the HUD keeps its `?? '—'` fallback so the rollout window (old image still serving the omitted field) degrades to the prior em-dash, not a literal 0. No HUD/`dist` change needed (the panel already binds `r.step_count`). Requires an operator image rebuild + Flux reconcile to take effect. Tests: `handlers_workflow_test.go` asserts the list rows carry `step_count` (WF-LIVE=2, WF-QUAR=1); `dao_workflow_list_test.go` pins the batch counter (multi-run counts, steps-less/unknown ids absent, empty input short-circuit).
- **HUD Work › Workflows list no longer shows a misleading `0/0 steps`; DAG node labels widened + tooltipped** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/WorkflowsPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/DagView.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): caught from live renders of `hud.flexinfer.ai`. (1) **`0/0 steps` bug:** every running workflow in the Work › Workflows list rendered `0/0 steps` even when its detail showed real steps (e.g. `Progress: 0/3` with a 3-node DAG). Root cause: the SSE summary the list binds to (`stores/workflows.svelte.ts` `applySnapshot`) carries `progress` + `current_step` but **not** the `steps[]` array, so `stepProgress()` always returned `'0/0'` for list rows (the detail header, fed by the REST `/api/workflows/{id}` payload, *does* have `steps[]`, so it was correct). A new `listProgressLabel()` prefers a real step count when present, else the `progress` % (which already drove the row's progress bar), else the `current_step` name — never a false `0/0`. The detail header keeps `stepProgress()` unchanged. (2) **DAG labels:** `DagView` hard-truncated node names to 13 chars inside 120px nodes with no tooltip, so `Validate canonical…` etc. were unreadable despite a mostly-empty canvas. Nodes widened to 148px, the truncation threshold raised to 22 chars, a `<title>` hover tooltip added with the full name, and the `min-width:100%` SVG stretch (which wasted vertical space) replaced with horizontal centering at natural size. Scope note: the sibling **Mills › Workflows** list shows `STEPS —` for the same reason, but its summary omits `step_count` *by design* at the operator boundary (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_workflow.go` — "deliberately a flat summary"), so the honest `—` there needs an operator-service change + redeploy and is filed as a backend follow-up rather than forced into this client-side slice. No `vitest`/`svelte-check` harness — documented exception; verified by rebuilt-bundle inspection. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **HUD `--fg-dim`/`--fg-muted` lifted to clear WCAG AA contrast; failing focus outlines moved to a new `--focus-ring` token (WCAG 1.4.3 / 1.4.11)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/DataTable.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/MetricCard.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/UnseenAboveChip.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): Slice **6b** of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`), the contrast/focus-indicator half of Slice 6 (the reduced-motion half shipped as 6a, !665). It had been deferred because the plan's suggested replacement hexes (`--fg-dim:#7AA0AC` / `--fg-muted:#6FA0AD`) *inverted* the dim↓muted luminance tiering (dim ended up lighter than muted). (1) **Text contrast (`a11y-fg-dim-fails-wcag-aa`, WCAG 1.4.3):** the old `--fg-dim: #355B66` reached only **1.97:1** against `--bg-elevated` and `--fg-muted: #5C8A96` only **3.84:1** — both below the 4.5:1 AA floor on the two lightest surfaces. They're lifted along the *existing* teal ramp (same R≈0.62·B, G≈0.92·B ratio as the original tokens, so hue is preserved) to **`--fg-dim: #689BA8`** (worst-case **4.75:1**, the minimal value that clears AA so the token stays perceptibly "dim") and **`--fg-muted: #77B1C0`** (worst-case **6.14:1**), which keeps the intended `dim < muted < secondary < primary` lightness order (L = 0.292 < 0.392 < 0.476 < 0.817) — correcting the plan's inversion. Verified by a relative-luminance script against all five `--bg-*` tokens. (2) **Focus indicators (`a11y-border-focus-contrast`, WCAG 1.4.11):** the global `button:focus-visible` outline plus the clickable-row (`DataTable`), clickable-card (`MetricCard`) and `UnseenAboveChip` focus outlines all drew with `--border-focus` (#2F6170), a non-text element that reaches only **2.1–2.9:1** against the backgrounds (below the 3:1 floor). A new **`--focus-ring: var(--info)`** token (#00C8FF, **7.4–10.0:1**) is introduced and those four outlines now reference it; `DataTable`'s sortable-`th` outline (already `--info`) is pointed at the token too, so every focus outline shares one source of truth. Decorative `border-color: var(--border-focus)` hovers are left untouched (Slice 10 palette territory). No `vitest`/`svelte-check` harness — documented exception carried from prior slices; the WCAG math *is* the kill-test. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`). With 6a + 6b, HUD audit **Slice 6 is complete.**
- **HUD keyboard-help overlay traps focus + has a close button; accordion toggles expose `aria-expanded` (WCAG 2.4.3 / 4.1.2)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/OverlayShell.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/CatalogPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): Slice 7c of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`), the overlay/accordion follow-up to the dialog (7a) and drawer (7b) focus work. (1) The **keyboard-shortcut help overlay** (`?`) had three a11y gaps: its backdrop was a `role="presentation"` element carrying an `onclick` (presentation role on an interactive node — contradictory; `a11y-keyboard-help-presentation-role`); the `role="dialog"` panel had `tabindex="-1"` but **no focus management**, so opening it left focus behind and Tab walked out; and it had **no visible close control** (Escape/backdrop-click only). The backdrop drops `role="presentation"` (it's now a plain presentational click-to-close `div`, `svelte-ignore`d, with Escape still wired in the global keydown handler), the dialog gains `use:focusTrap` (the shared action from 7a — focus-in on open, Tab/Shift+Tab wrap, restore on close), and a labelled `✕` close button (`aria-label="Close"`) is added top-right. (2) Three **accordion toggle buttons** in `OverlayShell` (the section / namespace / session expanders) tracked their open state only via a `class:expanded` / chevron glyph, with **no `aria-expanded`** — so screen-reader users couldn't perceive collapsed-vs-expanded (`a11y-overlay-section-no-aria-expanded`); each now exposes `aria-expanded={…}` bound to the same store state that drives its chevron. (3) `CatalogPanel`'s server-row expand button (`.name-btn`) gets the same `aria-expanded` treatment. (The broader icon-only-button `aria-label` sweep the plan grouped here was a no-op — the visible icon-only controls in `CatalogPanel`/`PresencePanel`/`CreateTaskModal` were already labelled by the 7a/7b passes.) Verified in the rebuilt bundle: `aria-expanded` ships and the keyboard-help backdrop no longer carries `role="presentation"`. No `vitest`/`svelte-check` harness — documented exception carried from 7a/7b. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`). With 7a + 7b + 7c, HUD audit **Slice 7 (focus trap + dialog/overlay focus management) is complete.**
- **HUD slide-in drawers now trap Tab focus, not just initial focus (WCAG 2.4.3)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/DetailDrawer.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/action/AuditDrawer.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelineRunDetail.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): Slice 7b of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`), the drawer follow-up to the dialog core in 7a. `DetailDrawer` — the **shared** right-edge drill-down panel behind six consumers (`MemoryPanel`, `tasks/TaskDetail`, `graph/GraphFullView`, `fleet/SessionDetail`, `servers/ServerDetail`, `Mills/BacklogDetail`) — claimed in its own doc comment to "trap focus when open" but actually only moved focus **in** on open and restored it on close; there was no Tab handler, so Tab/Shift+Tab walked straight out of the drawer into the obscured page behind it (WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order). It now delegates to the shared `focusTrap` action introduced in 7a — which adds the missing Tab/Shift+Tab wrap while preserving the same focus-in/restore behavior — replacing ~18 lines of hand-rolled `requestAnimationFrame` + `querySelector` + `previousFocus` bookkeeping (the action covers all six consumers at once). The two **standalone** drawers — `AuditDrawer` (recent-actions log) and `Mills/PipelineRunDetail` (the pipeline-run drill-down) — were `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` `<aside>`s with Escape-to-close but **no** focus management at all; both now carry `use:focusTrap`, so opening them moves focus inside, Tab cycles within, and closing returns focus to the row/button that opened them. Each drawer keeps its existing Escape handler and labelled close button, so the trap never strands focus. Proof is build + bundle inspection (the rebuilt bundle no longer contains `DetailDrawer`'s old hand-rolled "Focus first focusable element inside drawer" path; the shared action's behavior ships) — the HUD frontend has no `vitest`/`svelte-check` harness, a documented exception carried over from 7a. The remaining slice-7 surfaces (App keyboard-help overlay, `OverlayShell` `aria-expanded` toggles, and the broader icon-button `aria-label` sweep) are deferred to a "7c" follow-up. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **HUD dialogs trap and restore keyboard focus; toasts announce politely (WCAG 2.4.3 / 4.1.3)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/actions/focusTrap.ts` (new), `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/Modal.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/ConfirmDialog.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/widgets/Toast.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, services/loom-core!TBD): Slice 7 (core dialog half, "7a") of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`). The HUD's reusable dialogs (`Modal`, `ConfirmDialog`) had **no focus management** — opening a modal left focus on the element behind it, Tab walked out of the dialog into the obscured page, and on close focus was lost to `<body>` (WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order; `a11y-modal-no-focus-mgmt`, `a11y-detail-drawer-no-focus-trap`). Adds a single reusable `focusTrap` Svelte action (snapshot the active element on mount → focus the first focusable inside → wrap Tab/Shift+Tab at the boundaries → restore focus to the opener on destroy; Escape-to-close stays owned by each dialog so the trap never strands focus), applied via `use:focusTrap` on `Modal`'s `.modal` and `ConfirmDialog`'s `.confirm-dialog`. Companion a11y fixes in the same surfaces: `Modal`'s backdrop was wrongly a full-screen `role="button"`/`tabindex="0"` "Close modal" control (screen readers announced a giant button covering the page) — it's now a presentational click-to-close `div` (`svelte-ignore`d) with dismissal via Escape + the labelled `✕` close button, which gains `aria-label="Close"` (`a11y-modal-backdrop-button-role`, `a11y-icon-only-buttons-missing-labels`). `Toast`'s container is now a **persistent** `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="false"` live region (previously the whole container was conditionally added/removed, so additions were never announced) and the icon-only dismiss button gains `aria-label="Dismiss notification"` (`a11y-toast-no-live-region`). Verified in the rebuilt bundle: the focusable-element selector literal (`button:not([disabled])`) and the new `aria-live`/`aria-label="Close"`/`aria-label="Dismiss notification"` strings all ship. Proof is build + bundle-presence + the action's deterministic logic — the HUD frontend has **no** unit-test harness (no `vitest`/`svelte-check` in `package.json`), so the plan's vitest gate is a documented exception; the remaining slice-7 surfaces (`AuditDrawer`, `PipelineRunDetail`, `DetailDrawer`, the App keyboard-help overlay, `OverlayShell` `aria-expanded`, and the broader icon-button/`aria-expanded` sweep) are deferred to a "7b" follow-up. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`).
- **HUD honors `prefers-reduced-motion` (WCAG 2.3.3) and drops the dead `OTelStatusSection` component** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css`, `internal/hud/frontend/dist/`, removes `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/OTelStatusSection.svelte`, services/loom-core!665): Slice 6 (reduced-motion half) of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`). The HUD ran several ambient **infinite** animations — `pulse`, `breathe` (`.status-breathe`), `shimmer` (skeleton loaders), `ringPulse`, `glowPulse`, `dataFlash`, `shake`, and the `animated-dots` ellipsis — plus transitions on nearly every interactive surface, with **no** `prefers-reduced-motion` guard anywhere in `theme.css`, violating WCAG 2.3.3 (Animation from Interactions) for vestibular-/motion-sensitive users. Adds a single global `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block that collapses `animation-duration`/`transition-duration` to `0.01ms`, pins `animation-iteration-count: 1`, and forces `scroll-behavior: auto` on `*, *::before, *::after` — so when the OS reports the preference every ambient loop stops and transitions are effectively instant, while still firing exactly one `animationend`/`transitionend` (duration is `0.01ms`, not `none`) so any JS awaiting those events keeps working. The fix is **additive and global**: no per-component edits, and **zero rendering change** for users without the preference set. Companion cleanup: `OTelStatusSection.svelte` had **no importers** (confirmed via grep across `.svelte`/`.ts`) and never appeared in the built bundle, so the 139-line dead component is removed (closes the `tokens-otel-status-section-dead-code` finding). The **contrast-lift half** of Slice 6 (`--fg-dim`/`--fg-muted` AA bump) is deliberately **deferred** — the plan's suggested hex values (`--fg-dim: #7AA0AC` vs `--fg-muted: #6FA0AD`) actually **invert** the dim/muted luminance tiering, so they need recomputation plus a real render review before shipping. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt (`make hud-frontend`); verified `grep -c prefers-reduced-motion internal/hud/frontend/dist/assets/index-*.css` ≥ 1.
- **Cluster HUD presence/fleet stops showing "0 ACTIVE AGENTS" — bump mcp-go to the dash-less TOON decoder fix** (`go.mod`, `go.sum`, services/loom-core!TBD): the MCP hub serializes `agent_presence_list` results as a TOON list-of-objects in the **dash-less** form (`agents[N]:` followed by 4-space-indented object fields with no leading `-`), but the mcp-go TOON decoder pinned in `go.mod` (`v0.2.1-0.20260520023524-aa57e61f11bd`) rejected that shape — so loomd's `bridge`/`mirror` `unmarshalToolText` → `mcp.DecodeTOONToJSON` decoded the presence payload to nothing and the cluster HUD (`hud.flexinfer.ai`, the `mobile-hud` deployment) rendered **"0 ACTIVE AGENTS"** even with a populated roster. The decoder fix landed on mcp-go `main` (`fa6daf9` "decode dash-less list-of-objects for cross-version compat" + `aa12cdf` "parse dash-less list items at their actual field indent"; merges `c878f6a`/`cf46de1`) and the **local** Mac loomd was already rebuilt against it, but the **container** build resolves mcp-go from the pinned `go.mod` pseudo-version (`Dockerfile` builds with `GOWORK=off`, so `go.work`'s local `libs/mcp-go` path is bypassed) — so the cluster image kept shipping the old decoder. Bumps the pin `aa57e61f11bd → v0.2.1-0.20260606235418-cf46de19e009` (mcp-go `main` tip, includes both fix commits); the module's own `go.mod` hash is unchanged (behavior-only fix, no API surface change). Verified container-equivalent: `CGO_ENABLED=0 GOWORK=off go build ./...` and `go test ./internal/hud/...` (incl. `bridge`/`mirror`) both pass against the new pin, and `parseDashlessObjectItem` is present in the downloaded `cf46de19e009` module bytes. Once the loom-core image rebuilds and Flux rolls out the new `mobile-hud` image, `hud.flexinfer.ai` presence/fleet populates. Ref memory `project_hud_toon_dashless_decode`; upstream fix libs/mcp-go!5+!6.
- **HUD components stop silently rendering hardcoded hex fallbacks; keyboard users now see a focus ring on every button** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/styles/theme.css`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/ViewShell.svelte`, services/loom-core!642): a multi-dimension HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`) found ~250 occurrences of `var(--TOKEN, #HEX)` across Mills, `LiveSessionsCard`, `AlertsPanel`, `SpawnDetailPanel`, `ConfirmDialog`, `WeaverPanel`, and others that referenced tokens which **did not exist** in `theme.css` (the canonical palette is `--fg-*` / `--bg-*` / `--success` / `--error` / `--warning` / `--info`, but the components reached for `--text-primary`, `--text-secondary`, `--text-muted`, `--text-dim`, `--color-success`, `--color-info`, `--color-warn`, `--color-warning`, `--color-error`, `--color-danger`, `--color-muted`, `--surface-1` / `--surface-2` / `--surface-3`, `--bg-subtle`, `--danger`, `--scrim`). Because the tokens were undefined, every `var()` resolved to its hex fallback — so the brand palette never reached those panels and any future palette tweak silently failed to propagate. Fix is **additive aliasing**: a `:root` alias block maps the phantom families to canonical tokens (`--text-*` → `--fg-*`, `--color-*` → semantic, `--surface-*` → `--bg-*`, `--danger` → `--error`) plus a new `--scrim: rgba(6, 12, 16, 0.6)` token. ~250 refs collapse to canonical in one commit with zero per-component source edits. Companion a11y fix: the global `button { border: none; background: none }` reset stripped the native focus ring and there was no themed fallback, so keyboard users had no focus indication on dozens of clickable surfaces. Adds a global `button:focus-visible` rule (`outline: 2px solid var(--border-focus)`) plus dedicated `.nav-tab:focus-visible` (`App.svelte`) and `.view-tab:focus-visible` (`ViewShell.svelte`) rules for the primary + sub-tab bars (using `var(--info)` so the focus ring matches the active-tab accent). Embedded `dist/` rebuilt. Verified in the bundled CSS: `--text-primary: var(--fg-primary)`, `--color-error: var(--error)`, and `button:focus-visible` all present.
- **HUD `EmptyState` accepts `message` as an alias of `heading`, and several clickable-but-non-button surfaces are now keyboard-accessible** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/EmptyState.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/WeaverPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/CouncilPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/DispatchPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/WorkflowsPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/ReasoningPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/MemoryPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/StreamPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/TracesPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/SquadsPanel.svelte`, services/loom-core!644): Slice 4 of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`) chases three correlated defects. (1) `EmptyState.svelte` only accepted `heading`, but Weaver's two visible empty states and a handful of dormant Alerts/Shuttle sites passed `message="…"` — Svelte silently dropped the unknown prop, so those empties rendered with no copy. `EmptyState` now also accepts a `message` prop and derives the headline as `heading || message || 'No data yet'` so callers using either name get their text; WeaverPanel's two visible call sites are renamed to the canonical `heading=` at the same time. (2) `CouncilPanel.svelte`'s run-detail `<div class="row-summary">` was a click-only toggle (the `<!-- svelte-ignore a11y_click_events_have_key_events a11y_no_static_element_interactions -->` was the only thing keeping the build quiet) — keyboard users could not expand a run. It now follows the established `BacklogPanel.svelte:117-125` pattern: `role="button"`, `tabindex="0"`, `aria-expanded={isOpen}`, `aria-label="Toggle run …"`, and an `onkeydown` handler that fires the same `toggle()` on Enter/Space. (3) `DispatchPanel.svelte`'s four sortable `<th class="sortable">` headers (Agent, Tasks, Blockers, Attention) had `onclick` but no keyboard equivalent and no `aria-sort` — keyboard and AT users could neither invoke nor perceive the sort. New `ariaSortFor(key)` and `onSortKeydown(ev, key)` helpers add `role="button"`, `tabindex="0"`, a live `aria-sort` (`ascending`/`descending`/`none`), and an Enter/Space-to-sort handler on every sortable header. (4) Adds `:focus-visible` outlines (`outline: 2px solid var(--info); outline-offset: 2px; border-radius: var(--radius-sm);`) on eight interactive non-`<button>` surfaces that previously had hover styling but no focus ring: `.row-summary` (CouncilPanel), `th.sortable` (DispatchPanel), `.wf-item` (WorkflowsPanel), `.chain-header` (ReasoningPanel), `.tier-tab` (MemoryPanel), `.filter-chip` (StreamPanel + TracesPanel), `.domain-header` (WeaverPanel), and `.squad-name-btn` (Mills/SquadsPanel) — so keyboard focus is visible everywhere clickable. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt; the dist bundle's `focus-visible` occurrences jump to ≥13 in `index-*.css`.
- **HUD panels now surface store fetch errors instead of going silently stale** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/PanelShell.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{MemoryPanel,KnowledgePanel,TopologyPanel,ShuttlePanel,StreamPanel,TimelinePanel}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/servers/{InfraCards,RequestMetricsCard}.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/timeline.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!TBD): Slice 5 of the HUD audit (`.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`) — a systemic gap where ~13 panels' Svelte stores **set** `.error` on failed fetches (`memoryStore`, `knowledgeStore`, `topologyStore`, `daemonMetricsStore`, `streamStore`, `timelineStore`, etc.) but the panels never **read or rendered** it. When loomd was down, the proxy 5xx'd, or a single refresh failed mid-session, the panel just kept showing its last cached state — operators saw stale rows with no signal anything had broken, and a cold-start error was indistinguishable from "no rows yet". **Engine**: `PanelShell.svelte` gains an `error?: string | null` prop + an `errorAction?` snippet slot; the content area's render precedence becomes `loading → error → empty → children`, with a new red-bordered `.error-state` block mirroring the existing `.empty-state` shape (icon `⚠`, error text, optional retry-button slot) using `var(--error)` / `color-mix` against `var(--bg-secondary)` for the tinted background. **Consumer panels** (panels already pulling from a store with `.error`): **MemoryPanel** adds the CatalogPanel-style `.error-banner` above the items list (`role=alert`/`aria-live=polite`, auto-clears when the next fetch starts since the store resets `error → null`) AND closes the empty-state flash on tier-switch/refetch by gating it on `lastUpdated && !loading` (was `sortedItems.length === 0` alone, which fired in the brief window when the previous fetch had finished but the next one hadn't populated yet). **KnowledgePanel** + **MemoryPanel** match CatalogPanel's banner styling so all three look like one family. **TopologyPanel** doesn't use PanelShell; its render now branches `error && !hasData` → "Topology unavailable" / `loading && !hasData` → "Loading topology…" / `!hasData` → "No agents registered" / else → the graph (was: indistinguishable blank canvas for all three states). **ShuttlePanel** swaps two `console.error` calls in `fetchPolicies` + `savePolicies` catches for `toastStore.error(...)` (a 5xx on the policies route was previously logged only to the dev console) and adds `toastStore.info('Policies saved')` on the save happy-path. **InfraCards** mirrors the Cache card's 3-state pattern onto the **Tunnels** branch: `tunnels` initialized to `null` (initial/never-fetched) so it's distinguishable from `[]` (fetched, none active); `tunnelsError`/`cacheError` snapshot the shared `healthStore.error` per-call so a failure is attributable to the right card; an in-card error chip renders for both branches. **RequestMetricsCard** previously rendered the whole card only when `servers.length > 0` (a failed `/metrics` fetch hid the card entirely); the card shell now always renders and the body branches `error → in-card .metrics-error banner` / `loading + no rows → 3-row shimmer skeleton` / `!loading + no rows → "No request metrics" EmptyState` / else → the table. **StreamPanel** + **TimelinePanel** swap their empty-state heading to the error text when `store.error` is non-null AND `filtered.length === 0` (e.g. "Stream unavailable: …" instead of "No activity yet"). **timeline.svelte.ts** gains `lastUpdated` + `staleAfter=120_000` + an `isStale` getter (mirroring `streamStore`) and registers with `stalenessStore.register('timeline', …)` so the `ConnectionBanner` surfaces a stale-timeline pill when SSE silently drops or no agent events have hit the bus in 120s — completes the staleness cohort for the live-event family. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt; the bundle now ships **17** `error-banner`/`error-state` class references (was 6) — `+11` new rules across the new PanelShell engine block, the three banner sites, and the in-card error chips.
- **HUD SpawnTelemetry tabs render empty/error states via the shared `EmptyState` component and surface stream/auth context** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/SpawnTelemetry/{ActivityTab,UsageTab,TranscriptTab,ToolsTab,FilesTab,ErrorsTab}.svelte`, slice 8 of `.loom/hud-audit-plan-2026-06-06.md`): all six SpawnDetailPanel tabs rendered their empty and error branches as bare `<div class="tab-empty">` / `<div class="tab-error">` blocks with inconsistent text and no icon / heading / hint hierarchy, so the panel's six sub-views looked unrelated to each other and to the rest of the HUD's empty-state language. ActivityTab also conflated **two distinct empty states** — "no events yet" and "SSE stream is offline" — into one ambiguous "No activity events yet" message, hiding a connection failure from the operator; UsageTab silently rendered "No telemetry yet" when the real reason was a missing Labs admin token (the loader never even ran). The six tabs now import `../shared/EmptyState.svelte` and use it with `icon` / `heading` / `description` + `compact`. ActivityTab gates on `eventStore.connectionState`: `'disconnected'` / `'circuit-open'` → ⚠ *"Activity stream offline — Event stream is &lt;state&gt;. Activity will resume when reconnected."*; otherwise the previously-bare empty becomes ◯ *"No activity yet — Spawn activity will stream here as the agent runs."* UsageTab adds a `labsAuthStore.hasToken` short-circuit so the no-token case renders 🔑 *"Labs admin token required — Add a Labs admin token to view usage telemetry."* instead of the misleading "No telemetry yet" (kept for the token-present-no-data case). TranscriptTab / ToolsTab / FilesTab / ErrorsTab map their error branches to a ⚠ EmptyState carrying the same error text as the description, and their empty branches to per-tab icon + heading + hint (💬, 🔧, 📄; ErrorsTab's normal payload is errors so its empty state is ✓ *"No errors recorded — The spawn has not reported any errors."*). The now-unreferenced `.tab-empty` / `.tab-error` local CSS rules are deleted; `grep -c 'tab-empty\|tab-error' internal/hud/frontend/dist/assets/index-*.css` is `0`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt. Does not touch `EmptyState.svelte` itself or any other panel.
- **HUD Fleet "Live Agents" table cells no longer float in the vertical middle of each row** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/fleet/FleetTable.svelte`, services/loom-core!641): the Operations → Fleet table rendered every single-line cell (status dot, evidence pills, namespace, activity, heartbeat, Session/Traces buttons) centered in the row's full height, disconnected from the agent it described — and the `Heartbeat` header truncated to `HEARTBE…`. `DataTable` sets `vertical-align: middle` on every `td`; the fleet *agent* cell is a tall multi-line stack (id + meta-row + hierarchy pills), so each sibling cell centered itself in that tall row instead of sitting on the agent's top baseline. The intended `vertical-align: top` override existed only on the agent cell, written as `:global(.data-table.stable-layout) tbody td.agent-cell` — but that split form (a scoped `tbody td` combinator **outside** `:global()`) targets DataTable's internal DOM, which carries no FleetTable scope hash, so the Svelte compiler **dropped it as an unused selector** (verified: zero `vertical-align:top` in the prior bundle; the agent cell's `white-space: normal`/`word-break` were silently dropped too). Fix: both the agent-cell override and a new **all-cells** top-align rule now live fully inside `:global()`, anchored on `.fleet-table-card` so they stay confined to this table (specificity `0,3,2` beats DataTable's `0,1,2` default); sibling cells get the agent cell's top padding so all columns share one baseline; the Heartbeat column widens `80px → 108px`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt. Verified against the compiled CSS (rule present + specificity) and a before/after harness rendered with the real bundle.
- **Mills codex spawns pin `--model` to a ChatGPT-account-supported model (Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge blocker)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`, services/loom-core!640): the live A2 kill-test on 2026-06-06 (`gitops!230` flipped prod `stage_substrate`→harvester-vm; canary `MILLS-CANARY-A2-20260606-011720`; reverted `gitops!231`) proved the prior four fixes work — codex had the CLI + workspace, did not hang on stdin, authenticated via OAuth (`auth_mode: "chatgpt"`), and **started a turn** — then failed at the *first* spawn (`plan_slice`, on k8s; `implement` never reached a VM) with **HTTP 400 `invalid_request_error`: "The 'gpt-5.3-codex' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account."** `buildAgentCommand` ran `codex exec` with **no `--model`**, so codex used its CLI default (`gpt-5.3-codex`), which OpenAI **deprecated for ChatGPT-account (sign-in-with-ChatGPT) auth** ([developers.openai.com/codex/models](https://developers.openai.com/codex/models), fetched 2026-06-06: supported with ChatGPT sign-in are `gpt-5.5`/`gpt-5.4`/`gpt-5.4-mini`; `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` is ChatGPT-Pro only; deprecated: `gpt-5.2`, `gpt-5.3-codex`). The substrate-independent 400 escalated cleanly via the `nonempty_diff` gate (no junk MR). The codex exec now appends `--model <resolveCodexModel()>`: new `resolveCodexModel()` returns `SPAWN_CODEX_MODEL` (trimmed) or the `defaultCodexModel` constant (`gpt-5.5`) — the env override lets operators retune **without a rebuild** the next time OpenAI shifts the supported set (the failure class itself). The model is `shellQuote`d like the prompt. Tests: `TestBuildAgentCommand` now asserts `--model 'gpt-5.5'` present and `gpt-5.3-codex` absent; new `TestResolveCodexModel` covers default, env override, and blank-override-falls-back. Plan: `.loom/129`; evidence: `.loom/local/handoffs/mills-harvester-vm-slice-a2-killtest-2026-06-05.md`. (The SDK-driver path `buildSDKDriverCommand` is not the prod codex route — the canary ran `use_sdk_driver=false`.)
- **HUD now groups Live Sessions and Fleet "Live Agents" by workspace-agent root, and agent counts dedupe per-conversation `agent_id`s** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/agents.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/utils/fleetRows.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{liveSessions,fleet}.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/{LiveSessionsCard.svelte,fleet/FleetTable.svelte}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`): the lifecycle hooks mint `agent_id` **per conversation** as `<base>-<WS_HASH>-<SESSION_SCOPE>` (`pkg/generator/configs_hooks.go`), where `WS_HASH` is `cksum` of the git workspace root (stable per workspace) and `SESSION_SCOPE` is `cksum` of the conversation id (changes every conversation). One agent running several conversations therefore showed as several unrelated agents — `claude-code-552019522-2804496862` + `claude-code-552019522-3116397616` (same repo), `codex-4188162495` + `codex-4188162495-2303882182` — rendered as flat sibling rows, and the "Active Agents" headline / nav badge / footer "N live agents" over-counted (6 sessions read as "6 agents" when there were 5). The bug was purely in the deployed-HUD display layer: `parent_session_id`/`root_session_id` are *correctly* null for sibling top-level conversations (they are siblings, not parent/child), so the existing session-tree grouping (`fleet.svelte.ts` `sessionTree`) could never collapse them. New `rootAgentId()` strips the trailing session-scope segment (everything up to and including the first all-numeric segment; ids with no numeric segment pass through) and `groupSessionsByRootAgent()` collapses sessions by it. The Live Sessions card renders one header per logical agent with sessions nested; `summarizeUnifiedAgents().live_agents` + new `fleetStore.liveAgentCount` count distinct roots. `buildFleetRows` buckets session-tree roots by `rootAgentId` so sibling conversations nest one level under a lead (flagged `agentRootChild` → "same agent" pill instead of "root session"), with "N root groups" counting distinct workspace agents; genuine Mills/Weaver subagent hierarchies (real parent linkage) are preserved inside each bucket. Regression: runnable `agents.rootGrouping.fixture.ts` (17 assertions: `rootAgentId` across real id shapes, 6→5 collapse, count dedup) and `fleetRows.rootAgent.fixture.ts` (11 assertions: 2-root-group collapse, depth-1 nesting, `agentRootChild`), both via `pnpm dlx tsx`. Embedded `dist/` rebuilt.
- **Mills `plan_slice` no longer dies on prompt backticks run as shell commands (Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge blocker)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): `buildAgentCommand` interpolated the agent prompt with Go `%q` into a command string executed via `sh -c` (`backend.StreamExec` / `K8sBackend.Exec`). `%q` yields a *Go* string literal, not a shell-safe argument, so shell metacharacters in the prompt stayed live — backticks and `$(...)` were evaluated as command substitution before the agent CLI ran. The Mills canary `SpecDoc` (`cmd/loom/cmd_mills_pipelines.go`) wraps the fixture path and backlog id in backticks (`` `testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md` ``, `` `MILLS-CANARY-...` ``), so on the k8s `plan_slice` spawn path (run `PIPE-MILLS-CANARY-20260605-145334`) the shell tried to execute them — `sh: testdata/mills-canary/heartbeat.md: Permission denied` / `sh: MILLS-CANARY-...: not found` — and the agent received a prompt with the backtick spans silently stripped, blocking the first autonomous merge (north-star `autonomous_merges_24h` = 0). Affected all three agent types (claude-code, codex, gemini); the `buildSDKDriverCommand` path already used `shellQuote` and was unaffected. Swaps `%q` → `shellQuote(task)` on all three legacy CLI paths so every prompt metacharacter is passed through literally; `agentID` stays `%q` (controlled identifier inside the trap's single-quoted body). Complements the codex `< /dev/null` stdin-hang fix on the same line. Regression: `TestBuildAgentCommand_PromptShellQuotedAgainstInjection` executes the built command under a real shell with stubbed agent binaries and asserts a backtick/`$()`/`$VAR`-bearing prompt reaches the CLI verbatim with no shell evaluation (fails on the old `%q` form for all three agents).
- **Mills classifies the k8s exec-stream `502 Bad Gateway` (kubelet dial) as transient, not a code bug** (`pkg/mills/pipeline/error_class.go`): a `plan_slice` codex spawn whose `kubectl exec`/streaming failed because the apiserver could not reach the target node's kubelet (`error dialing backend: proxy error from 127.0.0.1:6443 while dialing <node>:10250, code 502: 502 Bad Gateway`) fell through `Classify` to `ClassCode` and **escalated** after the attempt cap (`turn_count=0`, `$0` cost) instead of free-retrying. The transient patterns only matched the `status 5xx` shape (GitLab/devbox clients), not the exec dialer's `error dialing backend` / worded `502 Bad Gateway` form — so a transient konnectivity blip (node Ready before and after) was indistinguishable from a real code failure. Surfaced by the `.loom/126` Slice A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test (2026-06-05): two consecutive harvester-vm canaries escalated at `plan_slice` on the k3s-w-10 kubelet dial 502, never reaching the `implement` stage on the VM. Adds `"error dialing backend"` to the transport-transient set and the worded gateway forms (`bad gateway`/`service unavailable`/`gateway timeout`) to the 5xx set, so these konnectivity blips become free retries (bounded by `transientRetryCap`) and land on a healthy connection instead of burning the attempt budget. Test: `TestClassify_KubeletExecDial502` (the real A2 `log_tail` fixture + worded 503/504). Note: this is the classifier half — a persistently-degraded node still needs infra remediation (cordon / restart konnectivity).
- **Codex spawns no longer hang/exit on `Reading additional input from stdin...` (the `.loom/44` agent-execution gap)** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): `buildAgentCommand`'s codex invocation now appends `< /dev/null`. codex 0.120.0+ `exec`, even with the prompt passed as an argument, inspects stdin — when stdin is a non-TTY pipe that is open but never written/closed, it prints `Reading additional input from stdin...`, treats stdin as additional prompt input, and hangs (or, on a session whose stdin EOFs oddly, exits 1) instead of running the single turn. **Both** spawn exec paths leave stdin in exactly that state — the K8s `StreamExec` sets `PodExecOptions{Stdin:false}` and the harvester-vm SSH session leaves `session.Stdin` nil — so codex started a thread + turn then died with `turn_count=1` and an empty diff. Surfaced by the harvester-vm A2 re-run after the workspace/CLI provisioning fix landed (`spawn-ced0192f6540`: `thread.started`/`turn.started` on stdout, `Reading additional input from stdin...` on stderr, exit 1); it is also the latent cause of codex's empty implements on k8s. The prompt is already an argument, so `< /dev/null` removes the spurious stdin read with no behavior loss. Ref openai/codex#20919. Test: new `< /dev/null` assertion in `TestBuildAgentCommand`.
- **mcp-devbox `harvester-vm` sandboxes SSH as `agent`, not the stale `ubuntu`** (`cmd/mcp-devbox/main.go`, comment fix in `internal/hud/app.go`): `cmd/mcp-devbox/main.go` hardcoded a `"ubuntu"` fallback for `DEVBOX_HARVESTER_SSH_USER`, which overrode the backend's canonical default. Slice 2d.5c moved the VM's cloud-init login + auth-file home to the uid-1000 `agent` user for home-parity with the K8s spawn pod (`backend.defaultHarvesterSSHUser = vmAgentUser = "agent"`), so a devbox-launched harvester-vm sandbox was SSHing as the wrong user and mounted agent auth files (`~/.codex/auth.json` etc.) would not resolve. The default is now empty so `NewHarvesterVMBackend` applies the single-source-of-truth `agent` default — matching the operator/HUD spawn path, which already passes the env value through (empty → `agent`). Closes the `.loom/126` Slice A1 leftover ("reconcile the SSH-user default to `agent` everywhere"); behavior is covered by the existing `TestNewHarvesterVMBackend` default-user assertion. Also corrects a stale `(default: "ubuntu")` doc comment on `app.HUDConfig.SpawnHarvesterSSHUser`.
- **Mills empty-implement runs no longer reach the MR stage / open 0-commit MRs** (`pkg/mills/gates/nonempty_diff.go`, `pkg/mills/gates/gates.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/runner.go`, `pkg/mills/pipeline/noop_dispatcher.go`): the `post_implement_gate` ran `{diff_size, scope, path_policy, secret_scan, commit_format}`, none of which fail on an empty diff — `scope`/`path_policy` early-return *pass* when `FilesChanged==0`, the size/secret gates pass on a zero diff, and the LLM rubric scores an empty diff 1.0. So an `implement` stage that produced **no change at all** (the agent did no work — e.g. codex exiting `turn_count=0` on the harvester-vm) sailed through every gate to the `mr` stage and opened a 0-commit MR (`head_sha=null`). That empty-MR false-positive killed every canary in the `.loom/44` autonomy round (`!518`/`!520`/`!522`) and the `.loom/126` Slice A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test (`!598`). Adds a deterministic **`nonempty_diff`** gate (fails iff `FilesChanged==0 && DiffPatch` empty), registered in `gates.Default()` and prepended to `post_implement_gate` so it short-circuits before the size/scope checks; with the stage's `RetryFrom: "implement"`, an empty implement now retries and then **escalates to a human** instead of producing a junk MR — independent of *why* the agent produced nothing. `NoOpDispatcher` (the in-code embodiment of the false-positive) now emits a non-empty placeholder diff while keeping `FilesChanged` empty so the scope/path_policy gates still pass on no-slice smoke items. Defensive guard only — the live agent-execution gap (codex-on-VM) stays gated separately. Tests: `nonempty_diff_test.go` table + updated `TestDefault_HasAllCoreGates`. Plan: `.loom/128-iteration-plan-mills-empty-diff-gate-2026-06-04.md`.
- **Harvester-VM spawns now provision the workspace + agent CLI at Start (Mills A2 empty-diff root cause)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/backend.go`, `internal/hud/spawn.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`): with the buffered-exec fix making failures visible, a direct codex canary on `substrate=harvester-vm` exited **127 in 176ms** with `cd: /workspace/services/loom-core: No such file or directory` **and** `codex: command not found`. The harvester-vm `Build` is a no-op against one shared base image, so a booted VM had **neither the repo nor the agent CLI** — unlike the K8s path, which hydrates the workspace via a git-clone init container and bakes the CLI into the per-agent runtime image. `HarvesterVMBackend.Start` now provisions over SSH after the VM is ready: it `git clone`s `<GitBaseURL>/<project>.git` into `StartOpts.WorkDir` and checks out the requested branch (created from `BaseBranch` when origin lacks it, mirroring `gitCloneInitContainer` — the VM disk IS the worktree), then runs a guarded, idempotent agent-CLI install (`StartOpts.AgentCLIInstallCmd`, built by `agentCLIInstallShell` from the same pinned versions as `agentCLIInstallLines`). The script is a fast no-op on a future curated base image (Phase B1) and on reused VMs; the git token is resolved via the existing `SecretResolver` and exported only for the provisioning SSH session, never persisted to the cloud-init env file. `HarvesterVMBackendConfig` gains `GitBaseURL`/`GitSecret` (wired from `SpawnGitBaseURL`/`SpawnGitSecret`); `StartOpts` gains `AgentCLIInstallCmd` (the K8s backend ignores it). Tests: `buildProvisionScript` (clone+CLI, https scheme, base-branch default, no-branch, CLI-only, empty no-op, empty-token guard, special-char quoting), `cloneRequested` table, `agentCLIInstallShell` (guarded + version-pinned). Refs `.loom/126` Slice A2, `.loom/45` §Slice 1.5/B1.
- **Council run duration is real, not always 0ms / "instant"** (`pkg/mills/clients/council.go`): `FlexInferCouncilEditor.Edit` never stamped `Sidecar.StartedAt`, so the `IsZero()` guard in `pkg/mills/council/artifacts.go` always fired and set `StartedAt` to the same post-write `now` as `EndedAt` — every successful council run wrote `StartedAt == EndedAt` → 0ms duration → an "instant" badge on every Council-tab row, even for multi-second runs with real findings. The editor now captures `started := time.Now().UTC()` before the `Chat` call and stamps it onto `Sidecar.StartedAt`; the writer's `IsZero()` guard preserves it. The Council tab reports real elapsed time and the "instant" badge correctly reserves itself for sub-1s crashes. Regression test `TestFlexInferCouncilEditorStampsStartedAt`. (Original branch `fix/mills-editor-stamps-started-at`, rebased + shipped 2026-06-04.)
- **Spawn stages no longer report SDK-level claude-code failures as success** (`internal/hud/spawn_claude_parser.go`): claude-code returns `subtype=success` **with `is_error=true`** for SDK-level failures (auth expired, billing, model unavailable) — the run never actually executed. The result parser ignored `is_error` on success subtypes (`claudeResultEvent` had no `IsError` field), so Mills marked the spawn stage **successful** with `turn_count=0` / `cost=$0` despite the agent never running — the same false-green class that hid failures in the Mills A2 kill-test. Now the parser decodes `is_error` and, on a success-subtype-but-errored result, normalizes `stop_reason` to `sdk_error` and routes it through the error sink. Regression test `TestClaudeParser_ResultSDKError`. (Original branch `fix/claude-parser-is-error`, rebased + shipped 2026-06-04.)
- **Harvester-VM spawns no longer silently swallow the agent result** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): the buffered (non-streaming) exec branch — taken by every `harvester-vm` spawn, since that backend isn't `streamExecCapable` — never fed the agent's output to the telemetry parser, never checked `execResult.ExitCode` (only the Go-level `execErr`; `Backend.Exec` returns a nil error for command-level nonzero exits), and discarded `StderrTail`. Net effect during the Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test: a codex run that exited in ~0.24s was marked **`completed`** with `turn_count=0` and **zero diagnostics**, hiding the in-VM failure. Now the buffered branch best-effort-parses the stdout tail, **fails the spawn on a nonzero exit** surfacing the stderr tail (new `bufferedExecFailure` helper), captures stderr into the agent-output context entry, and logs a full exec summary (exit code, durations, stdout/stderr tails). Confirmed by a live harvester-vm canary repro. (Full real-time telemetry on harvester-vm still requires making the backend `streamExecCapable` — tracked separately.) Unit test in `internal/hud/spawn_buffered_exec_test.go`.
- **Mills Overview health banner no longer falsely claims "No successful merge on record"** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_status.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{mills.svelte.ts,mills.systemHealth.ts}`): a residual of the same active-only blind spot fixed in !619. The `broken`-state banner detail line (`OverviewPanel.svelte`) reads `health.last_successful_merge_at`, but `computeSystemHealth` derived it **only** from `millsStore.pipelineRuns` — the active-only run list, which never contains a terminal `done` run — so it was ~always `null` and the banner wrongly read *"No successful merge on record"* even when the Mills had merged plenty (just not in the last 24h, which is exactly the `broken` condition). Adds an **all-time** lookup `PipelineDAO.LatestMergedAt` (`MAX(ended_at)` over `state=done`), surfaced as `last_merge_at` on `/api/mills/status` (symmetric with the existing `last_council_at`), and threaded into `computeSystemHealth` as an authoritative `lastMergeAt` override that wins over the blind derivation — mirroring the `mergedRuns24h`/`escalatedRuns24h` override pattern. Additive + graceful: until the operator ships the new field the frontend falls back to the prior (blind) derivation, no regression. Tests: `TestPipeline_LatestMergedAt` (DAO: nil before any merge, newest `done` wins over a newer escalation) + a `blind-list-override` case in `mills.systemHealth.fixture.ts`. No `OverviewPanel.svelte` change — the bug was purely the data source.
- **Spawn session-registration failures are now observable** (`internal/hud/spawn.go`): `runSpawn` silently swallowed `StartSession` errors at spawn-start, leaving `state.SessionID` empty — which makes `persistTelemetrySummary` skip the write, so the spawn's turn-level telemetry (`turn_count` / `stop_reason` / `last_message`) vanishes with no log. That blind spot hid the in-VM codex failure during the Mills A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test (codex exited `turn_count=0` with an empty diff, invisible from operator logs). Extracted `registerSpawnSession`, which logs a `Warn` on registration failure (or empty session id) instead of swallowing it. Regression tests in `internal/hud/spawn_telemetry_persist_test.go`.

### Security
- **Bump Go toolchain 1.25.10 → 1.25.11** (`go.mod`, `go.work`, `.gitlab-ci.yml` `GO_VERSION`, and the `golang:*-alpine` builder base in `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.custom-server`, `Dockerfile.loom-mills-operator`, `Dockerfile.custom-server.local`, `Dockerfile.local`): patches 3 stdlib vulnerabilities flagged by `security:govulncheck` — `GO-2026-5039` (net/textproto: arbitrary inputs included in errors unescaped), `GO-2026-5038` (mime: quadratic complexity in `WordDecoder.DecodeHeader`), `GO-2026-5037` (crypto/x509: inefficient candidate hostname parsing). All three are fixed in `stdlib@go1.25.11`. The govulncheck job (lint stage, `allow_failure:false`) was failing on `main`, which blocked the `deploy` stage from building images — so this also unblocks the post-!611 `loom-mills-operator` image build that carries the autonomy kill-switch (plan-42 Slice 1b).

### Added
- **Claude hook event names are validated against the installed CLI before emission** (`pkg/generator/claude_hook_events.go`, wired into `claudeHooks()` in `pkg/generator/configs_claude.go`): a live probe on 2026-06-09 proved Claude Code CLI 2.1.61 **silently disables the entire hooks config** when `settings.json` contains even one unknown hook event name — zero events fire, no error or warning anywhere (documented in `~/workspace/.loom/60-product-spec-loom-flightdeck.md`, "Risks"). One typo in `platform_profiles.yaml` (`session_end_event`, `heartbeat_event`) or a generator regression would therefore kill hook-based session tracking in every synced repo after `loom sync claude --regen`. The generator now resolves the accepted event set by scanning the locally installed `claude` binary for its embedded event-name enum (chunked `bytes.Index` anchor-and-expand on `"PreToolUse"`, ~0.1s over the 178MB 2.1.61 binary; labeled with `claude --version`), falling back to a pinned 18-event baseline extracted from 2.1.61 when no binary is probeable (e.g. CI) or the probe fails a sanity gate (≥8 events incl. the lifecycle core). Unknown event keys are stripped with a loud stderr `WARN` naming the dropped events and the provenance of the accepted set, so the rest of the hooks survive instead of all dying. Validating against the **installed binary** rather than docs matters: code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks listed 30 events on 2026-06-09 (including `PermissionDenied`) while the installed 2.1.61 accepts only 18 — `PermissionDenied` among the missing, and writing it is exactly the silent-death case. Audit result: all 5 events currently emitted for Claude (`SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `SubagentStart`) are valid in 2.1.61. Regression tests pin the baseline to the exact 2.1.61 enum, assert every emitted event is accepted (`TestClaudeHooks_EmittedEventsAreAccepted`), simulate the YAML-typo class end to end, and exercise the chunked binary probe across a >4MB read boundary. Complements the docs-drift checker (`vendor_specs.yaml`) — that asserts docs mention what we emit; this asserts the *installed CLI* accepts it.
- **`millsStore` + `otelStore` join the HUD SSE staleness cohort (plan-117 last open follow-up)** (`internal/hud/monitor/{otel,mills}.go`, `internal/hud/{app,embed}.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{otel,mills}.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!TBD): the two OverviewPanel stores were the last secondary stores still blind-polling with no SSE subscription, so they couldn't drop to the 60s watchdog cadence the rest of the cohort uses (plan-117 §"Remaining follow-ups": "bumping them to 60s would add a 30s data lag"). Closes that by adding the two missing event families. **OTel** (`hud.otel`): new `OTelMonitor` mirrors `CostMonitor` — a thin `RefreshFunc` over the daemon `loom/otel-status` RPC; its `OnRefresh` broadcasts the `bridge.OTelStatusResult` snapshot, and `otelStore` now subscribes (`applySnapshot` applies it directly — the json tags already match the `OTelStatus` interface) + registers `stalenessStore.register('otel', …)` (`staleAfter=90_000`). **Mills** (`hud.mills`): new `MillsMonitor` GETs the operator status endpoint directly (the status route is an open read — the proxy only injects the admin bearer for mutations) and broadcasts it; because the Mills store fans out to many operator endpoints, the frontend uses the event as a **refresh signal** (re-runs `fetchAll`) rather than a partial-apply, so the live tabs/drawers keep their full data with no per-tab regression. The monitor is only constructed when `LOOM_MILLS_OPERATOR_URL` is set (no `hud.mills` events on laptops), and `millsStore.isStale` is suppressed while `disabled` so the ConnectionBanner pill stays quiet there. Both stores switch their watchdog poll to `!eventStore.connected || this.isStale` and align to 60s; `ConnectionBanner` surfaces them automatically via the existing `stalenessStore.staleStores` registry (no banner edit). Monitors started at 30s (otel) / 15s (mills) server-side so one poll fans out to every browser over SSE instead of each browser polling. Tests: `otel_test.go` (zero snapshot, stop-idempotent, OnRefresh fires) + `mills_test.go` (status-URL construction incl. trailing-slash trim, httptest refresh decode+broadcast, non-200 → error, stop-idempotent). Plan: `.loom/117-implementation-plan-hud-ux-overhaul-2026-05-15.md`.
