- **Mills HUD: Backlog + Eval rows are clickable for detail (drilldown gap closed)** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/{BacklogDetail.svelte,BacklogPanel.svelte,EvalPanel.svelte}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, services/loom-core!634): the Mills row→detail drilldown was only ever wired for the **Pipelines** tab; **Backlog** (the work queue) and **Eval** were static read-only tables with zero click handlers — the operator-reported "can't click row entries for details, still mostly useless." The backend already proxied every per-entity detail endpoint (`internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go:41-101`) and a reusable drawer (`shared/DetailDrawer.svelte`) already existed, so this was a frontend-wiring gap, not missing capability — **no operator change**. **Backlog** rows now open a right-edge detail drawer (`BacklogDetail.svelte`, wrapping the shared `DetailDrawer`) rendering the full item from `GET /api/mills/backlog/{id}` (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_backlog.go:111` returns the whole `BacklogItem`): spec doc + anchor, slice decomposition, success criteria, budget/policy, dependencies, cost estimate, council-run + GitLab-issue refs, **cross-links to the pipeline run(s) executing the item** (active + history, via the new `millsStore.pipelineRunsForBacklog()` helper), and a **Start-pipeline** admin action (`millsStore.startPipeline()` → `POST /api/mills/pipeline/runs/{backlog_id}/start`) when no run exists yet. **Eval** rows now expand inline (matching the Council/Audit/Squads expander idiom) to show the rubric **breakdown**, judge, and full notes, and cross-link `pipeline_run` subjects into the run drawer. Store adds backlog-detail drilldown state mirroring the proven pipeline-run pattern: `selectedBacklogID` + `backlogDetailByID` cache, a `currentBacklogDetail` getter (named distinctly from the `openBacklogDetail()` method — a getter and method sharing a name collide as duplicate class members and fail silently), `open/closeBacklogDetail`, and `refreshOpenBacklogDetail()` wired into `fetchAll` for 15s live refresh. Council/Audit/Squads already had working click-to-expand drilldown and are intentionally untouched. All clickable rows carry `role`/`tabindex`/`aria` + Enter/Space keyboard handlers. Riskiest-assumption kill-test passed via operator source (deployed HUD is behind Cloudflare Access, returns 302). Plan + evidence: `.loom/65-plan-hud-mills-row-drilldown-2026-06-05.md`.
- **Agent-context recall second-stage reranker wired into `HandleUnifiedRecall` (flexinfer `/v1/rerank`, default-off)** (`pkg/agentcontext/service.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/service_recall.go`, `pkg/agentcontext/reranker_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): the recall `Reranker` abstraction + `FlexInferReranker` + `ApplyReranker` helper shipped in Slice A1 but were deliberately **never called** from the recall path (`ApplyReranker` carried a "NOT yet called … wired separately in a later slice (see .loom/88 §2.A1)" note). This lands that wiring as the FlexInfer hardware-utilization arc's S1.4 consumer slice. `Service` gains a `reranker Reranker` field constructed once in `NewServiceFromEnv` from `LoadRerankerConfigFromEnv()` (default `NoopReranker`/"off" → zero network I/O, response path byte-stable) with a `WithReranker` option for test injection. `HandleUnifiedRecall` now applies the reranker as a **second retrieval stage** over the merged context candidate set — after embedding retrieval + priority-sort + budget-trim, before the entries are returned — via a small `rerankRecallEntries` wrapper that gates on the wired backend (`off`/nil → no-op) and soft-fails to the embedding/priority order when the proxy is unavailable (timeout/404 annotate `rerank_status` and preserve order — recall never fails because rerank is down). When active, the backend tag surfaces on `recall_meta.rerank_backend`. **Enablement** (default-safe, opt-in): `WEAVER_RERANKER=flexinfer` (or `bge`), `WEAVER_RERANKER_BASE_URL=$FLEXINFER_URL`, and — for the homelab flexinfer proxy specifically — `WEAVER_RERANKER_MODEL=rerank` (the proxy's litellm alias; the generic default `bge-reranker-v2-m3` is **not** a routable alias there). **Live before/after evidence** against the proxy `/v1/rerank` route (bge-reranker-v2-m3 co-active with bge-large embeddings on the Radeon VII, gfx906): on a 5-query graded-relevance eval, mean nDCG@6 **0.554 → 0.778 (+22.4 pts)** and mean gold-doc rank **3.40 → 2.40**, with rerank moving the gold answer to rank 1 on 3/5 queries, tying on 2 hard cross-vocabulary queries, and **never regressing**. New tests pin the wiring: nil reranker and `off` backend are no-ops (empty backend tag, order preserved), and an active backend reorders + reports its tag. `go test ./pkg/agentcontext` + `go vet` green; `cmd/mcp-agent-context` builds.
- **Mills HUD autonomous-merge north-star + truthful health banner** (`internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/{mills.svelte.ts,mills.systemHealth.ts}`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_status.go`): the operator's `KPIWriter` has emitted `pipeline_merged_runs` (autonomous merges in the rolling window) every reconciler tick since 2026-05-19, but **nothing surfaced it** — the frontend KPI-snapshot interface omitted the count metrics, so the north-star "is the Mills actually merging anything?" number was invisible. Worse, `computeSystemHealth` derived `merges_24h`/`escalations_24h` from `millsStore.pipelineRuns`, which holds **only active runs** (terminal merged/escalated runs are excluded by the active-only pipeline endpoint), so those counts were ~always 0 — the Overview banner literally hardcoded *"0 merged in 24h"* and the `broken` state was barely reachable. Now: the store's `MillsKPISnapshot.metrics` exposes `pipeline_merged_runs`/`pipeline_escalated_runs`/`escalation_rate`/`council_runs`/`pipeline_runs`; `computeSystemHealth` accepts authoritative `mergedRuns24h`/`escalatedRuns24h` overrides from the 1d KPI snapshot and prefers them over the blind run-list derivation; the Mills Overview gains a leading **"Auto-merges 24h"** north-star card (green when > 0); and the health banner reports the real merged count. Also corrects the stale `handleKPIs` comment that claimed the recorder hadn't shipped. (No backend logic change — the recorder already worked; `KPIWriter`'s 7 existing tests still cover emission.)
- **Mills HUD pipeline run history** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`): the Pipelines panel could only ever show **in-flight** runs — `handlePipelineRunsList` returned the active-state union, so a run *vanished the instant it merged, escalated, or paused*, leaving an operator no way to review what the Mills actually did (the comment even noted "Slice 5.2 will add a query param to surface recent terminal runs" — never landed). Adds `PipelineDAO.ListRecentTerminal` (terminal states `done`/`escalated`/`paused`, newest-first, `since`-windowed, limit-capped at 200) and a `state=terminal` mode on the list endpoint (with `since=`/`limit=`; back-compat default still returns active runs). The Pipelines panel gains an **Active / History** toggle: History loads finished runs (default last 7 days) and the table now also surfaces **MR** and **Cost** columns in both views. History refreshes on the same 15s cadence only while the History view is open, so an operator who never opens it pays nothing. Tests: `TestPipeline_ListRecentTerminal` (DAO) + `TestHandlePipelineRuns_TerminalHistory` (handler, incl. 400s on bad `since`/`limit`).
- **Mills HUD global autonomy kill-switch (plan 42 Slice 1b — the 5th day-2 action)** (`cmd/loom-mills-operator/{handlers_policy.go,config.go,main.go,server.go}`, `pkg/mills/clients/gitlab.go`, `internal/hud/domain/mills/mills.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/{stores/mills.svelte.ts,components/Mills/OverviewPanel.svelte}`, `platform/gitops!216`, services/loom-core!611): closes the last of the five plan-42 day-2 HUD actions — a pause/resume autonomy button on the Mills Overview panel. It was deferred from !609 as the only action needing a cross-repo write. Routing (operator interview 2026-06-02) is a **GitOps auto-PR** flipping `policy.enabled` in `platform/gitops/k3s/mills/configmap-policy.yaml`, not a live write-through (Flux owns the file). New admin endpoint `POST /api/mills/policy/kill-switch` reads that file, flips the `# kill switch`-anchored `enabled:` line, and opens a branch+commit+MR (`action` ∈ `pause|resume|toggle`; a no-op opens no MR; `reason` is recorded in the commit + MR, which is the durable audit trail). The HUD proxies the route; the Overview button reads `policy_enabled`, opens the returned MR, and toasts the link. **Kill-test (2026-06-03):** the pipeline GitLab token (`loom-mills-gitlab`) gets 404 on `platform/gitops` and per the `platform/gitops/**` protected-path guardrail mustn't write there, so the kill-switch uses a **separate gitops-scoped token** (`loom-mills-gitops` secret; env `GITOPS_GITLAB_TOKEN`/`GITOPS_GITLAB_PROJECT`; unset → endpoint 503). Verified end-to-end that a gitops-scoped token creates the exact 1-line flip as a branch+commit+MR. New GitLab client methods `GetRawFile` + `CreateCommit` (commits API) + a `UserAgent` config (the public GitLab edge Cloudflare-403s the default Go UA with code 1010; the in-cluster operator bypasses the edge). Plan: `.loom/127`, `.loom/42`. **Live 1e operator kill-test PASSED 2026-06-03** against the deployed operator (`:20260603-170700`): an authenticated `POST /api/mills/policy/kill-switch {"action":"pause"}` returned `changed:true, previous_enabled:true, desired_enabled:false` and opened a real gitops MR with the exact 1-line `enabled: true→false` flip; the probe MR was closed unmerged and its branch deleted, so `main` policy stayed `enabled: true` (prod autonomy never actually paused).
- **Mills HUD day-2 action parity** (`pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `cmd/loom-mills-operator/handlers_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/mills.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/{shared/millsActions.ts,CouncilPanel,PipelineRunDetail,AuditPanel}.svelte`, services/loom-core!609): closes the HUD↔CLI parity gap for Mills day-2 operator actions so they no longer force a drop to a terminal, after the plan-42 Slice 0 operator kill-test passed (2026-06-02; all 5 documented actions wanted, gate ≥3). Wires HUD buttons for 4 of the 5: **Replay council** + **Dryrun** (CouncilPanel, reusing `POST /api/mills/council/run` + `/dryrun`), **Force-escalate** (PipelineRunDetail, in-flight runs only, behind a confirm, reusing `/pipeline/runs/{id}/escalate`), and **Audit by MR iid** (AuditPanel search → matched run(s) → run drawer). Adds the one missing backend piece: `store.ListByMRIID` + a `mr_iid` query param on the pipeline-runs list handler so the audit lookup resolves the run(s) that produced a merged MR (Loop B attribution), terminal or not. New `shared/millsActions.ts` `runAdminAction()` wraps mutations with confirm+toast; all calls route through the admin-injecting HUD proxy so the browser never holds a token. The 5th action (global pause/resume autonomy kill-switch) is deferred to a follow-up — it needs a cross-repo GitOps auto-PR and the HUD proxy has no `platform/gitops` creds; autonomy state is already surfaced read-only on the Overview panel. Plan: `.loom/127`, `.loom/42`.
- **GitOps-safe k8s ops tools + scoped guardrail** (`cmd/mcp-ops/main.go`, `cmd/mcp-ops/main_test.go`, `pkg/policy/engine.go`, `pkg/policy/engine_test.go`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`, `pkg/generator/templates/policies/gitops_flux.yaml`, services/loom-core!608): routine operations (cycling pods, rolling restarts) kept forcing a fall back to raw shell `kubectl` because the `ops_mcp` tools errored on kubeconfig resolution, and the blanket `gitops_flux` guardrail false-denied any tool call whose args merely *mentioned* a blocked verb (e.g. a note recorded via `agent_context_add`). Adds `resolveKubeconfig` (robust fallback: explicit → `$KUBECONFIG` → `K3S_KUBECONFIG` → `~/.kube/k3s.yaml` → `~/.kube/config`), so the tools work when the configured `platform/gitops/.kube/k3s.yaml` is absent on a local/fallback run. Adds three sanctioned, non-drifting tools — `k8s_rollout_restart`, `k8s_delete_pod`, `k8s_rollout_status` (kind constrained to deployment/statefulset/daemonset). Adds `PolicyRule.AppliesTo`: command guardrails now only scan the args of command-executing tools (shells, `exec`, `ssh`, `run_repo_script`); the tool-name check stays unscoped. Guardrails default to that exec-tool scope; `applies_to: ["*"]` opts back into matching every tool. The `gitops_flux` message now points operators at the sanctioned tools. Raw shell `kubectl` mutations stay blocked — the dedicated tools are the smooth path. Mirror `platform/gitops/mcp/context/registry.yaml` kept in sync.
- **Registry-driven SKILL.md generation for the `zed` and `opencode` sync profiles** (`pkg/skills/generator.go`, `pkg/skills/generator_bundle.go`, `pkg/skills/generator_codex_gemini.go`, `pkg/skills/registry.go`, `pkg/sync/ops_regen.go`, `pkg/skills/generator_bundle_test.go`, services/loom-core!602): `zed` and `opencode` declared themselves skill-capable (`SkillsTarget`/`SkillsHomePath`/`SkillsDirectToHome` in `pkg/sync/manager.go`) but the skills generator had no case for them, so `loom sync skills all` failed both with `unknown target: {zed,opencode}` and they never received any of the registry's skills — only `codex`/`claude`/`kilocode`/`gemini`/`antigravity` did. Adds a generic Anthropic-compatible SKILL.md bundle writer (`generateBundleSkill`) shared by these targets, mirroring the codex/gemini layout (`<skillsHome>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` + copied scripts/references/assets, atomic SKILL.md write). `generateCodexSkillMD` is refactored into `generateBundleSkillMD(skill, target)` so `${SKILL_PATH}` resolves per target; `ResolveInstructions` resolves it to `$HOME/.config/{zed,opencode}/skills/<name>`. Because `Skill.IsEnabled` defaults to `true` for unspecified targets, the full 85-skill registry reaches both profiles with **no per-skill `targets` edits**. `ops_regen.go` points `OutputDir`/manifest at the parent of `SkillsHomePath` for these targets, since Zed's skills home (`$HOME/.config/zed`) differs from its MCP-config `HomeDir` (`Library/Application Support/Zed`). Verified: `loom sync skills all` now runs warning-free across all 8 profiles; `zed` and `opencode` each generate 81 SKILL.md bundles into `~/.config/{zed,opencode}/skills` with valid frontmatter and no stray app-support dir. New tests pin bundle generation, default-enablement, and `${SKILL_PATH}` resolution.
- **`agent_loop` consumes the proxy prefix-cache-hit-rate header (F4 row-195 follow-up)** (`pkg/agentloop/{metrics,client}.go`, `pkg/agentloop/metrics_test.go`, `cmd/mcp-agent-loop/{tools,main}.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): mirrors the flexinfer client wiring ([flexinfer!536](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/flexinfer/-/merge_requests/536)) so the loom-core MCP agent loop closes the row-195 gap end-to-end. The flexinfer proxy now emits `X-Flexinfer-Prefix-Cache-Hit-Rate` when a request opts in via `X-Flexinfer-Want-Prefix-Hit` ([flexinfer!535](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/flexinfer/-/merge_requests/535)). `ChatClientConfig.WantPrefixHit` sets the opt-in header per request (library default `false`, backward-compatible); `TurnMetrics` gains `PrefixCacheHitRate` (`*float64`, `omitempty`) parsed from the response header — the **direct** prefix-cache signal even when the engine omits per-request `cached_tokens` (gemma4), distinct from the existing `PrefixHitRatio` (derived from `cached_tokens`). The `agent_loop_run` tool exposes a `want_prefix_hit` boolean param (default `true`, since the tool's purpose is the metrics report). New `metrics_test.go` pins: the header parses (and stays nil when absent), the opt-in header is sent + the rate round-trips without `cached_tokens`, and the default path sends no opt-in header. gofmt/`go vet`/`go test ./pkg/agentloop` green.
- **HUD mirror forwards per-session tool-call activity so distributed agents' "calls" surface on the central HUD** (`internal/hud/mirror/mirror.go`, `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge.go`, `internal/hud/embed.go`, `internal/hud/bridge/agent_contracts.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/handler_session.go`, `internal/hud/mirror/mirror_toolcall_test.go`, `internal/hud/domain/fleet/toolcall_ingest_test.go`, `internal/hud/embedded_event_bridge_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): completes the "Live Sessions / 0 calls" fix chain (after !583 agent_id + !588 embedded event bridge) for the **distributed** case. A Mac/laptop agent's tool calls go through its OWN local daemon, never the central `mobile-hud` daemon's EventBus, and the central daemon's `/events/publish` is loopback-only — so the central HUD never saw their per-session activity (only its own `agent_context` polling). The one existing daemon-to-daemon channel is the HUD presence mirror (`LOOM_HUD_MIRROR_URL`, posts `/api/agent/heartbeat` every 15s). This rides it: the local daemon's EventLog (populated by the !588 embedded bridge from its own `emitAudit` tool.call emissions) is read per active session via the new `App.RecentToolCallsForSession(sessionID, sinceUnixNano, limit)` (newest-first, returns the batch + the max event ts), and the mirror attaches up to 25 fresh calls as `recent_tool_calls` on each heartbeat, advancing a per-session cursor (`tcCursor`) so the same calls are never re-forwarded. The central HUD's heartbeat handler unpacks `recent_tool_calls` and re-broadcasts each as a `tool.call` event (`ingestRecentToolCalls` → `BroadcastAgentEvent` → central EventLog → `buildSessionTrace`), backfilling `session_id`/`agent_id` from the heartbeat and bounding ingestion to 50/heartbeat. New `mirror.ToolCallReader` seam (wired via `SetToolCalls(app)`); `HeartbeatRequest` gains `recent_tool_calls`. Tests pin: mirror forwards then dedups on the next cycle; the App reader filters by session + cursor and advances maxTS past a truncated batch; the handler re-broadcasts with backfill + bound. **Activation**: ships in the binary; takes effect when a distributed daemon runs this build with the mirror configured (and the mirror healthy — requires the !579 recv-budget fix locally). Central receive half verified via a live `/api/agent/heartbeat` kill-test.
- **F4 tool-loop-as-prefix engine exposed as an MCP tool (`cmd/mcp-agent-loop` + `pkg/agentloop`)** (`cmd/mcp-agent-loop/{main,tools}.go`, `pkg/agentloop/*.go`, `Makefile`, `mcp/context/registry.yaml`): ports the append-only ReAct engine — validated as a CLI in flexinfer slice 1 ([flexinfer!532](https://gitlab.flexinfer.ai/services/flexinfer/-/merge_requests/532)) and live-proven on the gemma4 APC canary (flexinfer matrix row 195, PASS 2026-06-01: 22.5× prompt growth with `upstream_ms` tracking the per-round token delta → prefix-cache reuse observed client-side) — into loom-core so any MCP client gets the same prefix-cache-paying loop. `pkg/agentloop` is mirrored from `services/flexinfer/internal/agentloop` (different Go modules → layout copied, not imported): `Conversation` enforces an append-only + mutability-ordered invariant (system+tools immutable prefix, history grows by `Append` only); `ChatClient` pins `X-Flexinfer-Cache-Key=session` and parses proxy instrumentation headers into per-turn `TurnMetrics` (`CachedTokens` stays nil when the engine omits `cached_tokens` — the gemma4 fallback); `Budget` encodes `maxModelLen − system − output` and returns a typed `BudgetError` (F4-413-as-feature, in-process) so the loop stops cleanly before HTTP 400; `FSTools` are real read-only, path-jailed `read_file`/`list_dir`. The new `cmd/mcp-agent-loop` server (mcpscaffold house style) exposes two tools: `agent_loop_run` (bounded live ReAct session returning a per-turn metrics report; **not** `always_allow`'d — hits an external endpoint + reads files) and `agent_loop_self_check` (offline wiring gate; `always_allow`'d, read-only). Wired into `MCP_SERVERS` + a Makefile target + a `registry.yaml` entry (`always_allow: [agent_loop_self_check]`, `FLEXINFER_PROXY_URL` env, 600s timeout). Verified: gofmt clean, `go vet`, `go test ./pkg/agentloop` (8 tests incl. the offline self-check), and a live stdio smoke of the built binary (`tools/list` → both tools; `agent_loop_self_check` → `passed: true`, all 5 checks green). Completes both F4 client forms (a CLI, b MCP tool).
- **`harvester-vm` spawn substrate wired into the prod `mobile-hud` deployment (Mills roadmap Slice A1 — deploy)** (`k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`, platform/gitops!196): the `mobile-hud` Deployment (which runs the prod spawn orchestrator) now mounts the harvester admin kubeconfig from the `harvester-kubeconfig` Secret (loom-hub ns, added in gitops!196) at `/etc/harvester/kubeconfig` and sets `SPAWN_HARVESTER_KUBECONFIG` + `SPAWN_HARVESTER_NAMESPACE=default` + `SPAWN_HARVESTER_STORAGE_CLASS=longhorn-image-mc9ph` (stock Ubuntu image until the curated `mills-devbox-base` image lands) + `SPAWN_HARVESTER_NAD=default/lan10g`. Combined with the daemon-config fix below, `initSpawnOrchestrator` now constructs the `HarvesterVMBackend` (was silently k8s-only), so a `mills-canary-harvester-vm` item routes implement+tests to a KubeVirt VM. SSH user left unset → backend defaults to `agent` (Slice 2d.5c home-parity). **Security FIXME**: full hypervisor admin kubeconfig in a network-facing pod — to be scoped to a namespaced harvester ServiceAccount. Unblocks the Slice A2 first-autonomous-merge kill-test.
- **`harvester-vm` substrate now wireable from the prod daemon (Mills roadmap Slice A1)** (`internal/daemon/config.go`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed.go`, `internal/daemon/hud_embed_test.go`, `cmd/loom/hud.go`, `.loom/126-plan-mills-full-vision-roadmap-2026-06-01.md`): the prod HUD/spawn orchestrator runs as `loomd → startEmbeddedHUD` (the `mobile-hud` deployment), **not** the `loom hud` subcommand — and that daemon path silently dropped all nine `SpawnHarvester*` config fields. `initSpawnOrchestrator` gates harvester-vm registration on `SpawnHarvesterKubeconfig != ""`, so with the field always empty in prod, **every `harvester-vm`-targeted spawn fell back to k8s regardless of deployment env**. Added the nine `SpawnHarvester*` fields to `EmbeddedHUDConfig` and extracted the env→`hud.Config` mapping into a pure, testable `buildEmbeddedHUDConfig(cfg, registryPath)` that reads `SPAWN_HARVESTER_{KUBECONFIG,BASE_IMAGE,NAMESPACE,STORAGE_CLASS,NAD,DEFAULT_VCPUS,DEFAULT_MEM_MI,DEFAULT_DISK_GI,SSH_USER}` (file config wins; env is the K8s-injection fallback). `SPAWN_HARVESTER_SSH_USER` is intentionally left unset so the backend applies its `agent` default for Slice 2d.5c home-parity; the stale `loom hud` flag help (`default: ubuntu`) is corrected to `agent`. **Inert until opted in**: with no env set, `SpawnHarvesterKubeconfig` stays empty and the substrate stays unregistered (current k8s-only behavior). New tests pin env-mapping, the safe empty default, and file-config precedence. This unblocks the Phase-A kill-test (first end-to-end autonomous canary merge through harvester-vm); the remaining A1 step is mounting the harvester kubeconfig into `mobile-hud` + setting the env in `k8s/base/servers/mobile-hud/deployment.yaml`.
- **`harvester-vm` codex home-parity kill-test PASSED + three live-only fixes (Slice 2d.5d)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm_objects.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm{,_objects}_test.go`, `.loom/45-product-spec-mills-harvester-vm-substrate-2026-05-25.md`, services/loom-core!577): the Slice 2d.5c kill-test owed in the entry below was run **live on-LAN** (`TestHarvesterVMBackend_Integration_CodexHomeParity`, `integration` build tag) and **PASSED 2026-06-01 (110s)** — but only after surfacing **three bugs the shape-only unit tests could not catch**, each a hard blocker on a real codex `SecretMount` booting + authenticating. (1) **Inline cloud-init 2048-byte cap**: the real 4784-byte codex `auth.json` (base64 ≈ 6.4 KB) embedded in `cloudInitNoCloud.userData` is rejected at VM-create by the KubeVirt admission webhook (`userdata exceeds 2048 byte limit`), so the VM never boots. Fixed by delivering cloud-init out-of-line via a per-VM Secret + `cloudInitNoCloud.secretRef` (new `CloudInitSecret` field + `secretGVR`). **Gotcha**: the wire key is `secretRef`, **not** `userDataSecretRef` (only the *network* ref is `networkDataSecretRef`); the wrong key is silently dropped, then the VM is rejected for "no userdatasource set". (2) **Base image lacks `qemu-guest-agent`**: stock-Ubuntu `longhorn-image-mc9ph` boots to VMI `Running` but `AgentConnected` never flips and no IP is ever reported, so `waitForVMReady` times out and the backend can never SSH in. Fixed with a self-healing first `runcmd` (`command -v qemu-ga || apt-get install -y qemu-guest-agent`), a no-op once the curated `mills-devbox-base` image lands. (3) **PVC/Secret ownerRef race**: the post-create ownerRef `Update` lost a conflict to CDI/Longhorn (`object has been modified`), orphaning a 20Gi PVC per run. Fixed with `setOwnerRefWithRetry` (re-Get + `retry.RetryOnConflict`) for both the PVC and the cloud-init Secret; `CleanupBuilds` now also label-sweeps orphan cloud-init Secrets. **Live PASS evidence** (SSH as `agent`): `$HOME=/home/agent`; `/home/agent/.codex.auth/auth.json` owned `agent:agent` and byte-for-byte == the k3s `cluster-agent-auth`/`codex-auth-json` secret; `~/.codex/auth.json` symlink resolves; re-run clean with no orphaned PVC/Secret. New regression assertion pins the `qemu-guest-agent` self-heal runcmd; `secretRef`/Secret-shape assertions replace the old inline-`userData` reads across the backend test files. **Honest scope**: the harness proves codex's auth file is in place, agent-owned, and resolvable (the load-bearing home-parity claim) — running the `codex` binary itself (the final "doesn't 401" check) is owed to the curated `mills-devbox-base` image and recorded as a separate end-to-end step. Full trail (off-LAN BLOCKED → on-LAN FAILED → fixes → PASS) in `.loom/local/handoffs/mills-harvester-home-parity-killtest-2026-05-30.md`.
- **`harvester-vm` agent-user home-dir parity (Slice 2d.5c)** (`internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm_objects.go`, `internal/devbox/backend/harvester_vm{,_objects}_test.go`, `.loom/45-product-spec-mills-harvester-vm-substrate-2026-05-25.md`, `.loom/125-iteration-plan-mills-harvester-home-parity-slice2d5c-2026-05-30.md`, services/loom-core!576): the harvester-vm devbox backend now provisions a cloud-init `agent` user with `home: /home/agent` (was `ubuntu`), mirroring the K8s spawn pod's uid-1000 `agent` user so mounted auth files resolve at the `$HOME/.…` paths every agent CLI expects (`injectAgentConfig` + every `/home/agent` SecretMount path). `HarvesterVMBackendConfig.SSHUser` now defaults to `agent` (new `defaultHarvesterSSHUser = vmAgentUser`); new `vmAgentUser`/`vmAgentHome` consts. **Riskiest-assumption discipline surfaced two cloud-init ordering traps**: (1) the `write-files` module runs *before* `users-groups`, so the agent user does not exist when secret files are written — they are now written **root-owned** (`vmWriteFilesOwner = root:root`); (2) pre-creating the user in `bootcmd` is unsafe because some cloud-init versions skip `ssh_authorized_keys` application for a pre-existing user, breaking SSH login. Robust fix: the `users:` stanza creates `agent` canonically (reliable SSH key) and a `runcmd` step `chown -R agent:agent /home/agent` (`vmSecretFileOwner`) hands the files over after the user exists. New `TestBuildVMManifest_CreatesAgentUserForHomeParity` pins `name: agent` / `home: /home/agent` / no `ubuntu` user / root-owned write_files / the runcmd chown; owner + SSHUser assertions updated across the backend test files. **Kill-test still owed**: unit tests pin the manifest shape but cannot prove a live VM boots with codex auth working — a live boot (SSH as `agent`, confirm `~/.codex/auth.json` resolves to the mounted file) must run before declaring codex unblocked on harvester-vm (queued as the next slice).

New tests pin: nil-policy fallback (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_Defaults`), per-stage lookup matrix (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_LookupHits`), validation errors on unknown keys/values/empty strings (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_Validate`), and a YAML roundtrip with the canonical spec example (`TestPolicy_StageSubstrate_Roundtrip`). **No runtime consumer yet** — the field is operator-configurable but the spawn dispatcher still picks backend at startup via `DEVBOX_BACKEND` env. Plan-doc 117 follow-up list updated: 6/8 marked ✅, mills + otel SSE wiring + staleness coverage for the 6 newly-aligned stores recorded as the next candidate slices.

- **Mills KPIWriter populates the five frontend Overview cards + Mills sub-tab count badges + Pipelines run-id shortening** (`pkg/mills/kpi_writer.go`, `pkg/mills/kpi_writer_test.go`, `pkg/mills/store/dao_pipeline.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/Mills/PipelinesPanel.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/ViewShell.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, services/loom-core!TBD): the existing `KPIWriter` was already recording `gate_pass_rate` + `cost_per_merged_pipeline_usd` (the latter gated on `mergedRuns > 0`), but `MillsKPIRow.svelte` renders five cards consuming a different key set (`cost_per_merged_change_usd`, `slice_to_merge_p50_seconds`, `gate_pass_rate`, `auto_merge_rate`, `regression_rate`) — so on the live cluster only one card lit up. **Backend**: `KPIWriter.snapshot` now also computes `cost_per_merged_change_usd` (today aliases per-pipeline cost until backlog→change attribution lands in Phase 8+), `auto_merge_rate` (= `mergedRuns / (mergedRuns + escalatedRuns)` — Mills always uses the auto-merge path so this measures autonomous-success rate), `regression_rate` (= `escalatedRuns / (mergedRuns + escalatedRuns)` — best-effort proxy until post-merge regression tracking exists), `council_roi` (= `mergedRuns / councilCost`), and `slice_to_merge_p50_seconds` (median wall-clock from `started_at` to `ended_at` over runs that reached state=done in the window). Median is computed in Go because SQLite lacks a built-in percentile function; new `PipelineDAO.ListByStateSince(ctx, state, since)` returns only the merged runs in the window so the writer doesn't pull the full table. All five keys are conditionally omitted when their denominator is zero so cards render "—" rather than misleading "$0" / "0%". New tests `TestKPIWriter_FrontendKeys_PopulatedWhenDataAvailable` / `TestKPIWriter_FrontendKeys_OmittedWhenInsufficientData` / `TestKPIWriter_P50_OddCountReturnsExactMiddle` pin the present/absent contract and both median branches. **Frontend (polish)**: `ViewShell` gains an optional `count?: number` field on its `subViews` prop, rendered as a small pill next to the tab label when `count > 0`; `App.svelte` enriches Mills sub-tabs (`pipelines`, `backlog`, `council`, `eval`, `policy`) with live counts from `millsStore` so an operator sees at a glance which tabs hold activity. Mills `PipelinesPanel.svelte` ids (~40 chars) are elided to `prefix-18…suffix-4` in the table while the row's `title` keeps the full id for hover + the drilldown drawer's click-to-copy. `pnpm build` clean; `go test ./pkg/mills/...` green across 13 packages.

- **HUD responsive layout + operator embed subset (Slice B5)** (`internal/hud/app.go`, `internal/hud/routes.go`, `internal/hud/domain_adapters.go`, `cmd/loom/hud.go`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/embedConfig.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/stores/router.svelte.ts`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/App.svelte`, `internal/hud/frontend/src/lib/components/shared/{DataTable,DetailDrawer}.svelte`, `internal/hud/app_test.go`, services/loom-core!TBD): two coordinated pieces of the HUD UX overhaul. **(1) Responsive ≤800px layout.** Extends the existing 768px breakpoint to 800px and reflows the top `.nav-tabs` into a bottom-fixed bar (thumb-zone navigation) with `min-height: 44px` tap targets and `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` aware padding. `DataTable` switches to stacked-card mode at ≤800px — header hidden, each `<tr>` becomes a vertically-stacked block card so wide horizontal tables stay readable on phones without horizontal scroll. `DetailDrawer` becomes full-screen at ≤800px with a slide-up animation, stopping short of the bottom-fixed nav. **(2) Operator embed subset.** New `EmbedSubset` field on `hud.Config` plus `--subset` CLI flag (`loom hud --embed --subset operator`) gates the HUD UI to Overview + Operations/Fleet + Activity/Stream when running inside another product's iframe.
